# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1996 Centennial

## Introduction by Lokanātha Swami

When we first set out the principles and goals for the Śrīla Prabhupāda Centennial, we thought that the celebrations could be viewed as a campaign to elect Śrīla Prabhupāda as the spiritual master of the modern world! Just as political campaigns put forward a party and its leaders, the campaign for the Centennial would present the philosophical hierarchy of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, His *avatāra* for the age, and Śrīla Prabhupāda, the spiritual master who set forth the Lord’s path for the modern world. We would establish in the minds of all that it was he, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who delivered the manifesto for the golden age through his books and built the house in which the whole world can live in peace.

**“My Mantra-Upāsaka”**

The *Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa* describes a conversation that took place in Dvārakā between Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Gaṅgā-devī. Speaking on behalf of all the sacred rivers, Gaṅgā-devī expressed her fears at the advent of the Age of Quarrel and the imminent departure of the Lord back to the spiritual world. She said, “O Lord, we can see that Your pastimes are about to end, and people are becoming more and more materialistic. You have been very merciful to us. After You leave, however, all the sinful people of Kali-yuga will bathe in our waters, and we will become overwhelmed with sinful reactions.”

Smiling, the Lord replied, “Be patient. After five thousand years My *mantra-upāsaka* \[worshiper of the holy name\] will appear in this world and spread the chanting of My holy name everywhere. Not only in Bhārata-varṣa but also all over the world people will chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. By this chanting the whole world will become *eka-varṇa,* one designation, namely, *hari-bhaktas,* devotees of Lord Hari. And because the devotees of Lord Hari are so pure, anyone who contacts them will become purified from sinful reactions. These pure devotees will visit Bhārata-varṣa and purify you of sinful reactions by bathing in your sacred waters. This period of worldwide chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa will continue for ten thousand years.” This *mantra-upāsaka* is Śrīla Prabhupāda. He himself often said that there will be a period of ten thousand years, a golden age, within Kali-yuga, when the whole world will become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

**“My Senapati-Bhakta”**

In another book, entitled *Caitanya-maṅgala,* it is recorded that Nārada Muni, while visiting the spiritual world, came upon Lord Gaurāṅga. The Lord told him of His mission to destroy the sinful activities of the Age of Kali. “With the powerful chopper of *nāma-saṅkīrtana,*” He said, “I’ll cut the hard knots of demoniac desires from the hearts of everyone. Even if the sinners reject religion or flee to foreign countries, still they will get My mercy. I will send *mor senapati-bhakta* to go there and deliver them.” *Mor* means “My,” *senapati* means “military field commander,” and *bhakta* means “devotee.” *Mor senapati-bhakta* thus refers to that devotee who will lead Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* army, or in other words that devotee whom Lord Caitanya will empower to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world. That commander of the *saṅkīrtana* army is Śrīla Prabhupāda.

**The Ācārya of the Golden Age**

Ātmatattva Prabhu has done thorough research in many *śāstras* and explains how they reveal Śrīla Prabhupāda’s very extraordinary position as the *ācārya* of the Golden Age. He recounts how, for thousands of years, preparations were made to set the scene for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s arrival. Lord Caitanya Himself played a major role, arranging and adjusting things, and the previous *ācāryas* kept themselves very busy as the time of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s arrival got closer. In one of his presentations, Ātmatattva explains the position of Śrīla Prabhupāda in a nutshell, using the example of a bow and arrow.

The four bends of the bow represent the four Vaiṣṇava *sampradāyas,* and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is the string that connects them. Without the string, a bow is useless. Lord Caitanya came and strung the bow. Next an arrow was needed, along with the proper direction for it to be shot. Usually an arrow has feathers at the end, which direct its course. In this case, the six Gosvāmīs are the feathers. The shaft of the arrow is the Gauḍīya Mission of sixty-four *maṭhas* established by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Mahārāja. The last element missing was the sharp point at the tip of the arrow. That sharp point is Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. This is the part of the arrow that hits the target and reaches the goal. And the target is every town and village.

**Five Symptoms of the Founder-Ācārya**

Śrīla Prabhupāda founded ISKCON. He did the groundwork. He is the founder-*ācārya,* and we must understand his position as such. Some or maybe all of the problems that arose in our movement after Śrīla Prabhupāda left have their origin in our not properly understanding this position of founder-*ācārya*. A scripture from the Śrī *sampradāya* called *Prapannāmṛta Tapana* explains that a founder-*ācārya* is known by five symptoms.

· First, he is *udhārika,* which means that he is the savior of everyone. The *Prapannāmṛta Tapana* goes on to explain that those who come after the founder-*ācārya* in the disciplic succession, who act as spiritual masters, are *upakārika,* his helpers. They are never to be equated, even after hundreds of generations, with the founder-*ācārya*.  
·Second, he is *dīnābhaya*. *Dīna* means “very fallen,” and *abhaya* means “to remove fear” or “fearless.” The founder-*ācārya* removes the fear of all the fallen souls by his teachings, whose nature is that anyone anywhere who takes shelter of them will become fearless.  
·Third, he gives purports to the scriptures carrying the Vaiṣṇava *siddhānta,* such as *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and makes them available to the world in *mahā-grantha,* or great books.  
·Fourth, he also gives purports to the songs of the Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas*.  
·Fifth, his name becomes identified with the philosophy of Lord Viṣṇu Himself.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s position as the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON exactly corresponds to this ancient definition of founder-*ācārya*. Establishing a relationship with a spiritual master in the line of Śrīla Prabhupāda first of all means establishing a relationship with him as the founder-*ācārya*.

**ISKCON Founder-Ācārya**

In the world today, especially in India, the title of Yugācārya is claimed by many. Yugācārya means “the *ācārya* for this age.” Naturally, it seems to apply to Śrīla Prabhupāda. However, if we consider the bright future that awaits the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement as per the predictions of the scriptures, we may rather see the title of ISKCON founder-*ācārya* as the most appropriate one for Śrīla Prabhupāda. The present times are just the beginning years of ISKCON. The Society is only thirty years old, and our ISKCON universe is not so vast yet. But as ISKCON keeps growing more and more over the next hundreds and thousands of years and the *saṅkīrtana* movement reaches every town and village, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s fame as ISKCON founder-*ācārya* will prevail all over the world. Vaiṣṇavas are famous as *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī.* As the preaching increases, his fame will increase unlimitedly.

**GBC Resolutions**

In 1994, ISKCON’s Governing Body Commission passed a significant, appropriate, and timely resolution to establish Śrīla Prabhupāda’s unique position Excerpts from this resolution are as follows:

· To fulfill the previous *ācāryas’* desire for a united worldwide preaching organization to expand Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission, Śrīla Prabhupāda founded the International Society for Krishna Consciousness as a distinct branch of the Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava *sampradāya*. Therefore he is the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON.

· Śrīla Prabhupāda is the foundational *śikṣā-guru* for all ISKCON devotees because he has realized and presented the teachings of the previous *ācāryas* of the Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya *sampradāya* appropriately for the modern age.

· Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books are the embodiment of his teachings and should be accepted as the standard by all future generations of ISKCON.

· Every ISKCON spiritual master is responsible for guiding his disciples to follow Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Qualities and His Followers’ Love for Him

But it is not by predictions of *śāstras* or by some legislation that Śrīla Prabhupāda can most securely be accepted and established in a unique, exalted position. These are insufficient. In fulfilling the predictions in the scriptures about the appearance of an extraordinary person, the Lord does all the necessary follow-up. He sends the most qualified personality, decorated with all the qualities of a pure devotee, to be the *ācārya*. And indeed Śrīla Prabhupāda is a most extraordinary person. Without a doubt, he exhibited all the characteristics of a soul totally surrendered to the Lord.

If we look at the list of qualities possessed by a pure devotee, we can see that Śrīla Prabhupāda had them all to a superlative degree. He had compassion for all living entities. He had unshakable faith in Kṛṣṇa and pure attachment to satisfying His desires. He was completely free from desires for name, fame, and glory. Not a single trace of lust, envy, or greed could be found in him. His humility was a gem. His determination was ironlike. His patience was infinite. His humor was sweet and sublime. His kindness was all-pervading. His enthusiasm was boundless. He was expert, silent, poetic, forgiving, and detached. He was a great spiritual master, an accomplished Vedic scholar, and a pure, confidential servant of Kṛṣṇa.

One who examines Śrīla Prabhupāda’s qualities will find it clear why Śrīla Prabhupāda commands such love and following. Sincere persons recognize him at first glance as the Lord’s empowered messenger.

This Vyāsa-pūjā book is full of sentiments of love for Śrīla Prabhupāda. As one goes through it page after page, one will smell the sweet fragrance of these loving offerings to Śrīla Prabhupāda by his followers. And it is not just his direct disciples who love him. The intensity of the love of his granddisciples for him is no less. This love is contagious because it springs from the heart, which is the sitting place of the soul. One generation will pass on this genuine love for Śrīla Prabhupāda to the next generation, and from generation to generation this love for him will be maintained for thousands of years.

**“You Do As I Did”**

One of the petals of the Lotus of Centennial Activities is Increasing Prabhupāda Consciousness. Consciousness is the driving force of the living entity. It dictates all that one does. When we become fully Prabhupāda conscious, Śrīla Prabhupāda will take total hold of our thoughts, feelings, and emotions. A devotee who has achieved this level of Prabhupāda consciousness will even think, feel, and do as he did. This is genuinely following in his footsteps. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s vision was for a spiritual movement that would last ten thousand years. We should carefully study how he implemented all his different plans and projects. ISKCON is his movement. If we want to do as he did, we need to clearly understand the answers to such questions as What was his vision for ISKCON? What were his desires? What were his instructions in different fields of activities? How did he manage the Society?

**Prayer**

On the occasion of the hundredth anniversary of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance, we are humbly praying not to forget him for a moment. In this way we will never forget Kṛṣṇa, because Śrīla Prabhupāda himself has attained the perfect stage of pure absorption in Kṛṣṇa.

Lokanātha Swami

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Tamāl Krishna Goswami

For a quarter century the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust has published an annual volume commemorating the appearance day of ISKCON’s founder-*ācārya,* our beloved spiritual master Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Each volume has been memorably graced by devoted offerings and specially selected photos. But this year’s book is unlike any of the others because it commemorates Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Centennial anniversary.

Centennial celebrations are important for every institution with a history long enough to merit them. They may commemorate their founder’s birth anniversary, as we are doing, or an important event in their history, like the date of their founding. Those celebrating the third, fourth, or fifth centennial have even greater reason to rejoice, for their cause has proven its worthiness by enduring the test of time. We can just imagine, therefore, the euphoria of a millennial event. In four years hence we shall be able to witness the magnitude of its madness.

I doubt that ISKCON devotees will share in this euphoria, apart from the opportunity it will provide us for preaching. We will not be fooled by the proclamations of millennial celebrants, for our thoughts have been sobered by Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teachings. While they herald as a grand victory human civilization’s rise from the “Dark Ages,” we devotees may have quite the opposite view. We shall agree that previous times were dark, but will strongly argue that the present time is not much brighter. Rather, we see the darkness day by day increasing as Kali spreads his influence.

Lord Kṛṣṇa foresaw this impending gloom, originally brought about by His earthly departure. Through the agency of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, His literary incarnation, He provided the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to illumine the path of self-realization for the people in general of this otherwise dark Age of Kali. Vyāsa told of God, and Godhead is light.

What is this light which Vyāsa revealed? Not the ordinary illumination of the sun or moon, which merely illumine modern civilization’s misdirected wanderings. Spiritual light, on the other hand, has a transformative power that enables those who take advantage of it to distinguish clearly reality from illusion. Those lacking such spiritual vision merely see the shadowlike reflections of reality. Believing such illusions to be real, they march forward into the darkness, chasing imaginary truths. Only the devotees, guided by the torchlight of transcendental knowledge gifted by Vyāsa, march in the opposite direction—back to home, back to Godhead.

As the representative of Vyāsa for this modern age, Śrīla Prabhupāda stood as a colossus astride this planet, holding aloft the torch of transcendental knowledge. Through his words and deeds he reinterpreted past history, reevaluated present events, and shaped the course of the future for the next ten thousand years. To properly and fully assess the effect his presence has had this world may be possible only after centuries have passed. What field of knowledge has he not commented upon? What aspect of human endeavor has he not redirected?

That time will surely come when the people of the world will recognize Śrīla Prabhupāda’s preeminence among all the great personalities of history. Time alone separates our humble devotee gatherings today from the hundreds of millions who will celebrate his second Centennial and the others to follow. When his Bhaktivedanta purports have chastened their intellects and calmed the heady euphoria of their temporal achievements, they will appreciate that their leaders are misleaders, the blind leading the blind;

that the glut in purchasable commodities has only increased human desire and tightened the knot of material bondage; that in spite of all discoveries in the field of medicine and improvement in human welfare, no cure has yet been discovered for birth, old age and death; that their furthest explorations into outer space can reach but one fourth of the Lord’s creation; that their philosophical investigation has led to existential hopelessness, concluding in suicide rather than enlightenment; that their literary taste prefers pornography and comics, food for crows; their taste in music and art, cacophony and chaos; their taste in family relationships, enmity; their taste in human relationships, animalism; their taste in education, everything but the science of the soul; their taste in religion, everything but the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

There is no end to this index of misconceived human “advances.” What the millennial celebrants herald as human triumph, devotees debunk as mankind’s folly.

When will the world catch up to Śrīla Prabhupāda? When will it realize that he offers the solutions to the questions that have perplexed the best thinkers through the ages? When will it throw off the shackles of sensual bondage, cease following blind leaders and believing false prophets? When will it hoist Śrīla Prabhupāda upon its shoulders and glorify him as he truly deserves?

When, depends on us. Śrīla Prabhupāda has drawn up the blueprint for future world sanity and placed it in our hands with the words, “I will be satisfied if it is recorded in history that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement saved the world.”

No small task. Millennial festivities are being prepared, speeches written lauding the heroes of old, praising those of today, and forecasting future successes. Funds are being collected, edifices erected, great personalities selected to bid the second millennium adieu and usher the third anew. Why should we allow this colossal endeavor to go in vain? Better that we appropriate it for a proper cause, as Kṛṣṇa once did the sacrifice meant for Indra. Wealth, intelligence, and all abilities are to be sacrificed for Viṣṇu. This is *yajña*! If the whole world utilizes all of its resources to glorify Kṛṣṇa’s purest devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda, imagine the good fortune for all! Such a full manifestation of the *saṅkīrtana-yajña* will alone satisfy Śrīla Prabhupāda’s desire.

Let us not waste a single moment. Let us dedicate our every thought, word, and deed to this goal, for it will be another thousand years before we again have such a chance. Are there any among us so daring, so bold, to remain here for that long? Better seize the opportunity at hand. Now is the time. All together …

Chant—  
Śrīla Prabhupāda kī jaya!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda kī jaya!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda kī jaya!

Tamāl Krishna Goswami  
Governing Body Commissioner

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Badrinārāyan dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Last year I served as the chairman of the international GBC, and I was amazed by the energy of your ISKCON. I remember when you saw the advertisement for the Sherwin\\Williams brand of paint—a paint can pouring its contents out over a globe. You remarked, “This is Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s movement.” You even liked the paint company’s motto: “We cover the world.” Because of your words I have always had faith that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement could be spread all over the planet, but my time as chairman revealed to me graphically that it not only *can* but actually *is* spreading and flourishing in innumerable towns and villages around the world. This is all happening by your potency and mercy.

While lying in Vṛndāvana during the last days of your *vapu* presence with us, you remarked how even in that condition “I am still beating on my *mṛdaṅga* \[your book translation and distribution\], and it is being heard around the world.” To our great relief and satisfaction, you are still beating on your *mṛdaṅga,* and it is increasingly being heard the world round.

Along with the scope of your ISKCON, I also felt its weight. How you managed to travel around the world twelve times and still be so peaceful that your self-winding wristwatch stopped is in itself amazing. As I advance in years I feel the effects, and I am obliged to adjust my pace accordingly. But you never wavered or slowed in the service of your spiritual master and Lord Caitanya. Certainly yours are the activities of a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra,* a *jīva* soul directly empowered by the Supreme Lord.

When you withdrew your physical presence, at first I was afraid that my memories of you and appreciation of all you have given me might fade over time. Again to my great relief and satisfaction, this has not occurred. Rather they have increased.

When standing on a mountain, one can often not appreciate its full size and scope. One sees only the rocks, trees, and water in the immediate field of vision. But with distance from the mountain, by traveling down the slope to the plain and looking back at the mountain, one then begins to take in the mountain’s full grandeur.

With the passage of time, I have increasingly come to appreciate your unique qualities and accomplishments and the timeless and univeral benefit found in following your words. You told us once, “I have given you the structure; now all you have to do is decorate it.” You paused in contemplation for a moment and then added, “Actually, I have also given you all the decorations … all you have to do is go and hang them.”

*tarko ’pratiṣṭhaḥ śrutayo vibhinnā*  
*nāsāv ṛṣir yasya mataṁ na bhinnam*  
*dharmasya tattvaṁ nihitaṁ guhāyāṁ*  
*mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ*

**“Dry arguments are inconclusive. A great personality whose opinion does not differ from others is not considered a great sage. Simply by studying the** ***Vedas,*** **which are variegated, one cannot come to the right path by which religious principles are understood. The solid truth of religious principles is hidden in the heart of an unadulterarted, self-realized person. Consequently, one should accept whatever progressive path the** ***mahājanas*** **advocate.”**

We don’t need to add or subtract anything. All we need to do is follow the path laid out by you, and even in that you have given us the perfect example by your dedication to simply following the orders of your own spiritual master.

In my travels for preaching and for my business, I see that all over the world sane people are crying out for redeeming social values and virtuous leaders. But they do not know the real cause of their problems or the process necessary for relief.

Prahlāda Mahārāja notes the real cause of their suffering: “O my Lord, because of lusty desires from the very beginning of one’s birth, the functions of one’s senses, mind, life, body, religion, patience, intelligence, shyness, opulence, strength, memory, and truthfulness are vanquished.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.10.8)

As for the cure, one can test if the pot of rice is done by sampling one grain. The members of ISKCON were also conditioned souls suffering under the same burden of burning desires and unhappiness. But by your sublime qualities and powerful message you have managed to capture and change our minds and hearts. You are the leader who has set the standard the whole world can follow.

The first time I heard you speak, you gave a simple yet profound example: “All material qualities and efforts are like so many zeros. But when you put the ‘one’ of Kṛṣṇa in front of them, they become a hundred, or a thousand, or a million.”

Nārada Muni further explains, “O good soul, does not a thing applied theraputically cure a disease which was caused by the very same thing?” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.33)

When describing His opulence to Arjuna, Lord Kṛṣṇa says, “I am victory, I am adventure” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 10.36)

You have given us a new life full of mission, purpose, adventure, and ultimately, if we are faithful, victory over suffering and death.

Please bless me that I will become exclusively and selflessly attached to your glorious service. Lord Kapiladeva explains this essential principle to his mother: “You must seek attachment to such holy men, for this counteracts the pernicious effects of material attachment.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.25.24)

And Devahuti replies: “I have fortunately been instructed by You, and thus a great favor has been bestowed on me. I thank the Lord that I have listened with open ears to Your pure words.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.22.7)

I cannot measure my good fortune to have been instructed by you, and I am daily experiencing what a great favor your words have bestowed upon me. I too thank the Lord that I somehow or other listened to your pure words with open ears, and I beg for your mercy that I may some day become a qualified son in the noble Bhaktivedanta line.

Your insignificant servant,  
Badrinārāyan dāsa

## Bhakti Chāru Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

I vividly remember the day when I was first introduced to you. I was fortunately included in the team that was going to travel with you from Allahabad to Calcutta. During the Kumbha-melā, one morning you decided all of a sudden that you wanted to leave Allahabad and go to Calcutta. The train started at night, with hundreds of devotees at the platform shedding their silent tears due to your imminent separation. I felt extremely fortunate to have this rare opportunity to travel with you.

Next morning I was taken to your compartment and introduced to you by Bhavānanda Mahārāja. Very mercifully you asked me to sit next to you. I felt so embarrassed. How could I sit next to you on the same seat, when my actual position is to become a speck of dust at your lotus feet? However, you insisted.

After a few initial exchanges you instructed me to translate your books into Bengali. That was the first instruction I received from you, and it became my life and soul. Since then my life has practically revolved around that instruction. By 1986 I had translated most of your titles—the *Kṛṣṇa* book, *The Nectar of Devotion, Śrī Īśopaniṣad,* and so on. I tried to offer the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu on the occasion of His 500th anniversary. Then only *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* was left to be translated. However, when I became a GBC in 1987, my most important service, translating your books, practically stopped. I justified this inability, thinking that saving your ISKCON at that most critical time was my most important business. But still, if at the end of each very hectic day I found that I had not spent any time translating your books, I felt as if the whole day had been wasted. Finally, in 1993 I resolved that I must resume my most important service with all sincerity and complete the translation of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to offer you on your hundredth anniversary.

Since then I have freed myself considerably from my GBC responsibilities and dedicated myself once again to translating your books. About a year ago, in 1995, when it seemed that it would be possible to reach my target and offer the Bengali *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to you on your 100th anniversary, I wanted to do something additional to glorify Your Divine Grace for your centennial year. I felt that although you are the most glorious personality in this world, so few people knew you. Therefore I decided to make a television serial on your life. I knew it would cost a lot of money, and I did not have any idea where the money would come from. Still I went ahead with the conviction that even if I am able to make only one episode, this world will benefit from it immensely.

While working on that project I could see how Kṛṣṇa actually wanted you to be glorified. It seemed that He was making all the arrangements. He arranged for the best director for this job, who is now chanting sixteen rounds and following the four regulative principles. Around him has developed a very nice team f professionals, all of whom have become your sincere admirers, and to make it complete one of the best cinematographers in the Indian movie industry has joined our team and is helping us make this serial an unforgettable piece of art.

We started shooting on March 8th, and on March 25th we submitted the first episode to the Indian National Television company, Doordarsan. The director general of Doordarsan was so impressed upon seeing it that he accepted it immediately and assured us he would give us very good slot for telecasting the serial. I hope that this serial will run for five years telecasting your glorious life all over India, to every town and village. I am confident that many other TV stations, like the British Broadcasting Company (BBC), will also telecast it all over the world. In this way we will be able to take you to every home and change the destiny of this world.

This year is a very special year for us. Lots of wonderful things are happening all over the world. Our centennial ministry, under His Holiness Lokanāth Mahārāja’s leadership, has been getting ready to celebrate your centennial in a gorgeous way. His Holiness Bhakti Svarūpa Dāmodara Mahārāja is arranging a world congress to synthesize science and religion in Calcutta. His Holiness Gopāla Krishna Mahārāja is going to inaugurate a beautiful temple in Delhi. His Holiness Jayapatāka Mahārāja will inaugurate an equally gorgeous one in Bangalore. And Yaśomatī-nandana Prabhu will inaugurate one in Ahmedabad. Śrīniketana Prabhu, another disciple of yours, is also trying to complete a colossal temple in Mauritius to offer you on the occasion of your hundredth anniversary. So many of your extremely dedicated devotees are going to make so many unique offerings to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Today I also came to your lotus feet to offer the Bengali translation of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and a television serial on the life of the most glorious *mahā-bhāgavata,* Your Divine Grace. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept my humble offering.

Aspiring to remain at your lotus feet eternally,  
Bhakti Charu Swami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Gurudeva Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet on this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, the hundredth anniversary of your appearance. May your fame and glories be constantly chanted in choice words by all those fortunate souls whose hearts have been touched and transformed by your merciful glance upon them. I beg to try to glorify Your Divine Grace, although I am most unqualified to do so.

Like hundreds and thousands of other conditioned souls in this present fallen Age of Kali, I have had the greatest and rarest good fortune of coming in contact with you, a pure devotee of the Lord.

*‘sādhu-saṅga’, ‘sādhu-saṅga’——sarva-śāstre kaya*  
*lava-mātra sādhu-saṅge sarva-siddhi haya*

**“The verdict of all revealed scriptures is that by even a moment’s association with a pure devotee, one can attain all success.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta Madhya-līlā*** **22.54)**

The pure devotee of the Lord is the personification of mercy and compassion in this material world. Indeed, without his causeless mercy none can attain the shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. By his mere presence the entire world becomes illumined and full of all good fortune. And by his absence the whole world becomes bereft of all auspiciousness. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for having come into my life, for I was simply groping in the darkness of ignorance and illusion until I met your surrendered and dedicated devotees. I remain eternally indebted to you and to your devotees.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have taught us that serving the instructions of the spiritual master, his *vāṇī,* is more important than serving his physical body, his *vapu*. This is the hard lesson we have all had to learn since your physical departure from this material world nineteen years ago. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I never could serve you directly when you were physically present, nor could I even speak to you personally. Yet I remain fully confident and convinced that you are my eternal spiritual father and that all your instructions are meant for my gradual advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I simply pray that I may receive your blessings to understand and follow these instructions to your full satisfaction.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, hearing your transcendental voice always brings me immense comfort and reassurance. Seeing your transcendental self in photos or on videos always rejuvenates my consciousness. Observing how new devotees become enlivened by hearing of your transcendental pastimes also brings me pride and great pleasure. The same joy is there when I hear your older disciples share their realizations and their personal memories of you and how eternally indebted they remain to you. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for continuing to shower your mercy upon us all.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are simply so great a personality. Never in my life had I met such a person. Never in my life had I fathomed that such an individual existed. I am but a small child—inexperienced, ignorant, and foolish. I simply cannot sufficiently appreciate or describe your greatness. I may speak of “surrender unto your lotus feet,” but actually I am not so surrendered. I may speak of “Kṛṣṇa consciousness,” but actually I am not at all conscious of Kṛṣṇa. I may speak of *kṛṣṇa-prema,* but actually I have no love for Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa is *su-hṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānām,* our closest and most dear friend, our eternal well-wisher—and yet I have lost contact with that friend, I have forgotten Him. How great is my misfortune! How fallen is my condition! Our biggest *anartha,* or fault, explains Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, is to have forgotten Kṛṣṇa.

I do not know when I will regain my lost friend Kṛṣṇa, but I am hoping against hope that one day I will find Him. Actually, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are my only hope! For you are eternally engaged in loving exchanges with Kṛṣṇa … you have Kṛṣṇa. Let me simply run behind you, begging and crying that you please always keep me close to your lotus feet so that one day I may also qualify to serve Him.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow me to become a surrendered soul at your divine lotus feet. Please forgive my innumerable offenses and continue to shower your mercy upon this undeserving fallen soul. I cannot sufficiently express my gratitude for what you have done by giving me and countless others this unique opportunity of pure devotional service. May your fame and glories continue to be known and chanted throughout the three worlds. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your aspiring servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In this historic year, the 100thappearance anniversary of Your Divine Grace, please bless me so that my mind remains fixed in your divine instructions and so that I can carry on my insignificant *seva* in your divine mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the personification of Śrīla Nārada Muni and Śrīla Vyāsadeva in the 20th century. You are the embodiment of Nārada-Vyāsa in your life’s mission under the guidance of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.

The scientific and philosophical teachings of Your Divine Grace are distinctly summarized in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.22, in the chapter entitled “Nārada’s Instructions on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*”:

*idaṁ hi puṁsas tapasaḥ śrutasya vā*  
*sviṣṭasya sūktasya ca buddhi-dattayoḥ*  
*avicyuto ’rthaḥ kavibhir nirūpito*  
*yad-uttamaśloka-guṇānuvarṇanam*

**“Learned circles have positively concluded that the infallible purpose of the advancement of knowledge, namely austerities, study of the Vedas, sacrifice, chanting of hymns and charity, culminates in the transcendental descriptions of the Lord, who is defined in choice poetry.”**

Your Divine Grace writes in the purport, “Human intellect is developed for advancement of learning in art, science, philosophy, physics, chemistry, psychology, economics, politics, etc. By culture of such knowledge the human society can attain perfection of life. This perfection of life culminates in the realization of the Supreme Being, Viṣṇu. The *śruti* therefore directs that those who are actually advanced in learning should aspire for the service of Lord Viṣṇu. Unfortunately, persons who are enamored by the external beauty of *viṣṇu-māyā* do not understand that culmination of perfection or self-realization depends on Viṣṇu. *Viṣṇu-māyā* means sense enjoyment, which is transient and miserable. Those who are entrapped by *viṣṇu-māy*ā utilize advancement of knowledge for sense enjoyment. Śrī Nārada Muni has explained that all paraphernalia of the cosmic universe is but an emanation from the Lord out of His different energies because the Lord has set in motion, by His inconceivable energy, the actions and reactions of the created manifestation. They have come to be out of His energy, they rest on His energy, and after annihilation they merge into Him. Nothing is, therefore, different from Him, but at the same time the Lord is always different from them.

“When advancement of knowledge is applied in the service of the Lord, the whole process becomes absolute. The Personality of Godhead and His transcendental name, fame, glory, etc., are all nondifferent from Him. Therefore, all the sages and devotees of the Lord have recommended that the subject matter of art, science, philosophy, physics, chemistry, psychology and all other branches of knowledge should be wholly and solely applied in the service of the Lord. Art, literature, poetry, painting, etc., may be used in glorifying the Lord. The fiction writers, poets and celebrated literateurs are generally engaged in writing of sensuous subjects, but if they turn towards the service of the Lord they can describe the transcendental pastimes of the Lord. Vālmīki was a great poet, and similarly Vyāsadeva is a great writer, and both of them have absolutely engaged themselves in delineating the transcendental activities of the Lord and by doing so have become immortal. Similarly, science and philosophy also should be applied in the service of the Lord. There is no use presenting dry speculative theories for sense gratification. Philosophy and science should be engaged to establish the glory of the Lord. Advanced people are eager to understand the Absolute Truth through the medium of science, and therefore a great scientist should endeavor to prove the existence of the Lord on a scientific basis. Similarly, philosophical speculations should be utilized to establish the Supreme Truth as sentient and all-powerful. Similarly, all other branches of knowledge should always be engaged in the service of the Lord. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* also the same is affirmed. All ‘knowledge’ not engaged in the service of the Lord is but nescience. Real utilization of advanced knowledge is to establish the glories of the Lord, and that is the real import. Scientific knowledge engaged in the service of the Lord and all similar activities are all factually *hari-kīrtana,* or glorification of the Lord.”

This is the synthetic and holistic vision of Your Divine Grace. Your teaching is that all forms of knowledge can be utilized in the service of the Lord. If this is assimilated, the purpose of life’s mission can be accomplished. Thus scientific knowledge becomes useful if it can be used in the glorification of the Lord. This is the essence of scientific preaching.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given a strong warning to the materialist scientists reminding them that they should not mislead the innocent masses by falsely presenting that the material scientific process is the sole epistemological criterion and that matter is the only ontological state for comprehending the nature of reality. You have pointed out that the main difficulty with the material scientists is the wholesale commitment to empiricism. This paradigm needs to be changed. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have presented the Bhāgavata and Bhaktivedāntic paradigm just to correct this mistake. In this effort you have translated all the relevant Vedic literatures into all the major languages of the world, thus educating the entire population of the world. Almost every thoughtful person is amazed to see how you have produced so many books within the short span of ten years. It is beyond the ken of any person. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are empowered by the Supreme Being, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, as the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva in the twentieth century.

Your teaching of the Bhāgavata paradigm can be assimilated from the *Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad* (1.1.4):

*dve vidye veditavya iti, ha sma yad brahma-vido vadanti—parā caivāparā ca.*

**“There are two categories of knowledge to be acquired by man, say the knowers of Brahman, the Absolute Truth. One is called** ***parā vidyā,*** **higher knowledge, and the other is called** ***aparā vidyā,*** **lower or ordinary knowledge.”**

Again, in the *Īśopaniṣad* (10) it is said:

*anyad evāhur vidyayānyad āhur avidyayā*  
*iti śuśruma dhīrāṇāṁ ye nas tad vicacakṣire*

**“The wise have explained that one result is derived from the culture of knowledge and that a different result is obtained from the culture of nescience.”**

Also, in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (7.4–5), Lord Kṛṣṇa proclaims,

*bhūmir āpo ’nalo vāyuḥ khaṁ mano buddhir eva ca*  
*ahaṅkāra itīyaṁ me bhinnā prakṛtir aṣṭadhā*

**“Solid, liquid, radiant, gaseous, ethereal, mind, intelligence ,and false ego are the eight separated states of My material energy. Besides these, O mighty-armed Arjuna, there is another, superior energy of Mine—the conscious living entities who are struggling with material nature and exploiting its resources.”**

Thus, in the paradigm celebrated by the *Upaniṣads* and the *Bhagavad-gītā,* two faces of the reality of nature are underlined: the material face, which is composed of the nonconscious entity—matter—and the spiritual face, which is composed of conscious entities—living beings, including man. Empirical sciences, because of their strict hypothetico-deductive procedure, try to perceive the reality of nature from the lower level and ignore the fact that there can be a transcendental perception of nature. This lower-level perception in the study of nature has ruled Western scientific knowledge over the last four hundred years. However, nature does not yield to the dictates of the scientific mind, and matter, therefore, cannot be the single ontological element in the study of nature. The worldwide, unanimous concern of theists and atheists, materialists and spiritualists, mechanists and nonmechanists about the future of the ecosystem is a clear indication that the mechanistic paradigm adopted by science needs a drastic change. In the Bhāgavata and Vedāntic paradigm, the Supreme Being, or Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, is the cause of all causes, the foundation of all foundations, *sarva-kāraṇa-kāraṇa,* and nature has to be looked at as a teleological system. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.10) Lord Kṛṣṇa offers the explanation:

*mayādhyakṣeṇa prakṛtiḥ sūyate sa-carācaram*  
*hetunānena kaunteya jagad viparivartate*

**“This material nature is working under My direction, O son of Kuntī, producing all moving and non-moving beings. Under its rule this manifestation is created and annihilated again and again.”**

In the entire spectrum of things created by the Supreme Being, man is regarded as the crown jewel of all creation. The singular focal point in the Bhāgavata paradigm is that man’s mission should be solely directed toward understanding his natural relationship with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. The first aphorism of the *Vedānta-sūtra* affirms, *athāto brahma-jijñāsā,* meaning “Now, therefore \[in the human form of life\], one should inquire about Brahman, the Absolute Truth.” This statement affirms that man’s basic role is to relate all his undertakings in the world to the spiritual dimensions of existence (*parā vidyā*). Scientific knowledge and all other aspects of knowledge are useful when applied properly. Thus the Vedāntic paradigm tells us that man has to see clearly his duty in the world; he has to be conscious of the fact that the foundation of his life is spiritual and that all his worldly affairs must be incorporated in this foundation. It is in this spiritual foundation of human existence that a solution to the manifold problems of our time is to be found. Thus the Bhāgavata or Vedāntic paradigm leads to a synthesis of science and religion or science and spirituality in the perception of nature and its variegated manifestations. From many recent developments in various fields of human activities, it appears that the materialistic scientific mind is beginning to recognize the spiritual dimension of nature. In this connection it is worthwhile to note a very significant landmark of the twentieth century. For the first time in history, more than thirty leading scientists from around the world, including a number of Nobel laureates, headed by Carl Sagan, the well-known cosmologist, made a moving appeal to all the religious leaders of the globe entitled “Preserving and Cherishing the Earth—An Appeal for Joint Commitment in Science and Religion.” They expressed profound concern for the protection of the ecosystem of our planet in a spirit of humility by realizing the inconceivable dimension of nature. A part of the appeal runs thus:

“We are now threatened by self-inflicted, swiftly- moving environmental alterations about whose long-term biological and ecological consequences we are still painfully ignorant—depletion of the protective ozone layer; a global warming unprecedented in the last 150 millennia; the obliteration of an acre of forest every second; the rapid-fire extinction of species: and the prospect of a global nuclear war that would put at risk most of the population of the Earth. There may well be other such dangers of which, in our ignorance, we are still unaware. Individually and cumulatively they represent a trap being set for the human species, a trap we are setting for ourselves. However principled and lofty (or naive and shortsighted) the justifications may have been for the activities that brought forth these dangers, separately and together they now imperil our species and many others. We are close to committing—many would argue we are already committing—what in religious language is sometimes called Crimes against Creation.…

“Problems of such magnitude, and solutions demanding so broad a perspective, must be recognized from the outset as having a religious as well as a scientific dimension. Mindful of our common responsibilities, we scientists—many of us long engaged in combating the environmental crisis—urgently appeal to the world religious community to commit, in word and deed, and as boldly as is required, to preserve the environment of the Earth.…

“As scientists, many of us have had profound experiences of awe and reverence before the universe. We understand that what is regarded as sacred is more likely to be treated with care and respect. Our planetary home should be so regarded. Efforts to safeguard and cherish the environment need to be infused with a vision of the sacred. At the same time, a much wider and deeper understanding of science and technology is needed. If we do not understand the problem, it is unlikely we will be able to fix it. Thus there is a vital role for both religion and science.”

Two hundred and seventy well-known spiritual leaders from eighty-three countries responded to and signed the appeal and presented it at the Moscow meeting of the Global Forum of Spiritual and Parliamentary Leaders, held January 15–19, 1990.

Similarly, the Union for Concerned Scientists (UCS) in the USA, which has a membership of over one hundred thousand leading scientists, prominent thinkers, and concerned citizens, including many Nobel laureates, is promoting various ways and means to help remove the environmental and ecological threats facing humanity. All these developments suggest the urgent need to include the moral and spiritual dimensions in man’s actions. The Second World Congress for the Synthesis of Science and Religion, to be held in Calcutta January 9–12, 1997, in honor and glorification of the 100thanniversary of your divine appearance, is a humble attempt to disseminate your divine teachings to the leaders of the world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we seek your divine blessings at every step so that we can present your teachings effectively.

In this most historic year of the celebration of the 100thappearance *utsava* of Your Divine Grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is most heartening to note that Your Divine Grace’s vision of educating and changing the perspective of the materialists and atheistic scientists is achieved at least in principle. You circled the globe several times at an advanced age distributing the timeless message of Bhāgavata science and philosophy for the benefit of everyone. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you will be known as the Nārada Muni of the twentieth century. Your glory as the personification of Nārada-Vyāsa of the twentieth century will be sung in every nook and corner of the world for generations to come. I feel most fortunate to be under your divine loving protection. All glory to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your most insignificant servant,  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti-tīrtha Swami

My dear eternal father Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you in your services to your spiritual master and to the world.

It was very wonderful being in Śrīdhām Māyāpur with so many of your disciples and granddisciples. This year’s festival shows how you took the order of your spiritual master so seriously. Due to the seeds you planted, so many men and women all around the world have come under the banners of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and have come to Māyāpur to honor your Centennial.

As we went on *parikrama,* how amazed the villagers were to see all of these colorful Vaiṣṇavas trying to absorb themselves in *caitanya-līlā*. Surely some were thinking, How has such a miracle taken place? But many knew it was due to the great mercy of the great *ācārya,* His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Every time we visited another sacred site, I felt so fortunate to be somehow associated with such a great heritage. I became more and more amazed, reflecting on how merciful you are. However, I must also admit how I felt simultaneously unfortunate that as we visited such places I was not able to presently perceive the transcendental *līlā*. I was thinking, How is it that we can be so close (in the same exact environment of the Supreme Lord) and at the same time be so far away and to be so dull? You have given us everything, but it is due to our own contamination that we are lacking.

Later, I thought that it is not just in these holy *dhāmas* where we have such great treasures that you have connected us with and given us, but every aspect of devotional service is similar. Everything we do in devotional service is full of mystery, adventure, and potencies. But many are dull like me and take so many things for granted.

It seems that all I can offer you on this most important Vyāsa-pūjā is a plea that you forgive me and those like me who have remained so neophyte and immature even though you gave and are giving us so many opportunities.

Since I am one of the Centennial coordinators for “Spreading the Holy Name” and “Enhancing Worldwide Recognition,” we are steadily trying to encourage the devotees in these areas. However, the real issue is not in creating something new or different but in properly appreciating what you have given and connected us with. When we do this, everything will ultimately take care of itself. I know this because you have emphatically told us so. But do I really believe this? Have I really accepted these considerations deep in my heart? Surely, I have not. For if I did, my heart would be open and this would allow me to behold these divine visions.

Dear eternal, beloved father, please open up my heart even though I am highly unqualified. Not for my sake, for I have little value, but so I will not continue to be an embarrassment to our great heritage and especially to you.

Your blind son,  
Bhakti-tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated and humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

As I admired last year’s and this year’s opening of your Puṣpa-samādhi in Śrīdhām Māyāpur, my profound respect and adoration for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, increased manyfold.

As I heard about and tried to participate in the wonderful activities of your auspicious Centennial Year, my profound respect and adoration for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, increases manyfold.

As I danced in the streets of downtown Calcutta and held in my hands that particular day’s newspaper with a wonderful Centennial-related article glorifying Your Divine Grace, my profound respect and adoration for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, increased manyfold.

As I reflected on various speeches delivered by senior devotees and influential personalities in glorification of Your Divine Grace during Centennial-related special occasions, my profound respect and adoration for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, increased manyfold.

As I heard the deep and meaningful realizations of my dear Godbrothers and Godsisters about Your Divine Grace, my profound respect and adoration for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, increased manyfold.

As I pondered over one of the recent publications about Your Divine Grace’s glories and expressed my earnest appreciation for it, the author asked me whether it increased my love for you. I hesitated to answer, feeling an inexplicable threat to the privacy of “my love” for Your Divine Grace. Politely I answered, “Of course.” Later I thought deeply about this point, and I have to admit that the limitation of “my love” for Your Divine Grace had been exposed. You showed your love and causeless mercy not only to me but to innumerable others as well. In this mundane world the increase of love for one person decreases the love for another, but the nature of spiritual love is just the opposite. I am feeling pain and at the same time relief to see the limiting boundary walls that surround my love for you collapse as more and more people begin to exhibit their appreciation and love for Your Divine Grace. You will certainly and inconceivably reciprocate their feelings.

As I notice how your true position as the founder-*ācārya* not only of ISKCON but of the next 10,000 years of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is gradually unfolding, my profound respect and adoration for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, increases manyfold.

The founder-*ācārya* is distinguished from all other *ācāryas*, and his qualities and activities are outlined in the *śāstras,* such as the *Prapannāmṛta Tarpaṇa:*

“Among all *ācāryas,* certainly he who is the deliverer of all people, who appears in this world for the purpose of removing the fears of the fallen souls, is the most perfect.”

“He sheds light on the ultimate meaning of the philosophical conclusions spoken by Kṛṣṇa, who is the speaker of *Bhagavad-gītā*. He also gives perfect purports to the evidences given by the *ṛṣis*.”

“He, the ocean of mercy, contributes great books for the welfare of the people that explain the meaning of Vedānta strictly in line with the previous Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas* and devotees. In this way he conquers all, defeating hundreds of different kinds of inauspicious philosophies by his arguments.”

A founder-*ācārya* holds a unique position for all time to come. Previously the *ācāryas* selected and severely tested the sincerity and qualifications of their disciples. But among the special characteristics of the founder-*ācārya* is his unlimited compassion. He accepts all those who take shelter at his lotus feet, without any discrimination.

You taught the whole world to become Kṛṣṇa conscious—to chant Kṛṣṇa’s names and render loving service unto Him. Now it is the turn of all the people of this world to become Prabhupāda conscious—to chant your name, to serve your mission, and thus to demonstrate their love for you. Only then will Lord Kṛṣṇa be fully satisfied and shower His divine blessings upon all.

As for myself, nothing compares to my profound adoration and ever-increasing love for Your Divine Grace, save and except that you have delivered me from the ocean of material existence.

Your unworthy servant,  
Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

My dear spiritual father, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You ordered your disciples to distribute books “by hook or by crook” and with “the ends justifying the means.” You told them that morality or immorality wasn’t an issue, and that a spiritual principle was at work that had nothing to do with the good and bad of the material world. Yet while executing that order, some disciples caused difficulties to other devotees, to others, and to the mission. You wrote in 1975:

“Regarding the controversy about book distribution techniques, you are right. Our occupation must be honest. Everyone should adore our members as honest. If we do something which is deteriorating to the popular sentiments of the public in favor of our movement, that is not good. Somehow or other we should not become unpopular in the public eye. These dishonest methods must be stopped. It is hampering our reputation all over the world. Money collected for feeding people in India should be collected under the name ISKCON Food Relief. Not any other name. And every farthing of that money must be sent to India, or better yet, buy food grains there and ship them here and we will distribute. But every farthing collected for that purpose must be used for that purpose.”

Yet, when I later brought up similar points to you in a conversation, your reply was not positive.

**Bhūrijana:** I don’t think there is so much enmity because of the books themselves.

**Prabhupāda:** No, I think that … I do not know what they are doing, but I have information that they’re not selling books.

**Bhūrijana:** Some I think. I think some.

**Prabhupāda:** But not …

**Bhūrijana:** Comparable amount.

**Prabhupāda:** Yes. Others who were transgressing the moral principle, they were selling more books. And those who are big moralists, they could not. … They should follow what their spiritual master says. Book-selling. There is no question of moral and immoral. They must sell. Just like in fighting. Where there is fight, the soldiers, to gain victory, there is no question of moral and immoral. He must....We want that book selling must be increased as much as possible. This we want. The same thing. Let the child take medicine. Never mind the father is speaking lies. Because as soon as he takes the medicine he’ll be benefited. End justifies the means. End is that everyone should have a Kṛṣṇa literature. Doesn’t matter what is the means. Because he has taken one Kṛṣṇa literature, that justifies everything. This is the principle.

You had explained the balance you desired years earlier, in 1972:

“Regarding the question you have raised about traveling *saṅkīrtana* parties and selling of books, yes, we want money. So that is the real preaching, selling books. Who can speak better than the books? At least whoever buys, he will look over. If you have to sell books, do it by hook or by crook. The real preaching is selling books. You should know the tactic how to sell without irritating. What your lecture will do for three minutes, but if he reads one page his life may be turned. We don’t want to irritate anyone, however. If he goes away by your aggressive tactics, then you are nonsense and it is your failure. Neither you could sell a book, neither he would remain. But if he buys a book, that is the real successful preaching. … So far irritation is concerned, a child is also irritated when he is given instructions, but that does not mean that we should stop....So if the *saṅkīrtana* parties in Satsvarūpa’s zone are irritating the public for selling so many books, that is one thing, but by their selling books they are doing the greatest service. What your one hour of preaching will do, they will hear and go away, but if they have one book at home, they will read, their friends will read, and something solid work is done. So the art is to sell many many books and not to irritate the public, so you may instruct all the others how to do this successfully. That is *sannyāsa*. That is GBC.”

Strongly implementing your instructions so forcefully presented, yet with balance, was not easy: “Sell books by any means,” “by hook or by crook,” but don’t irritate people by aggressive tactics or create unpopularity in “the public eye.” Yet, don’t stop book distribution even if the result is an “irritated” public. But don’t allow improprieties to continue.

Kṛṣṇa reserved for you, an empowered personality, a hearty challenge: to push us, who were generally young, passionate, and immature to act balanced, maturely, and with wisdom. We made mistakes. You relied for training on your leaders—the *sannyāsīs* and GBC men. In some ways, Prabhupāda, especially due to the needed investiture of authority in those leaders, many of whom were not yet mature, your time with us seemed to me—as many emergency periods are—the best of times and the worst of times. You patiently encouraged our successes and mended, as best as possible, our failures. Yet many casualties occurred in the ranks, often due to “friendly fire.”

Prabhupāda, somehow, “by hook or by crook,” you did it. You successfully implanted Kṛṣṇa consciousness within the unfriendly and barren Western soil: “As far as we are concerned, we have already started the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness, and many thousands of Europeans and Americans have joined this movement. Indeed, it is spreading like wildfire. The cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, based on the nine principles of devotional service (*śravaṇaṁ kīrtanaṁ viṣṇoḥ smaraṇaṁ pāda-sevanam / arcanaṁ vandanaṁ dāsyaṁ sakhyam ātma-nivedanam*), will never be stopped. It will go on without distinction of caste, creed, color, or country. No one can check it, and it continues to grow like wildfire.”

I am again and again astounded by your accomplishment, as well as by the combined theoretical and practical means you adopted to succeed. You defied natural law and created something from nothing. I remain astounded by your ability to satisfy all sides with your affectionate and expert dealings coupled with śāstric and practical wisdom. You, like the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, are loved by everyone, for you are unenvious, fully devoted, and wise.

With my faulty consciousness, it is impossible for me to deeply know your mind, for it is ever-so-deep and vast. Please astound me further by sparing a drop of wisdom for your tired, unsatisfied, and impoverished servant,

Bhūrijana dāsa

## Bir Krishna dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances in the dust of your transcendental lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your sincere followers!

The expanding nature of my service to you is helping me understand the nondifferent nature of you and your institution, ISKCON. ISKCON represents your desire to flood the entire world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is your tool to accomplish this purpose.

I can see that the best way for me to serve you is to be an instrument for ISKCON. I must give up my enjoying spirit—even the desire to enjoy spiritual life—to serve your mission.

I beg you to allow me to become your first-class servant, that is, a servant who understands your desires and acts to fulfill those desires, even without your asking him. I want this boon. In Vṛndāvana this year, I was embracing the desire trees and asking them for the same benediction.

I know very little about devotional service, I have no attraction for the process of devotional service, I have no good qualities, but I am convinced that by following and serving you and your mission all my shortcomings will be overlooked by the Supreme Lord and I will be allowed to serve you eternally.

Your servant,  
Bir Krishna dāsa Goswami

## Giridhārī Swami

**“Fight! Then Your Guru Mahārāja Will Be Glorified.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I had been trying for months to find the right words to praise you during your Centennial year, but nothing I could come up with seemed right. Then, the other day you gave me the answer yourself. I was listening to the morning-walk conversation of December 1975, from Sanand, India:

**Harikeśa:** You so completely destroy the opposition, it’s very hard to say anything more.

**Prabhupāda:** Yes. That you have to prove. You can eulogize your Guru Mahārāja, but you have to learn it and face the public and be strong to defend yourself. That is success. Not by praising your Guru Mahārāja. You’ll praise your Guru Mahārāja. That is not very difficult. But be victorious to the opposing elements. Then you will praise your Guru Mahārāja nicely. At home, you can praise your Guru Mahārāja, and Guru Mahārāja be satisfied—“Oh, my disciples are praising me.” That is not very … That is good. Respectful. That is the qualification. But you have to fight. Then your Guru Mahārāja will be glorified.”

I stopped the tape to think about what I had just heard. Your message was clear. A Vyāsa-pūjā offering has to be more than a poem in a book. Vyāsa-pūjā is a life-long commitment to learning the spiritual master’s teachings thoroughly and preaching them to others, even in the face of opposition. I thought about what you once told your Godbrothers: “An offering of fruits and flowers does not constitute worship.”

The morning-walk conversation kept running through my mind: “But you have to fight. Then your Guru Mahārāja will be glorified. … But you have to fight…”

I went to the *Śikṣāmṛta* to see what else you had said about fighting.

“So to take risk for Krishna is very good. You know the history of my starting the Society in 1966, it was all risky, and I was alone, but I took it, depending on Krishna.” (Letter to Gargamuni, February 12, 1968)

“The more we face difficulties, the more we draw the attention of Krishna. So in that way we shall be prepared to meet all kinds of difficulties in our preaching work.” (Letter to Upendra, April 22, 1970)

“Now, because you are on the field work, you have to meet so many opposite elements. And you have to satisfy them or fight with them with conclusive statements from *Bhagavad-gita*, and *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* and as such, it is needed that you should be thoroughly conversant with the truth.” (Letter to Guru dāsa, September 29, 1968)

“Regarding your consideration of management, no, we should take all risks for Krishna.” (Letter to Haṁsadūta, June 22, 1970)

Your own life exemplified these words of yours. You glorified your Guru Mahārāja by fighting to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world. You set the perfect example of how to glorify one’s spiritual master.

But your disciples have not yet mastered the art of seeing and following a good example. We need special encouragement and guidance. I think that’s why you chose your Centennial year to wake us up to our shortcomings and personally lead us in a year of glorifying Your Divine Grace, as a preparation for the long fight ahead.

During this year you have given us and the rest of the world a special opportunity to increase our meditation on your transcendental qualities and extraordinary accomplishments. You have taught us to look more closely at ourselves and helped us to root out numerous *anarthas*. You have taught us to give up selfish desire and work together under your guidance. You have set goals and given us the means to accomplish them. And you have made it perfectly clear that Your Divine Grace alone is inspiring us and guiding us forward.

This morning I was listening to another tape, and I learned that a disciple should never say “I want” to the spiritual master or Kṛṣṇa. Still, I ask you to let me humbly beg for three blessings: first, that you never kick me away from your lotus feet; second, that you always allow me to serve your followers; and third, that you allow me to learn your teachings thoroughly and empower me to fight.

Your humble servant,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāj Swami

**Merciful Chastisement**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Some years ago I wrote:

“I do not think that separation from Śrīla Prabhupāda is possible at any moment, provided we want to keep his association. He is ready and waiting. Any time we turn to him, he is there—there to guide us and help us in any way. Mainly he is there to give us instructions in service—and to chastise us if we disobey. But we should never go away.

“He always gives us the chance to leave him—if we want—if the chastisement is too heavy. But leaving him is the greatest loss, complete disaster. It is better to accept the chastisement and try to improve, difficult as it may seem, and win his favor again, rather than try to run and hide, avoiding or, still worse, trying to forget.

“Of course, Śrīla Prabhupāda loves us even when he chastises us. In fact, his chastisement is proof that he loves us, that he cares for us, and that he wants us to improve and become good servants of Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

Now, years later, I have realized even more deeply the import of these words. Reading the following verses in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* I thought of myself and Your Divine Grace:

“I committed a great offense against you, but you are so kind that instead of withdrawing your mercy you have done me a great favor by punishing me. … I did not know your full glories. For this reason, I threw arrows of sharp words at you in the open assembly. … I was going down to hell because of my disobedience to you, who are the most respectable personality, but you took compassion upon me and saved me by awarding punishment.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.7.13, 15)

In the purport you explain, “The insulting words used by Dakṣa against Lord Śiva were enough to have him thrown perpetually into a hellish life. But Lord Śiva, being kind toward him, awarded him punishment to neutralize the offense. King Dakṣa realized this and, feeling obliged for Lord Śiva’s magnanimous behavior, wanted to show his gratitude. Sometimes a father punishes his child, and when the child is grown up and comes to his senses, he understands that the father’s punishment was not actually punishment but mercy. Similarly, Dakṣa appreciated that the punishment offered to him by Lord Śiva was a manifestation of Lord Śiva’s mercy.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your chastisement is actually your mercy; neglect would really be punishment. Thus I am forced to conclude, “Who could be more merciful than you?”

Thus I also thank all your sincere disciples and followers who have chastised me on your behalf. I thank even those who are not considered your followers but who may also act on behalf of the Lord. Through their chastisement I hope to become a bona fide disciple:

“A sincere devotee willingly accepts the Lord’s merciful punishment and continues offering respects and obeisances to the Lord. Such a bona fide servant of the Lord, considering all hardship a small price to pay for gaining the personal association of the Lord, certainly becomes a legitimate son of God. Just as one cannot approach the sun without becoming fire, one cannot approach the supreme pure, Lord Kṛṣṇa, without undergoing a rigid purificatory process, which may appear like suffering but which is in fact a curative treatment administered by the personal hand of the Lord.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.14.8 purport)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are my lord.

Now our process is to humbly serve and favorably please the Vaiṣṇavas. So on this occasion I pray to them to continue to give me their mercy and guidance so one day I may learn to please them with some service—and thus please you and your Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your ever grateful, degraded servant,  
Girirāj Swami

## Gopāla Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Today is once again the auspicious day of your appearance on this planet, and devotees all over the world are enthusiastically celebrating the 100th anniversary of your divine appearance. Your Centennial celebrations were launched in the last week of December with the Prabhupāda Puṣpāñjali in Bombay, which was attended by more than 30,000 people. Each year it is a great honor to be able to offer our attempted praises, though our statements and feelings are insufficient to glorify you.

On this day we feel separation from you and regret that we did not take advantage of those rare moments of your personal association when you were on the planet. You came with the message of the absolute world for the benefit of the entire humanity. You offered the entire world a way in which to live peacefully by uniting under the banner of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your transcendental fame and glories increase every day as your movement continues to spread all over the world. More and more people are taking to Kṛṣṇa consciousness because of the influence of your books and because of contact with those who are preaching from your books. Your books are *śāstras*—transcendental weapons that cut through all the layers of ignorance and false ego and bring us to our constitutional position.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the real motivating force behind the entire preaching effort of ISKCON—book distribution, temple construction, *prasādam* distribution, farm development, etc. During your stay on this planet, you worked hard to establish ISKCON, the only institution that is giving a positive direction to the world. History will recognize you as the savior of humanity.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you bring about the remembrance of Kṛṣṇa because you are always thinking of Kṛṣṇa. You are the boat that carries us across the ocean of this material existence. You are the source of strength that will keep us pure. So on your auspicious appearance day, I want to beg you to grant me the boon that I may get attached to your lotus feet. This attachment by far exceeds and in fact includes all other boons. To love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, means to serve you by following your instructions. We should only focus on your desires and make them our life and soul. We should never doubt your words or misinterpret your words. We should know without any doubt that there can be no happiness in this world without full surrender to you and to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am completely dependent upon your mercy to have the strength, intelligence, and determination to stay on this path of devotional service. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the savior of the fallen souls. I am very grateful that despite my health problems last year you have allowed me to go on rendering practical service in your institution.

Your servant,  
Gopāla Krishna Goswami

## Guru-prasāda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet, which have traversed the entire world and unleashed Kṛṣṇa’s divine mercy.

A year has passed since your last Vyāsa-pūjā. So much time has gone by, and I feel that I have not glorified you properly. You are the only savior and friend for your surrendered disciples, and your words, example, and instructions are their only qualification.

Many generations of Vaiṣṇavas will come and go in our ISKCON movement, but all of them will know you as the main *śikṣā-guru,* the standard for all other devotees, and the founder-*ācārya*.

First to bring a real understanding of Kṛṣṇa to the entire world.

Orthodox in all practices and teachings, yet able to expertly adapt Kṛṣṇa consciousness to perfectly suit the fallen conditioned souls.

Undaunted in preaching and spreading *kṛṣṇa-kathā*.

Never discouraged, the supreme *sthita-dhīr muni*.

Devastater of atheistic thoughts and ways.

Ever well-wisher of all living entities.

Ready to do anything for Kṛṣṇa.

Always kind and loving to those who seek the shelter of your lotus feet.

Can always explain all Vaiṣṇava *siddhānta* perfectly.

All *śāstra* acknowledges his position as the perfect representative of Kṛṣṇa.

Resplendent with the golden hue of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Your mercy is all-pervading.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

I pray that you empower me, especially in this Centennial year, to help establish your glories and your movement for the benefit of all those who follow for the next ten thousand years. Surely by the distribution your books people will finally give up their bad habits and take shelter of the holy name. Then Vaiṣṇava culture can flourish all over the entire world. By the devotees;’ giving the perfect example of simple living and high thinking, all will see how to live properly, as you have outlined in your instructions. I beg you to let me realize that everything is already explained by you in full detail so that t I can give up my futile attempt to demonstrate what I know or what I can do. I just want to understand that my only hope is your causeless mercy.

Your most unworthy servant,  
Guru-prasāda Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

(sung to the tune of *Gopīnātha*)

**Prabhupāda**  
What mercy you poured on the fallen souls!  
Nectar from the lotus feet of the Lord.  
You traveled beyond Bhārat Bhūmi’s shores  
to take up the work of the Lord and His chores  
and deliver the fallen like me.

**Prabhupāda**  
You traveled to the West in advanced age  
to do what no one else has done.  
With only enough cash to spend for some hours,  
not even enough to pay for some flowers,  
but Kṛṣṇa was taking care of you.

**Prabhupāda**  
Alone you started to preach to us  
hippies who had lost our minds.  
You made us chant and hear the Lord’s names.  
You told us about His glory and fame  
and made us surrender to you.

**Prabhupāda**  
You preached, cooked, and cleaned in your storefront  
without anyone to help.  
The Lord’s pure devotee serving the lowest of men.  
Taking service from you proved that we were most fallen,  
but you gave to us freely out of love.

**Prabhupāda**  
You arrived at the airport in New York.
