Your effulgence was bright like the sun.  
Your lotus feet appeared right before my eyes,  
I looked upon them and was very surprised  
because you walked on the Earth.

**Prabhupāda**  
I became your initiated disciple.  
You gave me the name Harikeśa.  
You told me my name was a name of the Lord,  
and somehow it struck a harmonic chord  
that vibrated deep in my heart.

**Prabhupāda**  
You sent me to India to serve you  
although I was scared to death.  
In India, I was told, one got sick and then died.  
My mother on hearing this started to cry,  
but I went anyway ’cause you asked me.

**Prabhupāda**  
I tried to serve you with devotion  
although I could never become  
the pure devotee you expected me to be,  
free from all anger, lust, greed, and envy,  
who could surrender to you completely.

Prabhupāda  
Prabhupāda  
Prabhupāda  
Prabhupāda

Offered on the occasion of the hundredth anniversary of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance in this world by his humble eternal servant,  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hridāyananda dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On June 22, 1975, in Los Angeles, you declared that if your disciples were able to take positions as professors of philosophy and religion in the American universities, that this would “save America” and make America the true leader of the world. You declared this while discussing a proposal that we establish a Kṛṣṇa conscious program of studies at the Graduate Theological Union in Berkeley, California. You also stated that this program would “take some time” to manifest. Now, by your causeless mercy, it appears that this program’s time has come, and we are simply begging at your lotus feet that you kindly shower your blessings so that America can be truly be saved and lead the world to spiritual salvation.

With the passing of years, we see more and more clearly that Lord Kṛṣṇa is our only true leader and that you are His pure and greatest representative. Only Lord Kṛṣṇa and His pure devotees enjoy perfect intelligence, and therefore we humbly beg to be your willing and worthy servants.

Eternally at your feet,  
Hridāyananda dāsa Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

My dear spiritual father Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful prostrated obeisances at Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet on the occasion of your Centennial Vyāsa-pūjā celebration! You are an ocean of mercy beyond compare.

The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* describes how Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu delivered a dog who had received the affection of Śivānanda Sena. Often Your Divine Grace has mentioned how you came to save the world from a “cat and dog” life. Cats and dog spend their time looking for gratification of the senses and have no higher purpose in life. Although I was born in a “well-to-do” family, I simply wandered around looking for sense pleasure and had no higher spiritual purpose to my life prior to meeting Your Divine Grace. In effect I was a polished or sophisticated dog wandering the ways of America, and it was Your Divine Grace who picked me up from this horrible aimless existence and engaged me in the service of Guru and Gaurāṅga. Your Divine Grace gave utmost care and affection to this unqualified soul and drew me close to your lotus feet.

I can understand that without Your Divine Grace’s mercy I would have no standing whatsoever and would be totally worthless in all respects. I am eternally indebted to Your Divine Grace and see no possible way of ever repaying the unlimited debt I owe you. Still, I see no other way than trying with whatever strength I can muster to serve you as much as I possibly can to contribute something against this incalculable debt I owe Your Divine Grace. Yet with every passing moment Your Divine Grace increases my indebtedness by continuing to allow me to serve you. I can only pray again and again that I may never be separated even for a moment from serving your lotus feet.

Your Divine Grace is a spiritual revolutionary, and your declared purpose is to change the way the people of the world think. You want to save every human from cat-and-dog consciousness by bringing them to their original, eternal Kṛṣṇa conscious blissful state. Your Divine Grace instructed me with these words: “Expand the \[Kṛṣṇa conscious\] propaganda unlimitedly,” “Double book distribution,” and “I have given you the kingdom of God \[Māyāpur *dhāma*\]—now develop it!”

Your Divine Grace has also instructed ISKCON to develop Māyāpur and build the Temple of the Vedic Planetarium. Now with the potential unlimited expansion of both rural and urban Nāma Haṭṭa congregational preaching in ISKCON, we can see the possibility of fulfilling Your Divine Grace’s instructions through the combined cooperative global effort of the temple devotees and the expanded, enlivened, and empowered congregational devotees. On this sacred occasion I reconfirm my eternal surrender at your lotus feet and pray for your protection and your mercy to enable me in this lifetime to fulfill all the instructions Your Divine Grace has given me, as I am totally dependent on your mercy. Kindly bless all of us in ISKCON with the ability to work together cooperatively in the spirit of submissively serving Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet.

The spiritual grandfather is more merciful to the grandchildren than the spiritual father; therefore I know Your Divine Grace will be unlimitedly merciful to your representatives whom you have sent to me as my disciples. Although it is not necessary, still I beg you to give them your causeless mercy, as in this Kali-yuga there are so many obstacles to overcome.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your insignificant servant,  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Kavicandra Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I should serve your mission at any cost.  
Without that service my life is lost.

Of all sounds, your voice is the best;  
Let me ignore all of the rest.

Let me think big and to Kṛṣṇa look.  
Everyone should have your book.

Your mercy has planted the devotional seed;  
Now reading your books is what I need.

To Mahāprabhu you are attached.  
Your mercy can never be matched.

Your perfect teaching must go everywhere;  
For any obstacles we need not care.

I recently found the following statement by Śrīla Prabhupāda, which clearly states what it really means to glorify him. I am including it here because I think it can give a boost to the Centennial preaching.

**Haṁsadūta:** It would make a good article, what you’ve just spoken.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** So write.

**Harikeśa:** You so completely destroy the opposition, it’s very hard to say anything more.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Yes. That you have to prove. You can eulogize your Guru Mahārāja, but you have to learn it and face the public and be strong to defend yourself. That is success. Not by praising your Guru Mahārāja. You’ll praise your Guru Mahārāja. That is not very difficult. But be victorious to the opposing elements. Then you will praise your Guru Mahārāja nicely. At home, you can praise your Guru Mahārāja, and Guru Mahārāja \[will\] be satisfied, “Oh, my disciples are praising me.” That is not very—That is good. Respectful. That is the qualification. But you have to fight. Then your Guru Mahārāja will be glorified.

By the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda and ISKCON, which is nondifferent from him, I hope that I can go out and fight and not be satisfied that some disciples are praising me. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission is to teach everyone about Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and I pray that I will be able to work in that mission.

Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your divine appearance day! All glories to your Centennial celebration!

“Only out of His immense compassion does the Personality of Godhead reveal Himself as the spiritual master. Therefore in the dealings of an ācārya there are no activities but those of transcendental loving service to the Lord. He is the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead. It is worthwhile to take shelter of such a steady devotee, who is called *āśraya-vigraha,* or the manifestation or form of the Lord of whom one must take shelter.”

Both the Supreme Personality of Godhead and the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead possess names form, qualities and pastimes that are absolute.

You have unequivocally proven the transcendental nature of your name, your form, your qualities, and your pastimes, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Therefore the devotees chant, love, and faithfully serve your names; take *darśana* of, bow down to, and worship your form; imbibe, relish, and surrender to your qualities, and derive unlimited inspiration and bliss by discussing your sweet pastimes among themselves.

**Your Name**

Abhay Charanaravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

When your name is uttered, a devotee immediately remembers your sublime personality, along with Kṛṣṇa, His devotional service, and particularly the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. That is because you are always with Kṛṣṇa and your name is always with Kṛṣṇa’s name. When a person who knows who you are hears your name, he immediately recalls Kṛṣṇa, the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, and Kṛṣṇa’s devotees. Your name is synonymous with the science of *bhakti-yoga* and the destruction of the Māyāvāda farce. Taking shelter of your name drives away lethargy, doubts, fear, illusion, and sorrow. If, by your grace, I will be able to call your name at the moment of death, immediately you will mercifully come, along with the Pañca-tattva mantra, the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* and the *cintāmaṇi* prayers, to rescue me from a degraded birth in this material world. Let us take shelter of our spiritual master by chanting and singing his name. His name is famous all over the three worlds.

**Your Form**

Your appearance in this world was not an ordinary event. You descended from the absolute realm and utilized an apparently material body for the purpose of teaching the nine nectarean processes of devotional service to the conditioned souls. When Kṛṣṇa entered the arena in Mathurā, different people saw him differently. Only the devotees could understand His transcendental nature; and the *gopīs* were even beyond that understanding, being merged in such an exalted state of love for Him. Similarly, some saw you as a Bengali gentlemen, some as a great writer or philosopher, some as the founder of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement; only the devotees, though, could understand your transcendental nature beyond the appearances: the *nitya-siddha* envoy coming from Kṛṣṇa to fulfill Lord Caitanya’s prediction.

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s perception of you was the most perfect. As the supreme *mahā-bhāgavata,* he knew and saw your transcendental form and mission, and at the time you met by the order of the transcendental system, he instructed you to glorify the whole disciplic succession by traveling and preaching Gaurāṅga’s message all over the world. Remembering your form gives solace to the heart afflicted by material existence; your form is the embodiment of attachment to Kṛṣṇa and the cause of the sweetest devotional sentiments. Your *samādhis* are temples of your transcendental form. Wherever your form appeared, even in the most degraded places like New York or Rome or Mombassa, the place became attractive for the devotees. By your mercy I could serve in Calcutta for four years, and I developed a liking for that place. How would that be possible if not for your having appeared there? “Because you carry the Personality of Godhead within your heart, you turn all places into places of pilgrimage.” Let my desire be to remember, bow down to, and serve your beautiful form, because only in this way may I be able to transcend the plane of ordinary vision of the sense objects and perceive the form of the Supreme Personality of Godhead everywhere.

Let us take shelter of our spiritual master by constantly remembering his gracious form; his form is famous all over the three worlds.

**Your Qualities**

The twenty-six qualities of a pure devotee manifest fully in your personality. The proof is that your association has left indelible signs on the hearts of those who meet you. Material qualities do not really change a person’s life. Transcendental qualities do. To cite just a few.

**Kind to everyone.**

Material kindness is only a cosmetic applied to the false ego: it eventually wears off. Your kindness cannot be forgotten. You are identical with your sublime kindness. The remembrance of your kindness pushes me to leave this material world and seek your association in the spiritual world, for that kindness is my eternal shelter.

**Clean.**

You are so clean and pure. Once I was on guard at your door in Māyāpur during the late morning. I was leaning on the railing of the verandah, my back turned toward your door, chanting my rounds. As usual, I was spacing out while chanting, looking here and there and letting numerous thoughts go in and out of my mind. At one point, without any endeavor on my part, my mind became clean, peaceful, and fixed on the *mahā-mantra*. It was amazing! I cared for nothing else but chanting the holy name. It went on for a few seconds. Then I had a sudden realization and, straightening up my body, I could see you leaning on the adjacent railing beyond the pillar, chanting on your *japa* beads, smiling. Your presence had washed my mind of all dirt as a natural consequence. That is the power of the pure devotee’s perfectly clean heart.

**Magnanimous.**

Your magnanimity I experience every day as the strength to remain in the association of devotees while being engaged in the process of *sādhana* and absorbed in the preaching mission. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Had it not been for you, I’d be light years away from devotional service.

Your qualities are an ocean, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Let us take shelter of our spiritual master by bathing in the ocean of his transcendental qualities and distributing that water to whomever we meet. His qualities are famous all over the three worlds.

**Your pastimes.**

Inconceivable. Touching, seeing, smelling, tasting, hearing, speaking, moving, thinking, desiring only for Kṛṣṇa. No distraction, no inebriety, no delusion. By simple contact, turning the material energy into devotional potency. Whether it be in the form of a dictaphone, a thought, a vegetable, a brick, or a feeling, when it came in touch with you it changed its nature from material to spiritual by virtue of your perfect devotion to Kṛṣṇa. This movement is composed of spiritual books, spiritual vans, spiritual buildings, spiritual kitchens, spiritual masters, and spiritual devotees. This movement is your pastime. You enacted this pastime all over the planet (at least that is what our vision allowed us to see), by planting unlimited seeds of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti,* nurturing small devotional plants, and destroying the malicious weeds of materialism and Māyāvādism. Kṛṣṇa endowed you with the potency to expand yourself in the form of billions of books to reach the conditioned living entities. Certainly this is an unprecedented pastime caused by the unique intensity of your compassion. Every feature of your manifested pastimes on this earth is a portion of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* for the future generations. By Kṛṣṇa’s desire you became the artificer of a distinct feature of the spiritual world. When I go to India and enter Māyāpur- or Vṛndāvana-dhāma, a clear perception of the unique transcendental quality of the place becomes manifested in my heart. But when I enter ISKCON’s premises in these *dhāmas—*Śrī Māyāpur Chandrodaya Mandir or the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandir—a more intense and characteristic perception is aroused in my heart, a perception that carries the distinct flavor of your personality. And in the ISKCON *maṇḍalas,* the sweetest, innermost chambers are your personal rooms. Particularly the room in Māyāpur is the center of the center of the universe. Everything in that room is highly concentrated Prabhupāda-kṛpā. I am attracted by everything in that room—your desk, the lamp on the desk, the *gaddis,* the wall cabinet, the still cabinet, your bed, the small *prasādam* table, the chairs. The *mūrti* of Lord Caitanya in your bedroom is the most munificent of all. That room is the seed of the Mayapur project, and it will remain its holiest place even when, by your desire, the transcendental Adbhuta Mandir, along with the Māyāpur city, makes its appearance, blessing this universe and all the living entities in it.

Let us take shelter of our spiritual master by remembering, chanting, and taking active part in his pastimes. His pastimes are famous all over the three worlds.

On this auspicious occasion of the hundredth anniversary of your appearance, Madhusevita dāsa prays that all those who had any contact with you, the Personality of Servitor Godhead, Śrī Śrīmad Abhay Charanaravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda, be blessed with the desire to take shelter of your name, form, qualities, and pastimes, thereby becoming qualified to recollect the name, form, qualities, and pastimes of their Lordships Gaurāṅga and Nityānanda and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava.

For myself, I humbly ask to always remember you and never forget you and be eternally engaged in your service.

Your insignificant servant,  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Mukunda Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

For your Vyāsa-pūjā offering this year I am presenting my personal mission statement. I hope you will approve.

Your eternal servant,  
Mukunda Goswami

Mission Statement, Updated 7 April 1996, Queensland, Australia

1\. I am a loyal son to Śrīla Prabhupāda so that he can be proud of me. I endeavor to be everything he could hope for me to be. I will increase my meditation on Śrīla Prabhupāda (“Let me meditate on him being enthused as he enthused us”), trying to inculcate the vision that he is personally standing, sitting, walking, or riding (figuratively swimming or running) beside me at all times. I endeavor to always keep my mind filled with thoughts of Kṛṣṇa and Śrīla Prabhupāda and their teachings.

2\. My devotional service is primarily manifested through the art of writing, since my spiritual master instructed and ordered me to write as my “business.” I will endeavor to become as humble as possible.

3\. I will always chant at least sixteen rounds daily, strictly follow the four regulative principles, and read the Vedic literature regularly. I will endeavor to develop the twenty-six qualifications of a devotee. I will endeavor to constantly remember Kṛṣṇa and never forget Him and to attain pure love for Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. I will act according to Vaiṣṇava etiquette and follow as far as possible all the other detailed ritualistic devotional activities taught by our *sampradāya*.

4\. I will endeavor with all the strength and knowledge at my command to present Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the major publics of the world to help them realize Śrīla Prabhupāda and His movement as practical, philosophically stimulating, fascinating, and beneficial, so that they will be disposed to change their behavior in accordance with these realizations.

5\. I will do my utmost to help lead and manage (organize and administer) ISKCON Communications (IC) to the best of my ability, drawing primarily on the wisdom from Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teachings, but also sometimes on worldly knowledge if and when applicable. I will apply such “secular” knowledge strictly according to the spiritual principle of *yukta-vairāgya*. For preaching work, I will work with effective teams whose members will get significant results, who will continuously strive for superexcellent, “cutting edge” quality, and who make a full commitment to “make a positive difference” for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission. These teams will produce mission statements, goals, policies, procedures, project and job descriptions, and action plans.

6\. I will deal with Godbrothers and Godsisters as peers (like fellow “faculty” members). I will treat disciples, prospective disciples, and new uninitiated devotees as my younger brothers and sisters (“students” in the school metaphor) or as my children. I will endeavor to relate with them as an “older brother,” an “enlightened parent,” a guide, leader, coach, cheerleader, and facilitator. Wherever and whenever possible, I will assist them in their specific engagement in devotional service.

It is my solemn duty to clearly educate all my disciples to realize that I am a servant of ISKCON and the GBC body and that they are the authorities to whom I am accountable and responsible. I direct my disciples to generally regard and honor my Godbrothers with the same genuine level of respect they have for me (“One should respect one’s spiritual master’s Godbrothers as one respects one’s spiritual master.”—Śrīla Prabhupāda in the purport to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 5.147).

7\. I will try to always be personally interesting to all people who see or come into contact with me. I want to radiate peace and happiness, and therefore my demeanor will also include dressing and behaving at all times according to this vision of myself. This must be so because I represent Lord Kṛṣṇa, and I want my existence to attract as many people as possible to Him and His movement (ISKCON). This will also help me remember that I am first and foremost a servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa. I will endeavor at every opportunity to strengthen ISKCON.

8\. I will look after the physical side of my life in order to maintain the bodily and mental strength I need to help execute Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission and to follow Śrīla Prabhupāda’s daily example of enacting fitness principles and techniques.

9\. I will teach one or more persons as much as possible what I have learned, realized, and believe in regarding the effective realization and spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness so that whatever my contribution to society and the disciplic succession may be, it may not be lost but will continue through the agency of other devotees after I leave my body. I will produce and develop teaching materials (including written manuals and instructions, video and audiovisual presentations like CD ROMS, etc.) as necessary to help achieve this aim.

10\. My “successes” will be determined in large part by systematic and periodic review and accountability to superiors, subordinates, experts, and others, and to the principles of success and effectiveness that I have established for myself.

11\. I will increasingly endeavor to realize that the holy name is nondifferent from Lord Kṛṣṇa himself and that offenseless, attentive chanting brings direct realization of Kṛṣṇa Himself. I will also continuously endeavor to realize that constant and pure chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* both in *japa* and *saṅkīrtana,* is the highest benediction for all living beings, including myself.

12\. I am committed to do whatever is necessary to help make ISKCON Potomac more than just an “ideal” Kṛṣṇa conscious ISKCON center and community. It must be one that truly “makes a difference” for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s movement, a center devotees will want to join above all others in the United States, and one that neighbors and others who live outside the community will want to visit as often as possible and stay for as long as possible. It must be an ISKCON center that other temples seek to emulate. Since the location is Washington, D.C., and since it is the site of the IC Global and IC North America headquarters, I must do whatever is necessary to help make it THE “cutting edge,” premier, showcase ISKCON temple community in the United States.

## Nirañjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this day, the hundredth anniversary of your divine appearance in this material world, I would like to prostrate myself at your lotus feet and beg for your forgiveness for not having adequate realization to glorify you properly. Every year at this time I’m forced to beg something from you.

Just a few weeks ago, I was reading the following quote in a letter written by you dated August 14, 1976:

“If you feel at all indebted to me, then you should preach vigorously like me. That is the proper way to repay me. Of course, no one can repay the debt to the spiritual master, but the spiritual master is very much pleased by such an attitude by the disciple.”

I read this quote in a class and then proceeded to speak about how you vigorously preached. As I was speaking, my own realizations about how vigorously you preached when you were present with us began to grow. I felt blessed to catch a glimpse of your glory. When are you not preaching? You are beyond time. All the residents of the spiritual world are beyond time. But still, a hundred years ago you manifested your *prakaṭa-līlā* for those fallen souls still trapped in the mouth of *kāla-rūpa*. I am one of those very fallen souls, and therefore I am foolishly thinking that you are not preaching to me now. This dull-headed disciple of yours too often forgets that you are just like the all-pervasive Supersoul. “Like the sun, Your Goodness can travel everywhere in the three worlds, and like the air you can penetrate the internal region of everyone. As such, you are as good as the all-pervasive Supersoul.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.7) Just as Lord Paramātmā is beyond time and therefore knows past, present, future, and all living entities, similarly you also know everything that goes on within the hearts of your disciples, and at every moment you can deliver all the transcendental necessities required to pull your disciples out of *kāla-rūpa’s* clutches. I must always hear you! This is my hope and my prayer. I am helplessly begging before you again, as I do every year at this time. Please don’t ever stop preaching to me, for I know that this is my only hope. I have an eternal debt to pay back, one which may bring me back again and again and again. I must preach vigorously like you did. But first I must actually hear without stoppage. Then I can *begin* to pay back my debt to you, for one who actually *hears* from his spiritual master will never stop preaching.

Until then, since I am still trapped within time, I must continue to beg that you will allow me to become and remain,

Your eternal servant, birth after birth after birth,  
Nirañjana Swami

## Paraṁ-gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*evaṁ janaṁ nipatitaṁ prabhavāhi-kūpe*  
*kāmābhikāmam anu yaḥ prapatan prasaṅgāt*  
*kṛtvātmasāt surarṣiṇā bhagavan gṛhītaḥ*  
*so ’haṁ kathaṁ nu visṛje tava bhṛtya-sevām*  
*(Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 7.9.28)*

**“My dear Lord, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, because of my association with material desires, one after another, I was gradually falling into a blind well full of snakes, following the general populace. But Your servant Nārada Muni kindly accepted me as his disciple and instructed me how to achieve this transcendental position. Therefore, my first duty is to serve him. How could I leave his service?”**

In the purport to this very important verse, you explain that the service humbly offered to the spiritual master is the key to success in spiritual life. Since by such constant and unalloyed service the disciple becomes able to see the Supreme Lord, how can one leave such service?

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I was always disturbed by the influence of the three modes of material nature and attacked by the three kinds of miseries, and therefore I had no means of finding a solution to my distress, but by His causeless mercy the Supreme Personality of Godhead sent you to save me from this terrible ocean. Therefore, the only meaning of my life is to serve you.

According to the previous *ācāryas,* service to the spiritual master means following his instructions. In this sacred year of your Centennial anniversary, I'm humbly praying to the Supreme Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to give me the strength, determination, tolerance, and enthusiasm to continue to follow your instructions eternally and to be engaged in fulfilling your intense desire to see the people in general stop their sinful activities and adopt this *bhakti-yoga* process in their lives. By such transcendental practice, this planet will become really peaceful.

This Centennial year is important in many aspects, especially in uniting your family and giving everybody the opportunity to work together to help one another keep our spiritual lives in good health. Another goal of this year is to work seriously to demonstrate to the people of the world the greatness of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, how this philosophy is perfect, and how the mass of people in general can be happy again simply by following the instructions of the *Bhagavad-gītā*. This is your desire.

Personally I have no mercy or other good qualities required to accomplish such a mission, but I have complete faith and confidence in your instructions. Your Bhaktivedanta purports are causing a revolution on this planet, and we can see that the present horrible situation will change if we stay united and cooperatively work together under your perfect guidance. Praying to remain always engaged in the service of your sincere disciples and followers, I remain

Your eternal servant,  
Paraṁ-gati Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

**A Master Perfect**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, a master perfect,  
Completely pure vision, without defect,  
Mind fixed on Kṛṣṇa, his senses controlled,  
Spread the message of Kṛṣṇa, forceful and bold.

His movements were graceful, with the air of a swan,  
His compassion was vast, like the limitless ocean,  
His intellect sharp, he could show right from wrong,  
His instructions were potent, convincing, and strong.

And that is all because of his surrender to Kṛṣṇa  
And commitment to spreading his Gurudev’s mission.  
Not the least deviation from that could one detect;  
That’s why his preaching had such huge effect.

The ship of ISKCON with him at the helm  
Will take us back to the spiritual realm.  
He’s our link to Kṛṣṇa, and due all respect,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, a master perfect.

Your lowly servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami.

## Rādhānātha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

One century prior to this day you so mercifully advented into this world of death to carry us into the eternal *līlā* of Vṛndāvana, the spiritual world. From Śrī Goloka *dhāma* you have brought us the immortal message of unalloyed devotional service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vṛndāvana-candra.

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has descended as the most munificent incarnation of Godhead. Lord Caitanya is Kṛṣṇa who has come to experience the supreme love possessed by His own pleasure potency, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. He came to freely distribute pure love of Godhead through the sublime method of congregational chanting of the holy names of Kṛṣṇa. Lord Caitanya instructed Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu and Śrīla Haridāsa Ṭhākura to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the most fallen and suffering souls entrapped within the prison of Kali-yuga. This spirit of divine mercy has been passed down through the sacred line of disciplic succession.

O beloved Guru Mahārāja, O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have accepted Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s mission of compassion as your very life and soul. Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu gladly accepted a deadly blow on his head to give *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* to the most sinful Jagāi and Mādhāi. In the same spirit of absolute sacrifice you endured inconceivable hardships to deliver us from the sufferings of our countless sins. By your truly causeless mercy you painstakingly guided us step by step in the process of devotional service.

Following the pure principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is impossible for the *mlecchas* and *yavanas* of the West. However, Śrīla Prabhupāda, simply to reciprocate with the selfless love you bestowed upon us we were willing to make any sacrifice in our lives to please you.

My eternal prayer is that I may be a surrendered puppet in your benevolent hands. I helplessly fall again and again at your lotus feet and pray that these shackles of worldly attachments may be cast away from my crippled heart. At that time I may be empowered to exclusively dedicate my body, mind, words, and life to be a humble instrument in your loving hands. I pray that you may kindly forgive me for my countless faults and allow me to forever be the servant of your servants.

On every continent on earth devotees are celebrating the most glorious event of your centennial Vyāsa-pūjā. It was exactly twenty-five years ago that you personally gave me shelter in the sacred shade of your lotus feet. I was a lost soul desperately searching throughout the world for spiritual truth. Hearing the piteous cries of my heart, Lord Kṛṣṇa so mercifully guided me to Your Divine Grace. It was at your first *pandal* program in Bombay that you lifted me out from a crowd of thousands of lost souls and gave me a place at your lotus feet. You so kindly planted the precious seed of *bhakti* in my heart. I continued my fervent search for love and truth and was miraculously directed to the supreme most-holy of all holy places. Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma. Residing on the lonely bank of the sacred river Yamunā, I was pleading and pleading to Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī to save me. At that time you returned to Vṛndāvana and mercifully accepted me as your eternal servant.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, if it pleases you, please speak your supreme purifying words through my mouth and perform your divine will through my life. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept this fallen and ungrateful soul as a particle of dust eternally serving at your lotus feet.

Rādhānātha Swami

## Rāmāi Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Another year has passed, and it is again time for the anniversary of your most auspicious appearance day. This year is especially significant because all year the devotees have been celebrating your Centennial. By your mercy, this year we are all trying to stay more focused on spreading your glories to every country, town, and village. Your International Society for Krishna Consciousness is known practically everywhere, but you were such a humble servant of the Lord that, unlike so many other so-called *gurus,* you never promoted yourself for name, fame, wealth, and following. Others put themselves up on high pedestals, but you never took any credit for yourself; instead, you would say it was all the mercy of your spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa. You were bold and forceful for *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, not for your own aggrandizement; this is what distinguishes you from so many others. You are the true Jagat-guru, the spiritually empowered world *ācārya*.

It is said that Kṛṣṇa is more pleased when His pure devotee is glorified, and we can see that He is making all arrangements to establish you in a pre-eminent position. Your disciples and grand-disciples have been working very hard over the last few years assisting the Lord in this endeavor. Many prominent people, even in Western countries where they are very averse to praising anyone other than themselves, have come forward in appreciation, some very spontaneously. Recently one very well known critic in Australia, who for years had been making fun of the Hare Kṛṣṇa Movement, announced to his radio audience that A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami was the only one he considered a genuine *guru* out of so many. We saw a film that showed the chief minister of India’s Maharashtra state calling out “Prabhupāda ki jaya! Prabhupāda ki jaya! Prabhupāda ki jaya!” after a speech he gave to thirty thousand people. Mayors in New Zealand have planted trees and put up plaques in your honor. We have made dioramas, arrange photo exhibits, shown films, and given lectures and speeches describing your great spiritual leadership and qualities. Western and Eastern academics are appreciating more than ever your unparalleled literary contribution. Still we should never be satisfied until A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda has become a household name all over the world. This may happen over night, or it may take many lifetimes; it is all up to the desire of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and the desire of your disciples, grand-disciples, and future generations of disciples.

Everyone in this world is after some sort of fame, although it is temporary and fickle. But because you are such a surrendered servant of the Lord, fame will follow you for unlimited thousands of years. Even the demigods in the higher planetary systems must be astounded at what you have accomplished in a comparatively short time. Your achievements have brought together devotees and will ultimately unify the universe! This is not an exaggeration: what can a great *ācārya* not accomplish? If one simply has faith in *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, then not only can Kali-yuga be controlled, but everyone can cross over the ocean of Kali and his agents very easily and go back home, back to Godhead.

You are the compassion representative of the most compassionate Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and all the Pañca-tattva. We can see that they are manifesting their mercy through you and your followers all over the world. Who else has given so much without wanting anything in return? Generally everyone wants to take and take without much consideration for giving, but you gave us more than we could ever offer you in return. One special quality in you that I always noticed was your ability to engage practically anyone in devotional service, even those who some of your disciples thought were completely “out of it.” You are so kind, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you are still engaging multitudes of living entities even after your disappearance from our vision. Let there be no doubt that it is through your movement that this is going on. You once said that there is no difference between yourself and your movement. If all of us can just remain strong, sincere, and fixed in our determination to always serve you and your mission, then the desire of Lord Caitanya, Lord Nityānanda, and all Their associates will surely be fulfilled. You once said in Australia that sincerity was not enough: one had to be strong in one’s spiritual principles as well. We have to pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to give us that strength, and we have to try very hard to please you. If we think “Is Prabhupāda pleased by this activity?” or “ How would Prabhupāda have done this?”, then this is the proper consciousness for a disciple.

As far as I am concerned—well, what can I say? I am not much of a disciple. I think the only thing for me is to follow your real disciples. It is only through your mercy manifested through them and their kindness that I am still hanging around somehow or other. There are not enough letters in the alphabet or words in the dictionary to properly glorify you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. This year we see a strong spirit to rally, combine together, and try our best to push this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement even to the remotest regions of the world. By your mercy, it will happen.

Your fallen servant,  
Rāmāi Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your most glorious service, which has given such great delight to Kṛṣṇa and His confidential devotees.

When once more I essayed to survey the full dimensions of what you are and what you have done, in order to convey to others some just measure of your epic magnitude, I quickly learned how unequal I am to the task. I feel like some tiny assessor venturing to lay his yardstick alongside a monument so colossal its surfaces continue in all directions past the limits of vision.

Your greatness is more than I can conceive, to say nothing of measure. You are far too large; I, too small. What is more, my very proximity to you also limits my vision of your magnitude. In time, others will bring their eyes to bear upon you from across the long perspective of centuries, and they will be able to see dimensions far beyond the gaze of us who stand so close.

Still, we who once stood at your side could feel your greatness. It hovered all about, a vast, enveloping, unseen presence. We were amazed at the way you ever so lightly wore that greatness, as if you didn’t know it were there. As though you didn’t know who you were. We had never encountered such a union of true greatness with true humility. It seemed impossible. Simply put, you were inconceivable, and yet—undeniably—there you were.

In time, we came to know your secret: You honestly thought of yourself as a servant, and the greatness we all-so-palpably felt, you understood to be not your own but your master’s. So thinking, you saved the world with the ease of a youth carelessly gathering flowers from a summer meadow. How was it possible for you to be so ultimately serious and at the same time so utterly carefree?

I cannot stretch myself to your scale. Yet you kindly appeared on mine. Because I cannot speak of the great, the epic things, I shall work in miniature and recount some small things—little things I witnessed when I was close to you. Little things a little person could see, and yet, within their constricted frame, opened up upon vistas of greatness, like a sublime Himalayan prospect discerned through a tiny window or peephole.

All the revelations I will recount here came in airport terminals, those gateways and crossroads of the modern world, which you so often passed through on your ceaseless journeying to deliver the fallen souls of this fallen time.

I relate these incidents in chronological order.

\* \* \*

You were flying out of New York City, heading West, and as usual a large crowd of devotees turned out to see you off. It seemed as if a greater part of the residents of the New York, Boston, Philadelphia, and Baltimore temples was there. You entered the boarding area for your flight, and the devotees streamed in after you, many vaulting over the boundary railing. You leaned back in a chair, and the devotees near you sat on the floor, while those further away remained standing. Devotees completely filled the boarding area and spilled out the entrance into the concourse. There were a lot of us, yet it was an opportunity for those sweet, informal, intimate sorts of exchanges we all cherished.

I found myself standing at the periphery. After a while I began to notice a representative of the airline in a state of increasing disturbance. One after another, he would accost a devotee on the edge of the crowd and address him in an agitated way. The devotees would glance at the airline representative for a moment, and then return full attention to you, ignoring the poor functionary. Seeing a full-blown “incident” brewing up, I went over to the official and asked him the matter. He pointed out that none of the other passengers on the flight were able to enter the boarding area, for it was filled completely with devotees. Among us, only you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the actual ticket-holding passenger, should have been in the boarding lounge. Would I please help get the other devotees to leave? It was true that a big crowd of passengers stood outside in the concourse.

My attempts to do something met with no more success than the official’s. I appreciated the devotees’ priorities and realized the job required someone with more clout than I had. I managed to draw aside Rūpānuga Prabhu, our GBC representative. Rupānuga was endowed with what the army calls “command presence.” People do what he says. Yet this time he too got the brush-off. Everyone was focused on what you were doing, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and next to that even Rupānuga carried no weight.

Then Rupānuga Prabhu seized upon an extraordinary measure. Jumping upon a chair, he cupped his hands around his mouth and yelled with considerable force: “Hare Kṛṣṇa! All devotees *must* leave the boarding area immediately! All devotees leave right now!”

This awesome display of command presence was greeted with shocked silence. Offended faces swiveled up toward Rupānuga and gaped in wordless rebuke for this affront. But not you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I remember you looking up at Rupānuga with a mild, round-eyed gaze of slight concern. And before anyone else could gather wits together to react, you had risen to your feet and started toward the exit.

Rupānuga smiled. “Not you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,” he said.

Not you. When Rupānuga yelled, the disciples showed their pride; but the master was humble. How much was revealed in that instant of reflex action. Within a moment’s span of shocked silence, you disclosed a heart-stopping view of unfathomable humility and pridelessness.

\* \* \*

You had landed in New York’s La Guardia to a thunderous *kīrtana* by a hoard of devotees. From a *vyāsāsana* set up in the arrivals area you received *pūjā*, and we held on to every word as you preached boldly and wittily to reporters at a well-attended press conference. Then you set off down an exceptionally long concourse, a lengthening train of devotees strung out after you. I walked just ten or twelve feet behind you, and when I looked up the concourse past you, I saw something truly alarming. Two men, grotesquely fitted out, raced directly toward you on a collision course. A large television camera bounced and swayed on the shoulder of one, and all kinds of gear banged about his torso. (This was over twenty years ago, when the mini-cams were rather maxi.) Linked to him by an umbilicus of electronic cable, a chubby, red-faced reporter tried to hold on to a microphone and notebook while keeping his blow-dried hair in place.

When the pair crashed to a halt in front of you, you were forced to stop short, and when I in turn came to a standstill, I found myself next to you.

The pair had missed the press conference but were determined to get their story. Writhing and bobbing, they hurriedly sorted themselves out. One adjusted the camera, the other his face and hair. Still huffing, the plump reporter stuck the microphone into your face and breathlessly fired off his first question: “How does your group differ from other Buddhists?” he said.

I saw how your eyes were fixed upon the reporter’s countenance. “We have nothing to do with this Hinduism or Buddhism,” you said at once. “We are teaching the truth. And if you are truthful, you will accept it.”

The reporter’s “follow-up” question was sheer babble. You had just blown away layers of mental concoctions and brought him face to face with reality, the last thing he was prepared for. But I scarcely noticed him. I was taken away by an epiphany, made stunned and breathless myself by your words.

I was astonished by the way you answered—or didn’t answer—the question. You simply refused the mental platform on which the question was based. I wonder how many of us would have lamely corrected him on the same platform. “We’re not Buddhist but a branch of Hinduism.” Or, more smoothly: “We are Vaiṣṇavas, which is an ancient, bona fide part of the broader Hindu tradition.” This is not preaching at all. At best, it is a limp, inappropriate sort of “public relations.” Had you responded in those terms, you would have caused him injury by reinforcing his *māyā*. Therefore, you refused to accommodate yourself to his mental structures and categories, to ideas like “Hinduism,” “Buddhism,” and even “religion,” all of which are freighted with illusion. Instead, you brushed aside the categories of *māyā* and then presented reality: “We are teaching the truth.” That is preaching.

Your response was so quick off the mark, it testified to your preternatural presence of mind. I had a glimpse of an intelligence fixed absolutely in Kṛṣṇa.

You blew away illusion in another way. The reporter came with his “game” scripted out. Call it “Reporter Interview with Public Religion Figure, Type Exotic and Eastern.” He presumed both of you understood the situation and would play the game according to well-known rules. You dismissed all the roles and the rules. He was going to put you into his movie, but instead you effortlessly brought him into yours. He was programmed to be the “reporter” in a stereotyped, ritualized encounter. Yet you addressed him as a real person beyond all bodily categories, as a soul, and you confronted him with the soul’s momentous, fateful choice. I saw the way you gazed at him, with a look at once penetrating and caring, as you put him on the line: “And if you are truthful, you will accept it.” The reporter was scarcely prepared to be addressed as a soul by the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead. I am sure he had never met anyone who cared for him so completely or revealed as much to him of the real issue of his life.

The encounter was over in minutes, and you continued down the concourse. But the hair on the back of my neck was standing up, as it still does whenever I recollect the immense thing you did, standing in an airport concourse, with just a few small, simple words.

\* \* \*

I had come to see you off in the Calcutta airport. That year, I was very happy to be personally directed by you in some special service. From Māyāpur you had sent me to the University of Calcutta to inquire into the possibility of affiliating a proposed “ISKCON Bhagavat College” in Māyāpur with the venerable university. I had reported back to you when you arrived several days later at the Calcutta temple, and the next morning I went to see you off as you waited to board the flight to New Delhi.

I saw you waiting while your servant was checking you in. You were sitting on one of those awful seats, one of a row of molded plastic bucket-seats bolted to a steel rail. A small group of devotees sat at your feet. You saw me as I walked toward you. Your face brightened, and holding me in your gentle gaze, you smiled slightly; you beckoned to me with your hand and patted the seat next to you. Devotees scooted aside, and I begin to lower myself to sit on the floor. You patted the seat a little firmer. “Sit here,” you said. And you leaned toward me and began to confide in me some further thought about the project.

After that we discussed the failings of academic education, and so I told you about my introductory Hinduism course. This was in my first year of graduate school, in 1967, three years before I met devotees. Our instructor was a prominent and scholarly impersonalist *sannyāsī* from uptown Manhattan. He was someone you knew. In fact, our instructor had mentioned to us that the semester before, a “bhakti-yogi” had spoken to his class. That had been you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Then I told you a scandalous story about him: Whenever Indian students would be going home for a visit, he would importune them to bring back *biḍis* for him. Your face filled with surprise and amusement. “No!” you said, then added: “Just see.”

Of course, it was delightful to sit with you at ease, and amuse you with a (somewhat) relevant bit of ancient gossip. But what really shines like the full moon in my private iconography of you is this. I see you the moment you saw me coming toward you, and you made a few ordinary, homely gestures. I see your face light up in a smile of welcome; I feel your kind and steady gaze. I watch your hand raise and beckon me to come near and then descend in a graceful arc and go pat-pat-pat very precisely on the seat next to you. These are the plain, unexceptional, gestures of everyday life. Yet when I saw you in those few seconds, I also saw the visible termination of billions of ages in *mṛtyu-saṁsāra*. I saw you, in a few seconds on a cheap seat in the Calcutta airport, offer the greatest of all gifts not just to me, but to all sojourners in this world.

\* \* \*

Misers yearn to hold “infinite riches in little rooms.” What I have attained surpasses their wildest longings. I carry these absolutely precious spiritual gems in the small palm of my memory. At any time, I can open it and see the priceless jewels of humility, intelligence, and mercy—your matchless, boundless gifts to be grasped and kept even by those as small as I am.

Thank you for everything.

Your insignificant servant,  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Rohiṇīsuta dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

Today all ISKCON devotees have the wonderful opportunity to celebrate the Centennial of your appearance in this world, a place that year after year becomes more and more miserable. Your prophecies and those of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* are becoming manifest more and more. There are unlimited disturbances, such as long, cold winters and blistering hot summers, so much pollution everywhere, earthquakes, and wars. The demons constantly try to put many obstacles in the way of your preaching mission. And in addition there are all our own shortcomings and *anarthas*. From you and your books we know that this is the nature of the material world, and it all comes from the sinful actions and consciousness of the people. The real enemy is in us.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.15.43–44 states, “In the conditioned stage, one’s conceptions of life are sometimes polluted by passion, hostility, greed, lamentation, illusion, fear, madness, false prestige, insults, fault-finding, deception, envy, intolerance, passion, bewilderment, hunger, and sleep. All of this are enemies. Sometimes one’s conceptions are also polluted by goodness \[in form of sattvic sense enjoyment and *mukti*\].” Kali-yuga is an ocean of faults. But there is one wonderful opportunity: to take shelter in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, which you mercifully and selflessly brought to us. You write in your purport:

*“brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**“If one is fortunate enough to transcend all this so-called goodness and badness and come to the platform of devotional service by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa and the** ***guru,*** **his life becomes successful. In this regard, one must be very bold so that he can conquer these enemies of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Not caring for the good and bad of this material world, one must boldly propagate Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”**

Your translation of the next verse is as follows:

“As long as one has to accept a material body, with its different parts and paraphernalia, which are not fully under one’s control, one must have the lotus feet of his superiors, namely his spiritual master and the spiritual master’s predecessors. By their mercy, one can sharpen the sword of knowledge, and with the power of the Supreme Personality of Godhead’s mercy one must then conquer the enemies mentioned above. . . .”

This human form of body is a most valuable boat, and the spiritual master is the captain to guide the boat in plying across the ocean of nescience. The instruction of Krsna is a favorable breeze. One must use all these facilities to cross over the ocean of nescience. Since the spiritual master is the captain, one must serve the *guru* very sincerely so that by his mercy one will be able to get the mercy of Krsna.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we think that we have met Your Divine Grace by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of human being. The nectar of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s words and the descriptions of His activities are the life and soul of those who are always aggrieved in this material world. These narrations are transmitted by exalted personalities like you, and they eradicate all sinful reactions. Whoever hears these narrations from your books attains all good fortune. These books are broadcast all over the world by your will and mercy and are filled with spiritual power. We planned to distribute more books then ever during this special year. But being beggars, we know that it is only due to your causeless mercy that we may be able to offer you some nice results. We all know that book distribution is most pleasing to you, that it is your life and soul. It is the best way to try to pay back what you did for us and the whole world. Therefore please accept our humble service and bless us so that we can go on distributing your books, reading your books, and preaching about your books. That’s the only nectar in this world.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me so much opportunity to serve you in this transcendental movement.

Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Your thankful servant  
Rohiṇīsuta dāsa

## Romapāda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā*  
*dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.”** ***(Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.22)**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Countless obeisances will be offered unto you during this Centennial Vyāsa-pūjā day as we join worldwide in the joyful celebration of your divine appearance. In fact this whole Centennial year may well be the most significant, spiritually uplifting occurrence for the whole planet, for this entire century! When the pure devotee is worshiped and served very nicely, Kṛṣṇa is more pleased than when He Himself is nicely worshiped and served to the highest standard of excellence. As countless offerings, millions upon millions, will be offered to you on this day and during this year, surely Kṛṣṇa must be increasing in His unlimited happiness.

Such wonderful offerings of devotion are being presented to you this entire Centennial year—the opening of fantastic temples, representing the ripened fruits of so many years of hard work; special publications from scholars, youth groups, congregation members; devotional writings from your disciples honoring you; festivals, parades, memorials, seminars, mass rallies, *hari-nāmas,* disciple reunions—all in your honor; certificates for the study of your books; and so much more!

Not only are millions upon millions offering their praises, service, and eulogies to you, but by this glorification they are establishing that you are the most perfect recipient of such honor! Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura has said, *ei chaya gosāñi yāra, mui tāra dāsa tāṅ’ sabāra pada-reṇu mora pañca-grāsa*. As you once translated this statement by Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, “Why shall I become anyone’s *dāsa*? But if somebody is the servant of the Gosvāmīs, then I become his servant. Your dust of lotus feet, that is my food.”

Willingly or unwillingly, everyone is a servant. We have voluntarily become your loving servants, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you are the best of all the servants of the six Gosvāmīs. Prolific like Jīva Gosvāmī, a constant reciter of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* like Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmī, strictly adhering to the teachings of Rūpa and Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmīs, the final authority amongst all Vaiṣṇavas like Sanātana Gosvāmī, teaching us how to properly understand the relationship of all things to Kṛṣṇa, and filled with ecstatic devotion for Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, as were all of the six Gosvāmīs—these are your credentials. Because you are such an exalted an ardent follower of the six Gosvāmīs, we place ourselves at your feet, trying to become your servants also. According to Vaiṣṇava authorities, you are to be honored precisely in this way.

The standards you placed before us were in all respects the highest, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your expectations of us were immense, and your ability to inspire us to meet those expectations was equally immense. And the means that you gave us to meet those expectations was the simple following of your instructions, through the *paramparā*.

While flying recently in an airplane in America, I sat down in my assigned seat with a space between myself and an obviously well-to-do elderly woman passenger. As soon as she spoke, I knew she was a New Yorker. “Awesome! Awesome!!” she exclaimed. At first I did not know what to make of what she was expressing. I was dressed in my saffron-dyed silks, everything clean as a whistle, actually thinking at the moment how my mere presence in an airport, sitting in an airplane, moving here and there wearing the Vaiṣṇava dress you have given us, marks me as one of your mobile representatives. So when this lady was reporting her impression as “Awesome!” I couldn’t help but think that she was right! What you have accomplished by transforming yet another face in the crowd into a transcendental emissary of yours, singularly dedicated to simply canvassing for persons to take the same mercy of the six Gosvāmīs that you have given us, and through them the mercy of Lord Caitanya, and through Him the mercy of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa—this is certainly awesome!

Then I noticed that the same bespectacled and poised elite New Yorker was focusing on my *japa-mālā*. It has happened before that persons have asked with extreme curiosity, “What is that cast on your hand?” So she could know that I hadn’t damaged my hand and that it wasn’t wrapped up in some mass of bandage, I showed her that there were beads inside the bead bad. With a friendly smile I held up my *tulasī* beads for her to examine.

With the same vivacity and “New Yorker command” as her initial comment, this woman again repeated, “Simply awesome!!!”

Yes, it is true, the holy name is completely awesome. It is so powerful that even the most fallen can become transformed by the power of the holy name. Moreover, it is so completely awesome that Nāma Prabhu has made revolutionary transformations in the lives of those to whom you have so liberally distributed the holy name, all across the face of this planet. Even more awesome is how you are continuing to do the same! In so many novel ways, like the “Nāma parikrama” in Māyāpur, or the massive *kīrtanas* with thousands of people participating in India, or the similar festivals where your disciples are engaging thousands upon thousands of people in Eastern Europe and Russia—in so many unique ways, untold numbers of people are being purified by hearing and chanting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Yes, she was right, “Simply awesome!”

The fruit produced by the holy name being chanted in so many places around the world is yet another awesome challenge: how to manage the budding fruit of so many new devotees needing training, needing guidance, needing direction in their spiritual lives. Managing temples, raising the funds to maintain all of your wonderful projects—this is yet another awesome service you have given us.

In your very special, quietly modest yet awesome way, you have given us so many directions how to accomplish this task. A particular passage from one of your purports in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* inspires me very much in this regard. In classic eloquent fashion you have described how proper leadership can be provided to guide, correct, and manage this wonderful institution that you have created: “The modern elected executive head of a state is just like a puppet because he has no kingly power. Even if he is enlightened like Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira, he cannot do anything out of his own good will due to his constitutional position. Therefore, there are so many states \[all over the world\] quarreling because of ideological differences or other selfish motives. But a king like Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira had no ideology of his own. He had but to follow the instructions of the infallible Lord or the Lord’s representative and the authorized agent, Bhīṣmadeva. It is instructed in the *śāstras* that one should follow the great authorities and the infallible Lord without any personal motive \[or\] manufactured ideology. Therefore it was possible for Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira to rule the whole world, including the seas, because the principles were infallible and universally applicable to everyone.”

Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira had received ample instructions from Grandfather Bhīṣmadeva, and all of what had been taught to him by Grandfather Bhīṣmadeva was confirmed personally by Lord Kṛṣṇa. These are the infallible sources, unlike all other sources, which are guaranteed to have misconceptions, fallible ideologies, and all other features of human frailty.

If we have perfect faith in your divine teachings, and if we have the firm commitment and resolute determination to carry out those teachings in our practical lives, then everything you have given us to do and things you will continue to give us to do as time passes will all be accomplished. I have absolute faith in the means of success because I have absolute faith in you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and in the pure disciplic succession you represent.

As your own life is an unequaled testimonial of total self-sacrifice and commitment to the principle of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness for the benefit of all the whole world’s masses of conditioned souls, let me also make the same commitment to you, to my fullest personal capacity.

Pṛthu Mahārāja, as one of the incarnations of the Lord, was committed to the performance of philanthropic activity, specifically the protection of cows and the maintenance of brahminical culture, “as if he was working for his own personal interest.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you also worked tirelessly, far more so than even the most attached person acts to further his own interest.

Jarāsandha, although an *asura,* was equally “committed” to giving charity to *brāhmaṇas,* so much so that he knowingly was ready to offer his own head or his own life simply for this principle.

Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your commitment to the cause of Lord Caitanya, your compassionately giving your association to all yet maintaining the highest standard of purity and fidelity to the message of our disciplic succession—this is my greatest inspiration, in your teachings and your example. Throughout this year I have made it a point to renew my personal commitment to you, daily, to assist you in this mission. This gives me great strength and courage.

My commitment to further your mission and spread your pure instructions and teachings is a wellspring of unlimited inspiration, and this commitment is my most significant, however feeble, offering to you on this Vyāsa-pūjā day. Please accept the offering of this life, including whatever gifts you have offered me in the course of my service to you, in the dust of your lotus feet.

Your servant,  
Romapāda Swami

## Śeṣa dasa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:

Please accept my humble obeisances.

As your *gṛhastha* disciple, I have often cast envious eyes upon my *sannyāsī* Godbrothers, both lamenting the burden of my *āśrama* and desiring the facility for serving you that others possess. Forgive me, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Forgive me for my lowborn mentality. Forgive me for neglecting to learn from the example you set. For just as you taught your *sannyāsī* disciples how to serve you, you taught your married disciples the ideal way to serve in the *gṛhastha-āśrama*.

This realization came to me one day as my eyes fell upon some verses from Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s *Śaraṇāgati,* which are framed and hung on the wall of my home. Many times I looked at those verses, many times I failed to grasp their meaning:

“Whatever I am, whatever I possess,  
I offer to Your lotus feet, O merciful Lord.

All my possessions—body, brothers, friends,  
and followers, wife, sons, personal belongings,  
house and home I give to You, for I have become  
Your servant.

Family members and friends are all  
Your servants and maidservants. Whatever  
care I take of them is only as it  
relates to You.

You are the Lord of my house,  
Your happiness is my only endeavor.”

I have been thinking the *gṛhastha-āśrama* is my burden, but actually it is my service to you. I came to you alone and with nothing. You entrusted me with a good devotee wife, two wonderful devotee children, and devotee friends and family members. They have come to you for shelter, and you have given me the service of caring for them. How fortunate I am to be given such valuable service!

O how difficult it is to understand one’s real fortune in life! During your *gṛhastha* life, while faithfully executing your household responsibilities year after year, you tried to involve your wife and family in your preaching activities. They resisted, you persisted. I, on the other hand, have desisted. Despite having a family eager to participate in your ISKCON, all to often I surrender to the call of *māyā* and let material necessity rule my life.

You built a house in which the whole world can live, and gave your disciples the task of showing the world how to live there. You created the *gṛhastha-āśrama* for this purpose only, not to placate the demands of our senses. We … I have much work to do. If there be a struggle, I welcome it as self- purification. If there be happiness, let it stem from the success of your mission.

Bless me, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Bless me that I may learn the lessons of the *gṛhastha-āśrama*. Bless me with your potency to follow your example. Bless me that I may set an ideal example as your *gṛhastha* disciple.

Your servant,  
Śeṣa dāsa

## Śivarāma Swami

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

It is midday. I am standing on the roof overlooking Śrīdhāma Māyāpur. Your Samādhi stands prominent in the foreground. Below, final preparations are being made to accommodate devotees coming from around the globe. It will be the celebration of the Centennial of your appearance. There are others with me, but we do not converse. We are waiting. While meditating on a Centennial contribution of my own I open the book in my hand and read.

Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura is saying, “The teachings left by the founder-*ācārya* of the *sampradāyas* are to be especially honored. Śrī Rāmānuja, Śrī Madhvācārya, Śrī Nimbārkācārya, and Śrī Viṣṇu Swami are the four original *ācāryas* of the four Vaiṣṇava lines. One should accept only their teaching and conclusions and not others.”

You are the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON. That everyone knows today, although it was not so in the past. In 1970 devotees forgot your title on the new BBT building. You called it “a conspiracy.” There were other incidents, and each time you were emphatic that devotees not neglect your position.

Having of heard these events from a distance, I used to wonder, “Why was Śrīla Prabhupāda so insistent?” Even the breeze passing over pride never blew your way. You were the humble servant of your Guru Mahārāja. I thought, “It is a minimization of Śrīla Prabhupāda.” But I did not delve further into it. It was not clear to me what the founder-*ācārya* of a *sampradāya* is.

Here it states that the teachings of the founder-*ācārya* are to be especially honored. “Which teachings are especially honored? After all, Śrīla Prabhupāda is repeating the message of the disciplic succession.” But you were so emphatic. There must be more to it all. A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda was not just the spiritual master of ISKCON or another great devotee. He was the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Yet “the teachings … are to be especially honored.”

I look up past the Jalangi to the large banyan tree. Behind it, unseen from my vantage point, is Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda’s house. Sounds of the meeting are behind me.

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu had come to distribute spontaneous devotional service without discrimination. While touring South India He instructed that this unique gift be distributed worldwide. By His order devotional service was forever connected with its distribution. *Sādhana* and *sādhya* were wed with *pracāra*.

This unique blend of practice and teaching was put into practice by Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda and re-emphasized with greater strength by Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. Prior to their time Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism was generally dominated by *bābājīs,* their distinctive trait being solitary and secluded practice of *nāma-bhajan*. As a consequence, the dissemination of Lord Caitanya’s mission in India ceased. Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura both desired to re-establish the course of the *sampradāya*. They wanted active, worldwide missionary activity. But at the disappearance of Sarasvatī Ṭhākura the great success of the Gauḍīya Math in serving these aims abruptly came to an end.

Yes, it was you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who gave new life to Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism by stressing preaching as well as practice. Although you did not introduce anything “new,” you redirected the disciplic succession, which in your own words had become *asāra*. Though others may have not deviated in their practices, they had neglected preaching. In your eyes they had “no brain” for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Thus by your efforts you once again established the integrity of the message and practices of the *paramparā*.

Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura further states that one should accept only the teachings and conclusions of the founder-*ācārya* and not others.

Though the *siddhānta* may remain unchanged, there is methodology in its presentation. Preaching is not just a matter of transmitting knowledge. The founder-*ācārya* is empowered by the Lord to distribute the message according to time, place, and circumstance. You say, “An *ācārya* should devise means by which people may somehow or other come to Kṛṣṇa Consciousness. … In this connection *deśa, kāla, patra* should be taken into consideration.” Those not following the founder-*ācārya* are not qualified to criticize such methods.

By directing all members of ISKCON to preach, you set on course the direction of the *paramparā*. This instruction is your hallmark and bears its imprint on the Society up to the present day. To facilitate and protect this preaching on a worldwide scale, you have harmoniously expressed the teachings of Lord Caitanya in a unique way.

The system of *siddha-praṇālī* of the *bābājīs* was replaced by *nāma-saṅkīrtana* in the mood of the third *śloka* of *Śrī Śikṣāṣṭaka*. As a *sannyāsī* you performed marriage ceremonies to inspire the youth of the West. You emphasized that *rāgānuga-bhakti* as taught by Caitanya Mahāprabhu should not be imitated at the stage of *śraddhā* but would transform through grace after one practices *vaidhi* to the stage of *āsakti*. However, in order for a *sādhaka* to achieve *sādhya,* he must attract Kṛṣṇa’s attention and then His mercy, and to do that the *sādhaka’s* preaching activities must parallel his efforts to achieve *rāgānuga-bhakti*. Other practices that were conducive to preaching you adopted in the mood of *yukta-vairāgya*. Thus it was at your request that *śāstra* be made available for public distribution. You gave an institutional identity to Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana,* and as a jet-age *parivrājakācārya* you traveled to the land of the cow-eaters.

A gentle breeze blows, and with it the sound of your amplified *kīrtana*. I look up. The meeting is still under way.

“One should receive spiritual initiation in one of these four *sampradāyas* and not others. A devotee must understand that the *ādi-guru,* original spiritual master of the *sampradāya,* is the *śikṣā-guru,* and only his teachings are to be accepted and not those of any other scholar or teacher.”

I received initiation but I did not understand the *ādi-guru*. How it was that only your teachings are to be accepted and not those of any other scholar or teacher? You are the *śikṣā-guru,* for you have established the tenor of the teachings and practice for all members of ISKCON. Being faithful to you means being faithful to your teachings. These teachings are the heritage of anyone who claims to be your true follower. Responsibility is with the devotee to “know” you by knowing your teachings. To fail to do so is negligence in one’s role as a member and disciple. Such a person will be easily distracted by other “teachers,” not being fixed in your *śikṣā*.

“Only a saintly devotee who has understood the teachings of the *śikṣā-guru* is eligible to be a *dīkṣā-guru* for others.”

You instructed that to serve the mission everyone should take responsibility for others and become *guru*. Although one may not be fully realized, by representing your teachings one would be empowered. Through the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness one would no doubt achieve perfection. Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura emphasizes that all subsequent *gurus* in this line derive their authority by fidelity to the *ādi-guru*. A person may be conversant in the philosophy, but if he is not true to the teachings of the founder-*ācārya* he has no right to act as *guru*. Both spiritual master and disciple have equal responsibility to be your true follower, for you remain the *śikṣā-guru* for all subsequent generations of devotees.

Some time has passed, it is almost lunch time. We are called to return. It was an instructive break. I understand something I had not understood before. Behavior is connected to understanding, and understanding to revelation. By your grace I understand a little more the meaning of the founder-*ācārya*. By that understanding may I be more faithful to you, your mission, and your teachings. It is an insignificant offering for your Centennial. I offer my obeisances to those who are strict followers of your will. By their grace may I become more faithful to you still.

Your servant,  
Śivarāma Swami

## Śrīdhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day, one hundred years after your appearance in this world, I offer my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

I have been meditating on writing this offering for some time, hoping to write something worthy of one of your elder sons. Your greatness is reflected in your disciples and followers. In one sense, if I fail to glorify you nicely then I am a useless son. Cāṇakya compares such a son to urine.

I enjoy trying to motivate devotees to serve you, but I know that if I can understand what motivated you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I will have the perfect formula for helping others.
