Your life is a wonder and your achievements are innumerable. You achieved success in both quantity and quality. Your humility was demonstrated in many ways, but especially by your austerity and sacrifice. What motivated you to tolerate so many difficulties?

You lost your business, which could have helped you in your work. Your family not only did not support you but hindered you. What motivated you to remain steadfast in your determination to preach and to serve Lord Kṛṣṇa?

You worked alone with no assistance or encouragement from your Godbrothers. You tolerated poverty. You always made grand plans for preaching although you did not have any of the resources to achieve them. What motivated you to be so confident in the face of such obstacles?

Two vicious heart attacks on the boat to America, being alone in a foreign land with an austere plan for salvation for a generation of hedonists, the constant struggle to establish and maintain an initial base temple … What gave you the courage to face such difficulties? What motivated you to push on?

From our infancy in spiritual life, you were guiding, correcting, inspiring, consoling, empowering, and chastising but always loving us. From 1966 at 26 Second Avenue to November 14th in Vṛndāvana you never stopped serving your disciples. What motivated you to show us such kindness, compassion, and love?

Perhaps I know part of the answer because I think you answered my prayer to know you a little better. While listening to a cassette tape of a lecture you gave in Vṛndāvana in 1972, I was meditating on this very question, and as if to enter my thoughts your voice resounded: “The spiritual master is the servant of his disciples because he sees the disciples as representatives of his spiritual master.”

“My only qualification is that I try to serve the order of my spiritual master.”

You never stopped serving your spiritual master. Your mood and your goal was to render unflinching, unconditional, pure service to your beloved spiritual master. You loved him and served him by your body, mind, and words at every second. You loved us through your service to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.

If I am to achieve success in my attempt to preach and glorify Lord Kṛṣṇa, then I must first develop this same mood of service and glorification of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is my remembrance of you that will motivate me to do my duty in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The strength I need to conquer my unbridled senses; the humility to suffer the lot of my *pūrva-karma* in a jolly spirit, without feeling victimized by anyone or anything; it all comes from whatever development of love I have for Your Divine Grace.

I don’t feel that I have achieved any success, but by studying you in the eleven short years that you were with us, I can get a glimpse of the path, and that is the greatest gift you have given me. Please bless me to become your instrument and to be motivated to achieve your eternal service at some time in the coming millenniums.

Your eternal servant motivated only by you,  
Śrīdhara Swami

## Suhotra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my most humble obeisances in the shade of your lotus feet, which cools the fever of material desire in the minds of the fallen conditioned souls.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, may a comprehension of all you have done and continue to do for this most unworthy, bewildered, and frail-hearted soul now manifest as the sincerity with which this offering should be made. Kindly grant me the opportunity, in this life and in unlimited future lives, to personally repay you for accepting me, in Boston twenty-four years ago, as your disciple.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I write this offering in the most holy Śrīdhām Māyāpura, where your divine presence at the Puṣpa-samādhi Mandira is a tangible reality. My visits to you there leave me refreshed, inspired and deeply moved. The massive white marble dome topped by the golden *kalaśa* is the Meru of this age, the pillar that holds up the world. Gazing upon it, I am reminded of a saying: “One man with courage makes a majority.”

As architecture, the Samādhi Mandir is stunning. But devotees are not so very concerned with architecture. I’ve seen bigger and more ornate structures in various parts of the world, but they hold no sway over my mind as does the Mandir. There can’t be a comparison, because the Mandir was built in sacrifice to Your Divine Grace. Similarly, in the mundane estimation there are so-called great men whose names are household words worldwide. But whatever they did was for sense gratification. What you’ve done is for Kṛṣṇa. And, yes, in appreciation for that, Kṛṣṇa lets wax more and more the transcendental moon of your fame, besides which the fame of the fathers of nations, the learned savants and the heroes, is mere starlight. Soon, doubtlessly, your name will be on lips worldwide. But what sets you apart, what compels us to your lotus feet, what actually establishes you in our hearts and makes us your eternal followers, is simply that all of what you’ve done, big or small, you’ve done for Kṛṣṇa. The pastimes so powerfully depicted in the Samādhi diorama display—your struggles to serve your Guru Mahārāja in India from the 40’s till you left for America; the risky steamship journey you took at an elderly age; your preaching among the hippies; your inaugurating Ratha-yātrā in San Francisco; your flooding the world with your transcendental books—testify to your love for Him whom all the worldly icons of fame forgot, and helped others forget. One man with courage for Kṛṣṇa makes a majority Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please instill our hearts with your courage, and please keep us tightly under your protection. I am writing this homage during a lunar eclipse here in Māyāpur. A few nights ago I saw a comet. Two nights before Gaura-pūrṇimā, a ring of light appeared around the moon. Within some days after the next Gaura-pūrṇimā, a huge comet is predicted to pass by the earth. These are all inauspicious portents. With the rise of your movement in the world, the personality of Kali feels threatened. Just when he thought he had defeated religion with material science; just when he thought he had buried the burnt remnants of morality under an avalanche of “free love” and pornography; just when he thought the world had been seduced by the doctrine of “I am God”—you established ISKCON, thereby exposing the cheating of the scientists, the futile nastiness of illicit sex, and The Emperor’s New Clothes of modern man’s claims to supremacy over creation. Kali’s Medusan gaze is now upon us. He has the power to turn the hearts of all ordinary men and women to stone.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I fear for your society of devotees. I fear for myself in this society. My heart was already stone; and while you shattered it with the powerful hammer blow of your preaching, not all the granite chips fell away. Indeed, I foolishly clung to them. Now, once more feeling the petrifying grip of Kali upon my heart, I know that I must come under your full shelter, that I may not be frozen in my tracks. I know this—but I am weak and wavering. Help me, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Help your ISKCON. The entire material energy is pitted against us. If we remain ordinary men and women, we’ll be powerless to serve you. And that, for one who has known service to you, is a fate worse than death. There’s only one way to be saved from this: all vestiges of contamination must be resolutely excised and thrown away, and these bodies, minds, and souls must be fully dedicated to your lotus feet.

I am low, fallen, and troubled by the reactions of countless offenses in devotional service. If you do not rescue me, I will be lost. While I have no qualification to expect your favor, I cannot approach anyone else for shelter, as there is no one other than you in whom I can put my faith. Seeing my helplessness, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please do what you think is best.

Before closing, Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly permit me to directly address the hearers and readers of this offering, be they devotees or the simply curious, whether now be the 1996 Centennial Vyāsa-pūjā Day or any time thereafter. Dear friends, have no doubt that Śrīla Prabhupāda is with us here today at this gathering in his honor. I have no worth that I can see, yet Śrīla Prabhupāda keeps me in his service. You are exalted souls and have so much more to offer His Divine Grace than I ever will. Take his mercy, please. Let us, all together now, cry out from every last recess of our hearts: ŚRĪLA PRABHUPĀDA KĪ JAYA! ŚRĪLA PRABHUPĀDA’S 1996 CENTENNIAL VYĀSA-PŪJĀ CELEBRATION KĪ JAYA! ŚRĪLA PRABHUPĀDA, OUR ONLY HOPE, KĪ JAYA! Now let us all resolve to be united in his mission, eternally.

All glories to Your Divine Grace in this Centennial Year of your merciful appearance in this material world!

Your eternal servant,  
Suhotra Swami

## Tamāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I remember when Śrīla Prabhupāda directed that his *mūrti* form should be placed on his *vyāsāsanas* around the world so that he might preside over all temple activities. By doing so, His Divine Grace assured us that we would always be able to experience his physical presence. At the time I liked the idea very much, but over the years I have appreciated more and more just how merciful and all-knowing an instruction that was.

I am sure each of us feels Prabhupāda present when we gaze at his *mūrti* and worship him in *guru-pūjā,* while chanting our rounds, or during a *kīrtana*. We feel that he is very much present in the temple room just as he always was, and feel confident that he still hears our personal prayers and will relay them to Kṛṣṇa.

I especially appreciate Prabhupāda’s *mūrti* in his private quarters in Dallas. Circumstances have been such that I have been staying here continuously for the past year. I have the good fortune to reside just next door to Śrīla Prabhupāda. Quite naturally, I have taken responsibility to personally attend to Prabhupāda in many ways, as I was able to do when I served him as his secretary.

I find it remarkable that I can sit before Prabhupāda now in his deity form and feel the same soothing comfort I did when previously I had his *vapu* association. I’m able to express, though silently, my innermost feelings—my thoughts, my worries, my questions, and my devotion—and believe certainly that Prabhupāda hears me. I know this without doubt because of the satisfaction I feel within my heart. I feel my attachment for Prabhupāda growing daily in his association, just as it did previously.

Faithless persons may doubt that any real connection is taking place. They will argue that it is simply my own psyche reflecting upon itself, imagining some relationship with another. These persons clearly do not understand anything about love. By the same argument they use to deny the reality of my exchanges with Prabhupāda they would have to reject their own feelings for departed family and friends.

I am so pleased that Śrīla Prabhupāda has made himself available and allowed me to revive my personal service to him in this *vapu* manifestation. And because there were so many times we were alone together, I am happy that once again such private opportunities are presenting themselves. There are thoughts which one tells no one else but one’s spiritual master; there are times when one needs the shelter which no one else can provide except one’s spiritual master; for there are feelings quite unlike any others which one reserves only for one’s spiritual master.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for accepting me under all circumstances. Thank you for always being here for me. Without you, my life has absolutely no meaning, no worth. Please allow me to always remain attached to your lotus feet birth after birth.

Your servant,  
Tamāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Trivikrama Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept my humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I was deep in the night of ignorance,  
covered by dark from pole to pole.

Since time without end, such was my fate,  
filled to the brim with longing and hate.

But I was a man so I put on a show,  
so no one could see the doubts just below.

Pretending that I was happy and free,  
attempting to cover the actual me.

Until I met you with your loving gaze  
That penetrated through my artificial haze.

You saw me right to my very tip,  
a soul held tightly in *māyā’s* grip.

Sweet mercy, your mercy, made me to see  
That I was a victim of hypocrisy.

Our selfish acts are just a dream  
That stops our service to the Supreme.

By constitution we are meant to be  
dedicated to Him for eternity.

He is the One! He is the One!  
Whose holy name should always be sung.

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

Please bless me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I can acquire a little taste for this *mahā-mantra*.

One of your less important disciples,  
Trivikrama Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Atma-nivedana Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Journey of A Pure Devotee**

In 1896 you started your journey in this material world, a journey to distribute the divine gift you had brought with you from the spiritual world. This gift is a priceless one, but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, distributed it freely to all. Along this journey, at first none came forward to take this divine gift, so unfortunate were the fallen souls. But you were so concerned for the well-being of all the fallen souls caught up in materialistic illusion that you carried on regardless of their unwillingness to accept the gift of love of Godhead, *kṛṣṇa-prema.* Little did the world know that in the holy *dhāma* of Vṛndāvana you were preparing to carry far and wide the brilliant torchlight of knowledge to dissipate the darkness in which the whole world was wallowing. You filled a trunk full of this divine gift and sailed across the Atlantic Ocean to shower the world with the holy name of the Lord .

In 1965 you arrived in the USA. The lost souls of the world were waiting for you to shower upon them your causeless mercy, just as in India, after a really hot summer, people eagerly await the cooling monsoon. The world was immersed in the darkness of ignorance, but you showered upon them the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa, which dissipates the darkness and brings the divine light of knowledge.

1966 you launched the transcendental boat of ISKCON. In this boat you traveled the world fourteen times, showering the whole world with *hari-nāma saṅkīrtana* to cleanse the mind, and distributing *prasādam* to purify the body. In this way you prepared the fallen souls to receive the divine gift of love of Godhead. Your Divine Grace opened many transcendental ports in the form of your temples. Into these ports has staggered many a weary traveler who had been lost in this dark world; the warm welcome such travelers received relieved them from the fatigue and pain that had accumulated over many, many lifetimes.

I am one such weary traveler who was fortunate to come across one of your transcendental ports. In that port I found instant relief from all pain, as well as soothing comfort at your lotus feet.

Such is your transcendental ISKCON boat that it has many ports of call. Many a weary traveler visits one of these ports, finds relief, and then begins the journey of a lifetime—back home, back to Godhead

So on this auspicious day I humbly beg you to keep me as a passenger on this transcendental boat of ISKCON and allow me to serve all who may travel on it.

A servant of your servants,  
Ātma-nivedana Swami

## Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please allow me to offer my respectful obeisances on a day like this, and to renew my vow of surrender and loyalty to the noble mission of expanding your glorious movement. I know I don’t deserve such a privilege. Now, more than ever, I realize that I am only a hopeless neophyte whom you tolerate so that through your mercy I can receive the irrefutable proof of the unconditional protection of Lord Caitanya.

I am proud of you, of being your disciple and your instrument. Kṛṣṇa blesses me by giving me the words to communicate with you on a day as special as this, on which we celebrate the Centennial of your glorious birth. Even though humanity is not well informed of the fact, the most important events in its history have been, and will continue to be, the glorious appearance of both Lord Caitanya and you. The truth is that your appearance in this world is the most important event that has happened in the history of all time, because without you no one would have had access to the causeless mercy of Lord Caitanya, who without discrimination distributed pure love for Kṛṣṇa amongst the most fallen souls of this Age of Kali, namely us, your Western servants and your eternal devotees by your grace.

Even though the limitations of my body will not allow me to be physically present in India to pay homage to you on your Samādhi, I feel close to you and am united to those who will be present by a feeling of immense gratitude that sprouts from my innermost soul. I will never forget that you have saved me from illusion to help me become Kṛṣṇa conscious. You have taught me the supreme goal of life, and you have shown me the correct path that leads me to it. I have only to walk it.

For many years my vision of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness philosophy has been superficial, and I have not had a very mature attitude in transmitting it to others. For the first time, I can clearly see my shortcomings and seriously recognize my mistakes. The capacity to see myself as I am, I owe to you; the way of acting with common sense, I also owe to you. In each phase of my life, I have found in you the example and stimulation I needed to reach positive conclusions in regard to my mistaken actions. My determination to fulfill my duty and to end successfully everything I start is due to the inspiration I receive from you. In the midst of difficulties, your absence of fear teaches me to have valor. Lately, I have felt more able to note the great benefits of your worldwide preaching. Your movement is gradually becoming the living lungs of universal spirituality. It is you who, through your books, attract the new generations of devotees. We all know that you represent the realization of the utopia: the respiritualization of humanity. Human society is going through difficult times due to the crisis of values brought on by the advance of materialism. Traditional religious institutions do not have leaders with enough spiritual values to engender an authentic reconciliation of their followers with God. As time goes by, confusion grows.

We must seek to have ISKCON continue to be the real spiritual shelter the entire world needs. We must help the rest of the world to spiritually act out everything they are capable of becoming. Sometimes, I have the impression that our methods are well meant but not very effective. I am referring to the practical application of the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which is not always effected correctly due to our neglectful conduct and scattered thoughts. I think that we have spent many years imitating everything you did without really understanding its deep significance and transcendence. We are inexperienced and unstable. We surrendered ourselves to your service with great initial euphoria and little consistency over time. When we were able to strengthen our position in the movement, we stopped sacrificing ourselves to devote our efforts to enjoying our benefits. We do not want to probe our hidden motivations and review our behavior patterns because we are afraid to face new contradictions and to recognize our own deficiencies. We are still in internal disagreement: what we want to do does not agree with what we must do, and vice versa. We are not able to see our interior fragmentation. We need to see our transition from emotional dependency to self-sufficiency. We still need to discover our real aptitudes, and we must put our trust in your wise instructions. Personally, I am beginning to understand that the opportunities for changing and becoming better are disguised as hard work, so that we are unable to recognize them as such, and scare ourselves. In this respect, we must consider that the constant adventure of living in Kṛṣṇa consciousness bears risks that, if not accepted, can turn against us and destroy us.

My beloved Prabhupāda, I must personally confess that I have grown poorly and need your help more than ever. I have faith in your generosity, born of your complete surrender to Kṛṣṇa (in spite of the great adversities you had to face to serve Him here on Earth). It is very obvious that that importance is reflected in all of the acts of your life. Evil makes shame blush in the presence of your kindness and nobility. Your genuine solidarity with grieving people proves that you are the irreplaceable instrument of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. Your immense capacity for living does not leave anyone out. Your heart is so great and kind that the entire world can take shelter in it and experience new spiritual dimensions. The hard work of your entire life molds my thoughts. Your capacity to be an instrument of the Lord’s will, in each and every instant of your life, is the undeniable proof of your exalted position as a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa and founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. The institution you have created constitutes an energy field that spiritually nourishes even the most incredulous of us. Your loving capacity softens the toughest hearts. Your unsurpassable purity opens up new paths of light and hope for everyone. Your exemplary life is a hymn of perseverance. With it, you have taught us that there must be union or marriage between love and knowledge in order for one to be truly wise and to surrender completely to Kṛṣṇa. In reading your books, one continually discovers new aspects of the Absolute Truth. Personally, you have made me understand that everything God has given us through nature, man must use to achieve his spiritual learning.

My efforts to become more and more sincere force me to unveil my inner contradictions. But if I seriously accept my commitment with you, I cannot avoid that conflict. Accepting my own contradictions helps me to understand the rest better. I owe this progress to you. Thank you for still keeping me in your service, and forgive me for all my offenses.

Your grateful servant,  
Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you.

Your ISKCON is increasing and improving on all fronts, it seems, and that is most inspiring. It is also a practical proof of your transcendental potency, and a clear proof of the spiritual system of *paramparā*. The mercy is coming down through the previous *ācāryas* and is coming to us. But it is clear that it is coming through you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and not through anyone else.

In the brief history of your ISKCON there have been many ups and downs, as we all know. And in recent years there have been some rather traumatic downs, at least in some parts of the world. But now, in your Centennial year, it appears that all over the movement things have picked up very strongly and a renewed sense of vigor and spiritual vitality is pervading more and more.

This is all coming in the wake of a deepened mood of commitment to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and a very serious attempt to follow your instructions and to please you in that way. Of course, in some areas that mood was never lost, and your movement never faltered, but in other parts things became a little shaky sometimes.

This is all bearing out the message of the well known verse of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura:

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.”**

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (2.41) Lord Kṛṣṇa states:

*vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana*  
*bahu-śākhā hy anantāś ca buddhayo ’vyavasāyinām*

**“Those who are on this path are resolute in purpose, and their aim is one. O beloved child of the Kurus, the intelligence of those who are irresolute is many-branched.”**

In his commentary to this verse Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura makes the following statements: “The best kind of intelligence I can have is intelligence used in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Intelligence is defined as ‘fixed’ when it is intent upon my spiritual master’s instructions, such as chanting the name of Kṛṣṇa, remembering His activities, and performing service to His lotus feet. My spiritual master’s instructions are my *sādhana* and my life, both in the beginning stages of *bhakti* as well in *bhakti’s* perfectional stage. I desire only to follow his instructions; I accept nothing else as my life’s work, even in dreams. Whether I am happy or distressed, whether the material world remains or is destroyed, I don’t care. There is no loss for me. I simply must carry out the orders of my spiritual master. Fixedness upon his orders is determined intelligence and devotional service, and only by being fixed on his orders can such determined intelligence prosper.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you of course embodied the meaning and purport of these words, and it is up to us to try to apply ourselves to your service in the same spirit. To the degree that we do this, individually and collectively, we will be successful and your movement will spread all over the world in leaps and bounds. And to the degree that we do not do this we will fail and our Kṛṣṇa consciousness and the movement will shrivel.

So as this movement is now spreading strongly in all quarters, we can appreciate that it is your mercy. It is up to us to avail ourselves of that and not do anything independently. For your part, you said on many occasions that your mercy is always available—it is just a matter of whether we take it or not.

I pray that you will always keep your mercy available to me. I am slow to learn and hindered by many shortcomings and obstacles. Please be kind.

The lowly servant of your servants,  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhaktivaidurya Mādhava Mahārāja

Dear Srila Prabhupada, on this day I remember the lessons about Krsna consciousness that I learned from you in September, 1968. At the time you had not originally planned to come to New York. You came only after we committed a great blunder. That year Janmāṣṭamī was toward the end of August. We celebrated it in a rather unusual way. In those days our Godbrother Jaya Govinda dāsa was living in Vṛndāvana. In his letters he mentioned that local people sometimes dressed up as Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and that everyone respected them as such. Thinking that this was a bona fide Vaiṣṇava custom, the temple authorities decided to celebrate Janmāṣṭamī in that way. They arranged for two *brahmacārinīs*—Indira and Ekāyanī—to dress up as Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Personally I didn't like the idea at all, but the temple authorities had ordained it, so I had to go along with their plans.

Indira dressed as Kṛṣṇa-she wore a bright yellow silk *dhoti,* a beautiful flower garland, and a crown with a peacock feather on her head. Ekāyanī dressed up as Rādhārāṇī. After the morning program on Janmāṣṭamī day the two girls were ushered into the temple room. Seeing them, everyone was amazed. They did look very much like Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. The real surprise came when it was announced that just as when a picture resembles Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa it becomes Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and is worshipable, so these two girls, now resembling Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, were similarly identical with Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and should therefore be worshiped as direct manifestations of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Some devotees cleared all your pictures off of your *vyāsāsana* and prepared it as a throne for this new Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. After they sat themselves on your *vyāsāsana*, everyone bowed down to them, offering them obeisances. Upon rising, they recited prayers in praise of them as Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Afterwards a plate of *bhoga* that would normally have been offered on the altar was brought and offered to the two girls. After eating what they wanted, the remnants were distributed to the assembled devotees and guests. Thereafter incense, a lamp, and a peacock fan were offered to them. I was told to hold an umbrella over them, but in my mind I was considering them rascals for pretending to be Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and wanted to beat them over the head with the umbrella. The devotees and guests passed the day offering various types of worship to the two girls.

Several days later, the temple president called his brother in Los Angeles, where you were staying at the time. He told his brother about how we had had such a wonderful Janmāṣṭamī festival in New York so that his brother would relay the information to you. You were not at all pleased by what you heard. As soon as his brother finished speaking, you immediately got on the phone to New York to talk with the temple president. You told him, “You have committed a great offense. This is *sahajiyā.* I am coming there immediately to rectify the situation.”

One week later, on August 31st, you arrived in New York. Your flight arrived after dark. Not only that, your arrival at the temple was delayed by several hours because of the congested traffic in New York City. I had stayed behind at the temple to finish the preparations for your arrival. When you finally arrived, you quickly strode up to the altar, bowed down, and sat on your *vyāsāsana*. Then, after a few brief exchanges with some devotees, you proceeded to sing the prayers to the disciplic succession. Finishing these, you began intoning the *maha-mantra* with your deep, rhythmic and melodious voice: Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. As we were responding to the *mantra*, you looked out at each and every person seated in the audience. By the expression on your face you appeared very worried. As your eyes met mine and you peered deep into my soul, I saw that you were very concerned about my spiritual well-being. I was wondering why you were looking at me like that when I personally felt there was nothing wrong with me.

In your lecture you ridiculed modern civilization. You mentioned how your flight had taken a few hours, but after disembarking from the airplane it had also taken several hours to get from the airport to the temple due to all the traffic. Therefore, how had the automobile brought any advancement? Instead, this so-called advancement was simply becoming a disturbance. In this way, you continuously smashed the concept of technological development as advancement of human civilization. You explained that real advancement was development of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When you finished speaking, you went up to your room. As usual, the devotees were milling around in the temple room, intoxicated with the ecstasy of seeing you again. After a few minutes, your servant came down and informed us that you wanted everyone to come up to your room one at a time. We formed a line. Due to timidity, I got as far back in the line as I could. Gradually the devotees went up to see you in your apartment.

When it was my turn, I ascended the staircase, entered the apartment, and then went into the little back room where you stayed. I offered obeisances to you and then sat in front of you. Your eyebrows were knitted as you looked at me with deep concern. Then you said, “You are feeling all right?”

I replied, “Yes, Śrīla Prabhupada.”

You responded, “Then why you are so thin?”

I replied, “Oh, I’mjust eating a little and sleeping a little so I can become like a gosvami.” At the time, many of the devotees in New York City had been eating and sleeping very little. We were trying to emulate the austerities of Satsvarūpa and Jadurāṇī in Boston. We thought that if we reduced our eating and sleeping and chanted more rounds we would automatically come to the platform of *gosvāmīs*.

Śrīla Prabhupada replied to me, “Healthy means to be as big as Brahmānanda. You should become as big as Brahmānanda. *Brahmacārī* means to eat ten *capātīs* every day. If you eat ten *capātīs* every day, you will always be Kṛṣṇa conscious. So, you can do?”

In shock, I gasped, “Yes!” Brahmānanda was sitting beside me with a big smile. I bowed down and left, realizing the foolishness of my artificial attempts to become a *gosvāmī*. It turned out that you had instructed most of the devotees to eat more *prasādam*, but I was the only one you instructed to eat ten *capātīs* every day.

The next day the two girls again dressed up as Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. This time we all assembled at the Brooklyn Botanical Gardens. We entered and walked until we reached a pleasant meadow secluded by trees and flowerbeds. You sat down upon a large pillow. Then you began leading *kīrtana*, instructing the two girls to dance in front of you. We also stood and formed a circle around you. As we danced, our attention was fixed on you, like the petals of a lotus fixed around the calyx. We hardly noticed the two girls dressed as Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. When you ended the *kīrtana,* we all sat down on the grass. We were expecting you to speak something, but instead you surprised us all. You began asking different devotees to stand up and tell us their understanding of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I was terrified that you would call on me, but you only called on the senior devotees: Rāya Rāma, Brahmānanda, Satsvarūpa, Jadurāṇī, Puruṣottama, Advaita, Madhusūdana, etc. After some more *kīrtana,* we went back to the temple. That’s all there was to that festival. You gave the dressing up like Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa no special importance.

The next evening we had our usual program of *kīrtana* and lecture. The *kīrtana* was ecstatic and in the lecture you spoke in detail how we can develop pure love of God. When you asked if there were any questions, my heart skipped a beat when I saw one guest raise his hand. After hearing from the devotees for many months about Kṛṣṇa and Vṛndāvana, he had recently gone to Vṛndāvana. There he met one of your Godbrothers. The fellow took initiation from your Godbrother, who also told the new disciple that he had a relationship with Kṛṣṇa like Mother Yaśodā. After returning to New York, this disciple was always bragging how he had a Deity of Kṛṣṇa as a baby which he worshiped with maternal affection like Mother Yaśodā. Seeing his hand raised, you signaled him to speak. He asked: “Swamiji, if I have maternal affection for Kṛṣṇa like Mother Yaśodā, can I offer Kṛṣṇa Gerber’s baby food?”

You wrinkled your brow and asked, “What is that?”

He repeated his question and you continued to look at him with a perplexed expression. Then someone explained to you what Gerber’s baby food is. You replied, “Oh, no. We cannot offer canned food or food prepared by a nondevotee.”

Then he asked, “Can I prepare my own baby food for offering to Kṛṣṇa?”

You replied, "We do not worship Kṛṣṇa in that way. We follow the authorized process for worshiping Kṛṣṇa. Not that artificially we concoct something or try to jump to an advanced position. That is the *sahajiyā* way. We simply follow the process of regulated devotional service. Then gradually we become purified. That’s all.” The tone Of Your voice revealed that you were annoyed by the question. The guest was embarrassed that you had not encouraged him in his concocted worship. But we were more embarrassed that he had asked you such a stupid question. In those days I was the cook. The next day I was busy in the kitchen. It was the quiet part of the afternoon. I was just finishing cleaning the kitchen when your servant came and said that you wanted to see me in your room immediately. I asked your servant why you wanted to see me, but he was not aware. My mind raced as I speculated that perhaps you were displeased with my service. Shaking with fear, I went to your apartment and knocked on the door. You answered: “Come in.” Entering the room, I sat on the floor in front of you. Between us was a low wooden table which you used as a desk. Then you asked, “So, you can paint?”

Hearing your question, I did not know what to think. I thought, “Paint what? Paint the table? Paint the floor? Paint the walls? Oh no! Maybe Swamiji wants me to paint the outside walls of the apartment building!” In great anxiety I blurted out, “Paint what?”

You replied, “Paint pictures, just as Jadurāṇī is doing.”

In disbelief I said, “But I don't know anything about art.”

You replied, “That doesn't matter. If youjust have faith in Kṛṣṇa, then Kṛṣṇa from within your heart will guide you just as He is guiding Jadurāṇī. So, you can do?”

Somehow or other I managed to gasp a weak but affirmative “Yes.” Then you showed me some photographs of devotees chanting in San Francisco and Los Angeles. You told me to begin trying to reproduce the photos; then when I became more proficient I could paint Kṛṣṇa. You also told me that you were planning to open so many temples and to print so many books, and therefore you needed many oil paintings. You said, “Carry on with your regular duties, but whenever you get a little time, please try to paint.” Then you told me that I could go.

Thus it was that in September of 1968 1 came to understand the difference between concocted devotional service, like what the *sahajiyās* perform, and devotional service executed under the direction of the bona fide spiritual master. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your disciple striving to be your useful servant,  
Bhaktivaidurya Mādhava Mahārāja

## Bhakti-vidyā-pūrṇa Swami

**Opportunities Lost**

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

My dear lord and master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

So many years have passed by, as have so many opportunities to glorify you on this auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā day. But unfortunately—fool that I am—I allowed each one of them to slip by. Like a bewildered child, unable to ascertain the true values of life, I would think, “I am not qualified to glorify your greatness.” But upon introspection, I find I was simply worried what others might say of my feeble attempts.

Looking back now, I understand. Māyā tricked me in yet another way. Keeping me from another opportunity to surrender. Another opportunity to serve. I did not realize the sweetness of exchange based not upon literary expertise but upon sincere appreciation of your causeless mercy and affection.

- becoming tired from playing
- the small child
- helpless
- barely able to walk
- comes forward
- slowly
- with arms raised
- hoping
- and is gently lifted up
- by the awaiting arms

Today I pray that by your mercy I will now move forward. Resolute in determination to serve you. Giving up the ignorance of lifetimes past. That from today you may be pleased to accept us as one of your own. An instrument to be engaged as you best see fit. An instrument free from fear and attachment. Ready to assist, in any way, your bold preaching of the holy name.

Today I pray that, by your mercy, this foolish wayward servant of yours will not again allow another opportunity to be lost.

Bhakti-vidya-pūrṇa Swami

## Bhakti-vighna-vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious occasion of the 100th anniversary of your appearance in this world.

I thank you for accepting me as one of your spiritual children and for giving me a place in your family and an opportunity to serve the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

“The real life of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s movement is preaching.”

“Success is his who is pushing forward the preaching method. If we think that ‘by showing a temple Deity we get some money and rice and cloth and just peacefully live here, don’t bother about going to Fiji and all over. . . .’ That much success they have got. But that is not Caitanya Mahāprabhu.” (*A Transcendental Diary,* Fiji, May 2, 1976)

As you took the trouble to go to remote corners of the world and preach, so we are following in your footsteps and continuing your mission. You knew our Western proclivities, but still you wanted to give us a chance to offer some service to Kṛṣṇa.

“He said that his Godbrothers and every *sannyāsī* in India criticized him for giving *brāhmaṇa* and *sannyāsa* initiation and for installing Deities in the West, what to speak of allowing women to live in the temples.” (*A Transcendental Diary,* Honolulu, May 17, 1976)

You have limitless faith in the process of devotional service and the power of the holy name to uplift the most fallen souls.

*Pāpī tāpī yata chila, hari-nāme uddhārila:* “The holy name delivers those who are sinful and wretched.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given me so much, and I am giving so little in return. I am thankful for this occasion and the opportunity it gives for more hearing and chanting of your transcendental glories.

I pray that I may always remain part of your spiritual family.

Your most fallen son,  
Bhakti-vighna-vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

## Bhakti-vikāśa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wanted your *sannyāsīs* to do big things—

“Make more disciples than me.  
Write more books than me.  
Open more temples than me.”

To fulfill even one of these dictums seems for me almost impossible, but the challenge is relishable. I don’t want to do anything cheaply, in an imitative way, but I do want to please you.

The preaching field is open. There are hundreds and thousands of towns and villages throughout the world, and Lord Caitanya wants His name chanted in every one of them. Millions of people are eager to know about Kṛṣṇa, only they don’t know it yet. They have still to be woken up by your *saṅkīrtana* soldiers, who go to every human habitation on the planet bringing your books, your message, your mercy.

You said people would come to us in the millions. They will have to be trained, protected, guided, and nourished. You have given us many lifetimes of work, but time is short and our strength limited.

Therefore, on this most auspicious occasion of your Centennial Vyāsa-pūjā, I am falling at your lotus feet and begging for the mercy to dive deeply into the ocean of preaching ecstasy. May my efforts to serve you expand geometrically every year. Let me do something significant in your great mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every home and every heart.

*ekākī āmāra nāhi pāya bolo*  
*harināma-saṅkīrtane*  
*tumi kṛpā kari śraddhā-bindu diya*  
*deha kṛṣṇa-nāma dhane*

**“I do not find the strength to carry on alone the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **of the holy name of Hari. Please bless me by giving me just one drop of faith with which to obtain the great treasure of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa."**

Your eternal servant,  
Bhakti-vikāśa Swami

## Candramauli Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble prostrated obeisances at the dust of your beautiful lotus feet. Please allow me, on this most auspicious Centennial anniversary of your divine appearance in the world, to offer a few words of glorification, although I am very much unqualified.

Dear Gurudeva, while attempting to write this offering to you, I’m unable to find the words to express my feelings. As I examine myself further, I can understand this is due only to the fact that I actually have no devotion. My heart is like a barren desert, devoid of any real spiritual sentiments or genuine appreciation.

Therefore, I can only pray in utter desperation, “*Guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya*.” My only hope of becoming your disciple is to constantly listen to your precious words, which can push back the coverings of passion and ignorance that lay so heavily on my heart and mind. Then perhaps I may, by your loving kindness, be able to offer some small service that is pleasing to you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in March 1975 you were giving a Sunday Feast lecture at the Atlanta Temple. At that time a devotee asked you the question “What pleases you most, Śrīla Prabhupāda?” Your answer was “If you love Kṛṣṇa.” What a beautiful answer. The sum total of the spiritual master’s mission is to awaken the natural love for Kṛṣṇa that is in the heart of each and every living entity.

And you so mercifully gave us the formula. You said, “Out of all my instructions to my disciples, my most important one is to every day chant sixteen rounds of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* on beads.” But again, when I examine myself I must honestly conclude that I have no attraction for Kṛṣṇa’s name.

But I am hopeful that one day, by your grace and unlimited kindness upon this useless person, I may develop a slight desire to seriously, sincerely chant Kṛṣṇa’s name with feeling.

But then I realize that in order to develop some attraction for the holy name I must place myself in the position of being the humble servant of the devotees. I am filled with volumes of false pride and always attacked by lust; to become *tṛṇād api su-nīcena taror iva sahiṣṇunā amāninā māna-dena* seems impossible for me. All my activities and words are motivated by the desire for name and fame. My case seems hopeless. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help me open up to your causeless mercy and accept with a humble and grateful heart all that you have given and are presently giving.

The priceless gem of love of God which you are so freely distributing is truly a “matchless gift.” We can never really understand its true value, nor can we ever reciprocate this gift.

In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.22.48, you explain that the word *vinoda-patram* mean “joker.” “A joker’s activities simply arouse laughter, and a person who tries to repay the spiritual master or teacher of the transcendental message of Kṛṣṇa becomes a laughingstock just like a joker because it is not possible to repay such a debt.” So what can one do as your disciple that will make one worthy to be called your disciple? You end the purport with the statement “The best friend and benefactor of all people is one who awakens humanity to its original Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

One who makes the mission of the spiritual master one’s life and soul becomes dear to Kṛṣṇa and is a true disciple. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am so unqualified and always lazy, but I pray for your causeless mercy upon this useless soul, and perhaps one day I’ll be able to offer you my life totally at your lotus feet to do with as you please.

Begging to become the servant of your servants,  
Candramauli Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this Vyāsa-pūjā day I would like to thank you for your one moment’s association. I want to thank you for your *lava-mātra*. That instant began my spiritual life in San Francisco at Ratha-yātrā. I didn’t know who you were at that time, but somehow here I am twenty years later. It is amazing to look back on my life and recognize that for so many years I was headed in one direction—the direction of selfishness and self-destruction. I had heard of spiritual life, I had met devotees, but I felt certain that I could make spiritual progress on my own. Why should I need a teacher? Why should I believe that illicit sex must be given up to gain transcendent knowledge? Why should I follow some ancient scripture? Had humankind not advanced? What was the need for the gloomy prohibitions of the past? Freedom was the big word of the 70’s. We must be free, we must explore, we must challenge old ways of thinking. What *is* reality, anyway? Who can answer? I was alive with brave new worlds and brave new words. Still, in those quiet moments alone, which came often, I was forced to admit that my life felt like a shambles. There was something false there, something superficial. Something painful.

As the years had passed, the mood of my search to discover the missing element had gone from yearning to desperation. I was becoming desperate. There had been many micro-philosophies that I had heard and tried. Some were the specific themes of the 70’s, and others were more general: Tune in, turn on, drop out; free love; if it feels good, do it; the universe is going nowhere and therefore having no problem getting there; everything valuable is learned through pain—get a cinder in you eye; be here now; go with the flow; the truth can’t be spoken; everything is relative; become one. All these fragments I would resort to when I needed a philosophical quick fix to get me through a dark few hours of a meaningless life. They were within my easy grasp, like metaphysical spices for cooking some metaphysical preparation. Mental concoction. But I began to notice as time passed that they were losing their potency to relieve me. It was as if I’d left the lids off the jars and they were drying out and becoming flavorless. The senselessness, loneliness, and pain were intensifying, and my grab bag of catch phrases were all bleaching in the sun.

Many times I had assured myself, “Oh, now my life is going to be different; today I have a new realization; in the future I will look back on this day as a turning point.”

I would think that way, and the future would reveal the same old same old. Strangely, on the day when my life actually changed, I took no notice—nothing seemed special or intense.

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

This material world is such a dark forest, a forest of promised material pleasures and delivered material suffering. Some are quick and extreme, and some slow, tedious, and grinding. Sufferings abound everywhere, on every street corner, in great calamities and in painful quiet minutes in isolation. You have released me. Though your personal association was brief, your association of instruction has been long-standing. In preaching work it is easy to see myself in the same predicaments as the people I’m speaking to. These same catch phrases or their descendants are still ringing in our ears. I have been released, and I have a duty to attempt to release others. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help that desire grow. Help me endure whatever it takes to become empowered. There is nothing else to do. There is no other goal. Let me become just one more of the many who are part of your *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Your servant,  
Candraśekhara Swami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Your Divine Grace, it is 1996, and the flags outside the United Nations building are still increasing! Today, as before, you are the only peacemaker who can effect actual change in this world of strife and slaughter.

Even the ordinary peace achieved by animals is now but a utopian dream for mankind. The animal kingdom boasts species that can easily live together without attacking one another other. Yet the pack of humans currently inhabiting the globe cannot attain even this most rudimentary, bestial freedom from hostilities.

Noble peace workers implore us, “Just try to visualize: what if world peace suddenly broke out!” They hope that this exercise in creative thinking will help “free up” everyone’s latent capacity for brotherly love.

You taught us never to accept this saccharin—or any other. Real human life, you lucidly explained, is meant for more than just the cessation of societal strife. Cows and swans, for example, automatically live that kind of peace. Humans, however, by design, have the intelligence to choose real peace—a dynamic spiritual existence far beyond mere animal niceties.

With the advanced manifestation of consciousness the human body provides, we can settle up with Godhead—we can end our insurrection against Kṛṣṇa. Then we can taste that peace meant for human beings: the replacing of temporary, material sense objects with eternal, spiritual sense objects.

You have covered the globe with opportunities for this divine sensory activity. Your world-wide Kṛṣṇa consciousness network is a mechanism for the mass distribution of spiritual sensual experience. Indeed, you have unfurled a whole civilization of liberated sentience, far from the maelstrom of madness that purports to be human society. As you once said, “Don’t mind me saying so, but this Kṛṣṇa consciousness is perhaps the first attempt at making these people into human beings.”

Material resources are always limited. Material relationships prove always deficient. “If you’re a human being you must be pessimistic about material life!” you thundered, the first time I saw you. I sat before you, with my materialistic fiancīe anxiously breathing down my back. You continued to roar, “If you are optimistic about material life, you’re an animal!” Out the temple door she ran—and out of my life. “But in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, everything is always optimistic!” you concluded with a smile.

Amazingly, your books are able to convince readers that even if one does become a human by understanding the futility of material progress, still there is no great cause to rejoice. Like lifelong substance abusers that are both physically and psychologically addicted, conditioned souls can never restrain themselves. Without Hare Kṛṣṇa sense objects, they have no other choice: they must squeeze the dry rag of promissory materialism.

But the pursuit of material happiness automatically accrues greed, envy, anger, conflict, and distress—even in just the planning stage. Therefore the conclusion is that without the spiritual sensory gifts of Your Divine Grace, there must be war and disaster.

Trying to be an unconditional servant,  
Devāmṛta Swami

## Dhanurdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

A pure devotee does what Kṛṣṇa wants.

Kṛṣṇa’s desire (what He wants) is manifested through his devotees in disciplic succession. A pure devotee thus lovingly dedicates his every act, word, and thought to fulfill the orders (desires) of his spiritual master. The spiritual master’s orders represent the wishes of *his*  spiritual master, desires that, in *paramparā,* ultimately originate from the heart of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. The life of a surrendered soul is thus a source of pleasure to the Lord, for the Lord becomes joyful when His heartfelt desires are fulfilled.

And what are the desires of Kṛṣṇa? Śrīla Prabhupāda, you state in your own words, “Everyone should offer obeisances, from moment to moment, to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. These are the desires of the Supreme Lord, and one who fulfills His desires favorably is actually a pure devotee. Kṛṣṇa wants everyone to surrender unto Him, and devotional service means preaching this gospel all over the world.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you did what Kṛṣṇa wants. You took your spiritual master’s order as your life and soul. By your mercy the gospel of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu spread all over the world. How joyful Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara must be to drink the *rasa* of your intense devotion to expand their service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to become an object of your pleasure in my deeds, words, and devotion by selflessly serving your mission and chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* purely.

Praying to be of some pleasure to you one day by your mercy only, I remain  
Your servant,  
Dhanurdhara Swami

## Dhanvantari Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my humble obeisances.

How wonderful are the divine qualities you revealed to the world when personally guiding your society!

How wonderful are the divine qualities you revealed to the world when translating, commenting upon, publishing, and distributing transcendental literature!

How wonderful are the divine qualities you revealed to the world when personally orienting your disciples and preaching to guests and the public!

How wonderful are the divine qualities you revealed to the world when doing the most “ordinary” things like walking, speaking, smiling, playing instruments, singing, chanting, and taking *prasādam*!

All your activities were a wonderful demonstration of divine qualities.

How could it be possible for me, a conditioned soul, to glorify such a transcendental, wonderful person? Let me serve your lotus feet and absorb my mind in your inconceivable activities.

Your eternal servant,  
Dhanvantari Swami

## Dyutidhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Thank you for coming to save the fallen souls in this lonely Age of Kali. Your Divine Grace has released us innumerable spirit souls from Māyā’s grasp. Kṛṣṇa is yours, and you freely have the power to give Him.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Your very personality is a transcendental invitation drawing us back home, back to Godhead. All your achievements and influence attest to your being Kṛṣṇa’s pure, unalloyed devotee.

You have untiringly preached Kṛṣṇa’s glories all over the world and firmly established the *bhakti* cult of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Is there anyone more merciful than your very self? Your attributes and qualities are all superexcellent and sublime. You are truly the *āśraya* or shelter for the fallen souls of this age. To have known of you and existed in your presence is certainly Kṛṣṇa’s mercy.

On this auspicious day I would like to pray for your continued help and blessings. I know by your mercy you can implant the seed of pure *bhakti* in my heart. Please allow me a place in serving your great mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. By your example we can understand the meaning of pure, unalloyed devotional service.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you again and again for all your kindness.

Your servant,  
Dyutidhara Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

**The Real Person**

So many young minds  
brimming with sincerity,  
independent, quick-witted,  
self-spirited and free,  
attracted to one  
transcendent personality—  
His Divine grace  
A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami.

There’s been many leaders with  
followers aplenty,  
blind leading blind—  
“*śva-viḍ-varāha*” quality.  
You requested only one moon  
to light the sky of Kali,  
but your shine is so bright—  
reflected hundreds like thee.

Who else could have arrayed  
such a formidable army—  
scientists, philosophers,  
reformers of society?  
Equipped with Gaura’s *śakti*—  
*saṁvit* and *hlādinī,*  
spreading your message  
whole world community.

Each one of these men—  
strong willed and certain  
their own way of preaching,  
*prasādam,* and *kīrtan*  
is destined to conquer  
the illusory energy.  
But stern warning echoes—  
“Cooperative synergy!”

Democratic GBC—  
your sign of ingenuity.  
Not one man intelligent  
like combined group of thirty.  
How is this possible  
in Kali’s home court?  
“Your love for me shows”—  
or mission abort.

What was it they saw,  
their lives at your feet?—  
Heart full of compassion,  
surrender complete.  
Your only intention—  
*para-duḥkha-duḥkhī,*  
melts stone-like hearts,  
turned hippy to *sukhi*.

I can’t begin to fathom  
what magic you wove.  
Most powerful potion—  
that of Divine Love.  
We were all looking for it,  
unlimited reservoir.  
Hare, Kṛṣṇa and Rāma—  
priceless gems for the poor.

Your only possession—  
faith in Holy Name.  
Reciting *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*  
purifies the same.  
Forty rupees in pocket,  
a sack of dry grains.  
Though appearing all alone,  
Pārtha-sārathi at the reins.

Through illness and thefts,  
you tolerated all.  
Intent on Guru’s order,  
“Hearten clarion call.”  
*Yukta-vairāgya* exemplified,  
embracing one and all.  
You built the house for us to live,  
we decorate the walls.

Prime example of utility  
you’re executing now.  
Though nineteen years ago you left,  
still tell us why and how  
to celebrate Centennial  
as if the date were true (for a *nitya-siddha*),  
but you’ve encouraged us to do,  
and thus remember you.

And all you did you ascertained,  
by mercy of Guru.  
You said you had no attribute—  
obviously not true.  
Śrī Cakravartī Viśvanāth,  
his teaching held the clue—  
“Why worry what the future holds,  
if Master’s pleased with you?”

In a letter you wrote  
that a little sincerity  
is a difficult thing  
in the age of hypocrisy.  
But all these earnest souls  
assembled around you  
substantiates the person  
most genuine and true.

No pretense, no facades,  
no fancy gimmetry.  
Though descended from Goloka,  
no “holier” tendency.  
Just what you get  
is what you see—  
a hundred percent pure devotee.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You are the most real and genuine person. Please help me to be like you. Please help me be fixed as your servant, the servant of your servant, and the servant of all those I meet who will one day become your servant.

Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear most Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Whenever I’ve written my offering to you on this most holy of days, I’ve always risen earlier than usual to take full advantage of the still morning hours. Today, however, I am inspired to write amidst the multitudes, the noise, the commotion. Why the change? Because I am in the holy *dhāma* of Māyāpur, where daylight brings not passion and distraction but the unfolding of the spiritual realm in all of its beauty. The only noise is the constant chanting of the holy name, and the only activities are those of the devotees absorbed in pure devotional service at your lotus feet.

And by far the busiest place of all in this holy *dhāma* is your most worshipable *samādhi,* which reaches up to the heavens and attracts thousands daily to come and pay you homage. And that is where I now sit, praying to offer you some humble glorification on this most auspicious day.

As I try to find the proper words to praise you, a young Indian man in his twenties approaches me and looks over my shoulder to see what I am writing. I feel a little inconvenienced by his intrusion, but then I remember that I did not come here for solitude. I look up at him and smile. He smiles back and asks what I am doing.

“I’m trying to write something glorifying my spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

“Yes!” He says, enthusiastically, his smile broadening.

“Where are you from?” I ask.

“I am from Calcutta,” He says.

I tell him Śrīla Prabhupāda was born in Calcutta.

“Yes,” he beams, “and for that I am very proud.. I am a religious man, and I am very happy to be here now. It is a splendid place. As soon as I come here the mind changes to a religious mind.”

This man had a mustache, was not wearing a *dhotī* or *tilaka,* and did not have *japa* beads or *kaṇṭhī-mālā*. At first he had appeared to me to be just another ordinary face in the crowd, one face out of the billions in this world. But it quickly became apparent that this man’s devotion to you had been blossoming in a very special way. Who can imagine how many more seemingly ordinary faces in the crowd are bound up in affection for you? And how many countless more will you continue to bind as you preach worldwide through your devotees, and as thousands come here daily to your *samādhi* and feel your transcendental presence as you sit there so nobly in your deity form, as you display your *līlā* on the balcony above, and as your devotees melt everyone’s heart narrating your glories. By your desire, the Lord’s mission that every last soul be delivered, regardless of birth, race, caste, color, or even species, is being fulfilled.

As I continue writing, another man approaches me and asks the cost of building the *samādhi*. I tell him I don’t know. He offers *praṇāmas* and walks away. Afterwards I think, “Whatever the cost, who can put a value on such a priceless touchstone, which, by its grandeur and beauty is attracting thousands daily, and where, by your spiritual presence, you will purify and inspire all who come to see you for the next ten thousand years? Let all the gold in the world be used to erect innumerable such *samādhis* and attract the whole world so that you can transform this hellish planet into Vaikuṇṭha!

Next comes a man visiting from a distant city with his family. “Faith,” he says, after we talk for some time. “You are telling me so many things about your spiritual master, but unless I have faith in you I will not believe.

“That is true, “ I say, praying that I can somehow glorify you so that his heart will be touched.

“Well, then, should I see the guided tour of the *samādhi*?” he asks a little later. I tell him I highly recommend it. He says, “Then I will go. Because you are telling me, I will go.”

Prabhupāda, the faith of these conditioned souls is very tender. How many of them have I failed to inspire or even turned away from your lotus feet because of my neophyte behavior? We devotees are the only link that the world’s masses have to you and Lord Kṛṣṇa. If we want everyone to come to your temples, to purchase and read your books, to give their money in your service, to render *seva* within your society, they must have faith in us. You have given us the key to unlock the door to their hearts. If we can faithfully follow you, they will faithfully follow us.

One man enthusiastically walks up to me and shares with me his new discovery: “When I came here and sat down I forgot about everything. I feel like I am in the heavenly world.” That is the effect of your transcendental presence, Śrīla Prabhupāda. As Yudhiṣṭhira told Vidura, “My lord, devotees like your good self are verily holy places personified. Because you carry the Personality of Godhead within your heart, you turn all places into places of pilgrimage.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.13.10) True, this Māyāpur *dhāma* is already Vaikuṇṭha, but you have even further sanctified it because you are the personification of the fulfillment of the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. And when you preached in the bowels of New York, one of the world’s most degraded cities, you transformed even that hellish place into a *tīrtha*. All those who came under the spell of your transcendental influence indeed “forgot about everything” and were thus lifted far beyond all the noise, the filth, and the degradation that surrounded them. And that was the case wherever you went. Thus because of your momentous journeys traversing this previously barren and unfortunate planet, this world is now resplendent with so many holy places of pilgrimage.

I’m sure the longer I sit in this inspired atmosphere of your *samādhi* the more voluminous will be my words of glorification, for each and every occurrence in this very special place is a unique tribute to your unparalleled greatness. As your present and soon-to-be followers pass through the magnificent entranceway, you are sitting there regally in your deity form, waiting to welcome us all. During your lifetime you traveled everywhere to seek us out. Now people are coming from everywhere to seek you out. And your loving, transcendental presence touches their hearts.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you will be increasingly glorified for thousands of years by the multitudes as you shower your mercy upon them. Your magnificent centennial celebration is only the beginning. Who can envision the magnitude of your bicentennial, your quincentennial, and your millennial and ten-thousandth-year celebrations? And whatever the degree of pomp, to those who love you it will never seem enough to sufficiently glorify you. Your Divine Grace is the empowered representative of Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda, the captain of everyone’s ship, guiding all living entities through the dark, rough seas of Kali-yuga to the lotus feet of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories unto you.

Your humble, insignificant servant,  
Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

## Hanumatpreṣaka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda is so great that it is not possible for an ordinary person like myself to glorify him or illuminate him. I simply offer whatever little realization that I have and hope that Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and Lord Kṛṣṇa, who are so great, can make some worth of the contribution. The devotees are my shelter, and I hope that I can continue in the service of Śrīla Prabhupāda in their association.

All I can really say is that Śrīla Prabhupāda simply presents the highest truth as it is, without change. That truth liberates us from all the traps of the material world that would take us to our death. Śrīla Prabhupāda gives us the perspective on every problem, and that perspective allows us to go forward to the highest goal. We can go forward, and we can take anyone else with us. We simply have to chant our rounds and follow the principles that he has given us. His books are an ocean of mercy. Everything can be found within his books. May we just go on following the principles and reading his books and inspiring others to read his books. Nothing more needs to be done. Then this material world, which has been converted into a hell unfit for life, will all return to life, and every rock and every plant and every blade of grass will return to spiritual life and the whole world will become spiritual.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!  
Vṛndāvana-dhāma *kī jaya*!  
Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī *kī jaya*!  
Gokulānanda-candra Śrī Kṛṣṇa *kī jaya*!  
All the many *bhaktas* of Śrī Śrī Caitanya-candrodaya Lord Caitanya *kī jaya*!

Hanumatpreṣaka Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

On this auspicious day I am feeling so much separation from you, my beloved lord and master.

You once said that from the transcendental point of view there is no difference between the appearance and disappearance of a devotee. You gave the example of the sun, which while setting on the horizon is appearing somewhere else at the same time. Like the setting sun, you disappeared from our vision while simultaneously appearing in the transcendental abode, where you rejoined your associates in their eternal service to the Lord. How they must have rejoiced that day, and what festivities there must have been!

Yet those of us you left behind saw only the darkness of your absence. But as the sun is always reappearing, destroying darkness and giving light, so you too have continued to reappear in our lives, giving us hope and inspiration. Sometimes you appear in a manner which is most obvious, such as your books and your instructions, while at other times you guide us in most uncommon ways.

I realize this today, as I write to you from the city of Sarajevo, in Bosnia. The recent war here has stopped for now, but the streets are still stained with blood and the people are weary of fighting. I came to this tragic scene as your representative—but in reality I think you sent me to witness the glories of your devotees who preached here throughout the war. Just as Lord Kṛṣṇa sent Uddhava to Vṛndāvana to behold the selfless love of the *gopīs,* so you directed me to hell to see firsthand the surrendered devotees who are fearless in executing your instructions.

This small group of devotees, armed only with faith in *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, distributed books and *prasādam* throughout the four-year war, often at great risk to their lives. With less than the basic necessities of life, they carried on your mission of delivering fallen souls, unaffected by the horrors of war around them. Having no running water in the temple building, they walked three kilometers each day to fetch it. Because there was no electricity in the city, they used the light of candles. And as the local population gradually cut down each and every tree in the city to use as fuel for heating and cooking, the devotees dug up the stumps of those trees to heat their own temple. For *bhoga* they begged from door to door and depended on different relief organizations.

Cāṇakya Paṇḍita says that one should not stay in a place where there is not a clean flowing river, a qualified physician, pious *brāhmaṇas,* and a righteous king, and where people don’t respect you. I can promise you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Sarajevo meets all the above criteria of a place to be avoided. Despite this, the devotees had a valuable jewel that far out shines anything of value in this world—something that even Cāṇakya Paṇḍita did not know about—they had love for and faith in you. Because of this love and faith they stayed in this city, which is so full of vengeance and hate, even though they once had the opportunity to escape. The nature of real love is that it is always shared with others and never kept for oneself.

Despite seemingly impossible circumstances, they went out onto the streets of Sarajevo every day of the war—sometimes under the rain of mortar fire, shrapnel, and machine-gun bullets, sometimes having to step over charred dead bodies to distribute your love in the form of your transcendental books and *prasādam*. While everyone was struggling simply to stay alive, your devotees were struggling to help others—they were struggling to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

What are the use of my realizations and prayers, which are the wishful thinking of a day-dreaming child? In comparison with the real surrender of these devotees, anything I say is like the difference between someone who says he wants to do something and someone who actually does it.

I wanted to tell you about them Śrīla Prabhupāda. I know that you already know, but I wanted to tell you again because I am sure you would be very pleased to hear their glories chanted, just as Kṛṣṇa, upon hearing the glories of His devotees from the mouth of Uddhava, said:

kṛpālur akṛta-drohas titikṣuḥ sarva-dehinām  
satya-sāro ’navadyātmā samaḥ sarvopakārakaḥ

“O Uddhava, a saintly person is merciful and never injures others. Even if others are aggressive he is tolerant and forgiving toward all living entities. His strength and meaning in life come from the truth itself, he is free from all envy and jealousy, and his mind is equal in material happiness and distress. Thus, he dedicates his time to work for the welfare of all others.”

You once said that you would take upon your head the dust of the feet of any devotee who preached the holy names of Kṛṣṇa in the lands of Islam. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are my master, the most intelligent person I know. Why would you wear foot dust upon your glorious head? Because I know that you do only what is best, I have concluded that this foot dust must be something very valuable. It is therefore my earnest desire to have the dust of the feet of the Sarajevo devotees upon my head. Then one day I may be able to serve you with the same unshakable love and determination as they have.

Your eternal servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jagadīśa Goswami

**Making My Way to Your Side**

Making my way to your side,  
Giving up all I would hide,  
And thinking to stay there always.

Please give me something to do,  
Teach me to love Them as you,  
And pardon me for the delays.

Thank you.

Your servant,  
Jagadīśa Goswami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Every night, thinking, thinking. Everyone else sleeping, and Śrīla Prabhupāda is alone, light on, middle of the night, thinking, thinking. *Bhāgavatam*. *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. *Nectar of Devotion*. … “How to say it? How to say it so they’ll understand?” Thinking, thinking. Taking dictation from Kṛṣṇa within: “Do it like this.” Endless meditation and service. Rādhā-Dāmodara, New York—Vṛndāvana heaven, Western hell. Books, books. “If you every get money … print books.” The spiritual master’s order, the disciple’s heart and soul. “Print books, print books.” Print and distribute—preach, preach, preach, preach, preach.

I bow down at the lotus feet of my spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

His spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, ordered him to preach the message of Kṛṣṇa and Caitanya Mahāprabhu in English, and Śrīla Prabhupāda took this order to heart and followed it. By the blessings of his spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa, his efforts brought success—books, devotees, temples, a worldwide Hare Kṛṣṇa *saṅkīrtana* movement. And Śrīla Prabhupāda kindly engaged us, his disciples and followers, in assisting him and pushing forward what he had begun.

May we always take Śrīla Prabhupāda’s orders to heart and be useful to him, strictly following him, in his service to his spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa. And in so doing may we spread to others the good fortune of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that Śrīla Prabhupāda has given to us.

May the books, devotees, temples, and Hare Kṛṣṇa *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrīla Prabhupāda strongly and ever-increasingly spread throughout the world, reviving everywhere pure devotional service in love of Kṛṣṇa.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Krishna dāsa Swami

Worshipful Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace’s Centennial celebrations!

*yadā yadā hi dharmasya glānir bhavati bhārata*  
*abhyutthānam adharmasya tadātmānaṁ sṛjāmy aham*

**“Whenever and wherever there is a decline in religious practice, O descendant of Bharata, and a predominant rise of irreligion—at that time I descend Myself.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **4.7)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the above promise was given by Śrī Kṛṣṇa to Śrī Arjuna. It is said that sometimes Śrī Kṛṣṇa, instead of descending personally, sends his representative to reestablish the principles of religion.
