Worshipful Śrīla Prabhupāda, the significance of your appearance is being realized more and more as time passes on.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to this world to introduce the four famous Vedic principles in the West, i.e., abstention from meat-eating, from alcohol and drug consumption, from illicit relationships, and finally from gambling. In the early years, only a few people took up your message seriously, while the majority ignored your kind warnings. After your departure, various irregular events around the world have shown the harsh reality of creating and living in a godless society. Increasingly the medical authorities are advocating vegetarianism, and those who did not listen to your are listening now to their doctors and are giving up meat. Similarly, due to medical reasons and tougher traffic laws, many people are giving up their consumption of alcohol. Smoking of cigarettes has also been banned in many public places.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you preached “No gambling,” and yet the world has not realized the evil of gambling. The governments are exploiting the greed element in humans and are organizing national lotteries rewarding incredible prize moneys. Every week millions of people gamble their hard-earned money, encouraged by the governments. Commercial industry is using gambling techniques to lure customers and sell products. Instead of families talking about God, they are daily talking about winning he lottery. What a waste of the human birth! Soon the downside of gambling will show its ugly face, and we hope the world will reject gambling.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you preached against illicit relationships and divorce. I am sorry to confirm that divorces are still on the increase. Married couples fail to keep their vows of chastity and to remain faithful to each other for life, with the result that the children suffer. People should listen to your advice that marriage means doubling one’s service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, not halving it.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you promised us that we could make our lives sublime simply by chanting the holy names of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Your disciples took this instruction to heart and, under your directions, spread the chanting of the holy names, especially the *mahā-mantra,* all over the world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you yourself went around the world many times to give humanity the greatest gift, i.e., the process of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*. It is said in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.8.4), *mahad-vicalanaṁ nṝṇāṁ gṛhiṇāṁ dīna-cetasām:* “O my lord, O great devotee, persons like you move from one place to another not for their own interests but for the sake of poor-hearted *gṛhasthas* \[householders\].”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for being a wonderful example of a well-wisher for the whole world. I am trying to follow in your footsteps by preaching world-wide, but due to ill health my preaching work suffers at times. However, when I think how you laid the foundations of ISKCON at the age of seventy, I become more determined to also continue preaching world-wide.

When I first visited Mauritius, I was told that you had predicted Mauritius would become the first Kṛṣṇa conscious country in the world. I believe that this is going to be true. Seeing the spiritual mood of the authorities there, I suggested that some of the towns and roads be renamed with transcendental names. After a certain amount of pressure, the local authorities changed one of the town’s names to Vṛndāvana, followed by a change of names of other towns also. This event brought a lot of joy to those Bhāratia people whose ancestors had settled in Mauritius hundreds of years ago but who, for generations, had not had a chance to visit Bhārat. So by giving holy names to the towns, Bhārat came to Mauritius, all by your mercy!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in my prayers to Lord Kṛṣṇa, I always request that I remain His devotee. It is enjoined by Lord Kṛṣṇa in the *Ādi Purāṇa,*

*ye me bhakta-janāḥ pārtha na me bhaktāś ca te janāḥ*  
*mad-bhaktānāṁ ca ye bhaktās te me bhakta-tamā matāḥ*

**“My dear Pārtha, those who claim they are My devotees are not My devotees, but those who claim to be devotees of My devotees are actually My devotees.”**

Therefore, on this Centennial anniversary of your appearance, I want to rededicate my life in your service and claim that I am your devotee. For us, you are the thirteenth *mahājana*. We want only to follow in your footsteps. We strongly believe that whatever benedictions there are to be had in Kṛṣṇa consciousness will come to us simply by following your transcendental instructions outlined in your beautiful books.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are all missing you, but the Centennial year has made us feel your presence and has made us realize how much you have done for us. You have given us your transcendental books, Gaura-Nitāi *līlā,* Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa *līlā,* temples, *gurukulas, harināma-saṅkīrtana, sādhu-saṅga,* and *varṇāśrama-dharma.* In short, you gave us your life. We will always remain indebted to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you for giving the whole world the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your humble servant,  
Krishna dāsa Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this occasion of the 100thbirth anniversary of Your Divine Grace.

Finally, it is here: the most important event of the Centennial year. I consider myself very fortunate to be present in Calcutta, the city of your birth, on this most auspicious occasion.

This is not the only place a “celebration of a century” has been organized, however. More than five hundred ISKCON temples, farm communities, and Nāma Haṭṭa centers have been working to make this event the most memorable one. Your loving disciples and granddisciples, your congregation, your admirers and followers have gathered in unprecedented numbers. Celebrities and VIPs are here to offer their homages. And that is not everybody.

There are more than one thousand holy places here to show you appreciation by bathing you with their waters. And, above all of us, the Lord of Goloka must be here. Although invisible to our mundane vision, He would not miss such an opportunity, an occasion when His pure devotee’s glories are being chanted by everyone. The Lord says that He is present where His glories are being chanted: *yatra gāyanti mad-bhaktā tatra tiṣṭhāmi nārada*. But He likes to hear the glories of His devotees more than His own glories, so this is certainly the Lord’s chosen spot. Prabhupāda’s Āvirbhāva-tithi-mahotsava *kī jaya*!

The 100thanniversary of your birth is also the 30thanniversary of ISKCON. You once made a statement that ISKCON is your body. That body took birth in New York in 1966. There was a time you were alone, one person, though you visualized your ISKCON with devotees, temples, and books everywhere. You said that only time separates them from you. Now with only thirty years passed, your ISKCON body with its temples, devotees, and books has become a global phenomenon, with “Hare Kṛṣṇa” a household word.

Vaiṣṇavas are famous as *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī,* and this fame will increase as the preaching increases. Sometime in the past, the famous *Newsweek* magazine had an article about the growth of your movement in the Commonwealth of Independent States (CIS). The magazine described Hare Kṛṣṇa as the fastest growing religion, with more than 700,000 followers all over the CIS. This is just a humble beginning, just the foundation or groundwork on which your future movement will be built. This process of building ISKCON is to go on for 9,500 years and is to reach every town and village of the world. All glories to your International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s ISKCON *kī jaya*!

Your servant,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahā-Viṣṇu Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The books written by His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda with profound authority all reveal the Absolute Truth as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. Millions of fortunate persons formerly suffering in ignorance all over the world are at last feeling spiritual happiness and enlightenment because of these books.

Śrīla Prabhupāda spoke about the reason for his success in Vṛndāvana, on August 3, 1974:

“So our process is that you take the version of the authority, Kṛṣṇa, and you repeat it. Then your version is perfect. This is our policy. Therefore Caitanya Mahāprabhu says, “You rascal scholar, philosopher, scientist, don’t manufacture anything. *Yāre dekha tāre kaha kṛṣṇa-upadeśa.* That’s all. You become master.” *Yāre dekha tāre kaha kṛṣṇa-upadeśa*. You become master. You can teach others. Become a spiritual master. That’s all. … Whatever little success I have got—only for this reason. I have never said anything which is not spoken by Kṛṣṇa. I never said, “In my opinion.” I never said. You are so many students. I never said that to you. What is my opinion? One should know, “What is the value of my opinion? I am imperfect being.” This is called *tṛṇād api sunīcena taror api sahiṣṇunā*.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is true! It is your open secret! Although your words are sometimes so bold and chastising, still you are always more humble than the straw in the street, more tolerant than a tree!

Therefore on this Centennial Vyāsa-pūjā day of Śrīla Prabhupāda—my spiritual master, the ISKCON founder-*ācārya,* and the BBT founder-*ācārya*—I, his unworthy servant, pray for his forgiveness for my many failures and shortcomings as his aspiring servant. I pray sincerely for his mercy on me so that I can continue for the rest of my days trying to humbly follow in his footsteps as a servant of a servant of a servant.

Making this offering like a tiny lamp unto the sun, I remain  
Your humble servant,  
Mahā-Viṣṇu Swami

## Narmada Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your two lotus feet.

On the most glorious of all days, the appearance day of Your Divine Grace, I would like to offer some small words in praise of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s most dear servant. Just as it is said of Lord Śiva, *vaiṣṇavānāṁ yathā śambhuḥ*—Lord Śiva is the most glorious of all Vaiṣṇavas because he delivers the most fallen souls like ghosts and other such beings, similarly, you are also the greatest of all pure devotees because you have gloriously descended into this Kali-yuga to deliver the most wretched souls. Not only do you deliver them from their condemned, sinful ways of life, but you bestow upon them the most wonderful gift—devotional service in the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya.

By your powerful causeless mercy, this degraded world has been overflooded with transcendental literatures, temples, Deities, festivals, *prasādam,* and, most important of all, the holy names—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. You have taught that the chanting of the holy names of the Lord is the only medicine that can cure all the ills of Kali-yuga and elevate one to the platform of pure love of God.

My only hope for success in spiritual life is to be always engaged in the service of your lotus feet and to try to assist you in your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. But, as I am still so full of bad material qualities, I am begging you to please destroy all the demonic residues of material attachment that are still within my heart so that I will be a help to you in your mission and not be a burden.

One must conquer the modes of passion and ignorance by developing the mode of goodness, and then one must become detached from the mode of goodness by promoting oneself to the platform of pure goodness. All this can be automatically done if one engages in the service of the spiritual master with faith and devotion. In this way we can conquer the influences of the modes of nature.

Aspiring to be your fully surrendered servant,  
Narmada Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Each year a highlight of your Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony is hearing the devotees’ Vyāsa-pūjā homages glorifying you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. This is an opportunity to broaden my understanding and appreciation of your transcendental qualities and activities. Since I am a conditioned soul, my experience is limited, and I have not entered deeply into the profound depths of your transcendental glories. Still, because your glories are absolute and you have blessed your sincere devotees, hearing about your glories from their lips, or repeating what they have said, or remembering their insights into your divine personality, is purifying and gives me the opportunity to intimately associate with you.

When Gopīnātha Ācārya was trying to convince Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya that Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the Bhaṭṭācārya challenged him. He questioned Gopīnātha Ācārya: “What is the proof that you have received the mercy of the Lord?” Gopīnātha Ācārya replied that if one understands the transcendental potencies of the Lord, that is proof of one’s receiving the mercy of the Lord because the Lord is absolute, His potencies being nondifferent from Him. Therefore, hearing your transcendental glories sung by purified devotees is receiving your divine association.

Two prominent glories of yours are your transcendental books and the transcendental movement that you created. These were services that your rendered to please your Guru Mahārāja. You, more than any of your contemporaries, understood that success in spiritual life depends completely on becoming a surrendered servant to the spiritual master. From your own example and instructions, you emphasized to your disciples that all success, individually and collectively, can be obtained by strictly following the orders of Guru and Kṛṣṇa.

In the 1950’s before coming to the West you became struck with wonder when you read Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s commentary on the 41st verse of the second chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā:*

“The best kind of intelligence I can have is intelligence used in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Intelligence is defined as ‘fixed’ when it is intent upon my spiritual master’s instructions, such as chanting the name of Kṛṣṇa, remembering His activities, and performing service to His lotus feet. My spiritual master’s instructions are my *sādhana* and my life, both in the beginning stages of *bhakti* as well in *bhakti’s* perfectional stage. I desire only to follow his instructions; I accept nothing else as my life’s work, even in dreams. Whether I am happy or distressed, whether the material world remains or is destroyed, I don’t care. There is no loss for me. I simply must carry the orders of my spiritual master. Fixedness upon his order is determined intelligence and devotional service, and only by being fixed on his orders can such determined intelligence flourish.”

In a lecture in Los Angeles in 1975 you humbly said, “I have no personal qualification, but I simply tried to satisfy my *guru*. That’s all.” Your Guru Mahārāja instructed that if you ever got money then you should print books. Another instruction was to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the English language. Combining the instructions, you first translated and printed books in English. Then, like Hanumān’s burning Laṅkā and thus more than fulfilling the original orders of Lord Rāmacandra, you gave the monkeylike soldiers in your army the opportunity to attack Māyā’s kingdom by translating your books in different languages and distributing them around the world.

Your spiritual master understood that only a unified preaching mission could do something significant to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. To keep a unified mission, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Ṭhākura asked that his leading devotees form a GBC, with the idea of conducting the missionary activities in a cooperative spirit. Surrendered to the instructions of your spiritual master, you instituted a GBC in ISKCON and emphasized cooperation over personal achievement. Sometimes, when your leading devotees did not cooperate and thus disturbed the preaching mission, you removed them from their positions. You did not want only an official GBC body, but you wanted it to follow your instructions and example and thus set the example of cooperation in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

In your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 18.57, you express Rūpa Gosvāmī’s success formula. A devotee should always try to please Lord Kṛṣṇa by acting to fulfill His desires. What the Lord desires can be known through the disciplic succession’s instructions, which come down through the bona fide spiritual master. One who executes such Kṛṣṇa conscious service will naturally remember Lord Kṛṣṇa. It is obvious from your transcendental successes that no one was as empowered to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you. This is undoubtedly because you most perfectly understood the desires of your spiritual master and the disciplic succession and enthusiastically engaged in their service. You could thus chant the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa purely and be always absorbed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, your heart being a place of pastimes for Lord Kṛṣṇa and the Pañca-tattva. Thus as a personified place of pilgrimage (*tīrtha*), you attracted thousands of sincere souls to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Although I have not clearly understood your desires and am not strictly engaged in your service, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless me by allowing me to continue to associate with those devotees who are anxious for your mercy and who will thus help me steadily approach closer to your lotus feet.

Your aspiring servant,  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Puruṣatraya Swami

*yaḥ svānubhāvam akhila-śruti-sāram ekam*  
*adhyātma-dīpam atititīrṣatāṁ tamo ’ndham*  
*saṁsāriṇāṁ karuṇayāha śrī-kṛṣṇa-sandeśaṁ*  
*śrī-prabhupādam upayāmi jagad-guruṁ tam*

**“Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the spiritual master of the whole universe, who, out of your great compassion for those gross materialists who struggle to cross over the darkest regions of the material existence, delivered the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa after having personally assimilated the essence of all scriptures by experience.” (adapted from** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.2.3)**

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this unique and super-fortunate occasion of your Centennial day, the most auspicious day of the century, it is my sincere desire to express my immense gratitude to you for everything you have done for me. In fact, I have no ability to find suitable words to properly describe your greatness and your innumerable exalted qualities. Considering my ineptitude, I humbly beg you to kindly accept this simple but honest offering, this disclosure of what I can find in the innermost core of my heart.

By the causeless mercy of providence, I was blessed with your presence in my life, and this I regard as my greatest fortune. You have accepted me as your disciple and have given me the shelter of your holy lotus feet. O Prabhupāda, I humbly prostrate myself at your awe-inspiring lotus feet, with the whole of my self—my body, words, thoughts, intelligence, and very soul—imploring you that the shelter of your feet be eternally mine. I also beg you to protect me and guide me on the long and unpredictable path of perfection.

*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

The most certain thing in this world, besides inevitable death, is that nothing can give us factual protection, guidance, relief, hope, and inspiration other than your lotus feet. They are the real thing worth taking shelter of. The pristine light that radiates from your lotus feet is a reliable beacon that safely brings the surrendered soul to the absolutely secure harbor of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. Your feet are also comparable to a pious and all-merciful holy river that uninterruptedly flows into the endless ocean of Kṛṣṇa’s *prema-bhakti,* or to the providential rain that washes away all impurities and nourishes the creeper of *bhakti*. We may also take them as the superexcellent *sparśa-maṇi,* the touchstone that can transform a dull, mundane mentality into effulgent *śuddha-buddhi,* pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

So many qualities we can find in your lotus feet, O Prabhupāda! Actually these qualities are unlimited. Therefore, I humbly beg you again and again: O Prabhupāda, my eternal spiritual master, please be compassionate to me. Never allow me to give up the shelter of your magnanimous and benevolent lotus feet. In spite of my rebellious, unfaithful, skeptical, irreverent nature, please be merciful to me. Don’t kick me out of the shade of your lotus feet. There’s nothing except you that can inspire me and nourish my life. Without your holy association, my life would be useless and meaningless. I would wither like a plant without water. I would die miserably like a fish out of water. Please, tell me what I have to do to remain eternally under your protection. I assure you that I will do my best to deserve your blessings, and I promise to continue gradually improving the quality of my spiritual life, my behavior, my character, and my fidelity to you. I swear I will never disappoint you.

O Prabhupāda, my realization is that without your intervention my life would be just a total failure. I wonder what would have been my condition now. What would be my future, my destiny, without you in my life? What hope could I have? It’s absolutely certain that I would be irreversibly lost and embittered, tasting my total defeat, without any chance at all of any hopeful situation. As an ordinary conditioned soul, I was entangled in an intricate net of mental hallucination, seeking illusory, whimsical goals and wasting the most valuable opportunity of this human form of life. Being spiritually blind, immersed in gross ignorance, devoid of any sense of real self-esteem, I was surrounded by bad association, false friendship, and all kinds of cheating—even in the name of God. With uncontrolled senses, by the force of uncontrollable lust, I was passively allowing my consciousness to be dragged down toward the most obnoxious and dark states of existence. My condemnation was inevitable. *Māyā* was playing its macabre game with me—the more I tried to enjoy, the more I had to experience ever-growing frustration and anxiety. To counteract this agony, I was becoming more and more cynical, arrogant, hard-hearted, and self-destructive. Being completely polluted and bound by the tremendously heavy chains of impious *karma,* without any doubt I would have now been just one more among so many vampires of Kali-yuga, rotting in this endless *saṁsāra*.

But suddenly you appeared in my life like the sun, dispelling the darkness of ignorance and giving relief to my distressed heart. As a pure devotee and genuine representative of Lord Kṛṣṇa, you came to this world out of intense compassion for us, to distribute freely, without any discrimination, the most precious jewel of pure devotional service to Kṛṣṇa, as it was personally taught by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. When I examine myself, I don’t find any plausible explanation for my getting this supreme mercy. The only explanation I can find is that the Supreme Personality of Godhead eternally makes arrangements for rescuing the conditioned souls from the wheel of *saṁsāra,* and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were, still are, and will be for the next ten thousand years, an important piece in His plan for Kali-yuga. Therefore, I thank the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa for having kindly sent you to save us. Taking as mine the words of Śrī Uddhava, I pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa:

*naivopayanty apacitiṁ kavayas taveśa*  
*brahmāyuṣāpi kṛtam ṛddha-mudaḥ smarantaḥ*  
*yo ’ntar bahis tanu-bhṛtām aśubhaṁ vidhunvann*  
*ācārya-caittya-vapuṣā sva-gatiṁ vyanakti*

**“O my Lord! Transcendental poets and experts in spiritual science could not fully express their indebtedness to You, even if they were endowed with the prolonged lifetime of Brahmā, for You appear in two features—externally as the** ***ācārya*** **and internally as the Supersoul—to deliver the embodied living being by directing him how to come to You.” (*****Bhāg*****. 11.29.6)**

When you appeared in my life, what struck me most was your radical commandments called the four regulative principles. I was literally shocked by your straightforwardness. I never could imagine myself adopting such a strict style of life, but somehow I became convinced thanks to your powerful and pure words. By my accepting you, the darkness of ignorance was dispelled and the superexcellent light of transcendental knowledge manifested in my life. I at once got the most valuable and rare treasure of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This was the turning point in my life—the inexorable process of degradation, of gradual slipping down toward the infernal states of existence, suddenly was checked, like a magic trick, and I started my way up toward perfection. O Prabhupāda, you are the magician who gave me a new life and made me a new person.

You gave me the most valuable thing, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, even without my asking. Actually, as an ordinary conditioned soul, I had no idea what would be the best for me. But you, Prabhupāda, being fully concerned with the lamentable condition of the people of this age, delivered, out of compassion, the message of Lord Caitanya to everyone in this world, so much so that I had the opportunity to get your mercy even living in a remote place, far away from your presence. Now I remain close to you in your movement. And your presence, though not visible, gives me safety, encouragement, and inspiration. You saved me. You put Kṛṣṇa in my heart. You taught me how to live and how to die. You gave me everything I have now. How will I be able to repay you? How can I compensate you for giving me the ultimate benefit of life? You gave me the rarest thing one can obtain in this world. Therefore, I have this debt to you, and I want to repay you.

We learn how one can repay the debt to the spiritual master from the words of Lord Kṛṣṇa, which He spoke as He was recalling with Sudāmā Vipra the words of their *guru,* Sāndīpani Muni:

*etad eva hi sac-chiṣyaiḥ kartavyaṁ guru-niṣkṛtam*  
*yad vai viśuddha-bhāvena sarvārthātmārpaṇaṁ gurau*

**“This indeed is the duty of all true disciples (*****sat-śiṣya*****): to repay the debt to their spiritual master by offering him, with pure hearts, their wealth and even their very lives.” (*****Bhāg*****. 10.80.40)**

O Śrīla Prabhupada, I am so fallen and bewildered. I am not sure if I can be considered a *sat-śiṣya,* a true disciple. I sincerely would like to be one of your true disciples. I would like to firmly advance in spiritual life, to acquire all Vaiṣṇava qualities, to have my heart completely purified. I sincerely would like to abandon, once and for all, all material attachments and fully surrender unto your lotus feet, without any trace of personal considerations. But the stark reality is that I don’t have such determination to keep myself aloof from the influence of the modes of nature. My faith is not that strong, my mind is either agitated or distracted, my senses are voracious like tigers, and my heart is not pure. I feel myself to be a pretender, posing as a devotee but simultaneously allowing *anarthas* to remain rooted in my heart. O Prabhupāda, this is my pathetic and pitiable situation.

However, despite my weakness and disqualification, O Prabhupāda, I still have faith that you will protect me and thoroughly transform my life. Therefore I fall at your lotus feet, praying: Please, help me to free myself from all kinds of *anarthas*. Help me to purify my mind, my heart. Give me the strength to conquer the six enemies—*kāma, krodha, lobha, moha, mada,* and *matsarya,* which are constantly harassing me.

O Prabhupāda, when will the day come that I will see myself in a position worthy to be called your disciple? I am anxiously aspiring for that day. With my heart greatly pained, I also beg your forgiveness for all my offenses committed onto your lotus feet due to ignorance, negligence, or my fallen nature, either knowingly or unknowingly. With a blade of grass in my mouth, I humbly plead to you to kindly accept me as one of your true disciples. Please, don’t neglect this poor and insignificant disciple of yours. You are my eternal master and my best friend, and I depend on no one else but you. I am certain that without your mercy, I have absolutely no chance to achieve the highest goal of life.

You will force me to advance; you will give me tests for proving my sincerity to you; you will chastise me if I so deserve. You can do anything to me because you want only the best for me. Give me your special mercy so that I can experience tests and challenges in my spiritual life, because these situations will purify my heart, force me to be humble, and impel me to surrender more and more onto your lotus feet. O Prabhupāda, please endow me with the determination and faith to progress firmly toward perfection in knowledge, purity and devotion. O Prabhupāda, make me a fit candidate to deserve, in this very life, the ultimate boon of life, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s supreme gift, His superexcellent, all-auspicious and rare *prema*- *bhakti*.

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of all fallen souls, whose mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.”**

O Prabhupāda, tell me how I can repay you, how I can show my eternal gratitude to you. Tell me specifically what you want me to do to please you. You have been instructing us about all aspects of spiritual life, and you also have instructed us about this particular point:

“If you feel at all indebted to me, then you should preach vigorously like me. Of course, no one can repay the debt to the spiritual master, but the spiritual master is very much pleased by such an attitude by the disciple.”

In another instance you wrote:

“A Vaiṣṇava always feels himself incapable to repay his debt to his spiritual master; therefore he works very hard in order to try to repay that debt. He knows that without the mercy of the spiritual master one cannot preach this Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and therefore he always tries to act in such a way that he may please his *guru*.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today, on this highly significant occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I take the decision once and for all to keep these words as your supreme command to me. From now on, these words will be kept strictly and safely in my heart. From now on, I vow to dedicate my whole life—my intelligence, strength, experience, talent, in short, everything related to me—to the mission of propagating the message of Lord Caitanya. This task may take lifetimes. I don’t care. I know your intense desire to distribute the holy name of Kṛṣṇa everywhere, and I am ready to be an instrument in your hands. You have saved me from endless involvement in this *saṁsāra*. You have planted the *bhakti-latā* in my heart and given me residence in the house where all devotees can live. You have given me the instructions to live a progressive and pure life. And particularly you have given me the supreme opportunity to relate eternally to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda in loving service. For everything you have given me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am eternally indebted to you, and I feel very confident and happy.

Your aspiring sat-śiṣya,  
Puruṣatraya Swami

## Rādhā-Govinda dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Kindly accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to your divine lotus feet! All glories to your divine qualities! All glories to Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most blessed 100th anniversary of your appearance in this material world, my pleasure knows no bounds. Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa sent you here to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world and you fulfilled His desire. At our first meeting in Benares in 1971, you blessed me with the touch of your *bhakti-sadma* lotus feet. You always encouraged me to translate your books and to preach. O my eternal well-wisher, my spiritual father, your fathomless affection to me and all other souls made me surrender at your lotus feet. At Gorakphur (Gita Press), you were welcomed by Śrīpād Hanuman Prasad Poddar. I heard your confidential talks about Rādhā-Mādhava. Your affectionate order, “Stay in Vṛndāvana,” is my only solace. You gave me Vṛndāvana. This is my *prabhu-datta-deśa*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are an ocean of mercy. You are personified Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Again and again I bow down at your lotus feet. Kindly bestow your blessing so that I can serve your desire and remain fixed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I wish to remain an atom at your lotus feet. No one is able to describe your divine qualities in full. May your fame spread all over the three worlds.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant servant,  
Rādhā-Govinda dāsa Swami

## Rasānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please, accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet, which are softer than a petal of a rose and which are my only shelter.

Your lotus feet roamed all over this planet earth; therefore Mother Earth is jubilant with thoughts of your greatness. The heavy burden she is carrying is being balanced by the strength of your pure devotional service. She is proud, for she could bear the heaviness of your preaching. She cannot bear the heaviness of the untruthful Age of Kali, but she can bear with a pleasant mood the heaviness of your preaching. You toured the world fourteen times and brought a new light into the darkness of the Iron Age. Like the sun that arises in the east horizon and gradually illuminates the Western countries, your transcendental appearance and your full determination in coming to the land of the *mlecchas* is illuminating this planet with your transcendental association. What to speak of this planet—actually all the three worlds, namely the upper, middle, and lower planetary systems, are receiving the benediction of your descent.

Your appearance in this world of death is not casual or like the appearance of innumerable souls misusing their tiny independence. You came by Kṛṣṇa’s desire. You came to rescue us from this deep well of material existence. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so kind. Were it not by your kindness, I wonder how we would be liberated. One could say that Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu already has liberated all living entities in this material world with the holy names of Kṛṣṇa, but my question is, Who has accepted the task to spread the holy names all over the world? Though the battle of Kurukṣetra was already won, Lord Kṛṣṇa wanted to give Arjuna the merit of the victory. In the same way, you are so dear to the Lord that He wanted to see you spreading the holy names and be glorified with selected prayers in every nook and corner.

On this most meaningful day of your transcendental appearance, devotees all over the universe, including the demigods in heaven, are glorifying your wonderful achievements. Who would tire of hearing and chanting your exceptional qualities? Let all this material universe be saturated with your glories! Let us have only your lotus feet as objects of remembrance! When that day arrives when we will never tire of glorifying you, we will be known as pure devotees of Kṛṣṇa. Devotion to Govinda is false if devoid of an attempt to glorify and serve His pure devotee.

Your mercy is as unlimited as the waves of the ocean. Now this entire material world has become a place of pilgrimage due to your transcendental association. The planet Earth is jubilant because she now bears the imprint of your lotus feet. I would like to have thousands of mouths to be able to glorify you properly, but I am limited, and thus the power of my words are limited. I would like to be a pure servant at your lotus feet. I would like to be able to surrender all my senses in your service. I am depending completely on your mercy to enable me to glorify you efficiently, serve you skillfully, and surrender to you with all my intelligence. Please, let me remain at your shelter forever and allow me to have your *darśana* very soon.

Your eternal servant,  
Rasānanda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

From the depths of my heart I would like to offer you my feelings of profound gratitude and affection. The best way to do this is to reveal to you my personal thoughts as I sing every morning the glorious verses of the *Gurv-aṣṭakam.* Each time I sing them I dedicate them to you and get the opportunity to meditate on my tremendous fortune to be your disciple. Let me now give up everything and sing your glories for your pleasure and Kṛṣṇa’s.

1\. You are carrying the nectarean waters of Kṛṣṇa consciousness as a huge cloud carries the waters of the ocean. And you have poured your misery-dispelling and life-giving mercy-showers over the most intensely burning Western parts of the world. From that blazing inferno you rescued a conditioned soul whom you renamed Śacīnandana dāsa, and you have given this fortunate person an extra full amount of mercy, seeing that he had practically already lost all chance of survival and rejuvenation. Such are your glories.

2\. Because you personally relish the *kīrtana* of the Lord, entering into the realms of love of Kṛṣṇa, you are perfectly capable of inspiring us to chant the holy names as our first and foremost occupation. Where there is a strong electric current, the sparks jump over to even the most unlikely and unqualified material. Even wet grass bursts into flame. In this way you are expertly spreading the *yuga-dharma* even to the most unfortunate and thus fulfilling the Vedic prophecy for this age:

*kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye ’nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*

3\. A little while ago I was so fortunate to revisit your most beloved Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara. During the *darśana* I saw mental images of how you personally “obtained” Them from the hands of an Indian family who were not regularly worshiping Them. By your strong desire to bring us into direct contact with the Lord, you attracted Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa into all the main cities of the world—Paris, London, Los Angeles, New York, etc. Now Deities are blessing conditioned souls all over the world, even in many German towns. And you made it your personal responsibility to care for Them by instructing your disciples not to invent anything in the process of worship. And all these Deities attracted by you are so active in rescuing the fallen souls!

4\. One of your transcendental weapons to defeat the age of quarrel is *prasādam* distribution—an activity, like all the others you taught, that you personally demonstrated. In the early days of our German traveling *saṅkīrtana,* devotees bought mechanically prepared bread from the shops (naively hoping no bad *karma* would be transferred since machines were preparing the bread). But when you heard of it, you said that it could not really be offered to Kṛṣṇa and that instead we should take stacks of devotee-prepared chapatis with us—proper *prasādam*. Who can ever forget the taste of *prasādam* you personally cooked for Kṛṣṇa and offered to Him and then distributed to your disciples? The transcendental Lord is hungry for such preparations, and He also eagerly accepts what has been cooked and offered according to your guidelines. Such *prasādam* you have made available to the world. Such are your glories.

5\. We find that one naturally has an intense desire to hear about those one is most attached to. You never left Vṛndāvana, and you are always relishing the ecstatic news about Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava. In Śrīdhām Māyāpur I witnessed with my own eyes how you were overcome with ecstatic feelings when singing *Jaya Rādhā-Mādhava* before your lecture. The news of this world was only of interest to you when useful for preaching. Once after quickly glancing through *Time* magazine, you put it aside, saying, “Don’t read these newspapers; they make the material world appear so real.” It is so wonderful to see your example of being fixed on the transcendental level of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

6\. When I get to sing the sixth verse of the *Gurv-aṣṭakam,* it reminds me of how you are a resident of the spiritual world, engaged in very confidential service, but how you still care for me. That is how inconceivably caring and compassionate you are. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura said, “The Vrajavāsīs are truly infused with life, and therefore they preach to give life to the walking corpses of the mundane world.” You truly fit this description of a Vrajavāsī. Thank you!

7\. You are so close to Kṛṣṇa; you are the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead. You are on the same level as the Lord. This means that by becoming close to you one gains contact with the Supreme Personality of Godhead. By following your instructions with sincerity, we can meet the Supreme in your words. You carry the Lord in your heart, and from there He manifests in your books, letters, lectures, etc. You have Kṛṣṇa and you give Kṛṣṇa. This is all your mercy.

8\. The Lord has invested His mercy energy fully in you. I am a mercy case. Therefore, my connection to Śrī Kṛṣṇa is established and maintained through you. Like a helpless child, I am always dependent on your mercy. I know that this mercy is available—unconditionally—as long as I act as your dependent disciple. I wish to somehow attain the moment-to-moment awareness that only with your blessings will I be able to attain the mission of life—love of Godhead. In this year my present to you is the attempt to once and for all give you my heart—from day to day.

These are some of my meditations while singing the *Gurv-aṣṭakam* prayers. Many, many more thoughts of profound gratitude crowd into my head—too numerous to record in this offering. These thoughts have all been stimulated by you and your compassion and intense desire to save us. On this wonderful day I want to request you to kindly forgive my innumerable mistakes and to accept my humble and insignificant endeavors to please you.

All glories, all glories, all glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your humble servant,  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

It is early morning, the end of Kārttika, and the full moon is still out. I am alone, trying to surround myself with an aura of Prabhupāda consciousness.

O Prabhupāda, sometimes I have nothing to say because my heart is so dry. Those are the times when I have to turn to you, to call out to you with yearning. I know you will respond. I just pray that my crying out to you won’t be hollow and useless. I don’t want to live only in the busy-ness and agitation of my daily activities. I want to live in you. Far off I glimpse you as I might see you in a photo. You are taking your daily walk. Please allow me to catch up to you. You walk with affectionate disciples and you invite me to be among them. You said that anyone can come forward and love Kṛṣṇa. Anyone can be an intimate devotee. All the exchanges described in the *śāstra* are open to us. This is also true about our relationship with you. You will always welcome us into your entourage because we are your eternal disciples.

I am alone for a few moments, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I like to think of you in quiet places, natural places, because when the surroundings are peaceful, I am able to feel more and think better. The fact is, however, that I am rarely in peaceful places. Even if I go to the countryside, the mail comes and my thoughts never leave, people declare themselves our enemies or trucks rumble by too close for comfort. Still, by going out alone into the cool air of morning and feeling refreshed by dawn, I find myself more able to turn to you and ask you for the strength to serve.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you underwent so many difficulties to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. One morning you were walking in Dallas and you admitted that you felt ill. That morning you were supposed to fly to New Orleans. The devotees suggested that maybe you should cancel your flight. You thought about it for awhile, but then said you would go. You did not decide this based on what was best for your health and age, but on what was best for your preaching. It was not possible to travel to all the places where we needed you, even though you traveled full-time. Therefore, you did not stop when you felt ill in Dallas, but pushed on. And the illness? It passed or didn’t pass, but you remained determined and Kṛṣṇa gave you more and more opportunities to encourage your devotees.

I don’t want to be something other than a disciple. I don’t want to be just a connoisseur of memories. I want to remember you with devotion the way Kṛṣṇa and Sudāmā remembered their spiritual master. They remembered him through the service they performed for him and through the blessings he gave them.

Prabhupāda, you were concerned only with how to benefit the people and please your spiritual master. I want these concerns to become my emblem in devotional service, although I know I can never measure up to your standard. You once sat with us on the same level, eating the same meal we ate. You quietly delivered us from impersonalism and voidism and protected us from the dangers of the material world. Every day of your life with us was insecure. At the beginning, you had no fixed income and you appeared to be old and in ill health. Yet you did not take your life as full of calamity. Rather, you depended on Kṛṣṇa. You remained brave and graceful, like a lotus in a pond. We looked to you for hope, and our own insecure lives in Manhattan became less dangerous under your shelter. I pray to always remain under your shelter, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The Supersoul never leaves the living entity’s heart, even when the living entity forgets Him. Similarly, Prabhupāda, you never leave my heart even when I fail. I am confident that you accept me and that you know that wherever I am, I am dedicated to you. I have no cause in this world except your cause. Although I may not realize Lord Caitanya’s mission deeply, I know it is my mission too, by your grace. Lord Caitanya’s tree is vast and out-reaching. I am firmly situated on the A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami branch. I am one of yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servant,  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Subhāgha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

We are most fortunate that you have saved us from sinful life. I am a fool, a fallen soul. As our ever well-wisher, you want to guide us, but we don’t like to surrender completely. It’s a challenge, it’s a test. Please help us meet this challenge. Then we can get your boundless blessings and please Lord Kṛṣṇa and go back to Godhead, and our life will be successful.

Your fallen servant,  
Subhāgha Swami

## Umāpati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

A new decade in one’s life is a time for reflection. When the twenties turn thirty and the thirties turn forty, we like to take a little extra time to think about where we have been and where we want to go. What, then, can we say about a new century?

Of course, I have not reached a century yet, at least not in the present body, but the order of the spiritual master is the life and soul of the disciple. In a sense, then, my life began when Śrīla Prabhupāda came into this world. So I have been reflecting on a hundred years of Śrīla Prabhupāda and his mission.

I was reflecting in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s room at Rādhā-Dāmodara temple in Vṛndāvana this year. I was sitting next to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s eating place, and I could imagine Śrīla Prabhupāda himself sitting there years ago, looking out the window at Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *samādhi* and *bhajan-kutir*.

I could imagine Śrīla Prabhupāda thinking about his mission and writing the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* purports. Any mundane onlooker would have smiled with compassion at Śrīla Prabhupāda’s determination in the face of hopeless odds: “He has such innocent faith in the order of his spiritual master, but how can he ever make it to America? He is so poor he can’t even afford a ricksha to Mathurā.”

But the smug pity turns to awe when you realize that Śrīla Prabhupāda succeeded. And because of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s faith in his spiritual master, the world has been saved.

And I have been saved. I have become a little part of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission. And now I wait for the day when I will feel myself to be a tiny speck of dust empowered by the magnificent order of my spiritual master.

Suddenly my reverie broke, and it was 1996 again. I got up and returned to the Krishna-Balaram Mandir. I saw the crowds of devotees, and I remembered the crowds at Māyāpur—thousands of devotees had come to the festival. All of them, and still thousands of others, had been saved by the books that Śrīla Prabhupāda started at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple.

Every year more devotees come, and every year the Vaiṣṇava *saṅga* gets sweeter, and the problems between devotees are giving way to problems faced by devotees together. The arguing of the past has given way to the hugs and smiles of the present. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s words are coming true.

“Just don’t go away. Just don’t go away.” That was Śrīla Prabhupāda’s advice to those who were having doubts. If we could just somehow or other stick to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, everything would work out.

This was also Śrīla Prabhupāda’s advice to me when I left the temple in 1966 to go back to the world of *māyā*. I was standing in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s apartment one day, visiting the devotees. Śrīla Prabhupāda came into the room and walked up to me. “Even if you don’t want to be one of us,” he said, “you must associate with us. You cannot get away from us.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I don’t want to get away any more. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Thank you. Thank you. Thank you.

Your servant,  
Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇā Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories unto you.

When I contemplate the meaning of a Vyāsa-pūjā offering, a pastoral scene drifts into memory. It is your Appearance Day in New Vrindaban, 1972. I was standing at the spring house, which was just beside the pathway we knew you would tread on that blessed day. The path led up to a pavilion on the hill where Rādhā-Dāmodara waited, casting Their merciful glance upon the fortunate souls in the pasture below Them.

Across the flowering hillside, cows were grazing on grass and clover. They were decorated nicely with the gifts of the Earth for the celebration of your appearance.

The sound of *kīrtana* rose up from the base of the hill, proclaiming your arrival. You ascended the path with devotees following, singing and dancing in the joy of your company.

And then as you gazed upon the flock of Kṛṣṇa’s cows, tended by their cowherd boys, you beamed with pleasure and delight. To see that smile was to know first hand what Sally Agarwal meant when she called it “oceanic.”

How is it possible that an act so humble, so gentle and childlike, could conquer the impenetrable stronghold of my stonelike heart? Because it was backed by the love of Kṛṣṇa, the most powerful force in all of Creation.

Simultaneously and paradoxically, it is the gentlest force in all of creation. Never asserting itself without finding willing consent.

In contrast to our tender Lord, who does not force Himself into our lives, you, His servant, did assert yourself. Although we were stubborn and unwilling, you forced us to drink the nectar of devotion. If you had not brought Kṛṣṇa into our lives, we dare not think what our destination would have been. Therefore we must conclude that you are even more merciful than Kṛṣṇa, the very embodiment of His love.

Just to see your smile during that most blessed event was to personally behold the Lord’s desire for His parts and parcels, to see the way He wanted us to live and to behave. It also served as a living testimony to the simple beauty of redeemed life—the gift you came to bestow. By the potency of that simple act, our lives have ceased to be molded by fear, and are instead being guided by love for you.

After seeing you smile, we showered you with flowers and joined in the *kīrtana,* climbing the hill to the pavilion temple. Though the path was steep and rugged, nobody even noticed. We were irresistibly pulled onward and upward by the sweetness of the *vraja-bhakti* which emanated from you. And when the *kīrtana* ended, you explained the the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā.

Today I return to the same location on the side of the road to relive what I previously experienced. But the spring has gone dry and the cows are gone. The pavilion on the hill collapsed with the passing of time. But the saddest part of all is that you have withdrawn your *vapu,* your visible form, from the vision of this world. I find here only a fond remembrance. It is a picture of shelter in serving your *vāṇī,* your mission, wherever that may lead us.

The spring, being dry, may seem to be meaningless now. It could easly go unnoticed like any other forgotten place on the side of some abandoned path. But there is reason to return here, to pause and contemplate the significance of that well-spring of fond memory—because by stopping at places such as these we find a treasure house of meaning in an act so very simple, and yet so sublime in illustrating transcendental knowledge.

As I lingered there on the side of the path, I began to realize the tremendous significance of a former day, when we were just like the cows we were tending, wandering everywhere in search of greener grasses. Seeing our condition, you took compassion and gave us direction. Simply by a smile, you invited us to step forward and follow in your footsteps. Simply by a smile, you convinced us to leave all other pursuits that might lead us astray, reminding us that Kṛṣṇa is our only goal.

Now, years later, when so many circumstances have changed, let us continue to be ready to serve you. Whether that means we must place our hand on the plow or place it instead on the staff, whether figuratively or literally, whether here or there, now or then, let this be our only prayer.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, may your love always shine as my only guiding light so I may show honor and submission to my authorities, love and trust toward my Godbrothers, protection and affection for those you have placed in my care. For it is love alone that conquers the fears and sorrows of death and separation. It generates new life, like springtime flowers peeking through the snows after a barren winter, delighting those who have had the patience to wait.

Your love, as powerful in the little and hidden things as it is in things grand and obvious, is our shelter and inspiration, even when all other sources of sustenance seem to go dry.

Love is so easy to say and sweet to hear about in this age that the word has nearly lost all its meaning. Of course, words alone are cheap and meaningless if not backed by a life of commitment. Let not our words be only for show, a mere shower of flowers. We pray for your mercy that our words be grounded in commitment, surrender, and dedication.

These are the virtues which attracted us to you. And these qualities can only develop in us by your mercy. Your words and deeds are inspiring because they flow consistently as an offering of love to your Gurudeva and Kṛṣṇa. It is in you that we saw the appropriate and faithful response to the loving will of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. And because you exemplify the perfect servant, you naturally became the perfect master.

For twenty-four years now, the pain of separation has become, more and more, the prominent quality of our relationship. May that anguish serve to wean us of our hankerings for instant gratification, transcendental though they may be. The path grows steep and rugged without the smile of your encouragement. Be pleased to let our first steps behind you lengthen into a life-long walk of commitment from which there will never be any turning back. May we uphold the devotional standard you have given us, which requires obedience, sacrifice, and commitment.

May this life be offered as a sacrifice, leading us to eternal devotion, following always in your lotus footsteps.

Most of all, we humbly pray that our motivation be only to give you pleasure. Because it is your pleasure, conveyed by your smile, which brings the dawn of Kṛṣṇa’s love into this dark and dreadful age.

The fragrance of that flowerly pasture has drifted across the boundaries of time and culture, enlivening our hearts with the fragrance of love amidst a world so darkened with impersonalism.

And yet, I know it is your lotus feet, even more than your smile, which should be the object of my meditation.

Your feet represent the process of service, surrender, receiving guidance, and finding the shelter of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Whether Kṛṣṇa is pleased by our service, whether He even accepts our service, can only be confirmed by your pleasure. And your pleasure, which is our goal, aspiration, motivation, and purpose, was confirmed to us on that blessed day of Vyāsa-pūjā in 1972. Simply because we had the good fortune of seeing you smile.

Aspiring to remain the servant of your servants,  
Varṣāṇā Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

**A Mere Palmful of Water**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

This is your one hundredth birthday! Happy birthday, dear Gurudeva! I wish I could offer you the whole world. I wish I had millions of dollars to donate to your book fund, or hundreds of new books to show you that I just had translated into another language (like Harikeśa Mahārāja always does). I wish I could tell you of all of the achievements in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness that I have been responsible for so that I could offer them all to you. Yet, even if I could claim to have all these things to offer you, would that really be enough?

Are there any riches in this world which are sufficient to offer you? Are there any achievements that are worthy of satisfying our debt to you? What can I possibly offer you that has any value at all except folded hands containing water for your satisfaction? As Pṛthu Mahārāja states in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (4.22.47):

*yair īdṛśī bhagavato gatir ātma-vāda*  
*ekāntato nigamibhiḥ pratipāditā naḥ*  
*tuṣyantv adabhra-karuṇāḥ sva-kṛtena nityaṁ*  
*ko nāma tat pratikaroti vinoda-pātram*

**“How can such persons, who have rendered unlimited service by explaining the path of self-realization in relation to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and whose explanations are given for our enlightenment with complete conviction and Vedic evidence, be repaid except by folded palms containing water for their satisfaction? Such great personalities can be satisfied only by their own activities, which are distributed amongst human society out of their unlimited mercy.”**

What to speak of the great debt humanity at large owes you due to your merciful appearance in this world, is there any way that an insignificant *jīva* like me can possibly repay you? Any attempt to do so would surely make me a great fool. “The word *vinoda-pātram,*” you write in the purport to the above verse, “can be divided into two words, *vinā* and *uda-pātram,* or can be understood as one word, *vinoda-pātram,* which means ‘joker.’ A joker’s activities simply arouse laughter, and a person who tries to repay the spiritual master or teacher of the transcendental message of Kṛṣṇa becomes a laughingstock just like a joker because it is not possible to repay such a debt.”

Just see my plight! I have a debt that cannot be repaid, and yet I work to repay it! Surely I am a laughingstock. May I pray that Your Divine Grace be satisfied by your own activities, which you have distributed amongst human society out of your unlimited mercy? In exchange for your unlimited kindness, there is very little else that I can offer you which is of any value.

Therefore, I would simply be happy for you to know that I’m still clinging on, somehow or other, and I’m offering myself up for your satisfaction with a straw in my teeth. Please accept me as your property and engage me in your service. In this way, please make my life worthwhile. This is my earnest and sincere prayer on your auspicious Centennial Vyāsa-pūjā. So saying, I stand humbly with this folded palmful of water from 1,008 holy places provided me by your servants. I think I have nothing else of value to offer. Please accept.

Your eternal, worthless, and somewhat wretched servant,  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Adelaide

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are definitely divine bliss manifest in this world:

*rādhā-sammukha-saṁsaktiṁ sakhī-saṅga-nivāsinīm*  
*tvām ahaṁ satataṁ vande mādhavāśraya-vigrahām*

**“O spiritual master (Śrīla Prabhupāda), I continually offer my obeisances unto you, who are always in the presence of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and very much devoted to Her. You always reside in the association of Her confidantes, the** ***gopīs,*** **and you are the loving abode of loving devotion to Kṛṣṇa.”**

*he guro jñāna-da dīna-bandho*  
*svānanda-dātaḥ karuṇaika-sindho*  
*vṛndāvanāsīna hitāvatāra*  
*prasīda rādhā-praṇaya-pracāra*

**“O spiritual master (Śrīla Prabhupāda), O giver of divine wisdom, O friend of the fallen, you are the giver of your own bliss, and you are the only ocean of mercy. Although dwelling in Vṛndāvana, you have descended for the welfare of fallen souls like myself, and you are preaching the divine love of Rādhā for Kṛṣṇa. Please be kind upon me.”**

In this verse seven items are listed, and these are the symptoms of a real *sādhu,* a real *guru;*

1\) giver of divine wisdom—in the form of books, lectures, and realizations.

2\) friend of the fallen—all of us in this material world.

3\) giver of your own bliss—to your last breath you were translating the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* 10th canto.

4\) ocean of mercy—you fanned the spark of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in anyone and everyone.

5\) dwelled in Vṛndāvana—Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were a resident of Goloka and Gokula Vṛndāvana.

6\) descended for the welfare of all fallen souls.

7\) preaching the divine love for Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Who else came to the West before you did and delivered the message of Prema Puruṣottama Śrī Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu?

In this material world there is generally no *bhakti*. *Bhakti* is manifest in Goloka, and it is brought to this world by the residents of Goloka Vṛndāvana, who are all liberated souls. Otherwise there is no *bhakti* here!

Now, the symptoms of such a liberated soul, perfected being, resident of Goloka are listed in the *Hari-bhakti-vilāsa* (1.45–46):

*kṛpa-sindhuḥ su-sampūrṇaḥ sarva-sattvopakārakaḥ*  
*nispṛhaḥ sarvataḥ siddhaḥ sarva-vidyā-viśāradaḥ*  
*sarva-saṁśaya-saṁchettā ’nalaso gurur āhṛtaḥ*

**“One who is an ocean of mercy, who is fulfilled in all respects, who has all good qualities, who works for the benefit of all souls, who is free from lust, who is perfect in all respects, who is well versed in the scriptures, who knows the science of Kṛṣṇa, who can remove all the doubts of his disciples, and who is always alert in the service of Kṛṣṇa is known as a genuine** ***guru*****.”**

Concerning being free from lust, Śrīla Prabhupāda himself said that he had given up sex life, given up lust. And not only did Śrīla Prabhupāda remove the doubts of his disciples, but he removed the doubts of those who were not his disciples as well!

In this material world it is very difficult to free oneself from lust As Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (3.39),

*āvṛtaṁ jñānam etena jñānino nitya-vairiṇā*  
*kāma-rūpeṇa kaunteya duṣpūreṇānalena ca*

**“Thus the wise living entity’s pure consciousness becomes covered by his eternal enemy in the form of lust, which is never satisfied and which burns like fire.”**

The *sādhu’s* glory is that he is not affected by lust, and because he is freed from lust he can preach *kṛṣṇa-kathā* so purely that it enters into the hearts of the conditioned souls and removes the dirty things there. In the West, Śrīla Prabhupāda. you preached to the drug addicts, hippies, and down-and-outs, and also to those who were searching for something other than bland materialism. By your divine presence you give hope to those who were hopeless, and, in your own words, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave “new light” to those that were prepared to follow your divine word.

What else can be said? One must follow the divine instructions of a resident of Vṛndāvana or perish in this material world trying to enjoy the senses.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this divine, ever-blissful day of your divine appearance!

Your lowly servants in Adelaide, Australia.

## Agartala

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Most munificent and worshipable Śrīla Prabhupada,

On today’s universally most festive occasion of your divine appearance on this planet, kindly accept our aspiringly-to-be most submissive yet very unqualified obeisances at the liberating fragrance of your two divine lotus feet, which are the ultimate resort for the sincerely surrendered followers of Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupada, we take this unique opportunity with great privilege to express Our heartfelt gratitude and eternal and everlasting indebtedness to you for attracting its all to the paths leading to the supreme gift of love of God. But, alas! We are truly so unqualified to do justice to you by recognizing your supreme glory in that respect and imbibing your message for that highest possible achievement, which is still hidden from us.

Nonetheless, O Śrīla Prabhupada, to remain loyal to your heart’s wish that we, the very fortunate, ever-expanding family of yours, remain united through mutual cooperation, is still the prime motivation as well as challenge for all of us. Just as you are inconceivably so variegated in your total personality, so are your sincere followers in their personal understanding and appreciation of your opinions, teachings, and ideal behavior that touched their lives; and, hence, the so-called differences and discrepancies in their estimation and understanding of your absolute glories, which were manifested so variegatedly according to time, place, and circumstances. Therefore, who can dare say to have realized the glories of Your Divine Grace in totality?

Certainly this multi-opinioned scenario on some important issues with respect to what Your Divine Grace really meant through your divine teachings is the arrangement of Lord Gaurāṅga. We can only wonder at present, but with full conviction that with the passage of time things will evolve to manifest the presence of Your Divine Grace more brightly just to resolve all so-called differences and disagreements. May the divine sun of the Centennial Year Celebration shine forth to fully illuminate us all.

And certainly the very fast-paced Centennial Year festivities have become the source of our inspiration to do the utmost possible collectively to preach the glory of your unprecedented glorification of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and thus make your divine personality known all over the world (*ebe yaśā ghuṣuk tribhuvana*).

Aspiring for your causeless, incessant merciful glance—in that way we will remain unconditionally devoted to Your Divine cause and pleasure.

Your worthless and unqualified son and grandchildren in Agartala, India.

## Ahmedabad

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace. All glories to the auspicious appearance day of the pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa. All glories to Śrīmatī Rajani Devi and Śrī Gourmohan De, who became instruments to bring Lord Caitanya’s most powerful general to this world. All glories to His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Goswami Prabhupāda, who ignited the spark that fully illuminated your heart, and who infused you with *kṛṣṇa-śakti* to preach and deliver the world.

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.1.8), the sages headed by Śaunaka told Sūta Gosvāmī:

*vettha tvaṁ saumya tat sarvaṁ tattvatas tad-anugrahāt*  
*brūyuḥ snigdhasya śiṣyasya guravo guhyam apy uta*

**“And because you are submissive, your spiritual masters have endowed you with all the favors bestowed upon a gentle disciple. Therefore you can tell us all that you have scientifically learned from them.”**

Your example is the embodiment of this simple truth. Simply by remaining steadfastly fixed in the instructions of His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Goswami, you, like Sūta Gosvāmī, assimilated spiritual knowledge. Simply to gather information like an academic scholar is not to acquire *divya-jñāna*. *Divya-jñāna* is realized knowledge and can be transmitted only by a self-realized *guru*. That is why your Bhaktivedanta purports are so lucid, clear, and transparent. We have read so many wonderful spiritual books without your purports, and we have gathered so much information, but in all these other literatures the satisfaction, the purification, and the transcendental emotions that we receive from the Bhaktivedanta purports are conspicuous by their absence. The *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* have become so easy and clear to us now because of your purports. But for those who try to study them without your purports, they remain mysterious. Your purports are like easy-to-digest food for the sick people of 20th-century Kali-yuga. Any sincere aspirant of spiritual life will inevitably become Kṛṣṇa conscious by reading them.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, whatever positive religious fervor we see today in the world, especially in India, is somehow connected with your bold preaching. Indeed, the appearance of a self-realized soul illuminates the whole world like a brilliant sun. As a sun pierces through every little hole in a door or a window to illuminate a dark room, you pierced the stonelike hearts of Kali-yuga men to illuminate their souls. Whenever you saw a little chance, you entered. The concrete example is the Kṛṣṇa consciousness revolution in the Soviet Union. Who can imagine how it started with Ananta-śānti Prabhu? Kṛṣṇa is *acintya,* and so are you. *Vaiṣṇavera kriyā, mudrā vijñeha nā bujhaya*. No one can understand or estimate your glories.

Even though Kṛṣṇa is the Supersoul in everyone’s heart, He remains unfathomable. Mystic *yogīs* meditate on Him for millions of years to glimpse the toenails of His lotus feet. In the same way, you walked among us and laughed and joked with us as if you were one of us, and yet you were far, far beyond us in your consciousness. You are the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead. This is actually very simple for any ISKCON devotee to understand. But unfortunate as we are, we don’t understand this. We still try to bring you down to our level. If we would appreciate your greatness, we would automatically realize our smallness. It is this feeling of smallness that attracts Lord Kṛṣṇa to make us instruments in His plans. In your prayer that you wrote on *Jaladuta,* you wrote, “I have no devotion, nor do I have any knowledge, but I have strong faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. I have been designated as Bhaktivedanta, and now, if You like, You can fulfill the real purport of Bhaktivedanta.” You felt small, but Kṛṣṇa showed how big you are. This humility of heart is the most precious jewel of a devotee. It is the basis of real spiritual success. While preaching you were like a lion, but internally you were soft as a rose and as innocent as a child. We were fortunate to witness these wonderful qualities of yours.

The second verse of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* contains the words *nirmatsarāṇāṁ satām,* which you translated as: “of the one-hundred-percent pure in heart.” That is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, cent percent transparent. That is why whatever words come out of your mouth are the words of Kṛṣṇa and the ultimate verdict of the disciplic succession. This faith is our life and soul. We beg this faith from you.

*ekākī āmāra, nahi pāya bala,*  
*hari-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*tumi kṛpā kori’, śraddhā-bindu diyā,*  
*deho’ kṛṣṇa-nāma-dhane*

**“I do not find the strength to carry on alone the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **of the holy name of Hari. Please bless me by giving me just one drop of faith with which to obtain the great treasure of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.”**

Our only asset in this life is your causeless mercy; otherwise, what was our qualification in spiritual life? It was your intense desire to preach and deliver the world. So in order to fulfill this pure desire for His pure devotee, the most merciful Lord Kṛṣṇa chose us, the most fallen, unqualified bunch, to receive this special divine grace of yours. As Śrīla Bhaktivinoda has so nicely described:

*jogyatā-vicāre, kichu nāhi pāi*  
*tomāra karuṇā—sāra*  
*karuṇā na hoile, kāndiyā kāndiyā,*  
*prāṇa nā rākhibo āra*

**“I have no good qualifications, and therefore your mercy is essential for me. If you don’t bestow your mercy, I will simply cry and, desiring to live no longer, will give up my life.”**

You once told His Holiness Satsvarūpa Mahārāja, “If you love me, I love you.” Kṛṣṇa says the same thing: *ye bhajanti tu māṁ bhaktyā mayi te teṣu cāpy aham*. “One who serves me in pure devotion is in Me, and I am in him.” If we have faith in you, your words, your actions, your unique position, you will protect us because Kṛṣṇa will protect us. *Yoga-kṣemaṁ vahāmy aham.* Kṛṣṇa wanted to please you, so He added special grace points to pass us. But if we forget that and try to seek ecstasy somewhere else and ungratefully equate someone else with you, we become useless, *asāra,* again a mouse.

Once we asked you, “Śrīla Prabhupāda! Your Guru Mahārāja wanted you to print and distribute books in the English language and preach all over the world. What would you like us to do?”

You said, very nonchalantly, “Go door to door and preach.”

We asked the same question three different times, and you gave us the same answer: “Preach, preach, and preach.” This is your mission. There is no doubt about that. Before your departure you said, “I want to die on the battlefield.” You wanted to die preaching, and indeed that is what you did. Who else in the world has such spirit? Everyone else, your Godbrothers included, is enjoying the fruits of your hard work. Therefore we should all accept you as the *jagad-guru* and respect you as such. We should desist from comparing ourselves or anyone else with you. Unfortunately, we all fall into this trap. Some of us want to equate ourselves with you, and others want to equate someone else with you. This is the most dangerous disease. Better to remain perpetual fools at your lotus feet and do whatever little service we can offer you.

This is the year of your Centennial. You are eternal and your activities are all eternal, so to associate a small figure of one hundred with you is not proper. But if we can increase our enthusiasm under that pretext or expedite our offerings to you, glorify you, and bring others to you, you would smile and accept it. Therefore we want to humbly offer this most wonderful Śrī-Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Cultural Center and Temple to Your Divine Grace this year. Less than five months are left, and we practically would have to sleep in our boots to finish it. Please give us strength and make us worthy of rendering some significant service at your lotus feet. Thank you very much.

Your servants in Ahmedabad, India.

## Baltimore

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humbles obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to the celebration of your Centennial Anniversary, which has rejuvenated the entire Society in the transcendental happiness of trying to please you with various special offerings of devotional service.

Here in Baltimore, the devotees have gotten together to build Your Divine Grace a new *vyāsāsana,* and we are feeling a new sense of excitement and optimism as a result of our humble endeavor. We have also formed a committee to renovate and expand the temple building. Some devotees have renewed their commitment of distributing your books, while others are offering special prayers and *pūjās* for your glorification. All of us are actually experiencing your ecstatic association directly by placing your lotus feet at the center of our thoughts and activities.

The Baltimore temple is celebrating an anniversary of our own this year. On July 6, 1976, you came to Baltimore and blessed this part of the world with the imprint of your lotus feet. We are commemorating that momentous occasion by inviting all the devotees to return home with Lord Jagannātha, Śrīmatī Subhadrā, and Lord Baladeva after they enjoy their Ratha-yātrā pastimes in Washington, D.C. On July 6, 1996, we are having a grand festival, and we strongly desire that you will manifest yourself in our *saṅkīrtana,* just as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu (and all Their devotees) manifested Themselves in the *kīrtana* of Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, twenty years ago you benedicted us with your realization that Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara are the most beautiful Gaura-Nitāi Deities in the world. We can say truthfully that the beauty of Their Lordships can be enhanced only by your effulgent form standing before Them, just as the beauty of diamonds are enhanced by their being set in gold. Even though we all feel very unqualified to serve Your Divine Grace adequately, your perfect, *paramparā* vision transforms our lives in this miserable Kali-yuga from stressful to blissful. Thank you for allowing us the opportunity to serve your mission here in Baltimore and for sending such nice devotees here to help each other advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

We beg to remain  
Your eternal servants in Baltimore, Maryland, USA.  
(written by Dhīra dāsa)

## Barcelona

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda: Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Thank You Very Much.

Thank you for coming to this chaotic occidental world with the real message of peace and love, which enables us keep our lives in order.

Thank you for not letting your great age stop you from embarking on the great adventure of sailing across the ocean, crossing half of the world to give us the best of medicines.
