Thank you for explaining to us and making us experience the miraculous benefits of regularly singing and chanting the *mahā-mantra,* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

Thank you for giving us the hope that at the end of this dark, deep tunnel of misery and confusion there is a bright light shining with love and trust.

Thank you for giving us this house we call a temple, where we can live with people who have the same wishes as ours, who share our experiences and understand us.

Thank you for bringing us the forms of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Although we are still far away from understanding Them, They greet us with Their presence every day and shower Their blessings upon us.

Thank you for giving us your books, which contain the most intimate secrets of all time, yet which you have written in such a way that our narrow occidental minds can understand them.

Thank you for transmitting the preaching spirit in us, which, in practice, teaches us so many lessons.

Thank you for permitting us to take part in this wonderful family known as ISKCON, which is the only shelter in this dark world despite the natural discrepancies caused by the disappearance of a great *ācārya*.

Thank you for showing us how to prepare delicious *prasādam* meals whose main ingredient is love and devotion toward Kṛṣṇa.

For all these gifts and many, many more, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda *thank you very much*.

Your servants in Barcelona, Spain.  
(written by Suci-śrava dāsa)

## Basel

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

By your glories endeavor we now have the opportunity to take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and we can very easily attain the perfection of life, pure love for Kṛṣṇa. The service that most pleased you was when your followers pushed on this preaching mission, and by the mercy of our spiritual masters we have been given a chance to perform this service, for we have been enlisted in the army of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Your transcendental books, *prasādam,* and the holy name are the weapons you gave us to fight against this demoniac society. Please empower us to spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in the region given to us by our spiritual masters. Let us glorify you by becoming pure Vaiṣṇavas and show this deteriorating society an example of simple living and high thinking.

Your servants in Basel, Switzerland.

## Belgrade

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to you, Your Divine Grace, the only savior and shelter of everyone. All glories to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the supremely merciful Lord by whose desire Your Divine Grace came to deliver all conditioned fallen souls from this world.

Here in the Belgrade Temple, all of us—your granddisciples—are completely indebted to you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is only when, under the influence of material nature, we forget that you came to this world of darkness to give as the light of spiritual knowledge that our spiritual lives become a hard struggle. Blind and lost souls in the dark fog of the Kali Age are crying in the agony of bodily consciousness. Only your books are giving relief, guidance, and hope. Everything we need, you have given us in your books. They are transcendental manuals that teach us how to use this human form of life properly. Everything necessary is described in them, and by reading, distributing, and living them even the most fallen personality can become perfectly Kṛṣṇa conscious.

We don’t have complete realization of your divine personality, but we can see how your mercy changes the hearts of the people. In this country we can see how Your Divine Grace becomes the only shelter and savior for many people who are suffering so much from the effects of Kali-yuga. For example, thousands refugees from Bosnia, completely broken from the war, without any food or water, got *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* due to your causeless mercy. In this world of the cheaters and the cheated, you and your pure followers are the only ones who never cheat and are never cheated. Therefore we can see that often a person will give his last few coins to purchase one of your books. It is your purity that forces people to surrender, first just a little (“going in like a needle”), and when they start to read your words, you “go out like a plow,” taking away all the *anarthas,* ignorance, and doubts from their hearts.

We can also see how, because of your causeless mercy, thousands of people come to the Hare Kṛṣṇa concerts and festivals, where they chant the holy name of the Lord together with devotees. And we can see how you invest your spiritual potency into those great souls who are surrendered to your order and who give everything to satisfy you.

Please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, don’t forget us. We need your mercy now more then ever before. Please give us the strength to stick strongly to the principles of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement and your divine teachings. Out of your causeless mercy and that of your pure servants who carry out your desires, various unwanted things were exposed and then uprooted from our spiritual lives. Please do not stop this *ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanam* process. We all want to become useful, individually and collectively. Please bless us with the ability to act only in a way that pleases you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We want to offer you much, much bigger book distribution scores in the future. We would like to please you by distributing your big and mahā-big books and your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* sets again. We want to offer you huge *prasādam* distribution results also. We also want to please you by holding big preaching programs, increasing our Nāma-haṭṭa preaching and Food for Life programs, and by doing everything else we can do for you here.

We just want to beg you to please help us stay united, free from quarrels, and faithful to your orders coming to us through your faithful sons, our spiritual masters. Please accept our imperfect attempts to serve your mission, and please give us the intelligence and strength to advance on the way back to Godhead.

Your granddisciples in Belgrade, the Serb Republic.

## Berbice

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

This year marks your Centennial, and it is our plan to dedicate all of our activities for your pleasure. We understand that all the things you have done for us—the changes you have made in all our lives, and all the enlightenment that is meant to purify us form perpetual sufferings—are simply manifestations of your greatness, and thus we are all eternally indebted to you. You gave us renewed aspirations and hope, and we pray that by your benevolent mercy you will take us through our many struggles to attain perfection.

We are all your granddisciples, thwarted by many impeding karmic factors, inexperience, and immaturity but striving in our own little way to make a contribution in our lifetime as an offering unto you and to the Society you have created to last for the next ten thousand years. Our committee, formed about three years ago and reactivated about two years ago, is named the Berbice Śrīla Prabhupāda Centennial Committee. It has been the unifying force and has been able to preserve some degree of respectability for the Society here in Berbice. Some of its members are utterly dedicated and determined that the Committee stays together. Weekly meetings are held, and at the end of May we had our 70thmeeting. Having a forum to associate, discuss, plan, and commit ourselves for the progress of our Society here and for our own spiritual benefit is rewarding, and it is due only to your causeless mercy. If we had been acting individually, we would have been indulging more in self-aggrandizement. As a Centennial committee, we are trying to become more and more absorbed in your *līlā* and your contribution to modern society.

At the beginning of the year we inaugurated your Centennial with a grand *harināma,* and this took the form of fifty devotees participating in a five-mile stretch of chanting and dancing for well over two hours. Devotees and well-wishers who attended enthusiastically chanted and held banners and placards depicting our celebrations. We also distributed Centennial flyers and *prasādam*. There were television programs, and an article appeared in one of our leading papers here in Guyana. Over the course of the year we have been showing various highlights and activities of your Society, and people are becoming more and more impressed. We also conduct regular Food for Life programs, and we constantly glorify you during our Prabhupāda Night programs. However, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have not done justice to our beautiful Deities, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulacandra, since many have not as yet seen Their transcendental forms. So this year we hope to make our greatest offering yet: the construction of a temple for Them. This is all for your pleasure, so please empower us to accomplish this.

There is so much more we would like to do, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but the impediments are still there, and our conditioned state always creates havoc. Our imperfect senses and limitations can take us no further than our present attainment.

On this auspicious day and event, please benedict us with your mercy so that we may realize your dreams of a united Society, free from the afflictions of *māyā,* and let us all remain grateful for this gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you have brought with you, and let us all forever be your servants in the service of Lord Caitanya.

Forever seeking your blessings, we remain  
Your granddisciples in Berbice, Guyana.  
(written by Haridāsa dāsa)

## Berkeley

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most fallen obeisances at your spotless and transcendental lotus feet. Just before departing from this world, you told us that our love for you would be demonstrated by our ability to cooperate to push on this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in your physical absence. At that time such an ideal seemed easily attainable, as we had grown accustomed to working together as a spiritual family under your expert and loving guidance.

Shortly after you left, however, Māyā attacked us with such tremendous speed and force that what had previously been a feasible reality was reduced to nothing more than a fond memory, recalled in our collective nightmare of lamentation and confusion. Māyā’s tactic was simple but deadly: divide and scatter us by obscuring your real position as the central personality in ISKCON—the eternally living, worshipable *ācārya* of all ISKCON devotees, past, present, and for generations to come. As a result of this insidious ploy, disheartened devotees left your movement in great numbers, and many of your temples suffered.

Now, by your merciful inspiration, we are gradually beginning to regroup and work cooperatively to accomplish our original goal of spreading your preaching mission throughout the world. Such cooperation, however, requires a strong sense of unity and love, without which our attempts will be utterly feeble and doomed to failure. This sense of unity can be achieved only when we all agree to recognize and accept you as our common bond—our eternal preceptor—and firmly resolve to perform all of our devotional activities for your satisfaction. If we agree to honor you in this way, then we will automatically become related to one another as a spiritual family, and in this benevolent atmosphere of loving relationships, cooperative service and progressive development of love for Kṛṣṇa will become possible.

We are all indebted to you in so many ways, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Indeed, it is not possible for us to properly describe the extent of your causeless mercy upon us, let alone repay our eternal debt to you. You are the eternally liberated, pure representative of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, fully empowered by Him to relieve the suffering souls of Kali-yuga by offering them the nectarean, soothing balm of love of Godhead. You are fulfilling the prophecy of Lord Caitanya and the mission of all the previous Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas,* beginning with Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, that the holy name of Kṛṣṇa be heard and sung in every town and village.

In this year of the one hundredth anniversary of your divine appearance in this world, let us all agree to make you the center of our lives and fondly place you on the lotus of our hearts. Then, let us work together in harmony so that we may realize your presence there. In this way, the material illusion will gradually fade and we will one day awaken to the blissful spiritual reality of Vṛndāvana and our eternal loving relationship with you and the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa. At that time, we will garland you with the most exquisite and fragrant flowers, carefully gathered from the sacred forest of Vṛndāvana, and chant your holy name in ecstasy as tears of love flow from our eyes. That will be the perfection of our lives and the fulfillment of our innermost heart’s desires. Please smile upon us and bless us with the desire and capacity to work cooperatively toward that most cherished goal.

Your humble servants in Berkeley, California, USA.

## Berlin

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Like a rain cloud you traveled all over the world and poured the water of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy everywhere. You proved that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the essence of life and that it can save everyone from the most miserable existence, even in places like downtown New York. You proved that in Kṛṣṇa consciousness the seemingly impossible can come true and you delivered to the people of this world the most treasured key to love and happiness—devotional service to Kṛṣṇa.

By your grace there is a nice group of devotees here in Berlin; some are your disciples, some your granddisciples, and there also many friends of Kṛṣṇa and aspiring devotees. By your grace we will be able to celebrate for the seventh time the annual Jagannātha Ratha-yātrā on Berlin’s main street, the Ku-Damm, in a very colorful and blissful way. On this most auspicious occasion of your 100th appearance day, we pray combinedly, falling in the dust of your feet, to kindly empower us to overcome our limitations and make Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this seemingly most degraded town a huge success. Big cities like Berlin are hellish places, but for the devotees who want to please you and give out Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, they are most nectarean. It is here where we can receive the special mercy of our grandfather, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and make life a success by pleasing you and gaining Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. You often mentioned Berlin as one of the important cities of the world, together with Amsterdam, London, Paris, Montreal, and New York. And you expressed a strong desire that your disciples preach here and establish something solid.

As you wrote to your first disciples who came to Berlin, “I was praying that Kṛṣṇa would help you in every way to establish a center here in Berlin. … Berlin is one of the most important cities in Europe.”

You wanted a big temple in this city, and therefore we know that if we simply surrender full-heartedly to your preaching mission it will be a permanent success. Already the circle of friends and devotees is constantly increasing. Already the facilities and programs we can offer are expanding. You have already prayed that Kṛṣṇa will help us in every way. Now it is up to us to surrender to this mission with full dedication. Twice you wrote to the first three disciples who came to preach here that they should inquire into purchasing the famous Gedaechtniskirche, the most prominent landmark in central Berlin, as a temple. This may take us many more years. But we are now looking seriously for a large central location that will enable us to rescue more and more people from miserable materialistic life and establish the glory of the pure Vaiṣṇava teachings permanently in this place.

Life is very short, and yet we still waste time by not surrendering 108% to the mercy-nectar rain-cloud of preaching. Sometimes it seems almost impossible to overcome the material problems and modes of nature we are facing. But you said that *impossible* is a word in a fool’s dictionary. By the mercy of Kṛṣṇa and Guru, all is possible. You have proved it hundreds of times. We can even become eternally happy and give the highest happiness to the people of this world. We understand that this is your desire. Though there is darkness all around us and we feel foolish and helpless, we beg for your special mercy that we can endure and work together enthusiastically in loving cooperation. We believe that in Kṛṣṇa consciousness the most wonderful dreams can come true if we can somehow become your qualified tools for spreading the message of love of Kṛṣṇa to every door, even in this rotten materialistic city.

The potential to preach here in Berlin is unlimited. People are thirsty for this Kṛṣṇa consciousness. May your message of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* shine bright in the hearts of all the people. And may we never foolishly give up swimming and drowning in the nectar ocean of serving Kṛṣṇa, in the dust of your lotus like feet.

Jay Śrī Guru, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Hare Kṛṣṇa !

Your servants in Berlin, Germany.  
(written by Maṇi-mañjarī-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this auspicious occasion—the 100th anniversary of your appearance in this world.

Your appearance is as significant as the Lord’s—in fact, for us, it is more significant, because it is through you, the Society you’ve brought into being, and your other contributions (followers, literatures, festivals, temples, etc.) that our lives have been touched and, thus, forever transformed. Though we are undeserving, through your kindness we have received the priceless opportunity of perfecting our lives in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. With every passing year, as our understanding (individual and collective) matures, our appreciation for your wondrous achievements increases. Initially, one is struck by all that you have done—so many books, so many temples, so many followers, etc.

We have sincerely tried to follow your example (“Do as I do!”) In trying to follow you we have acknowledged that we must strictly follow the rules you have laid down, that we must be dedicated to repeating the message we’ve received from you for our own benefit and that of others, that we must work hard for Kṛṣṇa and spend all of our time in Kṛṣṇa’s service.

But we notice that we are not so effective even though we’re doing the same things you did. Increasingly we are become aware of the reason for the gap. Far more than anything that you have done, it is what you *are* that makes all the difference. While we are trying to do what have taught us to do, we are becoming ever more conscious that we have to be what you taught us to be.

We need to be as you are—to be genuinely concerned for others, to be people of integrity, to be more concerned about the development of individuals through rendering service than getting service done through people, to be bold and courageous, to be detached, to be funny, to be humble, to be loving, to be faithful, to be spiritual masters, to be patient, to be determined and unwavering, to be loyal and dependable, to be magnanimous, to be exemplary practitioners of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to be clear about the objectives, to be focused, to be committed, to be true and truthful.

We need to believe that Kṛṣṇa can make everything possible, we need to make sacrifices, we need to nurture others and believe in the potential of people.

It is only then that we can truly call ourselves your followers. It is these attributes—these hallmarks of character—that make all the difference. You could not have done what you have done without being who you are. And this is the real example you want us follow.

Being your followers means that we must pay the price to develop these great character strengths. We often hide behind all that we apparently do on your behalf. However, if we are not prepared to develop these characteristics, then we will feel a vacuum in our Society—and we’ll not be effective in touching and transforming others’ lives as you wish us to.

We may run temples, distribute books, hold festivals, or do so many other things to please you—but unless we follow your example in terms of personal character, we will not truly advance the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

People have followed you in the past, follow you still today, and will follow you for many thousands of years to come, not because of what you do or have done but because of what you are, Śrīla Prabhupāda—the most loving, radiant, and beautiful person they will ever know.

Even if one were to take away everything that you have done and all that is left is what you are, you would still be the greatest personality to ever grace this planet, besides the Lord Himself.

The greatest offering we can make to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is to pledge ourselves as wholly to the mission of the Lord and the *ācāryas* as you have done. Whatever words are being offered today in glorification of you must be accompanied by efforts to follow your example. In this way, the second century in the Prabhupādābda will further enhance your glories.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor, England.

## Bhaktivedanta Village

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories unto Your Divine Grace.

This is the 100th anniversary of your divine appearance, and your glories are being sung with even more eagerness and resonance throughout the world. So we join in this blissful praise, offered to the most worthy person, who mercifully brought Lord Caitanya’s movement to us fallen souls in the West.

Some of us joined your movement soon after your departure, and this glorious day brings some remembrances how it was to come to ISKCON in the wake of your presence here. In my eyes, as a newcomer, the most striking thing, which filled me with awe and wonder, was the deep impression you had left on everyone, as if you possessed the key to everyone’s heart. And it was a bit like missing the main part of a show or like coming too late to an important meeting and from afar seeing the person you had a rendezvous with just turning his foot around the corner and going out of sight.

In this case, of course, we could trace your footprints, fragrant with the flower petals of pure devotion and divine grace. After taking up practical devotional service under the guidance of your confidential assistants, we discovered the truth of association through *vāṇī*—your instructions. Being engaged in various services in the *saṅkīrtana* mission, we could feel true closeness with you. And so you continue to give your association to everyone who takes shelter of you. Through your purports or your taped lectures you are readily available to all of us.

We feel ourselves more in need of your mercy than ever, for the accelerating effect of Kali-yuga is being felt more and more. As participants of a new farm project in France, we feel chilled to the marrow when we hear news of how millions of cows are going to be slaughtered en masse England due to a new disease called “mad cow disease.” (It is actually man’s madness, for the disease has developed due to adding meat ingredients in the cows’ food.) So we don’t know how mother nature is going to react to such a mortifying sin, but since we know the laws of *karma* are always at work, it is not unthinkable that the world is heading for serious crises. And so soon the public interest may focus on pure ways of living—the ways of life our communities should exemplify.

Our project here is known as Bhaktivedanta Village, and since it carries your name we feel a special obligation to you to make it successful. Even though you never graced the ground here with the impressions of your feet, we are trying, by your grace, to run this community according to your guidelines. In your village people in search of real happiness will find shelter, learning the Vedic science of self-realization. Pure water will be available, as well as vegetables and grains cultivated from nonpoisonous earth. Children will be harmonious and happy, adults contentedly engaged in their occupational duties. You have given us this model for a peaceful society; we only have to work for its realization. In this we see the secret of opening the treasure house of your mercy, by which our insignificant lives can gain real value. You said 50% of your work still remained to be done, namely the establishment *varṇāśrama-dharma* in society. In humbly attempting to achieve this goal, we string you a garland of the best of our endeavors, humility and forgiveness between ourselves, and our loving surrender unto you and those who are dear to you and inseparable from you. May you bless us to fulfill another of your dreams.

Your unqualified servants at Bhaktivedanta Village, Dole, France.

## Bhubaneshwar

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. My task here is to glorify you on the occasion of the one hundredth anniversary of your appearance day. I frankly admit that I feel totally inadequate for the task. However, with your permission I will attempt the impossible.

You are both a fulfiller of prophecy and a prophet. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu declared, “The holy name will be heard in every town and village in the world.” Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura declared a year prior to your birth that one would appear very soon who would fulfill that prophecy. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were that person. When many of your senior disciples wanted to abandon this project here in Bhubaneshwar and go directly to Jagannātha Purī to start a project there, you prophesied that some day this temple at Bhubaneshwar would be the center of the city, and it has come true.

Once in Bombay you ordered me to sit in your room while you were preaching to some Life Members. I sat there and listened for almost an hour. When they left you asked me how I would learn to preach if I did not listen to you preach. You then asked me and the other devotee whom you ordered there if we knew a certain verse from the *Bhagavad-gītā,* but we did not. Then you inquired from us if we were reading your books every day and we admitted our neglect. You first scolded, then you instructed us that we must read your books every day; otherwise how could we learn anything? Then to our surprise you informed us that you read your own books every day, and you inquired if we knew why. Feeling extremely foolish at this point, we did not offer any answer but waited patiently for your explanation and instructions. When you told us that even you learn something every time you read your own books, we were of course stunned, but when you asked us if we knew why you learned something every time you read your own books, we were even more bewildered. Personally I was totally amazed at what transpired next. You were looking at us with great intensity, and your voice expressed both authority and mysticism. As you moved your hands gracefully through the air, your eyes opened wide and you spoke as follows: “Every morning when I sit down to write these books, Kṛṣṇa personally comes and dictates every word; therefore every time I read these books, even I learn something. And if you read them every day, then you will also learn something every time you read them.” You were the original channeler, and you had the Supreme Personality of Godhead as your personal guide.

Even by material standards, to write one hundred books in ten years is inconceivable. When you add to this the transcendental potency of these books, it transcends material comprehension altogether. The glory of your service to the entire *sampradāya* is expressed through your purports by your giving the commentaries of all the great *ācāryas* along with your own. For hundreds and even thousands of years these great personalities—Vyāsa, Madhva, Jayatīrtha, Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa, Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, Śrīdhara Swami, and many others—have waited for their transcendental glorification of the Supreme Lord to reach the multitudes of conditioned souls. What satisfaction they must be deriving from seeing the millions of books distributed all over the world to benefit the fallen conditioned souls! You have glorified the Supreme Personality of Godhead and his pure devotees by your service of writing and distributing these books, and you are therefore worthy to be worshiped by all fallen conditioned souls.

In a little known pastime that took place here in Bhubaneshwar, the magnitude of your accomplishments in writing these books was further revealed after your departure to the divine realm. When Gour-Govinda Mahārāja finished the translation of your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* from English into Oriya, he had compiled a list of questions on about fifty or one hundred words for which he was apparently having a hard time making the connection between Sanskrit, English, and Oriya. Oriya was Gour-Govinda Mahārāja’s native tongue, and he had taught English and some Sanskrit in the Orissan school system for twenty years. Still, finding the exact correlation between the Sanskrit, English, and Oriya was confounding him. Since he wanted it to be perfect and he wanted that no *paṇḍita* or scholar of Orissa should question Your Divine Grace’s work, Gour-Govinda Mahārāja went off to a small village in Orissa to see a great Sanskrit scholar he knew for verification. This particular man was one of the best in the world. Gour-Govinda Mahārāja spent several days with this man, and then, at the scholar’s request, brought him to the small hut where Mahārāja and I were staying in the deserted area that would later become the center of the city, as you had predicted. The scholar told me that he wanted to tell me personally that the words Your Divine Grace had picked in English to explain the meaning of the Sanskrit words were the most obscure and brilliant explanations he had ever witnessed in his forty years of Sanskrit study and teaching. He told me it took him hours and hours of research in his vast libraries of Sanskrit dictionaries and texts to find these obscure and brilliant meanings, and he was personally convinced that only someone who had been directly enlightened by the Supreme Lord could have conceived of them. You are the greatest scholar, author, *paṇḍita,* linguist, and teacher. Your glories are unlimited!

Your Divine Grace has made the greatest contribution to science by teaching us that life comes from life, thus establishing a new paradigm by which all scientific theories concerning the origins of life are to be judged. You are the greatest scientist!

Your Divine Grace has revolutionized the Western world’s view of vegetarianism by introducing *prasādam*. You educated us not only on the ethical, ecological, and medical reasons for a vegetarian diet, but you introduced everyone to the theological principles of vegetarianism as well by teaching us *patraṁ puṣpaṁ phalaṁ toyaṁ yo me bhaktyā prayacchati*. You showed us that real spiritual life was not dry and tasteless but full of flavor, lots of flavors. You are both the master of divinity and the master chef!

You revolutionized the world of art by introducing your windows to the spiritual world. You then made them three dimensional in the form of dioramas. You created and empowered the artists that created the art that displayed to the world the form, activities, associates, and residences of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You are the greatest artist!

You taught us to march to the beat of a different drum, the *mṛdaṅga,* to jingle jangle a different tambourine, the *karatālas,* and to sing a new tune, the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and the songs of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda and Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākuras. You are a master musician and the Pied Piper as well!

You taught us a new dimension in cleanliness, punctuality, discipline, opulence, and sense control, simply by teaching us Deity worship. You are the intimate servant of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa, and you taught us the first step in attaining that position.

You wrote the first articles, translated the first books, collected money to have them printed, and printed them and distributed them yourself in the tea stalls of New Delhi and the bookstores of the Lower East Side of New York. You then created a legion of followers to duplicate your actions and proceeded to distribute more transcendental literature than anyone else in history. You are the greatest of the book distributors!

You gave us a transcendental system of education in the form of *gurukula* so that our children could learn that ABC does not stand for at, bat, cat but Ananta, Balarāma, and Caitanya. You are the greatest teacher!

You gave us *varṇāśrama-dharma,* a means to organize society around simple living and high thinking. This system of self-sufficient living creates an atmosphere where a minimum amount of time is spent on material maintenance and a maximum amount of time is spent on spiritual development. You are the transcendental sociologist with the real remedy for society’s ills. We beg your forgiveness for not fully establishing *varṇāśrama-dharma*. We are working on implementing it.

You taught us how to make and sell incense, manage temples, organize *saṅkīrtana* parties, run restaurants, and do a hundred different things that we never knew how to do before you taught us. You are our father, our teacher, our advisor, our protector, our guide, our *guru,* and our ever well-wisher!

You first requested me to come to Orissa with Gour-Govinda Mahārāja in 1975. Our initial trip here was unsuccessful. Gour-Govinda Mahārāja took *sannyāsa* and returned here alone, and in 1976 I used to visit him when I was selling books to the schools and libraries in Orissa. In 1977, in spite of my unlimited protestations, you gave me the service of staying in Orissa permanently. I remember when you called me into the room (I was told later that you asked Satsvarūpa Mahārāja, “Get me the fat one”). When I entered the room you asked, “So, Bhāgavat Mahārāja, how are you?”

“Fine, Śrīla Prabhupāda.” I replied.

Then you told me, “So I would like you to stay here in Bhubaneshwar with Gour-Govinda Mahārāja and help him develop this project; you will be co-director with him.”

I explained that I had just received a letter from Ādi-keśava inviting me to go to New York to take up some management responsibilities.

You frowned, showing your dissatisfaction. “There are so many devotees in New York already. You don’t need to go to New York.”

I became nervous. “But Śrīla Prabhupāda,” I said, protesting, “I am not feeling well. I need to go back to the West to recover.” The distress was evident in my voice, but, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you would have none of it.

“You can be very healthy right here. There is clean air and clean water, and you can pass stool out in the open and take bath. This is a very healthy place for you.”

My anxiety grew. “But,” I further protested, “I do not get along with Gargamuni Mahārāja, and he is the GBC here.”

You dismissed this as a minor problem. “I will remove Gargamuni Mahārāja as GBC and you will work directly under me.” Now you made me GBC without title.

Grasping for some solution, I finally revealed my offense to you. I was convinced that upon hearing that my *sādhana* was lacking you would not allow me to take over an entire project. “I am not chanting my rounds, Śrīla Prabhupāda,” I confessed.

But you were not to be outdone. Your determination was clear. You paused briefly and then said, “I have noted that you work very hard, and when you work hard for Kṛṣṇa sometimes you cannot chant your rounds. That is all right as long as you work hard for Kṛṣṇa.” Everyone in the room sat in stunned silence, seeing your unflagging determination. I finally surrendered.

“It is stated in *śāstra,*” I began, “that by following the orders of the spiritual master and pleasing him one will make spiritual advancement and please Kṛṣṇa. So if you want me to stay here, Śrīla Prabhupāda, then I will.” My reward was instantaneous. Your face lit up with a smile a mile wide, and your eyes twinkled with satisfaction.

“Yes,” you said, “that is correct. You have understood my books.” That was in February of 1977. Later that same year, in the last days before you passed from this world, I confided to you when we were alone late one night that I was concerned about your leaving and about what I would do after you were gone. You simply said, “You should stay in Bhubaneshwar with Gour-Govinda Mahārāja.”

You know why I had to leave Bhubaneshwar. Over the course of the years, bereft of my service, I wandered here and there trying to fit in and find myself and some service, both in and out of ISKCON. Sometimes I would think of my days in Bhubaneshwar, and whenever Gour-Govinda Mahārāja came to the US on tour I would go to see him and he would remind me that Śrīla Prabhupāda had told me to stay in Orissa and manage the affairs there. But I was caught in the web of Māyā, doing business, making money, paying off loans.

I was planning to come to Bhubaneshwar in August of this year when suddenly you came to me in a dream. You asked me what I was doing. I said I was living in Alachua (Florida), doing some work, going to the temple, organizing some programs. You said, “What service are you doing?”

I replied, “Well, I plan to go back to Bhubaneshwar and help build the temple in Purī, open an Ayurvedic clinic, do *prasādam* distribution, and preach in the villages.” The whole time you were smiling broadly and shaking your head in a gesture of approval. Then suddenly you stopped and looked at me very seriously and said, “Remember, you must go back to Bhubaneshwar.” I woke up bewildered. When is it that I should go? Today? Tomorrow? Next year? I was not sure. I decided to wait for a while to see if there would be some sign of what I should do. One week later my dear Godbrother Gour-Govinda Mahārāja entered into *samādhi,* and then I understood that I had to go immediately! I left my high-paying job, withdrew my savings from the bank, and came back to the service that you authorized me to do in the first place.

Returning after all these years to do this service, I feel your presence and association more deeply then I have in years. I thought I was doing service for you in Alachua, but now I understand that since I was fortunate enough to be given service by you directly then I must do that particular service or else I am not actually pleasing Your Divine Grace. Once in Vṛndāvana you were talking to a few devotees in your room, and while speaking these words you looked directly at me and said, “We have associated previously, we are associating now, and in the future.” In spite of all my sinful offenses you have not forgotten me. You came in the middle of the night while I was sleeping in the lap of Māyā, and you rescued me from a fate worse than death. You have not allowed me to forget my promise to you to stay in Bhubaneshwar as you ordered. This is my *prabhu-datta-deśa,* the place where I have been ordered to perform my devotional service to Kṛṣṇa by my *guru*.

It is said that the spiritual master lives eternally in his instructions and the disciple lives with him. I have never felt that more deeply in my life than I do right now. As it is also stated, I shall never be able to repay my debt to you. All glories to you, my Divine Master Paramahaṁsa Paramapūjya 108 Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda on this, the hundredth anniversary of your birth! May your glories be spread throughout the three worlds!

The devotees in Bhubaneshwar, India  
(written by Bhāgavat dāsa)

## Boise

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you and to Lord Caitanya, on whose behalf you advented one hundred years ago. As we sit every week and try to grasp and understand your unlimited pastimes through the reading of the *Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta,* it occurred to us how fortunate we were to be born when you were on this planet.

You told us that we are fortunate to be born in the Golden Age of Lord Caitanya’s advent. Lord Caitanya appears only once in a day of Brahmā, that is, once every thousand Kali-yugas. Even the demigods desire to take birth during this Golden Age, when the ill effects of Kali-yuga are temporarily set back and the method for achieving Kṛṣṇa becomes so simple.

However, if it were not for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we would have never known about this Golden Age and would have wasted the rare opportunity. Without making even the slightest attempt for self-realization, we would have spent the human form of life in total sense gratification. We are so fortunate to have been born not only in the Golden Age but after your appearance. Without you we would have remained in complete ignorance, like swine, with no ability to distinguish between garbage and gold. The Golden Age might then as well have been the garbage age. Little would we have known or cared. It was only through you and your *vāṇī* that we could have perceived and experienced the golden moonshine of Śrī Caitanya-candra illuminating this dark age.

Lord Brahmā had to kill Indra, when he was a hog, to bring him back to his senses. Though we deserved the same fate and more, you, out of your causeless mercy, spared us and lifted us from the gutters of the material world. You patiently fed us *prasādam* and purports to bring us to the stage of asking “Who am I? What am I doing here? And how do I get out of here?”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are trying to be servants of your servants. Help us serve them so they can serve you so that we can all get out of here and maybe find a tiny spot under the shade of your lotus feet.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Your devotees in Boise, Idaho, USA.  
(written by Anantarūpa dāsa)

## Bombay (Chowpatty)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories unto you! All glories to the *saṅkīrtana* mission of Lord Caitanya, which you have spread all over the world!

Somehow or other, by some inconceivable and miraculous stroke of good fortune, we have all come to the shelter of your lotus feet within this ISKCON movement. What relief, comfort, joy, love, and security is there in this shelter! You have given meaning in life—the real meaning. Today we are all trying to serve Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha; we are chanting, dancing, and feasting together; we are discussing nectarean *kṛṣṇa-kathā* together and trying to preach this sublime message you have given us to one and all. Now we cannot imagine ourselves in any other situation. Śrīla Prabhupāda, where would all of us be today without you? We shudder to even think about it.

Your ISKCON, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the movement of this age. It is the movement of the present and the future. It is the beacon of light in these dark times, when religious values and institutions are crumbling, when even traditional Vaiṣṇava institutions are slowly losing influence, potency, and purity. But your ISKCON, Śrīla Prabhupāda, will become stronger and stronger, to show the way to all of us and to the people of this world.

Even for us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the magnitude of what you have given us has perhaps not fully sunk in. Eternal life, full of bliss and knowledge, with Kṛṣṇa and His eternally liberated associates in the spiritual world! Can we even imagine such a thing? What a future! And this is the future you have opened up for us, who would otherwise be rotting here in this dark world, lifetime after miserable lifetime.

And you have given this future not just to us here but to every single devotee in this movement. Indeed, you have given it to every living being in this world, because by your grace and with the vision and impetus you provided, Śrīla Prabhupāda, ISKCON is spreading and spreading. And one day this movement will capture the hearts of one and all.

Please grant us this boon: that we may forever remain within the cooling and fragrant shelter of your lotus feet and serve you and your mission with our heart and soul.

Your aspiring servants, at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Mandir in Chowpatty, Bombay, India.

## Bombay (Juhu)

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet, the merciful shelter for the suffering souls of Kali-yuga.

Because you are our life and soul, and especially because this is your most glorious Centennial Year, we, the humble office staff at your Bombay “office,” are trying more than ever to find different ways to bring the people of Bombay to your lotus feet. It’s easy to say “You are our life and soul,” but when we really feel it, and realize and act on it, then a certain peacefulness comes over us, and we become eligible to understand the message *anyābhilāṣitā-śūnyam.*

It’s been a long time since we have typed you a letter from your Bombay Office, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we would like to give you a little report. It’s been eighteen years since we had the grand opening of the new temple project. As we reflect back over the years, we see how you have always been there guiding us, sending us helpers, chastising us with a heavy hand or holding our hand like a loving father, and even serving us as Kṛṣṇa served Arjuna. Being busy in our “so important services,” we sometimes forget you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but you never forget us for a moment.

Now in your Bombay Office there are four GBC secretaries—Gopāla Krishna Mahārāja, Girirāja Mahārāja, Śrīdhara Mahārāja, and Rādhānātha Mahārāja. And there are so many departments: In addition to Śrīdhara Mahārāja as Temple President, Keśava Bhāratī Prabhu and Devakīnandana Prabhu as Vice Presidents, and Adityā Mātājī, there are Siddhānta Prabhu—Head Pūjārī; Dadhi-bakṣa Prabhu—Life Membership Director; Gopāla Prabhu—New Bhakta Director; Rasarāja Prabhu—Director of the Bhaktivedanta Institute with Kapila Prabhu—Manager of the B.I. and the Bhaktivedanta Swami Mission School; Surdas Prabhu—Director of the Bhakti-kalā-kṣetra and Congregational Development; Narmada Mahārāja—Manager of the BBT; Vakreśvara Paṇḍita Prabhu—Govinda’s Manager; Braja Hari Prabhu—General Manager; Dāmodara Prabhu—Manager of Book Distribution; Dr. Rādhā Krishna Prabhu—Centennial Coordinator; Abhirāma Prabhu—Manger of Accounts Office; Kāṇāi Ṭhākura Prabhu—Manager of BHĪṢMA; Mr. Kapadia—All India Chief Accountant; Nanda Kiśora Prabhu—Legal and Public Affairs; Nitāi-pada-kamala Prabhu—Manager of Devotee Prasādam; Hāḍāi Paṇḍita Prabhu—Director of Food for Life; Seva-rasa Prabhu—Director of the Bhaktivedanta Swami Music Academy; Bhakta Mangesh—Bhaktivedanta Youth Club; Meena—Guesthouse Maintenance; and many other departments and managers, as well as a large number of devotees and congregational members who are all serving wholeheartedly, so dedicatedly and sincerely.

The activities of all these departments are only meant to glorify Your Divine Grace and Lord Kṛṣṇa. Knowing everything, you send them help in many ways and make their efforts successful, thereby glorifying your devotees. Though they are trying to glorify you, you instead make them appear glorious by their service to you. In this way, there is a transcendental competition between Your Divine Grace and your servants to see who can glorify whom more.

How can we ever thank you enough, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us a chance to serve your lotus feet? By the activities of these departments, by bringing people to you, we are trying to show our thanks, but it is you who are directing the people to us, so again we are caught up in accepting service from you.

The city of Bombay, where you spent so much of your time while in India, is also changing, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Recently they changed the name from Bombay to Mumbai, indicating the intense body consciousness of the politicians. There are more and more scandals in the politics, but still, when we held your Premāñjalī Festival to inaugurate your Centennial Year Celebrations, more than 40,000 of the city’s leading citizens attended. The people of Bombay still remember you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and they came to offer their respects to you. Recently even your old adversary, Mr. Pushpakant Mhatrc, was a key figure in changing the name of Gul Mohar Road to Bhaktivedanta Swami Marg.

We are also happy to report that Bombay won the December Book Distribution Marathon, and we are planning to be first in book distribution for the whole year of 1996, which we hope will be most pleasing to Your Divine Grace.

The college preaching is also expanding, and more than seventy responsive college students come every Friday night for the Bhaktivedanta Youth Club programs. By your grace, a new congregational preaching program has been started, the Bhaktivedanta Swami Music Academy, which is spreading the holy name through the medium of music. Many cultured and educated persons attend these music classes and learn the philosophy of the *Bhagavad-gītā* along with their music lessons. We are also planning how to improve our administration in order to offer the best service to your life members, as we want to set the standard for systematically spreading your movement because this is your “office.” Plans are also being made to create the “Heaven on Earth” at Hare Kṛṣṇa Land, as you desired.

Actually, we realize that everything is being done by Your Divine Grace, and we are simply fortunate to be able to act as your instruments. We pray that we may always serve you in your mission to guide the people of Bombay to the ultimate goal of life.

Your office staff in Juhu Beach, Bombay, India.

## Boston

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances on this most auspicious day of the one hundredth anniversary of your divine appearance in this world.

On this special day, all over the world devotees in their different temples will take great joy in recounting their special connection with you because of the unique qualities possessed by each of the temples (or “satellites of Kṛṣṇaloka”) you have established. Wherever your devotees are serving becomes a holy place with a unique mellow of Kṛṣṇa consciousness because of the special circumstances that place has in the *saṅkīrtana* movement’s overall worldwide strategy, which is nicely connected to the mood of the Lord in His Deity form and the mood of the devotees who are serving there. How much more this is true for the places where Your Divine Grace performed your personal pastimes during your manifest *līlā*! Especially the places and temples where you personally asked the Lord to come as the *arcā-avatāra* of the Deity.

Certainly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, one of the greatest joys to your devotees in ISKCON is to visit your temples and recall with joy the pastimes you had with the devotees and Deities there. How wonderful it is, for example, to have the *darśana* of Rādhā-Govinda in New York, Rukmiṇī-Dwarkadīśa in Los Angeles, Rādhā-Londonīśvara in England, and all the other places Your Divine Grace made into sacred *tīrthas*.

Your devotion, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and love for Kṛṣṇa was always most wonderful and special for all the Deities you installed. Devotees were always very satisfied to serve the Lord in the place given to them for their residence by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. They remained satisfied by happily meditating on your love for the Deity of that place and the loving trust you invested in them to take care of the Deities and to take responsibility to preach to the fallen souls of that place on your behalf.

Knowing that you are sharing your own love for Kṛṣṇa by your seeming to become dependent on our service fills us with great feelings of affection for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Of course, we know that it is your mercy on us, to engage us in assisting your service to Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya. You have taught us that all services rendered by the disciples are actually rendered by the spiritual master, and that the disciples’ performance of that service is as his assistant. You have taught us that our purely understanding and entering into this service to you will bring us to the most desired stage of devotional service, the pure relationship between the spiritual master and the disciple, and, ultimately, the relationship between the devotee and the Lord.

You have written in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.2.6, “Each and every living being has his eternal form in the spiritual existence, and Kṛṣṇa is the central attraction for all of them. … The relation is one of the servant and the served. … This relation of servant and the served is the most congenial form of intimacy. One can realize it as devotional service progresses. Everyone should engage himself in that transcendental loving service of the Lord, even in the present conditional state of material existence. That will gradually give one the clue to actual life and please him to complete satisfaction.”

You wrote an amazing purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 6.19.5 that further develops the idea of “this most congenial relationship of master and servant” to the highest degree. Therein you have described that the most important aspect of the Lord’s self-sufficiency is that He depends on His devotees. You call this, Śrīla Prabhupāda, “the Lord’s causeless mercy.” Here is your wonderful purport:

“Sometimes the Lord, as the child of Mother Yaśodā, requests His devotee for some food, as if He were very hungry. Sometimes He tells His devotee in a dream that His temple and His garden are now very old and that He cannot enjoy them very nicely. Thus He requests the devotee to repair them. Sometimes He is buried in the earth, and as if unable to come out Himself, He requests His devotee to rescue Him. Sometimes He requests His devotee to preach His glories all over the world, although He alone is quite competent to perform this task. Even though the Supreme Personality of Godhead is endowed with all possessions and is self-sufficient, He depends on His devotees. Therefore the relationship of the Lord with His devotees is extremely confidential. Only the devotee can perceive how the Lord, although full in Himself, depends on His devotee for some particular work. . . .

“Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was quite competent enough to spread His name and mission all over the world, but still He depended upon His devotee to do this work. Considering all these points, the most important aspect of the Supreme Lord’s self-sufficiency is that He depends on His devotees. This is called His causeless mercy. The devotee who has perceived this causeless mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead by realization can understand the master and the servant.”

Humbly taking these sacred statements as the dust on our heads, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we, your servants at your Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīvallabha Mandir in Boston—where Your Divine Grace first placed his lotus feet in the Western world, thus fulfilling the greatest desires of the Lord, His associates, and all the *ācāryas* after centuries of ardent hope and desire—take with the greatest happiness the privilege to serve Your Divine Grace in the sacred place where the great dream finally came true.

On September 17, 1965, at 5:30 a.m., during the auspicious *brāhma-muhūrta,* you arrived in Boston Harbor. You marked the occasion on September 18 by dedicating your wonderfully inspired prayers, *Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma*. These prayers are the vanguard of the highest standard for all Vaiṣṇavas to follow. In humility and helplessness you prayed to the Lord to empower you to accomplish this seemingly impossible task of preaching to the Westerners. Here are excerpts from your prayer:

“How will they understand the mellows of devotional service? O Lord, I am simply praying for Your mercy so that I will be able to convince them about Your message.

“I wish that You may deliver them. Therefore, if You so desire their deliverance, then only will they be able to understand Your message. … Somehow or other Lord, You have brought me here to speak about You. Now, my Lord, it is up to You to make me a success or failure as You like. . . .

“O spiritual master of all the worlds! I can simply repeat Your message. So if You like You can make my power of speaking suitable for their understanding.

“Only by Your causeless mercy will my words become pure. I am sure that when this transcendental message penetrates their hearts they will certainly feel gladdened and thus become liberated from all unhappy conditions of life.

“O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands. So if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as You like.

“I have no devotion, nor do I have any knowledge, but I have strong faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. I have been designated as Bhaktivedānta, and now, if You like, you can fulfill the real purport of Bhaktivedānta.”

In great humility, you described yourself as “the most unfortunate, insignificant beggar.”

Although we are held in great awe and appreciation upon hearing your expression, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know that the true meaning of your humility-laden words reveal the unparalleled and exalted love between yourself and Lord Kṛṣṇa.

As you stated in your Sixth Canto purport, the most important aspect of the Lord’s self-sufficiency and His causeless mercy is that He depends on His devotees. You described this relationship between the Lord and His devotees as “extremely confidential.” Your *Jaladuta līlā* and the wonderful prayers that you spoke are the highest shining example for all devotees in the universe to look up to and worship as the greatest example of the confidentiality of the master’s and servant’s mutual dependence. This proves you to be the dearmost confidential servant of Lord Caitanya, whom you very remarkably describe in your Sixth Canto purport as depending on you personally as His devotee to do the work of preaching all over the world!

Everything about your voyage aboard the *Jaladuta* (appropriately named for you as the Lord’s emissary, “the messenger of the waters”) was wonderful and praiseworthy to the supreme degree!

You often took great pleasure in describing the events that led up to your coming to us from Vṛndāvana’s shelter, where you resided at the place that was the most sacred and dear to you—Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara’s temple in the shelter of the six Gosvāmīs. When you visited Boston in May, 1968, you were eager to visit the Commonwealth Pier to recall your arrival and the prayers you wrote to Lord Kṛṣṇa. This has become a happy tradition for us to commemorate each year—the sacred day of your arrival—by performing *harināma-saṅkīrtana* and describing the glories of Your Divine Grace at the Commonwealth Pier.

You were coming from the topmost abode of love of God at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, bearing the gifts of the Lord and His associates, to distribute the most rare fruits of Kṛṣṇa *prema* in a way that would prove to come forth in a torrent of mercy that had never before been seen or believed possible.

The vision of how this great mercy would burst forth was given to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on the *Jaladuta* voyage. The prayers that you spoke to the Lord and “the Lord’s taking charge of the ship,” as you describe in regards to Kṛṣṇa’s protecting you from two heart attacks at sea, were the harbinger of the wonderfully attractive potency you manifested thereafter in New York, San Francisco, and everywhere else. You won over so many sincere hearts in such a way as to go deep into their souls, so much so that they would cry unprecedented tears upon experiencing Kṛṣṇa’s love. And on and on throughout the world, the same powerful mercy kept expanding more and more and still to this day is increasing unlimitedly.

As you said in your prayers to Lord Kṛṣṇa of September 13, “As the vast mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu conquers all directions, a flood of transcendental ecstasy will certainly cover the land. When all the sinful, miserable living entities become happy, the Vaiṣṇavas’ desire is then fulfilled.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, since Boston was the first place you came to begin the distribution of Kṛṣṇa’s greatest mercy and love, we feel that service to you here has an extra-special quality of sweetness and mercy. That rare mercy that devotees express was the unique mood at 26 Second Avenue—which was not the same as the movement expanded—took birth and was in its original and most freshly-innocent form when Your Divine Grace first cast his vision in the West here in Boston. A substance is always most pure at its origin. Therefore, that extra- special mercy of Vṛndāvana that you brought and began and expanded the movement with is in its most rarefied and precious form here in Boston. Your beloved Deities of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīvallabha, whose names you have so appropriately given, represent the mood of worship, *prayojana-siddhi,* in which the devotee worships the Lord in complete perfection in spontaneous love of God, the ultimate goal of life.

We take it as our sacred duty and greatest happiness to always be joyfully absorbed in recounting again and again—and thus experiencing and appreciating more and more—the glories of your *Jaladuta* voyage and arrival from Vṛndāvana in the West, and to share it with all the people of the world.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your pleasure and the pleasure of your devotees, we would like to briefly review the events that led to your arrival in Boston. This will make known how triumphant and glorious your arrival pastimes were.

We herein present excerpts from Satsvarūpa Mahārāja’s poetic rendering of your biography, the *Līlāmṛta,* which very nicely describe your glories.

**From “Living in Vṛndāvana”**

Shouldn’t he stay in Vṛndāvana  
protecting himself from wealth and women  
and never cross over the ocean?  
Yes, if he can do it  
staying alone, without cheating  
but if he has in his heart  
orders and desires of the previous *ācāryas*  
to save the wretched people in Māyā  
those outside the holy places  
those outside all hope and scope  
of redemption by the Vedic process—  
if he has such a big heart,  
then he may hanker for money,  
to go to them.  
Then his hankering is “*lālasā-mayī*”  
and he will cry tears hoping to reach the fallen souls.

**From “The Calling”**

He moved into Rādhā-Dāmodara temple  
a room five-feet by seventeen  
with a view from the kitchen  
through the cement lattice-work  
of Rūpa Goswami’s samādhi

Rising at 1 a.m.  
while spiritually bright stars  
illumined the dark Vṛndāvana sky,  
he wrote on Bhāgavatam

The blessed purports came  
from his intelligent transcendental labors.  
His own English he apologized for,  
although it was perfectly clear:  
the inmates of the house of fire  
would know that he was calling them out …

Rūpa Goswami lived there and Jīva.  
All Six Goswamis gathered and spoke Kṛṣṇa *līlā*  
and honored *prasādam* sitting on the stone floor  
eating from leaves  
writing on other leaves  
chanting on *tulasī* beads  
dancing on the banks of the Yamunā.  
The literature they left is a vast treasure house  
in Sanskrit, and Prabhupada is writing in their footsteps,  
for a worldwide English-speaking people  
whom he has not yet met.  
But he is creating books for them,  
inspired by the Six Goswamis  
and his spiritual master.  
It is they who are blessing him—  
to live in Vṛndāvana  
as well as to leave Vṛndāvana  
and to ignite the world with *bhakti*.  
They are sending him forth.  
He lives in their place,  
and he prepares to leave with their permission.  
He loves their abode and the simple renounced life,  
but he is unhappy at the unhappiness of others.  
He will go out to fulfill a duty—  
into hell with Vṛndāvana’s message.

After much struggle and sincere endeavor on your part, you printed all three volumes of the First Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and did what was necessary to gain passage from India to America. Until you were—

**“Finally Going”**

Now only the ocean’s storms  
separated you from us.  
On our side,  
the hour was very late,  
but not too late.  
No one was waiting for you in exactly the form  
you were coming,  
yet we were aching for you nonetheless—  
as parched land awaits rain,  
as a lost child awaits the mother,  
as a lonely lover awaits the beloved,  
as the soul aches for God.  
The dumb tongues stuck, unable to utter “Kṛṣṇa,”  
the blind groped,  
the mad went madder, incurring reactions;  
and every moment more *jīvas* fell  
off the cliff of human life  
down into the abyss of *tamo-guṇa.*

Inching from the weathered pier,  
the black-hulled *Jaladuta*  
moved toward the seas, bearing him to America.  
By the law of the Almighty,  
the ship floated in water.  
By His will only,  
the clouds in the sky moved.  
Out of fear of Him,  
the planets were spinning in tremendous orbits,  
and by His inscrutable sweet will,  
the pure devotee was coming,  
to rescue the damned.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Our words certainly cannot adequately express our gratitude and appreciation for what you have done for us. So let us now simply pray on this sacred day of your Centennial Vyāsa-pūjā Mahotsava to have ever-increasing enthusiasm to carry all of your instructions on our heads and to pray as you prayed here in Boston: to become a puppet in the Lord’s hands, to dance as the Lord would like. Let us spend our whole life, and life after life, worshiping the meaning of your prayers to the Lord, which demonstrate the highest limits of devotional service in pure love of God.

We are certainly insignificant, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but by your mercy we are now “the most fortunate”—we simply beg to remain your eternal servants.

Your devotees in Boston, Massachusetts, USA.

## Bratislava

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In this very significant year we would like to join our Godbrothers and Godsisters in participating in Your Divine Grace’s unique Centennial celebration. Śrī Kṛṣṇa is especially pleased when His pure devotee is glorified, and therefore we want to enthusiastically join this nectarean ceremony.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have linked us to the disciplic succession rooted in the teachings of Lord Caitanya Himself.

You have brought here to the Western countries the Lord’s mercy in the form of International Society for Krishna Consciousness. You have given us and all other conditioned souls such a great opportunity. By your blessings we can listen to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā,* and we can taste the nectar of devotee association, book distribution *saṅkīrtana, hari-nāma saṅkīrtana,* and honoring and distributing *prasādam*. We have gotten a unique opportunity to serve you, to serve your followers, to serve all the *ācāryas,* and to serve Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself.

The only desire in your heart is the desire to satisfy your spiritual master. By your exemplary devotion and humble service you have not only translated so many books but also given us unlimited nectar in the form of the worldwide hare Kṛṣṇa movement. Via all of this we are experiencing the great happiness of spiritual existence, and we are becoming more and more conscious of the enormous debt we owe you, a debt we can never repay.

Our only desire is to try, by our service, to at least repay a little of this debt. Your heart-felt desire is to never stop translating, printing, and distributing books such as *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. Therefore please accept our humble attempt to translate and publish your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and distribute it as much as we can.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Your insignificant servants in Bratislava, Slovakia.  
(written by Raghunātha-priya dāsa and Śyāmalā-devī dāsī)

## Brisbane

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories to your wonderful achievements in changing the lives of so many lost souls. And in this Centennial year we are seeing the impact of your sacrifice and worldwide preaching on such a grand scale that would have been impossible to think of in the early to middle years of your physical presence.

As we observe the effects of your teachings upon our lives in the form of becoming free from crowlike activities and desires and developing a taste and keen interest in the topics of Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya, our appreciation for the gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness intensifies.

It is on the order of your holy master, Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda, and out of your own extreme compassion that Your Divine Grace has given such a wonderful thing.

We pray that just as you never forget the order of your Guru Mahārāja, we will never forget your instructions on how to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness without adding or subtracting. The storehouse of love of Godhead has been opened to the people of the world regardless of cast, color, or creed; this is your gift alone. You did this alone at the age of seventy, without any assistance from anyone.

This is your achievement only. Without the help of anyone other than the Lord, you gave this gift to the world free of charge. Your instructions were so simple and sublime: Chant, dance, and be happy, give up prohibited activities like meat-eating, illicit sex, intoxication, and gambling. There is no happiness in sinful life.

All glories to the higher taste of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and the shelter at your divine lotus feet!

Your servants in Brisbane  
(written by Svayambhu dāsa)

## Budapest

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**I offer my respectful obeisances to the lotus feet of my spiritual master, who forced open my eyes, which were darkened by ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

**We offer our respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**We offer our respectful obeisances to you, O spiritual master, disciple of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī! You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisance on this auspicious day! All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga! All glories to the ISKCON *gurus* and you!

In your books you write that a society without Kṛṣṇa consciousness is like a body without a soul. In this dark Age of Kali religiousness is practically lost, so the whole society is like a dead body. Your transcendental movement is the only thing that can revive this agonizing body.

The Centennial of your glorious appearance gives the whole movement a good opportunity to take account of what services we owe you. We see that your desires to please Kṛṣṇa and your spiritual master were unlimited, and therefore we also have unlimited opportunities to expand our services. This also proves your perfect transcendentalism: even though there are more and more of us doing more and more—from book distribution to *hari-nāma* to Food for Life—we still feel that what we are doing is less and less as newer and newer opportunities for devotional service arise in front of us.

Once you said that you are like Lord Rāmacandra, who had to win the battle against Rāvaṇa with monkey soldiers. Unfortunately, most of the time we are like monkeys. We are in a fallen situation, for our hearts are still full of material desires due to the reactions of our previous sinful activities. This makes it difficult to offer you service that is actually effective. But still, we are convinced that if we follow this wonderful process received from you—beginning with the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa—then we still be purified very quickly, and thus we can please you and the whole *guru-paramparā* more and more.

We are fortunate because you sent your perfect disciples, who gave us your books, made us know the science of devotional service, and always inspire us to take another step. Our humble request to you is to please bless us so that we can please our spiritual masters and the GBC, because we are sure that this is the only way to please you.

Out of your causeless mercy you gave us the opportunity to take our place on the ship of transcendental knowledge, and you are the rudder on that ship. We cannot express our gratefulness in any way other than just to try our best to make others know about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is well known to us that the result of our work is not dependent on us, so we offer our respectful obeisances to you again and again. We ask your blessings so that we will become qualified to be used by you as instruments in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Please help us advance individually in spiritual life so that we can cooperate effectively. Please help us enthuse others to accept and practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to teach it to others.

We request you to accept our tiny efforts and give us the opportunity to serve you with greater and greater strength and enthusiasm. In this way we will be able to please everyone’s well-wisher, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, along with His pure devotees.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, may your name and fame be spread throughout the three worlds! We take shelter at your lotus feet.

Your aspiring servants in Budapest, Hungary.

## Buenos Aires

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

There is a beautiful verse and purport in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (9.11.25), in the description of the pastimes of Lord Rāmacandra. The translation reads, “After accepting the throne, … Lord Rāmacandra ordered His younger brothers to go out and conquer the entire world, while He personally remained in the capital to give audience to all the citizens and residents of the palace and supervise the governmental affairs with His other assistants.” You explain in the purport that a devotee should not think of his own sense gratification and thus remain inactive. The Lord, like a great emperor, orders everybody to accept His supreme position, for this is His victory (*dig-vijaya*), and the devotee soldier has the duty to spread this philosophy of life.

Out of your casuseless mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us the same order—to go out and conquer the world for Kṛṣṇa. So many times we have heard you used to say how you felt that you had conquered an empire when one of your books was published or distributed. Therefore we want to follow your instructions—we want to please you by going out and conquering Māyā’s empire by producing and distributing your saintly and spotless books. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, be merciful and help us maintain this simple and blissful mentality.

Your insignificant servants in Buenos Aires, Argentina.

## Cagayan de Oro

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at one of the atoms of the dust of your divine lotus feet, our only loving shelter. All glories unto Your Divine Grace! All glories unto your most auspicious Mahā Vyāsa-pūjā Day celebration!!!

First, let our senses, mind, and intelligence be purified by remembrance of your pure devotion to Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Let us be fixed in our worship of Your Divine Grace by remembering how you have fully surrendered at the lotus feet of your spiritual master and how your own disciples exemplified in their lives the proof of your loving compassion.

From this remotest part of the world, we would like express our deepest gratefulness and indebtedness to Your Divine Grace through a song. Just as a picture paints a thousand words, may this simple song vibrate the best of Your Divine Grace. We humbly offer this song to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who are ever fearless in claiming the fallen souls back home, back to Godhead.

**Fearless One**

You are the fearless one, You are the fearless one,  
Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda.

You saved us from illusion by your compassion;  
Our eyes can clearly see by your mercy.

Prabhupāda, we thank you, Prabhupāda we thank You.

You rode on a boat bound for the West;  
Our lives were so empty—you gave us piety.

You revealed pure devotion in your humble actions;  
Your faith was so fixed—no one could shake it.

The world needs a hero exactly like you;  
You taught us completely that we may be free.

Ours is a humble attempt to glorify Your Divine Grace. Ours is a humble attempt to become part of your *saṅkīrtana* movement. Please engage us always in your devotional service.

Ever dependent on your mercy through your sincere sons and daughters, we beg to remain  
Your grandchildren in Cagayan de Oro City, Philippines.

## Calcutta

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Another year has gone by, and many promises have been made, but how many have been fulfilled?

Somehow, by your grace, we are engaged in your service. We don’t deserve this benediction. However, your mercy and patience are great. Please don’t kick us away.

You have given us the opportunity to chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, and by chanting we have developed the understanding that we are your eternal servants. Sometimes we forget this eternal truth and fall into the grip of the illusory potency. You forcibly rescue us by reminding us of our real business, i.e., devotional service to the lotus feet of guru, Vaiṣṇavas, and Lord Kṛṣṇa. Kindly protect our devotional creeper. We are your children and seek your protection. You are everything we have. There is nothing in this world that has any substance unless it is connected with you. Our attraction to mundane happiness is an illusion. Kindly dispel that illusion so that we may wholeheartedly and single-mindedly serve your mission. This is our desire. Please tolerate our weaknesses and guide us in this dangerous world of illusion.

We are at your mercy and beg to be able to carry out your orders for the benefit of all living beings. Correct our mistakes, chastise us for our offenses, and kindly encourage us in our attempts to please you.

We do not know who you really are and how dear you are to Kṛṣṇa. We are fortunate, however, to have had a glimpse of your greatness by witnessing your *saṅkīrtana* pastimes, your boldness in dealing with the atheists and impersonalists, your humility in not taking any credit for yourself, and your perseverance in the execution of the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are an the ocean of transcendental qualities.
