Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us everything and taken us out of this deep ocean of ignorance, where all living entities are suffering constantly due to their forgetfulness of Kṛṣṇa and the desire to occupy his position. You have situated us in a Kṛṣṇa conscious condition, our original position.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the intelligence to always remember that we are your eternal servants and that we all depend on your mercy to know who we really are. Please don’t let us fall into illusion again.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us your centers, temples, and farms—oases in the scorching desert of the material world—where we can live a peaceful, happy, and marvelous life, protected from the horrible and overwhelming tricks of *māyā*.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, don’t let us become selfish and use your temples for our own personal benefit, forgetting our duty, which is to use them for the benefit of other living entities who are suffering of misery. Please give us the strength to preach, to distribute *prasādam,* and to chant the holy name so that people can approach you through all the marvelous activities you have shown us.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please engage us in your preaching mission and its different activities, meant to save the world. Don’t let us become lazy or self-accommodating. Don’t let us forget who we have been and who we are now due to your causeless mercy.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us your mercy so that we can spread your holy glories to every village and hamlet, because your activities are beneficial both for the listener and the person who narrates them. In that way we can purify our existence by absorbing ourselves in remembrance of your personality and remain under the shelter of your divine lotus feet.

Your eternal servants in Madrid, Spain.

## Manchester

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

You said you sweated blood to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to build a house the whole world can live in. The boundaries of such a house are not brick and mortar, nor even institutional legislation. The boundaries of your house are where spiritual consciousness ends and material consciousness begins. Such a house is very big. The whole world may or may not become wise enough to live in that house, but if they wanted to, there is plenty of room. Such a house is big because it is not based on miserly material considerations. The consciousness of those who support such a house is that of *brāhmaṇas* (broad-minded) not *kṛpanas* (miserly-minded). They do not discriminate and judge people according to their body, as the *smārta-brāhmaṇas* do, thinking “This one’s body is respectable and this one’s is not.” They see “that everything is related to the Supreme Brahman, or Kṛṣṇa, and thus everything belongs to Him only.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 5.10, purp.) Thus those who protect your house do not offend any living entity, no matter how insignificant. Rather they see it as their sacred duty to protect and nurture each and every inhabitant.

Your house can accommodate everyone because there is no envy in this house. No one is thinking they are the doer and that they have to assert themselves as superior to all others. No one is therefore envious when they see someone else, even someone with an inferior body, excel, because they know that Kṛṣṇa is the ability in man and He can empower whom He pleases, not considering the material qualifications. In your house everyone is very dutiful because they know that the perfection of life, the feeling of ecstatic love for Kṛṣṇa, is the fruit of acting dutifully for the pleasure of Kṛṣṇa.

Therefore, those who are in the position of giving protection to others, whether on an international level, national level, or familial level, spare no pain to understand the concerns and needs of their charges and facilitate their spiritual growth. There is no dogma or false prestige in your house. In your house, love is the primary mover, not lust. No one is thinking to exploit another. Everyone is impelled by the desire to care for one another. In your house everyone vies with each other to show the most respect to the other. There is no pecking order, as in the material world; there are no disadvantaged people. A hierarchy of false prestige is hardly perceptible.

There is no intimidation and fear. The only fear is one born of love, the fear of not pleasing someone we dearly love. It is an ecstatic fear. Unlike the material world, the most highly placed people in your house are the most meek and humble. They are wise and discerning, but their wisdom is not dry; it is moistened with love. They discipline and keep your house pure with the charisma of their love. Their speech is sweet, their hearts are soft, they attract all the conditioned souls to them because they are attractive to both the *dhīra* and *adhīra*. In your house everyone is like a touchstone: whoever they contact becomes affected by the same sweet devotional qualities and wisdom. In this way, the boundaries of your house grow, and the material atmosphere is pushed back.

You sweated blood to build this house Śrīla Prabhupāda, because it is not easy to push back the material consciousness. You had to do so much in such a short time. Much of the time you had to work with ‘blind uncles.’ By this I understand that though the people you were engaging in your spiritual mission were not actually properly qualified with spiritual consciousness, you engaged them in pushing forward your mission anyway. Something like the surrogate mother used in experiments with monkeys. The surrogate mother is equipped with nipples which provide milk, but its body is also covered with spikes. When the baby comes to drink milk, it is also wounded with the spikes. So though we were not so qualified, you used us to give the milk of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others, despite our spikes. But you hoped that by the power of the holy name we would gradually shed our spikes and just give people the milk. You did not want us to stay blind uncles or surrogate mothers.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you were physically present, you were able to heal the wounds our spikes inflicted on others before they created schizophrenia. But now that you are physically gone, it is imperative we shed our spikes; otherwise your house may not survive. It will remain, if at all, only as a shadow of what it should be.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, despite the spikes, I am so grateful you facilitated my receiving the milk of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. But I also fear that unless we determinedly shed our spikes, we will gradually become useless in distributing the milk of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Certainly, we will not be able to distribute it as widely as you had hoped. Only the most thirsty spirit souls will risk our spikes to come and get the milk. So on this, your Centennial Vyāsa-pūjā, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with tears in my eyes I beg you to please imbue our hearts with the devotional wisdom to shed all our materialistic spikes.

Empower us to give up envy and false prestige so that we can become spiritual gems who can elevate all who come into our association. Free us of miserliness, the bodily conception, and the desire to exploit others. Imbue us with equal vision, compassion, tolerance in the execution of our duty, and above all … love. Please do not allow us to make ISKCON a shadow of the house you shed blood to build. Help us make it a vibrant, joyful, expansive, and safe house.

Your servants in Manchester, England.  
(written by Cintāmaṇi-dhāma-devī dāsī)

## Markham

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

It was September 1st, and 1896 was the year,  
One day after Janmāṣṭamī that you did appear.

You were appropriately named Abhay Charan, for it means, in complete,  
“One who is fearless, having taken shelter of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s feet.”

At the time of your birth the astrologers made a great prediction:  
At the age of seventy you would open 108 temples and be a great exponent of religion.

From the beginning you were brought up in Kṛṣṇa consciousness,  
For your father wanted you to be a devotee and ensure your spiritual success.

Even at the early age of five years old  
You began holding Ratha-yātrā just like the original.

In 1932, vou accepted initiation,  
For Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī gave you the vision.

For it was you Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa sent,  
And your life was proving it event after event.

You followed the order of your Guru Maharaja one hundred percent,  
While your Godbrothers looked on and ridiculed your intent.

But you knew You had to implement Lord Caitanya's mission,  
Giving all us neophytes a chance for salvation.

You made the long ocean passage on the *Jaladuta*  
To save us all from the grips of Kali-yuga.

As was predicted, at the age of seventy  
You took the course that would soon change history.

Arriving in Boston with nothing but Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa at your side,  
You prayed that the Supreme Lord be your guide.

With the touch of your hand and your purest endeavors,  
You began ISKCON and soon had thousands of followers.

Māyā was offering so many choices like a spinning kaleidoscope,  
But you showed us that serving Kṛṣṇa is our only real hope.

You had so many taking up Kṛṣṇa consciousness with such zeal,  
Because you were able to show us the path that was real.

You showed us how to simply transcend all the confusion  
Created so craftily by Māyā’s illusion.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, so far from your beloved Vṛndāvana did you come,  
To teach us, guide us, chastise us, and give us *prasādam.*

You taught us to distribute your transcendental books  
To every continent, country, town, village, and nook.

You taught us to spread *hari-nāma,* and with your merciful glance  
Allowed us to experience the greatest joy of *kīrtana* and dance.

You taught us how to worship the Deities so lovingly,  
Ensuring that that is what we would wish to do for eternity.

You taught us by both lectures and examples,  
And in the process opened hundreds of temples.

You taught us that serving Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is so wonderful,  
And for this we will be forever grateful.

By just giving us a drop of your mercy we are so fortunate,  
Without you we know we would still be in the claws of Māyā’s bait.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we owe everything to you.  
Without you we were lost in this material world without a clue.

Now it is 1996, your Centennial, one hundred years,  
You leave a legacy of spreading Kṛṣṇa’s name and eliminating our fears.

There is no way to really thank you for showing us the light,  
Except to humbly attempt to push your mission to new heights.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the strength  
To remain Kṛṣṇa conscious and never succumb to Māyā at any length.

Please allow us to remain serving at your lotus feet,  
And serving Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara with every heartbeat.

We bow down at your lotus feet, time and time again,  
For it is there we wish to forever remain.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Gurudeva!

Your humble servants Śubhāvilāsa dāsa, Āśālatā-devī dāsī, Mīnākṣī-devī dāsī, Indreśa dāsa, and all your faithful servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara Mandir in Markham, Canada.  
(written by Mīnākṣī-devī dāsī)

## Mauritius

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your Vyāsa-pūjā day!

For us this 1996 Vyāsa-pūjā is not an ordinary celebration, since it is the hundredth anniversary of your appearance in this material world. From 1896 to 1996—for this time the world has been blessed by your divine presence, manifest or unmanifest.

When you came to America in 1965 to reclaim the fallen souls of the Western Hemisphere, we also were touched by the cooling moonrays of your transcendental instructions. During the period of 1966–1977 you traveled practically non-stop around the world, delivering the message of “back to Godhead.” Just as the full moon moves across the night sky and sheds its cooling moonshine upon all living entities, similarly you moved across this planet with the nectarean instructions of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Somehow or other we were fortunate enough to be blessed by your presence, and by your causeless mercy we were engaged in your service to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.

We do not know for how many millions of births we had been wandering up and down in the 8,400,000 species of life. But it seems that we met Your Divine Grace by the will of providence, just so that we might accept you as the captain of the ship to cross the ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of the human beings. We also met your devotees—and tasted invaluable *sādhu-saṅga;* we accepted *mahā-prasādam*—and tasted nectar; we did some menial service—and tasted *ānanda;* we followed your instructions—and tasted the meaning of attachment to you; we chanted Hare Kṛṣṇa—and started to lose the taste for the material world.

The process you gave delivered exactly what you promised.

Now, almost nineteen years after your disappearance, we are churning the ocean of your pastimes more and more, and we are relishing increasing nectar all the time. When we watch video recordings of your pastimes we feel that you are *personally* present in the room. When we hear your tapes, we feel transferred into a temple where you are giving *Bhāgavatam* class. When we read your books, it is as if you were preaching to us face to face.

There is absolutely no doubt in our mind that you live on by your divine instructions and that anyone who follows these instructions lives with you. We have been able to realize this personally. When we are conscious of you, things go on nicely in devotional service and we feel transcendental bliss. And if we forget you, *māyā* creeps in and we feel the modes of nature taking over.

During this Centennial year we are making a special effort to remember you more, to do better, more conscious service, to become responsible and compassionate, and to be good representatives of Your Divine Grace. We especially desire that all human beings should know you and your glories. That special mercy you gave to us—we would like to give it to others. But unfortunately we do not have the *śakti* that you had.

Kindly make us your instruments in this mission. This is our only request. We hope you will grant it to us.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your aspiring servants at the ISKCON Vedic Farm in Mauritius.

## Māyāpur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your beautiful lotus feet. Thank you for allowing us to have your *darśana* at yet another—most prominent—location here in Māyāpur, your Puṣpa-samādhi Mandir.

In your bamboo-and-straw *bhajana-kuṭi* near the main gate—your first residence in Māyāpur when there were only rice fields—you give the living example of your motto for spiritual life: *simple living and high thinking*. Where your disciples could see open fields and little else, you saw where a great spiritual city would rise, manifesting the vision of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura. We bow down to you in your humble residence and pray for your blessings: May we imbibe your ways of perfect simplicity and pure thought, eagerly working to implement the glorious vision of the previous *ācāryas*.

Between two wide verandahs on the second floor of the Lotus Building, as “Vaikuṇṭha breezes” blow, you sit on a white *āsana* in your room behind your desk, illuminating the statements of *śāstra* with your Bhaktivedanta purports. It is here that you nourish disciples and guests with nectar from your lips and make transcendentally revolutionary plans for the world, beginning with plans for this holiest of *dhāmas*. Let us prostrate ourselves on the white sheeted mat before you and thank you for being so accessible to us in your conversations, lectures, and books. May we accept your words—both the soft encouraging words and the hard correcting words—as our very life and soul, again and again and again.

At the back of the temple room you sit on your *vyāsāsana*, gazing intently at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava, who gaze smilingly back at you and your followers. “There is no question of separation” between you and Kṛṣṇa, even as you seem to be some thirty meters away from the altar. In your presence it becomes clear that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead as you expand the eternal truths of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in your sublime, straightforward lectures. You cut through all possible misconceptions, making bright and clear the real path of perfection. Let us offer our worship to you daily in *guru-pūjā,* with *ārati,* flowers, *kīrtana,* and heartfelt *praṇāmas,* remembering always how you open our darkened eyes to the dazzling splendor of spiritual existence, knowledge, and bliss. May we be blessed by you to properly see and serve Their Lordships Śrī Nṛsiṁha-deva, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava and the *sakhīs*.

And now also in your imposing yet graceful Puṣpa-samādhi Mandir you sit regally in a vaulted chamber of marble and gold, bigger than life-size, with kind but determined countenance, tirelessly receiving the throngs coming for your *darśana* and blessings. Although it is not a place you sat during your manifest pastimes, we perceive your active presence here as commander of a great world mission, as you sit facing your Commanders, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura at his *samādhi* and Lord Caitanya at His birthplace to the north. It seems that here especially you supervise the grand projects of your movement, reminding us that “‘impossible’ is a word in a fool’s dictionary” and that there must be “work now, *samādhi* later.” Here you are indeed present in *samādhi*—full absorption in the transcendental activities of the Lord in His mood as the perfect devotee of the Lord. Let us prostrate ourselves before you here, offering flowers of surrender to you in your Puṣpa-samādhi, and then let us stand with folded hands awaiting your orders. May we always hear your directions clearly and carry them out with simplicity and determination, with no motivation except to please you.

In the awesome atmosphere of your Puṣpa-samādhi we are humbled. You are indeed drawing the whole world’s attention to your lotus feet, and we, your servants, find ourselves in the limelight of the glowing effulgence of your transcendental fame and glory. Please let us, shedding all pettiness of selfishness and fear, live up to the legacy of your greatness. Please allow us to “do the needful” to make of the Māyāpur project a truly spiritual city from which the whole world will take inspiration, and to which the whole world can come for an eternal festival of *nāma-saṅkīrtana*. You wrote in a *Bhāgavatam* purport (5.19.24):

“The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement has established its center in Māyāpur, the birth site of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, to give men the great opportunity to go there and perform a constant festival of *saṅkīrtana-yajña,* as recommended herein (*yajñeśa-makhā mahotsavā*) and to distribute *prasādam* to millions of hungry people hankering for spiritual emancipation. This is the mission of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.”

From your chamber here in your Puṣpa-samādhi you are exhibiting the factual activities of *samādhi,* conducting the eternal *saṅkīrtana* of Lord Caitanya. Let everyone come and receive your blessings here in your Puṣpa-samādhi Mandir, and then let us humbly help you engage all in the constant festival of *saṅkīrtana-yajña*. Offering you heaps of flowers of surrender, we beg you again and again to please let us become steadily more qualified to serve and assist you in your superexcellent service to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava.

Begging to be your humble servants, we remain  
The residents of Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma, West Bengal, India.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa-kṣetra dāsa)

## Melbourne

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

We beg for your mercy and encouragement. By following you we please you. By pleasing you we uncover an immense wealth of strength and enthusiasm. We feel happy. We are satisfied. Our minds become clear. The purpose of Kṛṣṇa consciousness becomes obvious. Problems become opportunities and life starts to make sense. We get a hint of what it might mean to be Kṛṣṇa conscious. No more hazy, fuzzy-headed confusion.

As you have described, we can become like you. Determined, confident, patient, enthusiastic, always depending on Kṛṣṇa. Always seizing an opportunity to preach. Bold but sensitive. Constantly mindful of the need to provide the chance for others to perform service to the Lord. Always aware that Kṛṣṇa is protecting us. Especially one who does His work. Prepared to work with anyone so long as he or she is prepared to share Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Happy to share the great spiritual vision you hold.

You are the ideal leader. Setting the example we all aspire to follow. Though you have moved to other realms, you are still acting just as successfully as before.

“He lives forever by his divine instructions and the follower lives with him.”

As the world *ācārya,* you travel far and wide, preaching and maintaining your charges. You collect your thoughts, rise up and then leave for future preaching fields to distribute the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. To distribute the message of the *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. Unlike the conventional *guru,* you hardly stay in one place longer than a week. However, you maintain a worldwide family of devotees through your tireless preaching. Through your books, lectures, letters, and conversations and the ever-expanding association of devotees and temples all over the world.

Where previously there was no hope, there is now life and enthusiasm. Those who seriously serve you live with you and know you. This is a miracle. In this way the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement continues and expands under your divine presence.

Please continue to shade us with your presence. Please let us live with you by simply following you in every way.

Your servants at the Mahāprabhu Mandira in Melbourne, Australia.  
(written by Aniruddha dāsa)

## Mexico City

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We are celebrating the appearance day of Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. It being the Centennial Celebration of his advent in this world, we will try to write some words of appreciation for all that he has done and is doing for us. Śrīla Prabhupāda has changed the course our lives 180 degrees. Of course, we know that whatever we write will be inadequate, because how can one express the full extent of one’s love and appreciation for someone? It is not possible. But it is especially impossible in this case, because the object of love is the wonderful transcendental personality Śrīla Prabhupāda. That transcendental personality has come from the spiritual world, and therefore his gift is transcendental, beyond the laws of material nature. What he has done for countless people in this world is reawaken them to their spiritual lives; because this gift is completely spiritual, it is not subject to the natural deterioration caused by the time factor, which exterminates everything in this mundane world.

Our homage is not some official formality; it is a heart-felt expression of gratitude for the kindness and merciful attention that His Divine Grace has shown us. This mercy of his was the crucial turning point in our very existence, the point when we turned toward real life, spiritual life, and began to awaken to our real position—servitorship to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda came to reveal to us the spiritual sky and the very root of our existence, the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and by his instructions and personal behavior he showed us the way back home, back to Godhead.

After Your Divine Grace’s visit to our ISKCON temple in Mexico City, you were so pleased with the temple and the devotees that on December 13, 1972, you wrote the following in a letter to our Godsister Candrāvalī-devī dāsī: “I was happy to see your nice temple in Mexico City, and I am always thinking of that place and all the nice devotees there.” Even though so many years have passed, we try to keep fresh in our hearts the memories of your transcendental activities and preaching in your visit to the temple in Mexico City, because that is our source of inspiration in the service to Your Divine Grace. Also that was the first time we were fortunate enough to have your transcendental *darśana*.

Your Divine Grace, you always inspired us by writing very nice encouraging letters to the Mexican *yātrā* and your faithful servants there: “I saw for myself how nice you have created the Mexico City temple of ISKCON, and I was very much inclined to those devotees. Only I had some difficulty with language difficulty to preach, but still I could see how pious they were and eager to listen anyway. That is the only qualification, if someone is eager for spiritual improvement.” (letter to Citsukhānanda dāsa, December 11, 1972) We understand it is due to your mercy only that wonderful things can take place, and we hope some day to serve you very nicely even though we have no qualification to do so. Only by your blessings can we become sincere in our desire and be able to take advantage of your unlimited mercy.

We understand that by hearing your tapes, by reading your books, and by remembering your activities and your preaching here in Mexico we will become more and more eager to serve Your Divine Grace. Two months after you blessed us with your visit, Lord Śrī Jagannātha, Śrī Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā, being pleased by the nice service to his pure devotee, came to reside in our temple. They inspired the devotees to increase book translation and book distribution and to make the Deity worship even nicer. People came forward to help, and others joined to become full-time devotees. We began cooking wonderful Sunday Feasts that every week attracted more than two hundred guests, who packed the temple. Your Divine Grace again wrote:

“The reports that have come from our Mexico City temple have always been encouraging and I am glad to learn that now you wish to increase your service still further. Kṛṣṇa is unlimited, and His devotees’ service should also be unlimited. … You have done nicely in worshiping Jagannātha, \[so\] you can order marble Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Deities.” (letter to Turya dāsa, January 27, 1973)

The Mexico temple was the only temple in Latin America that you personally blessed with Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Deities. This was due to the eagerness of the devotees and the local people to please you.

After you returned to the spiritual world, we faced so many legal problems, but by your mercy we won in court and secured the building that you liked. (“Try everything to remain in that house in Mexico City. I like that house very much.” (letter to Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami, May 28, 1975) And now Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madanagopāla have Their own temple, due to your intense desire to please Them and the sincere effort of the devotees.

A few months before you left, you blessed us (“May Lord Jagannātha bless all the devotees who try to please him by the Ratha-yātrā”) by giving us the inspiration to put on the first Ratha-yātrā festival in Guadalajara. It was by your mercy only that the local government granted the permit, which it ordinarily would not have done due to the strong Christian influence there. In this way the Ratha-yātrā car, with Lord Jagannātha, Lord Balarāma, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā seated upon it, rolled down the main streets and into the main square in Guadalajara. The festival was reported on the front pages of the main newspapers. In this way you introduced the full program of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Mexico.

We understand that you were very, very successful in your preaching everywhere because you were always thinking of the previous *ācāryas*—trying to follow them and please them. When you said that Puerto Vallarta was the most beautiful place you’d seen except for the birthplace of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, we understood that although physically far from India—somewhere on the Pacific Coast of Mexico—you were thinking about Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda and his appearance place.

You are always ready to reciprocate any service rendered to you. A nice close friend of the devotees paid the airplane tickets for you and your party when you came to Mexico City, and after you had visited the temple you visited his place at Puerto Vallarta, purifying it. You were pleased with him, and you wrote, “He is a very good soul.” And you described his place: “It is very nice place, and I want to come there for spending some time.” (letter to Citsukhānanda dāsa, August 8, 1972)

We pray on this glorious Centennial appearance day that you will please give us the strength to be able to do wonderful things in your service and again be able to send you encouraging news. We also pray that you will give us the determination to spread the chanting of the holy name—the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra—*all over the country, along with your wonderful books, and that in this way we will attract many conditioned souls to the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Your servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madanagopāla Mandir in Mexico City, Mexico.

## Monterrey

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Maṅgalācaraṇa* (Invocation)

*se maṅgalācaraṇa haya tri-vidha prakāra*  
*vastu-nirdeśa, āśīrvāda, namaskāra*

**“The invocation involves three processes: defining the objective, offering benedictions, and offering obeisances.”**

*Vastu-nirdeśa* (Defining the Objective)

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.17.27), Śrī Kṛṣṇa instructed Uddhava,

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**“One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.”**

The *Hari-bhakti-sudhodaya* (13.2) states,

*akṣṇoḥ phalaṁ tvādṛśa-darśanaṁ hi*  
*tanoḥ phalaṁ tvādṛśa-gātra-saṅgaḥ*  
*jihvā-phalaṁ tvādṛśa-kīrtanaṁ hi*  
*su-durlabhā bhāgavatā hi loke*

**“My dear Vaiṣṇava, seeing a person like you is the perfection of one’s eyesight, touching your lotus feet is the perfection of the sense of touch, and glorifying your good qualities is the tongue’s real activity, for in the material world it is very difficult to find a pure devotee of the Lord.”**

In the *Ādi Purāṇa* we find this verse:

*ye me bhakta-janāḥ pārtha na me bhaktāś ca te janāḥ*  
*mad-bhaktānāṁ ca ye bhaktās te me bhakta-tamā matāḥ*

**“My dear Pārtha, one who claims to be My devotee is not so. Only a person who claims to be a devotee of My devotee is actually My devotee.”**

The *Padma Purāṇa* says,

*ārādhanānāṁ sarveṣāṁ viṣṇor ārādhanaṁ param*  
*tasmāt parataraṁ devi tadīyānāṁ samarcanam*

**“\[Lord Śiva told goddess Durgā:\] My dear Devi, of all types of worship, worship of Lord Viṣṇu is best, but better than the worship of Lord Viṣṇu is the worship of His devotee, the Vaiṣṇava.”**

*Āśīrvāda* (Offering Benedictions)

*janame janame sabe pitā mātā pāya*  
*kṛṣṇa guru nahi mile bhaja hari ei*

**“Everyone gets a father and mother, but if one gets the benediction of Kṛṣṇa and** ***guru,*** **he can conquer material nature and return home, back to Godhead.”**

The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.18.13 and 4.30.34) states

*tulayāma lavenāpi na svargaṁ nāpunar-bhavam*  
*bhagavat-saṅgi-saṅgasya martyānāṁ kim utāśiṣaḥ*

**“The value of a moment’s association with the devotee of the Lord cannot be compared even to the attainment of the heavenly planets or liberation from matter, and what to speak of worldly benedictions in the form of material prosperity, which are for those who are meant for death.”**

**“Even a moment’s association with a pure devotee cannot be compared to being transferred to the heavenly planets or even merging into the Brahman effulgence in complete liberation. For living entities who are destined to give up the body and die, association with pure devotees is** ***the highest benediction*****.”**

In the *Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* (6.23) we find this verse:

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

*Namaskāra* (Offering Obeisances)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

*vṛndāvane ramaṇa-reti prasiddha-bhūmau*  
*tatrāpi kṛṣṇa-balarāma-supāda-mule*  
*jñānaṁ paraṁ parama-kṛṣṇa-sudharmīty uktaṁ*  
*dantas tu deva prabhupāda namo namas te*

**“In the most holy land of Vṛndāvana, in Ramaṇa Reti, at the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, you are preaching the topmost knowledge of the Supreme Personality, Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is the fountainhead of religion. O master of the senses, O my lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me offer my obeisances unto you again and again.”**

*namas te namas te kṛpā-pūrṇa-dṛṣṭi*  
*namas te namas te mahānanda-dhātrī*  
*namāmo namāmaḥ punar rakṣa rakṣa*  
*prasiddha-prabho pāhi māṁ dakṣa dakṣa*

**“I repeatedly offer my obeisances unto Śrīla Prabhupada, whose glances are full of mercy and who bestows transcendental bliss. O renowned lord, please, please protect me and kindly shower your mercy upon me again and again.”**

*bhaktisiddhānta-śiṣyāya bhaktivedānta-nāmine*  
*prasannāya praśāntāya tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the dearmost disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, your name is Bhaktivedānta, and you are always calm and joyful. I offer my obeisances unto you, who are my eternal** ***guru*****.”**

*taṁ tvā gatāhaṁ śaraṇaṁ śaraṇyaṁ*  
*sva-bhṛtya-saṁsāra-taroḥ kuṭhāram*  
*namāmi sad-dharma-vidāṁ variṣṭham*

**“O Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have taking shelter of your lotus feet because you are the only person of whom to take shelter. You are the ax that cuts down the tree of material existence for your servants. I therefore offer my obeisances unto you, who are the greatest of all transcendentalists.”**

Your servants in Monterrey, Mexico.

## Montreal

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In the First Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* there is this excellent verse:

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.”** ***(Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.22) (This verse was offered to Sūta Gosvāmī by the assembled sages in the forest of Naimiṣāraṇya).**

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are likewise the captain who have brought all of us onto the safe shelter of your great ship of mercy, your transcendental mission. This life-saving ship is propelled by the strong winds of your pure teachings, which are constantly keeping us on the sure course of the *bhakti-mārga,* pushing us relentlessly on through the raging storms of Kali-yuga.

This great ship of mercy has unlimited room to offer shelter to all mankind. By your expert stewardship, you have chartered the treacherous ocean of Kali and have thus cleared the way to allow all sincere souls to be elevated to the secure vision of life’s *paraṁ-gati,* the supreme destination in the safety of Lord Caitanya’s lotus feet.

This is the miracle of your mercy. Thus there can be no doubt that you are that rarest of intimate servants of Śrī Caitanyadeva. By His perfect desire, He has empowered you as His mercy incarnation to fulfill His munificent will of benedicting suffering mankind with the highest fortune, *kṛṣṇa-prema*. Such is the immeasurable wealth of your legacy, and also the scope of our debt to you. Although this debt will remain eternally unpaid by us, at least we can offer you our lives, our sincerest gratitude, and our constant fidelity.

Let us always remember your magnanimous gifts to us all, by which our shipwrecked lives have been saved forever. Let us recall these very inspiring words of solace you gave us in the introduction to *Songs of the Vaiṣṇava Ācāryas:* “To save ourselves from Māyā’s great power, we must always merge in the transcendental mellow of *kīrtana-rasa,* for *kīrtana-rasa* is the safest situation within this material world.”

You also once wrote, “*Kīrtana* is the fuel that drives our spiritual life forward.” Among all your matchless gifts, all impregnated with your limitless *kṛṣṇa-śakti, kīrtana* is the most potent and life-transforming. You brought us deep into the boundless ocean of *kīrtana-rasa* and allowed us to bathe our weary minds and hearts in the rejuvenating nectar of *ānandāmbudhi-vardhanam,* the ever-expanding ocean of Kṛṣṇa’s nectarean splendor. *Kīrtana* includes the glorification of the Lord’s pure devotees, which also becomes *śabda-brahma,* pure transcendental sound.

In our imperfect attempt to celebrate the Centennial of your glorious appearance in this world, we want to totally immerse ourselves in the *kīrtana* of your glories and continue to relish the *paraṁ dṛṣṭvā,* the nectarean ocean of your pastimes. This remembrance of your greatness allows us a further glimpse your pure soul’s spiritual beauty and opulence. In that vision, however incomplete, we are propelled to the highest spiritual platform, where we can, through your greatness, gain a taste of our eternal soul’s loving relationship with Kṛṣṇa.

That supreme happiness, your gift to this world bereft of love, is available only by attaining the service of your lotus feet. Thank you again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving our lives their real meaning by allowing us whatever service we can offer at your lotus feet.

Your unworthy servants in Montreal, Canada.  
(written by Gokulānanda dāsa)

## Moscow

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

This year, because of the special effort of your dear disciples and dedicated servants, the people of this world are becoming more and more aware of your continuous presence on this planet. Your presence in your ISKCON society, your presence in your wonderful books, and your presence in the hearts of thousands of Vaiṣṇava preachers is increasingly experienced by an ever-growing number of men and women who are searching for transcendental peace. This is the year of your Centennial celebrations, the year of massive spiritual education, when people are receiving the amazing news of your selfless lifelong struggle to give them the message of Godhead. A message that nobody wanted and a message that everybody was hankering for the most. Please allow us to quote the following passage from your *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 25.168–172):

**TRANSLATION:** Among His own associates Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu laughingly said, “I came here to sell My emotional ecstatic love. Although I came to Vārāṇasī to sell My goods, there were no customers, and it appeared necessary for Me to carry them back to My own country. All of you were feeling unhappy that no one was purchasing My goods and that I would have to carry them away. Therefore, by your will only, I have distributed them without charging.”

**PURPORT:** When we began distributing the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in the Western countries, a similar thing happened. In the beginning we were very much disappointed for at least one year because no one came forth to help this movement, but by the grace of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, some young boys joined this movement in 1966. Of course we distributed Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s message of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* without bargaining or selling. As a result, this movement has spread all over the world, with the assistance of European and American boys and girls. We therefore pray for all the blessings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu upon all the devotees in the Western world who are spreading this movement.

**TRANSLATION:** All the devotees of the Lord said, “You have incarnated to deliver the fallen souls. You have delivered them in the east and in the south, and now You are delivering them in the west. Only Vārāṇasī was left because the people there were against Your missionary activities. Now You have delivered them and we are all very happy.”

In June 1971 you came to Moscow. Only Russia was left because the people there were against your missionary activities. You stayed in a hotel very close to Red Square. There is a photograph that shows you from behind, walking down a paved road. A humble *sādhu,* walking with a cane on the right side of the road in the early morning hours. You are the only person in this photograph. To your left across the street is the Museum of Russian History. On your right is one of the corner towers of the Kremlin’s boundary wall. Ahead of you, on the far side of Red Square, is the silhouette of St. Basil’s Cathedral, and to the right of the cathedral but a little closer to you is the Lenin’s mausoleum. It is a very peaceful picture, inspiring us to meditate thus:

You came to Moscow just one year after Lenin’s Centennial celebration. He had been born on April 22, 1870. Since 1921 his embalmed body has been on display in a gas-filled glass box in his mausoleum. Up until very recently, students in all Russian schools would offer *pūjā* and flowers to Lenin’s monuments and glorify him with selected hymns and verses. What is left of this glory now? The monuments are crumbling. The materialistic way of life in this country has been doomed since the very moment you placed your foot on the pavement of Red Square. A humble  *sādhu …*

There is another photo, well known to all. Wrapped in chaddar, you are walking past St. Basil’s Cathedral, keeping it on your left side. Your neck is bent slightly back, your head upright. You look confident, determined, and aristocratic. Like a conqueror. It seems as if your are looking into the future. We don’t know what you were seeing or what you were thinking. Perhaps you remembered Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s vision? You must have thought of how to help the people in this country, help them mitigate their unlimited sufferings in their struggle for material existence. Again you proved the words of the *Bhāgavatam* to be true, *tulayāma lavenāpi na svargaṁ nāpunar-bhavam / bhagavat-saṅgi-saṅgasya martyānāṁ kim utāśiṣaḥ.* You didn’t spend much time in Moscow, nor did you meet with so many people. But the lives of some of those whom you met changed permanently. As a result of your short visit, now, twenty-five years later, Kṛṣṇa consciousness has developed into a large, well-known spiritual movement in Russia. You wrote,

“People from all parts of the world must call for the devotees of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who are compared to swans swimming around the beautiful lotus feet of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu or bees humming around His lotus feet in transcendental pleasure, searching for honey. The dryness of material happiness cannot be moistened by so-called philosophers who cry for Brahman, liberation, and similar dry speculative objects. The urge of the soul proper is different. The soul can be solaced only by the mercy of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His many bona fide devotees, who never leave the lotus feet of the Lord to become imitation Mahāprabhus but all cling to His lotus feet like bees that never leave a honey-soaked lotus flower.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are certainly the most wonderful of all swans who took shelter at the beautiful lotus feet of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. We are daily admiring and repeating the words of the poetry of your Bhaktivedānta Purports, which are spiritual manifestations of your transcendental ecstatic emotions.

“I came here to sell My emotional ecstatic love.”

Your humble servants in the Begovaya Temple in Moscow, Russia.

## Murāri Sevaka

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

**“When will Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, who has established within this material world the mission to fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya, give me shelter under his lotus feet?”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I am thankful for this occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā. In order to glorify you even more on this Centennial day, all the devotees and members offer you these prayers. I am most grateful to you for teaching me to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra*. Without these holy names of Kṛṣṇa no one in this material world can survive. Without my Godbrothers to remind me of you I would be lost; without your books I would be dumb; without the Hare Kṛṣṇa *kīrtana* I would be deaf; without Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Nitāi Gauracandra I would be blind; without your temples I would be lame. Without you I would have no spiritual master. Without ISKCON the world is lost.

Jvarajit dāsa

\* \* \*

The Jewels in Lord Kṛṣṇa’s crown are His glorious pure devotees, of which His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda shines brilliantly, a beacon of hopeful and strong light in this current age of hypocrisy and quarrel. Thank you, my dearmost spiritual grandfather, for giving devotional service most abundantly and completely and for teaching us by example, showing all that by their own desire they may follow in your footsteps back to home, back to Godhead. By your special mercy, lovingly and completely bestowed upon me, will I ever hope to become a surrendered and fixed-up devotee so that I may reflect the magnitude of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy and somehow begin to repay my awesome debt to you? Bowing at your luminous lotus feet, joyous with the unlimited nectar, and armored with the higher taste, I beg to remain yours in service.

Yogarūpinī-devī dāsī

\* \* \*

I am extremely grateful to Śrīla Prabhupāda because he is giving me my life. I didn’t know who I was, nor did I know if there is a personal God—who He is or whether I could know Him or even how to know Him. I didn’t know why I was put in this world, nor why it is here, nor why it is working the way it is, nor what I should do or not do, nor how to do anything properly. Śrīla Prabhupāda is teaching me the value of this life, how insignificant is material life, how rare and valuable is this human birth, and what we can attain. Śrīla Prabhupāda is teaching me, for the first time in hundreds or thousands or millions of years or more, what is life itself and so-called death, what is reality and illusion and their various manifestations, what and who is God, His names and Their glories, His nature and qualities, His personalities and activities, His abodes, the manifestations, interactions and depth of His love and the varieties and ecstasy of His service. Śrīla Prabhupāda is the only one in at least several billion people who cares enough and is qualified enough to deliver so many wayward souls from such ignorance as we are covered by. For allowing me to know, realize, and ultimately attain such levels of God consciousness as he is presenting, I owe him my life, my eternity.

Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, out of His kindness, has given us our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda. And Śrīla Prabhupāda, out of his kindness, is giving us Kṛṣṇa.

*Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, Prabhu Nityānanda, Śrī Advaita, Gadādhara, Śrīvāsādi-gaura-bhakta-vṛnda. Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

Your servant,  
Nirguṇa dāsa

\* \* \*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so merciful that you left your peaceful home in Vṛndāvana to travel the turbulent Atlantic to preach in an even more turbulent America full of sin and hate. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving the name Murāri Sevaka to the land in Tennessee, thus blessing this land and making it into a transcendental abode for the devotees to prepare for the arrival of the presiding Deities, Nitāi-Gauracandra, who came to this holy place just after your departure from this world.

Thank you for your translations of *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* which you presented so brilliantly that even the nonscholarly commoners can read them and be absorbed in the pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Gaurāṅga.

Thank you for establishing the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness and setting up your senior disciples into the management positions of the GBC. Concerned for all the living entities of this planet for thousands of years to come, you carefully crafted your plan to propagate Kṛṣṇa Consciousness and give shelter to the troubled souls of this planet, and thus your ISKCON will continue generation after generation, as long as the devotees work in cooperation and harmony and follow the instructions of your books.

Thank you Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Jitakrodha dāsa

## Nava-jiyaḍa-nṛsiṁha-kṣetra

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances on this most auspicious day of your appearance

First we would like to thank you for the opportunity to take part in this celebration of the hundredth anniversary of your appearance, which is surely being celebrated throughout the whole universe. We feel the deepest gratitude to you for coming to this decadent Western world. If you had not traveled to America out of compassion, following your spiritual master’s instructions to make the people there Kṛṣṇa conscious, what would have been our fate?

Although you had almost no material means and did not know if you would find food to prepare *prasādam* (you took an extra bag of grains with you), and although your body was no longer young and several of your Godbrothers had had to return to India unsuccessful—still you went to America, with a ticket you practically got by begging. Who else had ever shown so much courage and renunciation?

In New York City, the first young people became attracted by your love and devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa and started to help you. From these humble beginnings you developed your society into a worldwide movement in such a short time! Your way of acting reminds us very much of the appearance of Śrī Matsyāvatāra. As a tiny fish He appeared before Mahārāja Satyavrata and asked to be saved from the big fish. The king happily fulfilled this desire and took the small fish to his hut in a waterpot. Overnight the fish grew too big for the waterpot and asked to be put into a bigger container. The king agreed and put Him in a big well. But soon that also became too small, so Mahārāja Satyavrata put the fish in a lake, but soon that also was too small. Matsyāvatāra quickly grew bigger and bigger, filling up all the bodies of water the king put Him in. When He occupied the huge ocean, the great devotee Satyavrata realized Him to be the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

ISKCON is also steadily increasing, and the devotees are taking on bigger and bigger tasks. One day the whole world will realize the true nature of your movement. Your only desire, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that all living entities finally listen to the instructions of Lord Kṛṣṇa so that they will become free from all suffering and reawaken their love for Him.

Please engage us also in your merciful mission and give us the strength to perform loving devotional service.

Your humble servants at the Nava-jiyaḍa-nṛsiṁha-kṣetra farm community in Germany.

## New Dvārakā

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

Since your last visit to Los Angeles, in June of 1976, this once-pleasant city has become a pandemonium plagued by overpopulation, unemployment, increased crime and violence, and the threat of a devastating earthquake. Consequently, many people have moved out of Los Angeles to reside in nicer places. We, however, feel most fortunate to be residents of New Dvārakā because you blessed this city by spending a total of two years here and making it ISKCON’s first world headquarters. Los Angeles is filled with places of your divine pastimes, and those places are sanctified by your purity. Thus, as we perform our service in New Dvārakā, we have the great good fortune of being constantly reminded of you.

New Dvārakā was the first property purchased by ISKCON. It was so important to you that you asked other temples to donate funds for the down payment. You established New Dvārakā as the world headquarters to set the standard for all other temples to follow. Thus you stayed here for over seven months in 1970 and personally supervised the Deity worship, the construction of the altars, the placement of the vyāsāsana, etc. You even chose the colors for the temple and Deity rooms. You also developed the *saṅkīrtana* party, established the proper procedures for the *Bhāgavatam* class, and introduced the chanting of the prayers to Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva for the protection of ISKCON. You then invited ISKCON leaders from around the world to come and study the standards set in New Dvārakā and so they could emulate them in their respective temples. You created such an enlivening atmosphere that any devotee who visited New Dvārakā became completely enthused in devotional service. In May of 1970 you wrote:

“Now at the present moment I am concentrating my energy in this Los Angeles center as ideal for all other centers in respect of Deity worship, Arotrik, Kirtan and other necessary paraphernalia. As I have curtailed my moving program, I wish that you may come here at your convenience and stay a few days and see personally how things are going on; and by meeting with me personally for all necessary instruction, I hope simultaneously in all Centers the activities will be of the same standard.”

It was here that big book distribution first exploded in America due to your inspiring words to the New Dvārakā *saṅkīrtana* party. With the help of the new-*bhakta* program, which also began here, by 1975 we had so many devotees that every terminal of the Los Angeles airport had devotees distributing your books from 9:00 a.m. to 9:00 p.m. And it was the New Dvārakā devotees who traveled to other temples to train others in the art of book distribution.

“I am very pleased with the distribution that the New Dvaraka temple is doing. When I hear of your advancement of devotional service by book distribution, it invigorates me although I am an old man. Although I am traveling all over the world, my mind is always in Los Angeles.” (letter, November 20, 1975)

It was also here that your Bhaktivedanta Book Trust was finally able to catch up with your translation work by producing seventeen volumes of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in two months. Your desire for “living books” in the form of diorama exhibitions also became a reality in Los Angeles through the First American Theistic Exhibit (F.A.T.E). And it was here on your morning walks that the seeds of the Bhaktivedanta Institute were sown through your regular attacks against materialistic scientists, thus inspiring a scientific revolution and also giving the world the book *Life Comes from Life*.

Through your inspiration, New Dvārakā became the center for audio-visual projects. Kṛṣṇa conscious films were produced, ISKCON Television (ITV) was begun, your *bhajans* were professionally recorded, your tape ministry was established, and the “Kṛṣṇa” radio show was developed and distributed throughout America.

To help support the *gṛhasthas* and fund temples, you gave the idea for an incense business. Inspired by your vision, the Spiritual Sky incense company soon monopolized the incense market and grew into a multi-million dollar business. Thus you relieved the financial anxieties of the temple and the *gṛhasthas*.

Since your wonderful pastimes pervade every corner of the temple, we cannot be in New Dvārakā without thinking of you. For example, when we walk down the temple alley, we recall how the devotees would affectionately greet you every morning as you entered your car to go on your walk and how they would run down the alley to follow you as you left.

The area in front of the temple reminds us of how in the early 70’s the devotees would spontaneously line up as you walked from the street to the temple to greet the Deities. At this time, devotees would offer you flowers and you would reciprocate by handing a flower to each of them. This soon became a daily ritual of loving exchange.

Your garden reminds us of you because you loved to spend time there. It was the scene of many enlivening conversations with important guests. Often you would challenge professors to accept Kṛṣṇa consciousness and help you spread the movement. And every evening you would sit almost motionless, listening in rapt attention to a *Kṛṣṇa* book reading.

The front lawn reminds us of you because whenever the grass was turning yellow you would say, “Why don’t you water the grass?” The magnolia and eucalyptus trees around the temple are also there to remind us of you. You enjoyed receiving magnolia flowers from devotees who would climb high in the trees to pick them. And every day a devotee would being you eucalyptus twigs that you would use to brush your teeth.

And of course your original rooms are here; your *darśana* room, your bedroom, and your study, where you kept your Sanskrit *Bhāgavatam* volumes with the commentaries of the *ācāryas*. This was one of your favorite places to do translation work, the place you felt was your home. It was here that you began and finished *The Nectar of Devotion*.

“Again, I am so much burdened by this administrative work that I feel great difficulty. I was very anxious to return to my Los Angeles home to sit down for translating work.” (Letter, April 4, 1974)

You spent endless hours in your *darśana* room instructing ISKCON leaders, sharing intimate moments with devotees, and preaching to guests. I remember asking a *sannyāsī* who had just come down from your rooms what you said. He replied that you had been explaining how model ships are built inside bottles. After another *darśana* a devotee told me that you said we are all philosophers and should always be talking philosophy. We were always anxious to extract the nectar from devotees who had your *darśana*. Thus your rooms became like the ocean of milk where the nectar of *kṛṣṇa-kathā* was constantly being churned.

The temple room reminds us of you because you gave hundreds of wonderful classes here to full-capacity audiences. When you would chant “Jaya Rādhā-Mādhava,” all the devotees would gradually stand up and dance. Your powerful presentation of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* inspired this community to grow to over 350 devotees during your lifetime. And after every class there would be a *kīrtana* in which you would lift the devotees off the ground simply by nodding your head and smiling.

You also visited many places in the city outside the temple compound, places that remind us of you today. For example, the airport was the scene of many ecstatic *kīrtanas* heralding your arrival in Los Angeles. Hundreds of exuberant devotees would greet you with tears in their eyes, their hearts melted by that special Vaikuṇṭha smile you reserved for such occasions. With flower petals strewn at your feet as you walked, regal umbrella held over your head, onlookers amazed and confused, devotees dancing and jumping wildly, the Los Angeles airport was immediately transformed into the spiritual world by your presence. The book distributors feel blessed to be able to distribute your books daily in the very place you walked and thus turned into a holy *tīrtha*.

Even driving in Los Angeles reminds us of you. When we go down Venice Boulevard we remember that this is the same route you took when you drove to the beach for your walks. We often pass Cheviot Hills Park and think of the many morning walk conversations you had there that we can now read in *Life Comes from Life*. When I was recently driving on Hollywood Boulevard, I recalled the time you came there one evening in December of 1969 just to personally see the Saturday night *harināma* party.

We no longer perform *harināma-saṅkīrtana* in Hollywood but instead go to the Santa Monica Promenade. We are so fortunate to go there because just a few blocks away is the place you favored most for your morning walks, the park above the Pacific Coast Highway. We also have a regular *harināma* program in Venice Beach. We are especially blessed to be at this spot because you also took your morning walks at this beach. In fact we even meet people who remember seeing you there.

Even the Los Angeles weather reminds us of you because you liked it. You said it reminded you of the weather in India.

Your presence strongly remains here because wherever a pure devotee has spent so much time, he continues to live. Anyone who offers obeisances to you here will certainly get your mercy. We admit that whatever success we have had is due to your presence here, due to what you set in motion when you first visited Los Angeles in 1967.

On this auspicious occasion of your one hundredth anniversary, we pray that we can preserve what you developed by always making New Dvārakā the standard in all aspects of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and the place where new and dynamic Kṛṣṇa conscious projects continually take birth.

Your servants,  
The residents of the New Dvārakā community in Los Angeles, California, USA.  
(written by Mahātmā dāsa)

## New Gokula (Millfield)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this momentous day, the incomparable magnitude and nature of the contribution you have made to the world is being embraced and appreciated by all but the most unfortunate. From a historical and scholastic point of view, the achievements of your life are unique and wonderful and will only become more so with the passing of time. From the grateful, devoted viewpoint of those whose lives you have touched, changed, and delivered, your presence and accomplishments will always stand alone, as a buoy of rare divinity atop the ocean of material greatness.

Here at the New Gokula Farm we see your divine self and your mission of liberally spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness as the mercy of which we are made. You have not only delivered Kṛṣṇa freely to the contaminated masses, but you have transported, marketed, and “merchandised” Kṛṣṇa in such an expertly dynamic way that no one can avoid the transcendental taste of devotional service. Your presentation of the Vedic literatures has forced us to see reason; your distribution of the holy name has allowed us real happiness; your endless preaching has created our good fortune; and by your personal example you have taught us the security of surrender.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, to appreciate you is not a difficult thing. You have constantly and unerringly given of yourself to create a life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness for others and a future for the whole world.

Your devotees at the New Gokula community in Millfield, Australia.

## New Gokula (Vancouver)

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of all fallen souls, whose mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your mercy the holy name is being chanted throughout the world. Some thought that the statement of Caitanya Mahāprabhu that the holy name would be heard and chanted in every town and village was a poetic overstatement meant to encourage devotees only. Some unfortunate souls envious of your success even had the audacity to say that if Lord Caitanya wanted Kṛṣṇa consciousness spread in the Western world He would have done it Himself.

The truth is that the Lord actually takes pleasure in seeing His devotee take a higher position than that of the Lord Himself. Kṛṣṇa wanted Arjuna to emerge victorious in the battle of Kurukṣetra, although the outcome of the war was already destined by the Lord’s will. Lord Caitanya similarly wanted you to take the credit for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your monumental preaching achievements speak for themselves. You are undoubtedly a most intimate and empowered confidential associate of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Your ability to inspire conditioned souls to take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the greatest mysticism. Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, is personally revealed to one who hears your words. You enable even the most stone-hearted, unqualified conditioned souls to directly experience God in person. Mental speculators and *yogīs* are unable to approach even the perimeter of this realization, even after hundreds of thousands of years of arduous endeavor. By your grace even a child can understand Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. This inconceivable potency of contact with Your Divine Grace is the greatest fortune. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* speaks of such association:

*tulayāma lavenāpi na svargaṁ nāpunar-bhavam*  
*bhagavat-saṅgi-saṅgasya martyānāṁ kim utāśiṣaḥ*

**“The value of a moment’s association with the devotee of the Lord cannot even be compared to the attainment of heavenly planets or liberation from matter, and what to speak of worldly benedictions in the form of material prosperity, which are for those who are meant for death.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.18.13)**

Lord Caitanya said to Sanātana Gosvāmī,

*‘sādhu-saṅga’, ’sādhu-saṅga’—sarva-śāstre kaya*  
*lava-mātra sādhu-saṅge sarva-siddhi haya*

**“The verdict of all revealed scriptures is that by even a moment’s association with a pure devotee, one can attain all success.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **22.54)**

You have changed the hearts of countless souls, turning them away from *māyā* toward the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. The powerful material tide is being reversed by Your Diving Grace.

The most difficult and severe *yoga āsana* is easily accomplished by those who simply submissively hear from you—that is the *daṇḍavat āsana*. The rigid encrustation of false ego acquired over countless births of the material sojourn dissipates almost to nil for those who hear your nectarean descriptions of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and engage in your service.

*prasaṅgam ajaraṁ pāśam ātmanaḥ kavayo viduḥ*  
*sa eva sādhuṣu kṛto mokṣa-dvāram apāvṛtam*

**“Every learned may knows very well that attachment for the material is the greatest entanglement of the spirit soul. But that same attachment, when applied to the self-realized devotees, opens the door of liberation.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **3.25.20)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda your association is the most valuable and worshipable commodity in this creation.

Let your glorious and wondrous activities be broadcast throughout the world. Only the most rigid steel-framed heart would not melt in loving devotion upon hearing of your transcendental *līlā*. Your pastimes are an ambrosial nectar that increases in mellow the more they are churned by hearing and recitation. The more the world comes to hear of you, the more it will become attracted to you. It is an irreversible process. Although out of infinite humility you insist that no credit is yours, we experience directly that Kṛṣṇa wants his pure devotee to be glorified and known throughout the world. This is our life’s purpose.

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, we pray to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madanamohana to give us the ability to deliver your association to all conditioned souls.

Praying to remain eternally in your service,  
The devotees of New Gokula Dhāma in Vancouver, Canada.

## New Goloka

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious Centennial anniversary of your appearance.

This year all over the world devotees have been celebrating your Centennial anniversary in so many wonderful ways. All of our ISKCON publications have encouraged so many people to take part in serving your *saṅkīrtana* movement. In many parts of the world grand, magnificent temples have been opened in your honor. So many festivals are being held all over the country to engage people in dancing and chanting the holy name of the Lord, which is your heart’s desire.

While thinking about writing this offering, I began to meditate on what we can offer you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Here we are in Hillsborough, North Carolina, a very remote and insignificant part of the world. What can we present to you this year? We haven’t built a new temple or done anything that externally might seem so extraordinary. Our offering to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, from New Goloka Mandira, is to rededicate ourselves to follow very strictly the guidelines that you have set down for one’s advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, as well as the preaching and spreading of this *saṅkīrtana* movement. In all the different aspects of our temple we want to imbibe your mood, fulfill your desires, and bring you pleasure by our activities. We want to glorify you in such a way that when people come to the temple or meet a devotee on *saṅkīrtana* they are attracted to your lotus feet.

Something very wonderful is happening here by your causeless mercy. We see a whole new generation of boys and girls coming to join this movement. Some have parents who are devotees, and some are fresh out of the material world, but we see a whole new generation stepping forward with enthusiasm and determination to serve you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The devotees are eagerly learning how to cook for the Deities in the way that you instructed us—in a way that is pleasing to Kṛṣṇa. The devotees are enthusiastically cooking and distributing *prasādam* at the universities. It is said that you live on in your books, and this we are practically seeing as a whole new generation of devotees are aspiring to please you by going out and distributing your books. We are also seeing your older disciples coming back to assist and serve you. Let us all unite together, disciples and granddisciples, to carry on your transcendental movement. Let us give up our envy and petty differences, which stand as obstacles on the path of pure devotional service. If we all carefully read your books and follow your instructions, we will realize that the only solution to the material problems are your instructions. Here at New Goloka, under the shelter of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Golokānanda and your dedicated disciples, we aspire to be your eternal servants.

Your disciples and granddisciples at New Goloka Dhāma, in Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa-priyā-devī dāsī)

## New Govardhana (Khasadeśa)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble, respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your holy appearance day! All glories to the messages of Kṛṣṇa consciousness brought by you to deliver the whole world!

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-*  
*trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa-guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.” (*****Gurv-aṣṭaka*** **1)**

One hundred years ago, by the will of the Lord Kṛṣṇa, you descended from the spiritual abode to this world, a place full of hypocrisy and quarrels, to deliver the living entities afflicted by material duality.

*janasya kṛṣṇād vimukhasya daivād*  
*adharma-śīlasya suduḥkhitasya*  
*anugrahāyeha caranti nūnaṁ*  
*bhūtāni bhavyāni janārdanasya*

**“O my lord, great philanthropic souls travel on the earth on behalf of the Supreme Personality of Godhead to show compassion to the fallen souls who are averse to the sense of subordination to the Lord.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **3.5.3)**

The wind may carry dust or fragrance, but the wind has nothing to do with these things. Similarly, you came to this world to revive the dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness within the hearts of the living entities, but you actually had nothing to do with this mundane world. Your only purpose in coming here was to teach us by example and precept how to love Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Your whole life is just like a light tower, guiding the ignorant living entities so that they may know how to live their lives and how to transcend birth and death.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, one who knows your activities can be inspired to go back home, back to Godhead. But how can we know you? When Lord Kṛṣṇa came, fools derided Him, not knowing His supreme nature. So what ordinary men can know of you. You were so humble. You did not manifest any mystic power like summoning the wind or the rain or foretelling the future. Neither did you create any philosophy of your own: you simply repeated the words of your spiritual master. Nevertheless, what you have given to this world is not at all ordinary. The books you compiled are many. The philosophy they contain is profound yet easily understood and readily accepted by all. You have established ISKCON, a huge organization. You have transformed so many fallen souls into pure devotees. All these acts are incredible.

We are unfortunate because we cannot physically associate with you and learn from you. For us, you are like the Supreme Lord Himself, who seems to be both nearby and far away. Still, we can associate with you through your videos, audio tapes, and books, as well as through your dear son, our spiritual master, Tamāl Krishna Goswami. In this way we can relish a little bit of the nectar of your pastimes. We are like someone dying of thirst: though we may hanker to drink up the whole ocean, a few drops of water are enough to give us unlimited joy. Spiritually speaking, we are all withering. Therefore, a few drops of the nectar of your pastimes can make us feel completely happy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are as worshipable as the Lord, of whom you are the dearest servant. You kept spreading the glories and messages of the Lord while undergoing so many difficulties. So who can be dearer to the Lord than you?

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari (Kṛṣṇa).” (*****Gurv-aṣṭaka*** **7)**

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Who would say that you have not come to us here in Khasadeśa? You have. You are with us now and will be with us in the future. You are always with us. Yes, you are with us in the form of your books. Who would say that you are different from your books? Through your books we can have the authoritative instructions of *guru, sādhu,* and *śāstra*. Who would say that you have left us and are no longer guiding us? You are still guiding, maintaining, and protecting us. You have constructed a house that is big enough to accommodate the whole world.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to shower your mercy upon us and place us at your lotus feet.

*rahūgaṇaitat tapasā na yāti*  
*na cejyayā nirvapaṇād gṛhād vā*  
*na cchandasā naiva jalāgni-sūryair*  
*vinā mahat-pāda-rajo-’bhiṣekam*

**“My dear King Rahūgaṇa, unless one has the opportunity to smear his entire body with the dust of the lotus feet of great devotees, one cannot realize the Absolute Truth. One cannot realize the Absolute Truth simply by observing celibacy \[*****brahmacarya*****\], strictly following the rules and regulations of householder life, leaving home as a** ***vānaprastha,*** **accepting** ***sannyāsa,*** **or undergoing severe penances in winter by keeping oneself submerged in water or surrounding oneself in summer by fire and the scorching heat of the sun. There are many other processes to understand the Absolute Truth, but the Absolute Truth is only revealed to one who has attained the mercy of a great devotee.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **5.12.12)**

However, in order to gain your mercy, we have to distribute your mercy, i.e., your books. The more we distribute your books, the more we will gain your mercy. The more mercy we gain, the more books we can distribute. In this way, the whole world will be filled up with your mercy and your books. We are praying that we can be a small wave in the torrents of this *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Distributing Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books *kī jaya*!  
Distributing Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books *kī jaya*!  
Distributing Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books *kī jaya*!

Your grandchildren in New Govardhana, Khasadeśa.  
(written by Nitāi-caraṇa dāsa)

## New Govardhana (Murwillumbah)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Each year at this time we tend to stop and reflect on our status and endeavors in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and by your grace we become increasingly aware of the need to apply ourselves in earnest in devotional service. One day this life will be over, and the true value of our application in our service to you will be put to the test. We pray that we may pass the test and that we will have spent a life sincerely dedicated somehow or other to service in your mission, which will help carry us forward to the ultimate goal—to be everlastingly situated in service to the Supreme Lord and His devotees.

The source of our greatest inspiration is your tireless dedication and heroism in the establishment and expansion of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. We become awed by your wonderfully deep qualities of compassion, love, forbearance, and determination, and in comparison we feel so insignificant.
