The outstanding quality of your devotion is selflessness, and how much we as disciples can make progress in this quality will greatly determine our individual and collective success in our attempts to serve you. As you indicated, “Vaiṣṇava” means desireless—in other words a true devotee has no interest separate from those of Lord Caitanya, and he can therefore be truly successful in his devotional attempts. We pray that by continuously trying to assist you in whatever way we can, we may imbibe some of your qualities and so become genuine and worthy “Prabhupādānugas.” We further pray that we may maintain solid footing in service to you.

Most importantly you stressed to us the need to overcome our personal differences and thus unite our energies in the *saṅkīrtana* mood, and so you declared that our love for you would be shown by how much we co-operated to push on this movement in your absence. If we can imprint this instruction upon our hearts and hold it dear, coupled with the necklace of *tṛṇād api sunīcena taror iva sahiṣṇunā / amāninā mānadena kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ,* surely we will qualify ourselves for your mercy, without which we will find it difficult to make actual progress.

Please bless us that we will not be complacent or miserly by being satisfied with a pleasant life without due regard for the urgency of the task at hand, which you have duly given us. You have made it absolutely clear that there is really no other hope for the world but the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we owe a great debt to you, and we hope that we may be able to apply ourselves with on-going energy and enthusiasm to thus qualify ourselves as worthy recipients of your mercy.

Your humble servants at the New Govardhana farm community, Murwillumbah, Australia.

## New Govardhana (San Diego)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

This year, as we celebrate the centennial anniversary of your appearance, your servants worldwide have resolved to offer wonderful projects to glorify Your Divine Grace. We in San Diego are also making special efforts to increase our service to you this year. It seems important, though, that while we make so many outward arrangements we should focus on internal offerings as well, meant for refining our consciousness, cleansing our hearts. This may ultimately make the other offerings acceptable. In *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.26), Lord Kṛṣṇa tells us

*patraṁ puṣpaṁ phalaṁ toyaṁ yo me bhaktyā prayacchati*  
*tad ahaṁ bhakty-upahṛtaṁ aśnāmi prayatātmanaḥ*

**“If one offers Me with love and devotion a leaf, a flower, a fruit, or water, I will accept it.”**

Your purport explains that pleasing Kṛṣṇa “is very easy and can be attempted even by the poorest of the poor, without any kind of qualification.” That certainly describes us denizens of Kali-yuga; our only asset, our only qualification, is your causeless mercy. You further explain that Kṛṣṇa “is not in need of anything from anyone, since He is self-sufficient, and yet He accepts the offering of His devotee in an exchange of love and affection.” So it seems that we can best ensure that you will find our offerings acceptable if we make them in this spirit of love. And at the end of your manifest pastimes you told us that we could best demonstrate our love for you by cooperating to maintain ISKCON and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. So perhaps our first internal offering should be to develop this cooperative spirit, individually and collectively.

From the very beginning of your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* purports, you condemn the competitive spirit. In explaining the *Bhāgavatam’s* second verse, you tell us that the endeavors for *dharma, artha, kāma,* and even *mokṣa* are driven by the desire for sense gratification, for which we compete with each other. The Lord’s devotees, on the other hand, rise above all such competition because “they are nonenvious and pure in heart”; moreover, you explain, “the transcendental devotees of the Lord are not only free from material envy but are well-wishers to everyone, and they strive to establish a competitionless society with God at the center.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have introduced such a competitionless society in the form of your International Society for Krishna Consciousness. You have repeatedly instructed your disciples that cooperation and appreciation are the essential principles of Vaiṣṇava relationships and necessary ingredients for our success. “Our society is like one big family,” you once wrote Upendra, “and our relationships should be based on love and trust.” And to Kṛṣṇāveśa dāsī you wrote, “Our movement is based on love and trust, so if we do not cooperate, then how is that love and trust?” You told Mādhavānanda that we should work for Kṛṣṇa “without being envious of others. … In material life there is simply envy of another’s progress, but in spiritual life one encourages another. ‘Oh! You are doing very nice.’ That is Rādhāraṇī. She says, ‘Oh! here is a very nice devotee. Please, Kṛṣṇa, you accept him.’” And you wrote Rāmeśvara that, rather than the malicious competition of the material world, “in the spiritual world there is appreciation: ‘He has done so nicely, I could not do so well.’”

This is not idle, utopian sentiment. We can understand that material competition grows from envy based on the bodily concept of life. As long as we remain attached to any designations based on the temporary material body and mind, we exploit and envy others. But devotional service to Kṛṣṇa frees us from attachment to such designations, as we hear from the *Nārada Pañcarātra*: *sarvopādhi-vinirmuktam*. And you have taught us that “since we are pledged to work for Kṛṣṇa, we should follow the principle of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu: *tṛnād api suṇīcena taror iva sahiṣṇunā*.” If we take to heart Lord Caitanya’s instruction that we remain more humble than a blade of grass, more tolerant than a tree, free from expectation of any personal prestige but always ready to offer respect to others, then cooperation and appreciation become our natural mode of working.

Śrīnivāsa Ācārya describes the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛṇdāvana as *dhīrādhīra-janapriyau:* dear to all, gentlefolk and ruffians alike, because they were *nirmatasara*, completely free from envy. You told us that we should also display ideal Vaiṣṇava character; otherwise, you said, our society will simply become another fool’s paradise. We pray that we may always strive to give up competitiveness born of envy and to satisfy you by cooperating to spread Lord Caitanya’s teachings. If we can purify all our Śrīla Prabhupāda Centennial offerings with such humble cooperation, then everyone, saint and sinner alike, will appreciate ISKCON as an oasis in the desert of Kali-yuga and eventually find shelter at your lotus feet.

Your unworthy servants in New Govardhana Dhāma, San Diego, California, USA.  
(written by Babhru dāsa)

## New Hardwar (Khasadeśa)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet again and again.

On this most auspicious day if the whole universe, the centennial day of your appearance in this world, all of us devotees of the Hardwar center in Khasadeśa are jumping and dancing and crying “Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!” with the rhythm of your *mṛdaṅga* playing in the spiritual world. You are there in Goloka Vṛndāvana playing the *mṛdaṅga—*we believe in this. It is not something imaginary, because through only three years of devotional life we got enough experience of the powers of the transcendental sound vibration coming from your big *mṛdaṅga,* the Bṛhat-mṛdaṅga. This is your books, and the carrier of the message of your books, our Guru Mahārāja, is your hand that is beating the *mṛdaṅga* so expertly. The beating means the distribution of your books.

The devotees in Hardwar are a very, very small portion of this ever-increasing Bṛhat-mṛdaṅga. As soon as your magic hands touched us, “beat” us, we became stimulated and regained new life. We began to vibrate sounds despite our weakness. Gradually we acknowledged how sweet and how strong your beating is. It is sweet because it attracts and enlivens the peacocklike devotees and pious men and makes them dance. And it is strong because it breaks the hearts of the demons, making them sometimes sad and sometimes roaring mad. Your beating is soft like a rose and hard like a thunderbolt.

After three years this beating has become our life and soul. We identify ourselves with your beating. Although we have not seen you face to face, we are familiar with the melody of your Bṛhat-mṛdaṅga beating. This is so wonderful that simply by following this melody we are being purified, and moreover the whole population of Hardwar is becoming purified because you have said that even by reading one page of your books, or by reading only one verse or even one word or even by simply seeing the book, one becomes purified and his life is transformed. By three years of nonstop book distribution, just imagine how many conditioned souls have been uplifted in Hardwar! The whole city is becoming like a holy *tīrtha*. This is the inconceivable potency of the Lord’s pure devotee! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Now, according to the divine desire of the Lord, externally the beating of your Bṛhat-mṛdaṅga has suddenly stopped in Khasadeśa. This stopping is just like what a supremely expert musician does when he temporarily stops the beat and lowers the tone, thus showing his prowess and talent. Actually, now your beating is going on in such a subtle and confidential way that only your serious followers can hear and appreciate the beauty of its sound because now your beating is going on internally, penetrating into the deep heart of your followers in Khasadeśa. We can feel this especially wonderful beating as we begin struggling to find your shelter through the more serious reading of your books, through the more attentive chanting of *japa,* through our attempts to preach, or just through our crying for your mercy in the midst of so many opponents. In so many ways we are hearing this deep-sounding beating of your Bṛhat-mṛdaṅga by your expert hand, our dear Guru Mahārāja.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, these are your pastimes in Hardwar.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlā in Khasadeśa!

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your grandsons in New Hardwar, Khasadeśa.

## New Kusuma-sarovara (Khasadeśa)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

On this auspicious 100thanniversary of your birth, please accept obeisances from your fallen children in the dust of your lotus feet.

We are the most, most fallen souls. We are so ignorant and deep in *māyā* that we are not willing to return back to Kṛṣṇa and serve Him.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our Gurudeva, who is one of your best sons, has been taking great risks to preach in Khasadeśa, despite so many difficulties. Because of his preaching, fallen souls like us have been able to get your mercy and start Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

When I was in New Hardwar, I could sell only a few books a day. One day I prayed to your picture in *Yoga for the Twenty-first Century,* wishing that you would bless me to sell more books to please my Gurudeva. On that day I miraculously sold more than two dozen books. I cried on the way hack to the temple. It is only by your causeless mercy that I was able to do this devotional service. You are so compassionate to all the fallen souls. By your purity, Kṛṣṇa consciousness has spread all over the world. Let us glorify you on the auspicious day of your appearance.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us again so that small children in Kṛṣṇa consciousness like us can one day go to Śrīdhām Māyāpur to glorify you together with our Gurudeva.

Your grandsons and granddaughters in New Kusuma-sarovara, Khasadeśa.

## New Māyāpur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. As we read in Hari Śauri Prabhu’s diary about your ten-day visit to New Māyāpur in 1976, we become very much convinced of the necessity for developing a nice Kṛṣṇa conscious village here. It will be a place that will give shelter to devotees of the French *yātrā* and visitors, protecting them and their children from the influences of the hellish materialistic cities (as you called them). Simultaneously, it will be a big preaching project, setting a good example for the rest of society.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wanted very much to create a self-sufficient Kṛṣṇa conscious community in a nice country setting, with a peaceful atmosphere. Therefore, on this very auspicious occasion of the Centennial anniversary of your appearance, we fall at your lotus feet and pray to please give us faith, strength, and determination to develop a nice village in New Māyāpur *dhāma* in the coming years.

Your servants in New Māyāpur dhāma, France.

## New Nandagram

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

As time passes, more information is revealed about your wonderful achievements and the hardships you endured in establishing the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. You are *dṛḍha-vrata,* steadfastly determined to spread the holy name to all souls.

Eating simply and austerely, distributing *Back to Godhead* in the hot Delhi summer, lugging huge heavy boxes of printed books back to Vṛndāvana, walking miles to save a few paisa on bus fares (not to use for your own comforts but to increase your facility for expanding Kṛṣṇa consciousness)—in this way you struggled in India to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Then, on the order of your spiritual master, His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī, you went to the United States with forty rupees and a trunk of books—virtually nothing. You had reached the age when most people are absolutely fearful about their security for the future. Your security was your unflinching faith in Kṛṣṇa. Even though you had no men and no money, you nevertheless saw the temples of Kṛṣṇa manifest all over the world.

You said that if a devotee takes one step toward Kṛṣṇa, He will take ten steps toward the devotee. You are our eternal inspiration.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this Centennial celebration of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

Your servants at the New Nandagram community in Bambra, Australia.  
(written by Vasusreṣṭha dāsa)

## New Navadvīpa (Honolulu)

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our repeated prostrated obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Again a very joyful opportunity has come whereby we are given the chance to speak our appreciation of yourself, to pray for the increased desire to serve you, and to beg for your causeless mercy, which can make all this possible. Of course, this opportunity is your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration.

Although any sincere devotee can see that we are only able to perceive and appreciate the tip of the iceberg of your fathomless qualities, it is really very easy to glorify you. For those of us who have taken and are taking advantage of your invaluable service to your spiritual master, His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda, in the form of your translations and commentaries on the cream of the Vedic scriptures, we can pick any of the innumerable descriptions and glorifications of the pure devotees and spiritual masters. You in every way embody and often far exceed such glorifications. As such, we conditioned souls who somehow or other are receiving some degree of your mercy often feel very unqualified to attempt to glorify you. Whatever we say will only scratch the surface of your glories.

But glorify you we must. It is our duty and obligation. To you who have saved us from the most fallen and degraded lifestyles and destinations, who have shown us the path of devotional service to Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva and Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, we owe a debt which we will never be able to repay. To glorify you is one of the sublime ways of showing you we know we are eternally indebted to you and that we want to begin paying off this debt. There is no activity that gives more pleasure to your surrendered servants than to glorify you. This is a small example of the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness which you have given us. We have to do that which gives us the most pleasure.

In contemplating scriptural references to glorify you, I’m sure many devotees will go to those verses which specifically glorify you as an individual and the miraculous services and accomplishments you’ve offered to your spiritual master and the Supreme Lord.

You are the devotee that Lord Kṛṣṇa empowered to deliver the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to this whole world to bring about a revolution in the impious lives of the misdirected civilization. You are the empowered devotee who has taken the Lord’s holy names to every town and village. You are the great general (*sena-pati*) predicted by Locana dāsa Ṭhākura to spread Lord Caitanya’s movement all over the world. Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura glorified you when predicting that people from so many different countries of this planet would come together in Māyāpur chanting “Jaya Śacīnandana! Jaya Śacīnandana!” You are that empowered devotee who Lord Nityānanda predicted would manifest the Adbhuta Mandir in Śrīdhām Māyāpur that will cause the whole world to come and worship Lord Caitanya.

For any ordinary man, whatever he does is finished when he leaves that body. For an extraordinary man, his accomplishments may survive two or three generations before turning to dust. But for the topmost devotees, their devotional qualities and service are glorified for thousands of years. This certainly will be true for you also. But along with this fact you have created a transcendental organization which is the personification of your spiritual desires and which continues to execute and make manifest those desires. This transcendental movement, which you named the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, will continue to grow and prosper for the next ten thousand years. It is certain that this accomplishment is and will be unparalleled for the entirety of this Kali-yuga.

To be allowed the opportunity to serve you by participating in this movement in whatever capacity possible is of the greatest benefit to any individual. To the degree that we associate with and serve the extremely advanced devotees who are surrendering their whole lives to your service, this is so easily perceived. Mistakes are made by one and all, but because so many of your sincere followers are complying with your perfect instructions to cooperate and serve together in this movement, you have filled and always will fill in the gaps and shortcomings of your servants, and Kṛṣṇa will be pleased. This is the perfection of our life: to please you and the Supreme Lord. By your transcendental instructions and movement you have made this so very easy.

This year of your Centennial anniversary is very special to all of your followers. We are supposed to always think of you and serve you and thus become more and more surrendered to you and your will. These Centennial festivals and objectives are giving us the inspiration to measure up to your expectations of us.

Here in New Navadvīpa Dhāma it is especially pertinent that we’re doing our best to raise the spiritual standards, especially for those of us who live in or do full-time service for your temple. Purity is the force, the foundation of our ability to fulfill your instructions. We recognize that if we are not spiritually strong and healthy we cannot help even ourselves, what to speak of others. As you know, Hawaii with its natural beauty and moderate climate can lull the weak conditioned souls and even neophyte devotees into a false sense of thinking they are able to enjoy these material bodies and this material world. We promise you that we who are living here in your temple will follow your instructions of *sādhana-bhakti*. We will strictly follow this process and pray to you for the softness of heart to try our best to inspire others to take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the degree of their desire. Preaching is your mandate to all who will listen. Your unrestricted mercy is open to all who will fully accept this request. When we see the futility of sense gratification, subtle and gross, by your grace we will be able to begin to act as your instruments and representatives. Please give us the ability to remain in our eternal position as your servants. We have or are cultivating this as our only desire.

Your aspiring servants in New Navadvīpa Dhāma, Honolulu, Hawaii, USA.  
(written by Rājendranandana dāsa)

## New Orleans

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to your divine service.

We are gratefully engaging in your devotional service. The numerous conditioned souls make a huge market. There is no shortage of customers, although the variety available are tightly bound to the lower modes of nature. Certainly we have our difficulties acting as your instruments. However, if you desire their deliverance, surely Nitāi-Gauracandra will be merciful to them.

We hope to avoid the pitfalls of pride and anger, and beg to continue as your servants. Please bless us to acquire the necessary qualifications to accomplish your work here.

Sincerely,  
Your servants New Orleans, Louisiana.

## New Pāṇihāṭi (Atlanta)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you on the 100thanniversary of your appearance day! How fortunate we are to be present during this special time in Vaiṣṇava history. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have afforded us so many rare and wonderful opportunities that we would otherwise never have had access to because of our situation at birth.

Whenever we think of you we feel great inspiration to carry on with the mission of spreading Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. Indeed, it is difficult work, and it certainly demands of us every ounce of enthusiasm, patience, and firm conviction that we can summon. But we can think of no greater activity that we could dedicate our lives to.

You guide us and inspire us by your example. Your words are our constant source of strength. From you we get hope for someday attaining steady consciousness in devotional service. We feel that your blessings are with us, and for this we are eternally grateful. We pray that we can properly conduct ourselves so that you can say, “This one has done me some service.”

You have given us every possible resource to achieve the highest goal of life, *kṛṣṇa-prema*. What more could we ask of you? And from us you simply demanded that we “love Kṛṣṇa.” What mercy, what supreme mercy!

Whenever we hear others speak of the good qualities of the great men and women in history, we think of you. Actually we wish that we had the ability of these writers, poets, and scholars so that we could properly glorify you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to be patient with us and give us your protection and blessings, and we are sure that one day, by your grace, we will go back home, back to Godhead.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your humble servants in New Pāṇihāṭi Dhāma, Atlanta, Georgia, USA.

## New Pāṇihāṭi (Guyana)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

O merciful and compassionate one! Please accept our most humble and repeated obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

Today we are trying in our own tiny way to observe and celebrate the most blessed event of our life, your Vyāsa-pūjā and the 100thanniversary of Your Divine Grace’s birth. As such, we feel it incumbent to say a few words in your glorification, but how can we, your insignificant grandchildren, say anything close to what befits such a wondrous, superhuman, transcendental personality as Your Divine Grace? Begging for your causeless mercy, we therefore make this humble offering as an insignificant petal of love at your divine lotus feet.

You are the excellent transcendental person and the special mercy manifestation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who says, *ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit:* “One should know the *ācārya* as myself and never disrespect him in any way.”

Today the world is awestruck as crores and crores of devotees are pouring forth, hailing from all the corners of the globe. Devotees and the denizens of heaven are seizing the opportunity and are coming to Mṛtyu-loka to attend and participate in your most glorious Vyāsa-pūjā, your most blessed *abhiṣeka,* the tumultuous *kīrtana,* and the sumptuous feast of transcendental *prasādam.* All this glorification of Your Divine Grace is another wonderful expression of your mercy. This glorious event is not only a source of spiritual awakening, bliss, and inspiration for your disciples, granddisciples, well-wishers, and friends, but it is also a cause of awe and reverence for many onlookers throughout the world. The spiritual import and transcendental mellows of these festivities will inspire generations for millenniums to come.

The prime entity, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, has unfailing causeless love for us, His eternal fragmental parts and parcels. This love causes Him to accompany us from time immemorial throughout different universes and planetary systems, in various species of living entities, as the Supersoul, or Paramātmā. This manifestation of the Lord within the heart, who directs our wanderings in accordance with our desires, has a special desire of His own to award us the perfection that yields the peace and happiness for which we have wandered everywhere in different universes in vain.

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**“According to their** ***karma,*** **all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.”**

Accordingly, the Supreme Lord descends once in every day of Lord Brahmā in His original form of Śyāmasundara, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. His wondrous pastimes and flute-playing captivate the minds and hearts of all the living entities, filling them with boundless transcendental happiness. He annihilates the demoniac kings and rulers and re-establishes the eternal principles of *sanātana-dharma*. He gives the following instructions:

*sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁ śaraṇaṁ vraja*

**“Abandon all varieties of religion and just surrender unto Me.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **18.66)**

*yat karoṣi yad aśnāsi yaj juhoṣi dadāsi yat*  
*yat tapasyasi kaunteya tat kuruṣva mad-arpaṇam*

**“Whatever you do, whatever you eat, whatever you offer or give away, and whatever austerities you perform—do that, O son of Kuntī, as an offering to Me.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **9.22) He describes the true** ***mahātmās*** **as s*****atataṁ kīrtayanto mām,*** **“always chanting My glories.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **9.14)**

He then comes as Lord Gaurāṅga, the combined incarnation of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and Kṛṣṇa, and sets the example of how one should follow these wonderful precepts. In this wonderful form He inaugurates the *saṅkīrtana* movement of the chanting of the holy names:

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

Infusing this *saṅkīrtana* movement with His own *kṛṣṇa-prema* and the *audārya-rasa,* the mood of compassion, which was never before available, Lord Caitanya joins with His confidential associates in freely distributing pure love of Kṛṣṇa, *kṛṣṇa-prema,* to all, regardless of previous qualifications or disqualifications. And the more they distribute love of Kṛṣṇa, the more it increases. Pouring torrential showers from His unlimited compassion, the Lord, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, blesses the world with love of Godhead.

O Paraṁ Guru, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has selected the wondrously excellent personality of Your Divine Grace to fulfill His mission. In this way He is establishing His kingdom on earth as it is in the heaven and bringing about the desired peace, harmony, goodwill, and universal brotherhood among all mankind and living entities, with God, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the supreme father of all beings, at the center. This is the unsurpassable glory of Your Divine Grace.

As Arjuna was the hero of the Battle of Kurukṣetra, so Your Divine Grace is the hero of the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Through Arjuna the Lord annihilated the demoniac elements and re-established the principles of *dharma*. Through Your Divine Grace the Lord is bestowing love of God freely to all. Now all sincere seekers can come and enjoy boundless transcendental happiness and be promoted to the highest perfection of life, *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

How can we fathom your divine personality? At the advanced age of seventy, when others would have rested on their laurels, you crossed the Atlantic, suffering two heart attacks. With only forty-two rupees, the equivalent to seven US dollars, you came to the United States of America with the single desire to fulfill the instruction of your Guru Mahārāja. Struggling alone, with no help from your Godbrothers, you single-handedly established the International Society for Krishna Consciousness in 1966. During the next twelve years you circled the globe fourteen times, established 108 temples, managed the affairs of the growing ISKCON society, wrote over sixty volumes of Vedic philosophy, and planted the seed of love of God on every continent. Verily you are the *jagat-guru,* and it is you who have given the lawbooks for God’s kingdom on earth for the next ten thousand years.

The Centennial celebrations are taking place at a very opportune time in the history of our ISKCON society. Pivoting on a glorious lotus of twelve petals of activities, these celebrations carry such depth, magnitude, and quality that they are all unparalleled in history. They are all highlights of our expression of love for you. Actually, these celebrations are all your miracle, your activities, and through them, like a touchstone, you are re-uniting and re-invigorating the ISKCON Prabhupāda family. Simultaneously its members, having matured with years of realizations, are becoming re-dedicated to the sublime mission of the family, Kṛṣṇa’s family. Internally the celebrations are a tremendous source of strength and inspiration, and externally they are having such a tumultuous and resounding effect abroad that they are sure to further disintegrate the shell of atheism, impersonalism, and voidism that surrounds the world.

So in observation of this most blessed event of your magnificent Vyāsa-pūjā, we want to humbly express our heartfelt thanks for the kind mercy you are showering on us here in Guyana, and also all over the world.

Although you never physically came to Guyana, we are receiving Your Divine Grace and your spiritual presence through your wonderful and excellently competent disciples, who are always with you and who are taking you wherever they go. We know that all these celebrations are graced by your personal presence and that of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

Here in Berbice we are trying to construct a new temple complex to house their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulacandra and to expand the preaching. There is weekly Food for Life, apart from the Sunday Love Feast, a television broadcast, *pāndal* and home programs, *harināmas,* book distribution, and fortnightly Prabhupāda nights. Presently we are increasing our spiritual relations with our neighbors, Trinidad and Surinam, and are holding joint exchange programs in your honor.

Kindly continue to shower your special blessings on all of us so that we may become a little more purified and thus able to render some menial service to your divine lotus feet, under whose shade we beg to always remain.

Your granddisciples in New Pāṇihāṭi Dhāma, East Berbice, Guyana.  
(written by Viśvarūpa dāsa)

## New Ramaṇa Reti (Warsaw)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories to the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya! All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

You are the dearmost personality for everyone in ISKCON and for everyone who accepts your books and teachings. How can we, your grandchildren, who did not even see you in person, glorify you? Yet we have one consolation: we understand that you will always be present for is in your books. Your books are the best gift you could ever offer to the whole world. Because you are very compassionate toward the people of the Kali-yuga, you translated the cream of the Vedic knowledge—*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Because of reading this *Purāṇa,* many former *mlecchas* and *yavanas* are now dancing and chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. This is possible only due to the mercy of a pure devotee like yourself.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, twenty years have passed since you inspired your glorious disciple Harikeśa Swami to preach in Poland. Due to the great effort of this saintly person, the preaching became successful. Now more and more servants of yours preach in our insignificant country. Many of your grandsons spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement through book distribution, too.

Always desiring to stay in the shade of your lotus feet and always thirsty for your mercy, we remain  
The servants of your servants in New Ramaṇa Reti Dhāma, Warsaw, Poland.

## New Sanket (Khasadeśa)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept our most humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have come to know you through pictures, videotapes, our *guru’s* glorification of you, and other devotees’ descriptions. At an age at which any man would retire and just wait for death to come, you crossed the ocean to save the fallen souls. We are impressed by all the things you did and are still doing.

On a videotape we saw that when you were singing Hare Kṛṣṇa, tears were gliding form your eyes like pearls. This also caused us to shed tears—tears of happiness. We think that you must know us. Our Gurudeva, Tamāl Krishna Goswami, must have mentioned us to you, for he once wrote in a letter that he would pray to you for our protection. So we believe that you are with us, taking care of us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, unfortunate circumstances have robbed us of the opportunity for devotional service. Now there are no more meetings or splendid *kīrtanas,* no more wonderful *prasādam* or book distribution. So many ways to approach Kṛṣṇa have been taken away. When the devotees had to leave us, our hearts broke and filled with anger and sorrow. Those were hard times. We thought, Shall we give it all up (as some did), or shall we stand up and start again from where we left off? We had not properly appreciated our good fortune and the love of the devotees until we were about to lose them.

We never met you personally, but we bear your example in mind. Being the messenger of Kṛṣṇa, you are our guiding star. With staunch faith we remember all our happy and fulfilling experiences in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, knowing that it is by your grace that we have come from doubts, through trial, then involvement, and on to the courage to overcome the setback. Now that we have gone through this recent test, all other suffering seems insignificant. We cherish every opportunity for devotional service, and it is with joy that we look back at the obstacles and how we overcame them by keeping faith in your words. Now our hearts are filled with courage and confidence.

We have this dream, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we want to build a shrine for you at the foot of the mountains. Not only will the place be beautifully located in an uncontaminated natural setting, but there is also an old saying that the higher a place is, the closer it is to God.

We very intensely desire the association of your devotees, and feeling like lost sheep, we humbly request you to grant us this favor: that one day we may again join your flock and stay with devotees in a holy place.

We do not know whether all the devotees in the world will read this letter, but we want to tell them all, “Please cherish every second when serving Kṛṣṇa. We, who are desiring to do many services freely but cannot—we envy all of you. We pray to all of you for your mercy and benedictions. We are longing to be together with everyone.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are maintaining our faith that your good self and Śrī Kṛṣṇa are guiding us. We humbly bow down again and again at your worshipable feet.

Praying to remain engaged in your wonderful service, we remain  
Your grandchildren in New Sanket, Khasadeśa.

## New Śāntipura (Khasadeśa)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, here is our situation: we are in anxiety. We offer obeisances to your lotus feet every day, we offer you the “Śrī Guru Vandana” prayers every day, we study your books every day, we often hear your lectures on tape, and we intoxicate ourselves with your sweet *bhajans.* But after all this we still find out that we don’t understand your glories. You are a unique devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa, and thus there is you are nondifferent from the Lord Himself.

How can we understand that? How can we glorify you properly on this most auspicious day without receiving the blessings from you yourself? You are so far and so near to us as well. We know that you are very dear to every living entity in this whole universe. The air we are now breathing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is created by you. The house we are now living in Kṛṣṇa consciousness was built by you. But how can we describe your glories? A baby cannot understand, cannot remember, and cannot describe how he was born from the womb of his mother.

One thing we know is that at a certain moment we were picked up by you from this ocean of misery. And we know the we were reborn in the vibration of the *mahā-mantra—*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

We are now in anxiety, for we actually are not fully conscious of the value of what we are holding in our hands when we distribute your books. We are in anxiety because we do not properly understand the message delivered by you when we try to convince others. We are in anxiety because we do not know whether we can be used in the mission of Lord Caitanya as qualified instruments and servants of the servant of the servant of the servant of yourself. We are in anxiety because we know that we are always in debt to you but do not know how we can possibly repay even a little of what we owe you through our imperfect activities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, are you really pleased with what we have done this past year? Are you satisfied with our chanting, our reading, our *kīrtanas,* our cooking, the new *bhaktas* that are coming? Are you satisfied with the service we are trying to render to our Guru Mahārāja? Will you continuously bestow your blessings upon us?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are in anxiety because we are the grandsons of someone who is the savior of the whole world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day we are all in anxiety, and so we pray to you to let us remain in that anxiety and cook it unto transcendence. The more anxious we are about rendering service to your lotus feet, the less anxious we will be about our bodily happiness, which only leads us to repeated birth and death.

Let there be no other way, and let there be no other anxiety.

Your most unworthy grandsons in New Śāntipura, Khasadeśa.

## New Śāntipura (Poland)

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances at the lotus dust of your lotus feet.

Thank you for giving us your hand and leading us to the supreme happiness.

Thank you for dispersing the fog of ignorance with your illuminating words.

Thank you for agreeing to be the transcendental captain of our boat, which is sailing among dangerous waves of illusion.

Thank you for the key to the treasury that is the nectar flowing from chanting Kṛṣṇa’s holy names.

Thank you for setting a perfect example of Vaiṣṇava behavior—in this way you made it possible for us to follow in the footsteps of great *ācāryas*.

Thank you for your courage and dedication to your spiritual master’s mission. Now we know what a genuine relationship between a disciple and the *guru* means.

Thank you for the books that are the basis of our life—books that are like a life preserver keeping us afloat on the surface of the ocean of human imperfections.

Thank you for establishing temples—transcendental places sheltering us from the degradation of Kali-yuga.

Thank you for kindly accepting our service and not rejecting us due to the mistakes we are committing.

Thank you for constantly attempting to fan even the smallest sparks of our devotion out of your desire to see every one of us burn his *anarthas* in the fire of love of Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you for you sweet *bhajans,* which make all the inauspicious disappear. The transcendental sound of your voice enables us to feel the sweetness flowing from glorifying Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you for your disciples, who perfectly guide us in our spiritual life by correcting all our mistakes and inspiring us to serve you.

Thank you for your spiritual teachings, full of truth and logic. Thanks to them, we can understand the difference between life in the material swamp and spiritual life.

Thank you for your sympathetic glances, which have saved us from death in *māyā’s* embrace.

Thank you for *sādhu-saṅga,* devotee association, which is so important for us to advance in spiritual life.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being present then and now. A devotee never dies, and thus your glories will always be sung throughout all three worlds, since the greatness of a Vaiṣṇava is proportional to the number of devotees created by him or her.

We will always consider you a perfect example of a servant of a servant of a servant of the one who maintains the *gopīs*—the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Trying to serve you with our bodies, words, and minds, we remain  
Your grandsons and granddaughters in New Śāntipura, Poland.  
(written by Bhaktilatā-devī dāsī)

## New Tālavana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. On this auspicious day, one hundred years ago, Lord Kṛṣṇa allowed you to enter this material world to bless the fallen souls.

How can we realize your unlimited mercy? We are feeling completely unqualified to properly glorify you.

By your mercy, Kṛṣṇa is revealed. By Kṛṣṇa’s grace, we can understand you and your mission. You established temples, spread the Holy Name, and distributed your books throughout this planet. You left to your disciples and granddisciples the service of establishing *varṇāśrama-dharma*.

By your mercy you found time to visit southern Mississippi, a remote corner of this world, far removed from the spiritual serenity of Vṛndāvana. Here you established New Tālavana. How significant was that visit! The austerities you performed for our benefit are unfathomable.

By your mercy, give us strength and intelligence to fulfill your mission. At New Tālavana we are endevoring to fully understand your desire. Help us realize that Kṛṣṇa will allow your desire to be fulfilled. We pray simply to be your instruments.

By your mercy, you explained our constitutional position, our right to choose. Let our choice be to serve you. By our choosing you, life is simple and sublime. By our choosing *māyā,* life is complicated by lust, anger, and greed, and it becomes full of anxiety.

By your mercy, help us associate with you. Give us the strength to fight Kali’s infuence, to study your books, to purely chant Kṛṣṇa’s Holy Name. Remembering you every day will alleviate our desires to enjoy separate from Kṛṣṇa.

By your mercy, individually we can cooperate by following your instructions. Collectively we can cooperate to serve and love you.

By your mercy, please accept our humble offering, and allow us to remain at your lotus feet.

Your servants at the New Tālavana Farm in Mississippi, USA.

## New Vraja (Khasadeśa)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept our humble obeisances . All glories to Your Divine Grace. All glories to your merciful appearance on this planet.

I feel so fortunate to write you this offering.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a 100 percent pure devotee. The only reason why you came here to this planet was to save us, to show us the wonderfulness of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, to drag us back to Godhead.

You give us the *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* All this Vedic literature you presented provides us with nectar, which is the only thing that can satisfy the soul. Kṛṣṇa conscious life is so nice. Deity worship, *kīrtana, prasādam,* preaching—everything is so enjoyable. You are so powerful, your “magic” is so great, that you can turn hoglike people into bona fide *sādhus*.

The holy *dhāma* you have shown us is unimaginably attractive, the *bhajanas* you have taught us to glorify Kṛṣṇa are marvelous, the beauty of Deity is indescribable. Oh, after being mad after material happiness for who knows how many years, you have given us what we should really want. The most unfortunate people in the world are those who have tasted Kṛṣṇa consciousness and then dropped away.

Lord Kṛṣṇa is merciful: He speaks the *Bhagavad-gītā* to establish *dharma* on this planet. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is even more merciful because He reveals to us the topmost relationships with Kṛṣṇa. But you are the most merciful because you have made all of the Lord’s mercy available to us through your books and your preaching. Not only that: you have tied a collar around our necks and are tirelessly dragging us back to spiritual world.

Why are disciples of yours like my Gurudeva so strong, so determined, so powerful? Because they are totally charged up with both Prabhupāda-śakti and Prabhupāda-bhakti.

If you had not have come, we cannot even imagine how miserable and ignorant our lives would have remained, like maggots in a toilet

Thank you, Prabhupāda. Thank you. You are an *amala-bhakta,* a topmost pure devotee. We feel so fortunate to be your grandchildren, the disciples of your outstanding sons.

We are trying to repay you for even a little drop of your mercy by becoming true Prabhupādānugas.

Please accept our humble obeisances again.

Your grandsons and -daughters in New Vraja, Khasadeśa.

## New Vṛndāvana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

New Vṛndāvana, 1976. You were touring your yet-unfinished residence here, what would become known as the Palace of Gold. At that time the devotees described that when the building was completed the walls would be adorned with gold and jewels. “The devotees are my jewels,” you declared. You were always unimpressed with any amount of opulences bestowed upon you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we do not fully understand who you are, or what you’ve given us, nor how your words are affecting the course of the entire world. Please be merciful unto us give us a taste for chanting the holy name purely and allow us to assist you in your mission. Please give us even a small understanding of your jewellike devotees who are so very precious to you. The devotees can honor you by honoring each other, for one who takes to devotional service is so rare in this world.

Sometimes in rushing ahead to preach, build temples, or distribute books, we have neglected or mistreated one another. As you have shown, it is so important that the devotees tend to each other. “Charity begins at home,” you have said. In one letter you wrote,

“It should be our definite policy that nobody is ill treated that he may go away. We recruit a person to join us after spending gallons of blood. Everyone comes for reformation, you cannot expect everyone to be perfect, rather it is our duty to make everyone perfect as far as possible. So we shall be very much cautious and careful in this connection.” (letter, 23 August 1973)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we cannot tell you about any grand plan for New Vṛndāvana—that it’s expanding by leaps and bounds or that more people are visiting your Palace than ever before or more facilities have been built. Right now, New Vṛndāvana is just trying to be a community of devotees. And community means relationships. How we work. How we serve. How we relate to one another. And maybe, in this age of alienation, anxiety, and estranged relationships, to work together as a loving, caring, devotional family may just be the very thing that will attract people to your movement.

Your vision for the expansion of Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world is multi-faceted and far-reaching. But you proceeded so humbly in this world that even though practically everyone knew about Hare Kṛṣṇa, hardly anyone knew about you, the bearer of this wonderful gift. You are so very merciful that you do not want even one conditioned soul to be left out in getting the nectar of the holy name. Indeed, this movement can and should engage everyone in the glorification of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Even though we are unworthy, lacking any good qualities, our constant prayer is that we can somehow or other render some service that will be pleasing to Your Divine Grace.

The inmates of the New Vṛndāvana farm community, West Virginia, USA.

## New York (Brooklyn)

*kṛṣṇa se tomāra kṛṣṇa dite pāro*  
*tomāra śakati āche*  
*āmi to’ kāṅgāla, ‘kṛṣṇa’ ‘kṛṣṇa’ boli’,*  
*dhāi tava pāche pāche*

**“Kṛṣṇa is yours. You are able to give him to me, for such is your power. I am indeed wretched and simply run after you, crying, “Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!” \[from “Ohe! Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura” (*****Śaraṇāgati*****)\]**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*Praṇāmas! Daṇḍavats!* Obeisances from the front, from behind, and from all sides. Thousands of obeisances at your transcendental lotus feet on this most auspicious occasion of your Centennial appearance day and Vyāsa-pūjā celebration.

The desire of all of your loyal servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandir on this most blessed occasion is to see your beautiful *mūrti,* the perfection of our eyes, to bathe your divine form as we touch your lotus feet and remember your sweet association, the fulfillment of our bodily touch, and to please Your Divine Grace with eloquent praises, the truly beneficial purpose of our tongue’s words.

We want to see Your Divine Grace, we want to touch Your Divine Grace, and we want to glorify Your Divine Grace. Why? It’s simply because our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda is a pure devotee of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda. A rather simple and utterly significant reason why we surrender our hearts at your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have implicit faith in Your Divine Grace. We know you have no hidden material agendas—politics was never a word in your dictionary—and impersonal idiosyncrasies run in fear at the mention of your name.

So we trust you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with our lives; we trust you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with our hearts; we trust Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda with the task of re-awakening our dormant love for the lotus feet of the all-merciful Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and the all-attractive Divine Couple Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda.

In the purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.3.14, Your Divine Grace presents a wonderful realization that is quite appropriate to offer in glorification at your lotus feet on this auspicious day, “One cannot become the Lord’s pure devotee without taking the dust of a great devotee on his head (*pāda-rajo-’bhiṣekam*). A pure devotee is *niṣkiñcana;* he has no material desire to enjoy the material world. One has to take shelter of such a pure devotee in order to attain his qualities. The pure devotee is always free from the clutches of *māyā* and her influence.”

All of us are unfortunate, insignificant beggars, praying for a drop of your divine mercy to purify our hearts. We are praying for a long lifetime of unmotivated devotional service offered at your divine lotus feet. We are fervently praying, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the long-awaited day when Your Divine Grace reveals to us the true identity of our covered souls, that true loving relationship with Guru and Śrī Kṛṣṇa that fulfills life’s goals. And we shamelessly pray and beg, foolish yet obedient dogs at your lotus feet, that you please give us all the blessings we may require to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa *kīrtana* and make Your Divine Grace smile, to dance for the pleasure of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindadeva and make Your Divine Grace chuckle in delight, and to give up all material desires and learn to love Lord Kṛṣṇa, which brings true happiness to your transcendental heart.

We are all very eager to receive the dust from your transcendental lotus feet on our heads and in the core of our hearts. We come eagerly every day to *maṅgala-ārati,* hoping for that glorious dust; we eagerly chant the sacred *hari-nāma japa,* hoping for that glorious dust; we daily attend your ecstatic *guru-pūjā,* hoping for that sacred dust; and we are very eager to perform birth after birth after birth of devotional service to your divine lotus feet, hoping and hoping against hope that we, Your Divine Grace’s foolish, unfortunate, insignificant beggars, will get even a particle of that transcendental dust of mercy that emanates from the sacred lotus feet of the most glorious Vaiṣṇava *ācārya,* His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the transcendental autocrat of ISKCON, the intimate associate and loving confidante of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and the eternal divine couple, Śrī Śrī Rādhā Govinda. Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

In the purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.21.43 you so nicely state, “It is said that one who has unflinching faith in the Supreme personality of Godhead, which means unflinching faith in the Vaiṣṇava, or the pure devotee of the Supreme Lord, develops all the good qualities of the demigods. *Yasyāsti bhaktir bhagavaty akiñcanā / sarvair guṇais tatra samāsate surāḥ* (*Bhāg*. 5.18.12). Prahlāda Mahārāja also said, *naiṣāṁ matis tāvad urukramāṅghrim* (*Bhāg*. 7.5.32). Unless one takes the dust of the lotus feet of a pure Vaiṣṇava on one’s head, one cannot understand what the Supreme Personality of Godhead is, and unless one knows the Supreme Personality of Godhead, one’s life remains imperfect. A great soul who has fully surrendered to the Supreme Lord after understanding Him fully and after repeatedly undergoing austerities and penances for many, many lives is very rare. The crown of a king is simply a big load if the king or head of the state does not actually bear the dust of the lotus feet of *brāhmaṇas* and Vaiṣṇavas.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda pure devotion is as much infectious, in a good sense, as infectious diseases. We are therefore very eager to be completely infected by your glorious transcendental association, and we have unflinching faith that by your mercy we will also become infected with pure devotion to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

By hearing your sweet and beautiful *bhajanas* we become enriched with your transcendental qualities, by hearing the words of nectar emanating from your lotus mouth during your spiritual world discourses, we become more enriched with divine spiritual qualities mercifully expanding from your pristine pure heart, and by tasting the remnants of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* after being further benedicted by your purifying touch, we become most fully enriched with the incurable traces of *prema* and *bhakti,* which we tightly embrace with the understanding that this irreversible infection of *bhakti* will bring about the final and permanent demise of the miserable material side of our life. On this glorious 100th anniversary of your transcendental appearance, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we, your insignificant menial servants, want to rededicate our activities to serving your ISKCON mission, to rededicate and constantly direct our hearts’ loving emotions to the service of Your Divine Grace throughout the insignificant remainder of this lifetime, and to rededicate ourselves to eternally serving and striving to please Your Divine Grace throughout the eternity of our *jīva* existence.

Your Divine Grace is the spiritual master of the entire world. All the unfortunate fallen conditioned souls on this planet have become candidates for good fortune because, on a glorious day in 1896, a century ago, Your Divine Grace appeared in order to fulfill the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu by spreading the sublime message of *kṛṣṇa-prema* to enrich the entire world, to enrich the fallen souls and melt their stonelike hearts with your loving mercy.

Your transcendental qualities, glories, and fame are spread throughout the three worlds, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Indeed, Your Divine Grace is so famous among the demigods in heaven, among all your aspiring menial servants, among their servants also, and even among the confidential eternal *līlā* associates of the Supreme Lords Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda in the *parama-dhāma* of Goloka Vṛndāvana. We can now easily see how the whole world will eventually become inundated with Kṛṣṇa consciousness as Your Divine Grace’s fame grows more and more, centennial after centennial, cementing your reputation as the greatest pure devotee of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, as the most merciful Ācārya Ṭhākura, the savior who entered the hellfire of New York’s Lower East Side and held the world at your command by the power of real love, *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

Your Divine Grace entered a place not fit for a gentleman, Haight Ashbury 1967, and taught the whole world how to dance to the eternal spiritual tune of a real poet, a real *mantra,* a real preacher, a real servant, a real savior, and a real lover of mankind and of the Supreme Father of all mankind, Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the transcendental cowherd boy. In the *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, when discoursing with Rāmānanda Rāya, asked, “What is the greatest fame?” Rāmānanda Rāya replied that to be known as a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa is the perfect fame.

We know that when a person becomes famous as a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa his fame is never to be extinguished. Therefore we feel very safe in saying that because of Your Divine Grace’s fame as the spotlessly pure devotee of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa we are very confident that this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will keep growing bigger and bigger and spreading further and wider throughout every town and village of this miserable world. With the passage of each century, the centennial celebration honoring your divine appearance will be attended by more and more recipients of your sweet mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And eventually, by the potency of your fame caused by your glories’ and divine qualities’ being broadcast far and wide, the whole world will chant in unison, “Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*! Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*! Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!”

On this auspicious, glorious, once-in-a-lifetime occasion, all of us who understand the importance of serving an exalted pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa such as Your Divine Grace feel that by your association we have become the most fortunate persons in the universe.

Your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is our only true wealth, our only true pleasure, our only true happiness, our only true need, and our only true goal, birth after birth.

Your Divine Grace is unquestionably the bona fide representative of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa in the authorized chain of disciplic succession. When we seek the favor of Kṛṣṇa, we know His favor is available only by contact with Your Divine Grace through the process of service, surrender, and development of full Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

No Māyāvādī teacher or *karmī* teacher can deliver Kṛṣṇa’s favor to our doorstep.

We must receive Lord Kṛṣṇa’s message without change; since Your Divine Grace are a direct representative of Kṛṣṇa, we consider you fully qualified as such a pure messenger.

By connection with you, we consider ourselves the most fortunate persons, for we have come in contact with such a wonderful and confidential associate of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda.

The Vedic literatures state, *tad vijñānārthaṁ sa gurum evābhigacchet:* one has to search out a *guru* to understand the affairs of the spiritual world. It is also stated in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, tasmād guruṁ prapadyeta jijñāsuḥ śreya uttamam:* one who is very interested in understanding the activities of the spiritual world must search out a *guru*—a bona fide representative of Kṛṣṇa. Furthermore, Kṛṣṇa personally states, *ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyāt:* “One should know the *ācārya* as Myself.” Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is safe to say that the position of *guru* is exclusively meant for the bona fide representative of Kṛṣṇa. A devotee should definitely understand this and take shelter and true spiritual advantage of your powerful purifying association through the via medium of hearing your purports, lectures, *bhajanas,* and letters. Your transcendental *śikṣā* is available to everyone, and the only price is the intense desire to have it.

We always desire to be situated at the shelter of your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We know that by your merciful association we will become free from the perplexities of life. We know that by your divine Kṛṣṇa conscious instructions and guidance Lord Govinda’s lotus feet are attainable in this very lifetime. We sincerely regret and apologize for our lack of surrender, our lack of complete dedication to Your Divine Grace, and our lack of perfection on this divine path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the topmost *yoga* system.

But all of us who are sincere, and those of us who promise to strive to become perfectly sincere, promise to offer our eternal selves, our eternal life, and our eternal bliss, knowledge, and love at your divine lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because Your Divine Grace is the perfect and pure eternal swanlike loving devotee of Their most beautiful Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda.

On this most blessed occasion, we, your eternal menial servants, offer Your Divine Grace the most beautiful and perfect prayer:

**Gurudeva (from** ***Śaraṇāgati*****)**

*gurudev!*  
*kṛpā-bindu diyā, koro’ ei dāse*  
*tṛṇāpekhā ati hīna*  
*sakala sahane, bala diyā koro’*  
*nija-māne spṛha-hīna*

*sakale sammāna korite śakati*  
*deho’ nātha! jathājatha*  
*tabe to’ gāibo, hari-nāma-sukhe*  
*aparādha ha’be hata*

*kabe heno kṛpā, labhiyā e jana*  
*kṛtārtha hoibe, nātha!*  
*śakti-buddhi-hīna, āmi ati dīna*  
*koro’ more ātma-sātha*

*jogyatā-vicāre, kichu nāhi pāi*  
*tomāra karuṇā—sāra*  
*karuṇā na hoile, kāndiyā kāndiyā,*  
*prāṇa nā rākhibo āra*

**(1) Gurudeva! By a drop of your mercy make this servant of yours more humble than a blade of grass. Give me strength to bear all trials and troubles, and free me from all desires for personal honor.**

**(2) O lord and master! Invest me with the power to properly honor all living beings. Only then will I sing the holy name in great ecstasy and will all my offenses cease.**

**(3) O lord and master! When will this devotee be blessed by obtaining your mercy? I am low, fallen, and devoid of all strength and intelligence. Please make me your beloved servant.**

**(4) When I examine myself, I find nothing of value. Your mercy is therefore essential to me. If you are not merciful, I will constantly weep, and I will not maintain my life any longer.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we beg for your mercy so we may remain your menial servants forever.

Your fallen insignificant servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandir in Brooklyn, New York.  
(written by Rāmabhadra dāsa)

## New York (26 Second Avenue)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:

We had long known about the storefront where you started the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Some of us had visited you here when this was your only temple, others had read about your pastimes at Second Avenue in books. Like your first students in 1966, we came to 26 Second Avenue hoping to catch a glimpse of you and sit a few minutes in your presence. We wanted to see Swamiji. To our amazement, you invited us in and gave us service. We were not qualified, yet you entrusted to us the care of your *mahā-tīrtha*.

For us, paying the rent seemed challenge enough, but you asked us to become fixed in devotional service and share this devotion with others. Unqualified though we were, we could not refuse you.

In the evenings we open the door to your storefront and watch as the tiny room fills with people eager to hear about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Pilgrims to this *tīrtha* are struck by the double presence of glorious past and dynamic present. If we were maintaining a shrine for sentimental visitors, you would not approve. But here, in this outpost of the spiritual world, you are gathering the youth of today to demonstrate the vitality of your maturing movement.

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who simultaneously have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our daily service at 26 Second Avenue reminds us of this verse: we serve you with the kind of devotion reserved for God. Through that devotional service we learn the purports to your books. Even our guests gain an understanding of this verse as they learn the history of your “Matchless Gifts” temple and see you worshiped in action.

Now there are many *gurus* in your *paramparā,* but you are the *param guru,* the Prabhupāda at whose feet the masters sit. By your mercy great saints follow you. We regularly bathe in their grace and are thus reminded of you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’ve given us a big responsibility. You and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu have asked us to teach the message of Kṛṣṇa to the inhabitants of New York City, a place considered so hellish that some of your best disciples will not visit here. Through contact with the material nature we sometimes become callused and cynical about the daunting task of giving Kṛṣṇa consciousness to this city. We are embarrassed to come before you with this matter, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but in order to satisfy your desire to preach here, we must have your direct mercy. How can we be effective without your mercy?

We love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we know you love us. Kindly arrange for your love to lighten the burden we sometimes feel. We want to carry more. Give us that innocent vision of *yoga-māyā* so that we do not see *mahā-māyā* looming impenetrably before us. Guide us as *caittya-guru* while we preach to and engage others. Train us to avoid mundane distractions.

We need your help to make us fixed (*niṣṭhā*). We don’t want *bhava* or *prema* just now. We have heard you ask us to convert just one percent of Americans to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We know your strong desire for us to preach. Your glance in our direction can help us to be fixed in your purpose for coming here to New York. Kindly take us in hand. We are active and ready, but not altogether fit. We are asking you to train this squad of devotees so that we might become an army in New York City.

As you prayed to Kṛṣṇa, we pray to you. Make us dance like puppets on strings. You have already sent us intelligent young people, wealthy professionals, and even millionaires to engage and train. We now need your help in our lives and theirs. We’ll rise to the task, but please guide us. Kṛṣṇa is far from us, but we see you, we know you. You are everything to us. We depend upon your mercy.

Your servants at 26 Second Avenue, New York City, USA.

## Osaka

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet.

It was in 1978, when I was twenty, that I got *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* at the L.A. airport. That was how I got a devotee’s association for the first time in my life. After the incident, I spent my life for a while without devotees’ association. Sixteen years went by, and I got devotees’ association by the grace of Lord Kṛṣṇa. I started to appreciate your greatness, Śrīla Prabhupāda, through my Guru Mahārāja. I learned that you had translated Sanskrit scriptures into English, and that some of your English books had been translated into Japanese. I read these books and became a devotee.

Now we are enthusiastically distributing the books of Your Divine Grace so that those who do not know Kṛṣṇa at all can get knowledge of Him. Nothing could be more wonderful than this. Just by the mercy of your books, I am now engaged in devotional service and giving others a chance to engage also. Here in Japan, more and more people have been accepting Kṛṣṇa consciousness, especially in the biggest city, Tokyo, and now here in Osaka, the second biggest city. I have firm faith that by pleasing my Guru Mahārāja through spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I can please Your Divine Grace, and that by pleasing Your Divine Grace, I can please Kṛṣṇa.

Please let me engage in your devotional service.

Your servants in Osaka, Japan.  
(written by Satsvarūpa dāsa)

## Oslo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet.

It is a great honor for me to write this offering on this hundredth anniversary of your birth on behalf of the Norwegian *yātrā*. To glorify you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is not difficult, for you are an ocean of good qualities.

The great *ācārya* Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura wrote this famous poem:

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
When thou art living still in sound!  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live  
And, living, try to spread the holy name around.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are still living among us in the sound vibrations recorded in your books, lectures, conversations, and letters, and therefore associating with your instructions is just like associating with you. But not only that: when I look at your picture and see your warm smile, your majestic features, and your transcendental pride, it is not at all difficult to relate to you. Hearing you sing a *bhajan* also gives a very direct experience of you. Yes, you are still here.

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.32) the Supreme Lord states,

*māṁ hi pārtha vyapāśritya ye ’pi syuḥ pāpa-yonayaḥ*  
*striyo vaiśyās tathā śūdrās te ’pi yānti parāṁ gatim*

In the Age of Kali everybody is born a *śūdra,* and many of us come from even lower backgrounds. By writing your Bhaktivedanta purports—your devotional ecstasies, as you called them—you have fulfilled the promise given by Lord Kṛṣṇa in this verse, for you have made it possible for low-class people like me to develop love for and understanding of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. By ordering your followers to distribute the Bhaktivedanta purports in every town and village, you have enabled all the fallen souls of Kali-yuga to understand the most sublime philosophy, a knowledge beyond this world. You have given hope to the hopeless, life to the lifeless, and love to the loveless. And you will continue to do so for the next ten thousand years.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like a transcendental touchstone. Any person who comes in contact with you, no matter how high or how low, will have all his desires fulfilled. Not just any desires, but the desires of the spirit soul: the desire for truth, knowledge, and happiness, the desire for Kṛṣṇa. Your mercy and kindness act like a magnet, attracting all of us to give up our bad habits and surrender to Kṛṣṇa. Only because of your mercy have we gotten a chance to attain the highest goal of existence, entrance into Goloka Vṛndāvana. We owe our lives to you.

This year, 1996, all of your followers are celebrating your Centennial. It is a great year in the history of ISKCON. I would like to take the opportunity to pray for your mercy. Please guide us in every step we take. Establishing ISKCON in all its aspects is not easy. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please guide us and bless us that we may be able to work according to your desire, leaving aside everything else.

Your servants in Oslo, Norway.

## Ottawa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious occasion of the Centennial celebration of your glorious appearance in this world, all your disciples, granddisciples, and congregational members of your ISKCON are enthusiastically working together to increase the *saṅkīrtana* movement for the spiritual benefit of everyone. Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for giving me the opportunity to also participate in the association of your dear servants and loving followers, for I realize the importance of such association.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are addressed as *jagad-guru* because, as the perfect *ācārya,* you are responsible for teaching everyone in this degraded Age of Kali, by your personal example, how to execute the *yuga-dharma,* the recommended process of religion for this age. You have fulfilled the prediction by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu that His holy name would be known and chanted in every city, town, and village. Anyone who sincerely takes to this process of hearing and chanting the holy names of the Lord quickly develops a higher taste and gives up the taste for material activities. This higher taste gradually leads to the development of our dormant love for the Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, may your unlimited glories as Lord Kṛṣṇa’s perfect ambassador become known to the entire world, and may we always remain fixed in this memory of you by keeping your instructions alive in our hearts and continuing your mission within your ISKCON.

Your aspiring servants in Ottawa, Canada.  
(written by Guru-prasāda)

## Panama

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda kī jaya!

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, perfection in devotional service is found in Your Divine Grace.

Simply remembering your name automatically eases our minds. Pondering upon your transcendental activities gives us enthusiasm (*utsāhāt*); seeing your ever-fresh lotuslike face immediately inspires confidence in us (*niścayāt*); hearing your words, your preaching, brings us patience (*dhairyāt*); hearing your melodious transcendental chant (*śravanam*) inspires us to continue repeating the holy name of the Lord (*kīrtanam*) and reminds us that its the best way to remember Kṛṣṇa (*viṣṇoḥ smaraṇam*); by observing your perfect renunciation (*saṅga-tyāgāt*) and how you so faithfully followed the path of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja (*sato vṛtteḥ*), we feel inspired to serve Your Divine Grace throughout eternity.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all of us pray that your merciful glance never leaves us.

Your servants in Panama.

## Paris

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

You exemplify the perfection of the spiritual master, fixed in knowledge of the scriptures and their practical application according to time and circumstance, always following in the footsteps of the previous great *ācāryas,* expert in ever increasing the ocean of mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You are an ocean of compassion for all conditioned souls and are ever fixed in pure *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

On this, the year for celebrating the Centennial anniversary of your appearance in this world, devotees everywhere in the world are searching their hearts and minds for ways to attempt to repay you for having saved them from the clutches of the fearful material energy. Every sincere devotee wishes to be instrumental in fulfilling your vision of a world revolution that will shower the nectar of the ocean of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy upon all conditioned souls. Your disciples and granddisciples are performing amazing feats of transcendental wizardry in a determined effort to serve your mission. May their great enthusiasm and example serve as a reminder of our own inadequacies and inspire us to greater effort in the service of your transcendental vision for a unified world under the banner of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your servants in this *yātrā* are humbly begging for the mercy of your divine lotus feet. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, we dream daily of so many great accomplishments we could bring as an offering at your lotus feet. But as we struggle in various ways to serve and please you, we know instinctively that the gift you hanker most for is purity of the heart. Be merciful unto us and give us the strength to renounce all worldly pretense and foolish posturing. In spite of our resistance, make us humble and dedicated servants of your lotus feet. Only then will our many offerings have any meaning and give you pleasure. Take away all vainglorious attempts to impress you and give us sincerity instead. Only then will we be able to fulfill your greatest desire: To see pure devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa blossom like a beautiful lotus flower sprouting from the ocean of Kali-yuga.

Give us the intelligence to distribute your books in greater and greater numbers, for they are an injection of *bhakti* into the social body. When spirit souls become convinced by your transcendental message, may we welcome them properly in your family and train them as you have taught us. Pure devotional service in the mood of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is no longer a hidden treasure. You have brought it out in the open and distributed it all over the world. May we humbly carry our share of your great legacy: “Preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness and be happy.” Such is our poverty that even on your anniversary we must beg from you. With tears in our eyes we humbly request your blessings. Please empower us to be faithful representatives of the great example you have set for the whole world to follow. Do not deny our humble request on this most eventful day of your Centennial appearance. *Hā hā prabhu karo doyā, deho more pada-chāyā*. Under your shelter we wish to go to war in your transcendental army; without your protection we are as helpless as Arjuna was at the beginning of the great Battle of Kurukṣetra.

Your servants, at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Parisīśvara, in Paris, France.

## Penang

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We, your spiritual grandchildren in Penang, are humbly attempting to glorify you on the occasion of your Centennial celebration.
