Of course, we are most unqualified to do so because not only don’t we have the purity but we are also drowning in the pool of material existence. The scorching heat of material desires is simply burning up our tender devotional creeper. Nevertheless, we are embarking on this humble attempt because just by remembering you we feel so much purified, and this remembrance is a soothing balm to our tender devotional creeper. Therefore we beg you to please accept our humble offering at your lotus feet.

How could words possibly describe the sacrifice you made for us? Here we would like to quote a passage from a lecture found in the book *Fortunate Souls,* by your sincere disciple Dānavira dāsa,

“The spiritual master says, ‘I am the servant of Kṛṣṇa. Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and serve Kṛṣṇa.’ And he helps the disciple to stay on the path of godly life. And if there is some falldown in the course of performing our activities, he is always there to forgive and help us on the path again. He is always forgiving.

“The responsibility of the spiritual master is that he must accept the reactions for the sinful activities of his disciples. We cannot serve Kṛṣṇa directly because we have to be qualified to serve Kṛṣṇa. The qualification is that we have to be pure in heart. The only way we can become pure in heart is by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and by performing service to Kṛṣṇa. At the same time, we cannot perform service to Kṛṣṇa, because He doesn’t accept service from those who are not pure.

“Therefore, the role of the *guru* is that he intervenes on behalf of the disciple, appealing to Kṛṣṇa, ‘My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, these are all Your parts and parcels. Somehow or other they have been overwhelmed by the material nature and have become sinful, and their hearts have become polluted for so many lifetimes. Now, I know that You want them to return back to You. But then the only way they can return back to you is to chant Your holy name with a pure heart and serve You with a pure heart. So please, if you feel like doing it, then absolve them of all sins. Give them an opportunity to serve You so that they can begin the path of devotional service. Let me take all the sins. I am already so fallen. It will be no loss to You if I have to stay here in this material world some more, but give them the opportunity to return back to You.’ That is the responsibility of the spiritual master.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have so mercifully taken the sinful reactions of your disciples and given them the opportunity to be purified. Your most benevolent gift to us (the most sinful rascals), however, is the wonderful and unique opportunity to participate in the Lord’s pastime of *saṅkīrtana*. Lord Caitanya says in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,*

“I am the tree of love of Godhead. I am also the gardener. As a gardener it is My duty to take care of the tree and its fruits and to market them. I cannot eat all the fruits Myself. And I alone cannot give them out to everyone. Therefore I want everyone to help Me give out this *prema-phala,* this fruit of love of Godhead. This is My pastime.”

In creating this worldwide *saṅkīrtana* movement, you have most mercifully given us the opportunity to help Lord Caitanya distribute His teachings. If even the greatest sinner helps the preaching activities of ISKCON, knowingly or unknowingly, he becomes the most fortunate recipient of Lord Nityānanda’s mercy. This unique gift that you gave the conditioned souls—the rare jewel of participation in the pastimes of Lord Caitanya, the most munificent form of the Lord—is unparalleled in the history of Vaiṣṇavism. His Holiness Lokanāth Mahārāja therefore glorifies you as the *yugācārya*. You are, indeed, a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra,* the personification of Lord Nityānanda’s mercy and compassion.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, here we are in one corner of the world, isolated and deprived of the association of the Lord’s devotees. On the order of your servants we are simply trying to push on this movement. We ourselves are most ill-educated and lame. Your lotus feet are our only guide. We can simply pray for your blessings so that our humble attempts to follow the instructions of your servants will be successful. Your servants have given us the task of spreading your message to everyone here in Penang. You wanted this. You took on the greatest austerity, sacrificing the association of Lord Caitanya and His associates in Goloka, and came down to this miserable material world to spread love of Godhead. We therefore fall at your lotus feet begging for your mercy so that we will be blessed with a drop of that austerity. We cannot repay you. We can only sincerely try to serve you life after life, birth after birth. But still we will remain indebted to you, the most glorious master.

The insignificant servants of your servants in Penang, Malaysia.

## Perth

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your divine appearance. On the occasion of this most blessed event of your Centennial Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, please accept this offering from your disciples and granddisciples in Perth, Australia. Just as the viceroy is empowered to accept offerings on behalf of the king, so you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the pre-eminent representative of Vyāsadeva for this age, can guarantee that whatever we offer to you will go to Kṛṣṇa via the *paramparā* system. Pure, clear water is transparent and free from contamination, like the heart of a pure devotee, in which the Supreme Personality of Godhead is visibly manifest. Because you have this transparent quality, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have been able to give the shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet to anyone who seeks the soothing rays of the shelter of your lotus feet. In return you are able to act as the transparent via medium and deliver our offerings to Lord Kṛṣṇa without any impediment or personal motivation.

It is stated in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.7.44),

*svacchaḥ prakṛtitaḥ snigdho mādhuryas tīrtha-bhūr nṛṇām*  
*muniḥ punāty apāṁ mitram īkṣopasparśa-kīrtanaiḥ*

**“O King, a saintly person is just like water because he is free from all contamination, gentle by nature, and by speaking creates a beautiful vibration like that of flowing water. Just by seeing, touching or hearing such a saintly person, the living entity is purified, just as one is cleansed by contact with pure water. Thus a saintly person, just like a holy place, purifies all those who contact him because he always chants the glories of the Lord.”**

As we are about to bathe you in the sacred waters collected from one thousand holy places, we are reminded that all these holy places are actually assembling before you and taking shelter at your divine lotus feet because you are able to purify all these holy places single-handedly: *tīrthī-kurvanti tīrthāni*. You have stated that the holy places and the river Ganges purify only after prolonged use, but one moment’s association with a pure devotee is immediately purifying, for one will be enlivened to inquire about his real self-interest. We are able to keep ourselves in the best of the holy places by hearing the transcendental activities of the Lord from the lips of such a pure devotee as you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and by endeavoring to follow your instructions.

You visited Perth once, in May 1975, and performed various *līlās* here for a total of ten days. During that time the devotees heard from you various nectarean instructions concerning your vision for establishing a Kṛṣṇa conscious temple. Now, after twenty-one years, we are coming to you on this auspicious day and praying before you to give us the purification and dedication necessary to act as instruments in your hands for the fulfillment of Lord Caitanya’s mission here in Perth.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants in Perth, Australia.

## Philadelphia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

I remember when you came to Philadelphia for our Ratha-yātrā, in July 1975. At that time our temple was a small, old four-bedroom farmhouse with tarpaper shingles, sitting on a little piece of land on a small block of Germantown twin-houses. You stayed across the street in our recently purchased *gṛhastha āśrama,* one half of a twin. The neighborhood wasn’t great, and you encouraged us in our search for a new building in a better location, even complaining of the constant barking of the many neighborhood dogs.

The temple was so small that it was hard to accommodate all the visiting devotees. Besides filling the tiny men’s and women’s *āśramas,* devotees were camping outside in vans and even neighbors’ porches. Many had to stay some distance away in the large house of a congregational member. When you would return from your morning walk, all the devotees greeted you by chanting and jumping up and down on the front porch; one morning part of the porch fell through. During *Bhāgavatam* class, there were as many devotees outside on the wrap-around porch vying for a glimpse of you through a window as in the temple room. We *pūjārīs* would simply stay in the Deity room after the greeting of the Deities, sitting down inside the chain, rather than give up our spot for your *darśana*. As it was usually the custom for the devotees to ask you what you thought of a temple after a visit, we were worried about what you would say about Philadelphia. That was the era of stiff competition to please you, and word would spread. What you said, Śrīla Prabhupāda, was that “Everyone is very eager to hear.” And certainly we couldn’t have been more pleased to hear that!

I feel very honored to write this humble Vyāsa-pūjā homage on behalf of the Philadelphia community of devotees, as I can see that being eager to hear has continued as a quality of our community. Your words were really a benediction upon us.

I was reminded of your words about the Philadelphia temple during the conference in Detroit last November, when scholars from around the world gathered to honor your Centennial. For three days the attractive setting of the Detroit temple, the lovingly prepared and tasty *prasādam,* and the assemblage of devotees and friends combined to create an especially warm and uplifting atmosphere. This already wonderful atmosphere was transported to a completely different level during a talk given by Santośa Prabhu. His topic was “Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Contribution to Devotional Music.” He would have us listen to little sections of music, demonstrating a particular *rāga* or tune and making some comment. At one point he played the “Happening” tape of you leading the Hare Kṛṣṇa *kīrtana*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when your voice entered the room it displaced the air! The moment was heavy, and all around one saw eyes filling with tears. No one moved or made a sound; we were stunned. When Santośa turned off the tape, again we breathed. But for several more moments there was absolute silence. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were there among us, having entered through our ears to sit within our hearts. The conference went from wonderful to transcendental.

Later that evening, when some of us were remarking on the extraordinary effect of your chanting, Brahmānanda recalled your early instructions to always give full attention to the holy name (unlike when one uses tapes as background music for other activities.) Of course, one could argue that there were many auspicious aspects to that conference. But the fact remains that it truly became a blessed event when we all listened eagerly and with full attention to your sweet chanting of transcendental sound.

If we can learn to hear from you purely, Śrīla Prabhupāda, then we can follow in your footsteps in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every town and village. I pray for your blessings upon us all that our eagerness to hear continues.

Eventually, history will tell how the single most important event of the twentieth century was your causeless, merciful kindness in making Kṛṣṇa consciousness available to the fallen souls. Because of your love for Kṛṣṇa and your unlimited compassion, you came to this material world to rescue us fallen souls. Each one of us who have been blessed by your gifts of deities, *prasādam,* association of devotees, Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy, service to Kṛṣṇa and His devotees, and, most important, the holy name can best honor you by giving others your association. This can be done if we hear your instructions and follow them.

It is certainly tempting on Vyāsa-pūjā day to only emphasize how extraordinary you are, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and how extraordinary were your activities. Certainly it is proper for us to do so But I also want to emphasize your humanity. We can remember an old man, self-described as coming to the West at the fag end of his life, eventually landing on the lower East Side of New York, going out daily to a nearby park to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa without any apparent immediate success. We can remember the heart attack on the *Jaladuta,* the stroke so soon after beginning your ISKCON society, the theft of your manuscripts, and the many setbacks you experienced in establishing temples, especially the temple in Bombay. Hearing of the many difficulties and impediments only serves to emphasize your faith and determination, and it encourages us to overcome the impediments to our own service. If we emphasize your greatness to the point of placing you in some unreachable category, then we lay the groundwork for failing you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you told us to “become just like me.” If we say, “Yes, but Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa. I am just a fallen conditioned soul,” then we give ourselves an excuse to avoid the challenge of becoming pure ourselves. Under the guise of humility we let ourselves “off the hook” from hearing and following your instructions. Certainly you did not have the best material to work with: Western meat-eating hippies in most cases. But your faith in the holy name and your commitment to the order of your spiritual master kept you steady in rescuing us. Now we must continue. Our success is actually your success.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us that we may follow in your footsteps, become pure devotees of Kṛṣṇa, and help others become Kṛṣṇa conscious. Only in this way can we truly glorify Your Divine Grace.

Your servants in Philadelphia. Pennsylvania, USA.  
(written by Saudāmaṇī-devī dāsī)

## Port Moresby

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace. All glories to you, who are continuously giving us your association through your transcendental books, lectures, and sweet *bhajanas*.

On this supermost auspicious occasion of your 100th appearance day celebrations, we find ourselves most unqualified to glorify you.

You are so merciful toward the conditioned living entities that you have given us your Centennial celebrations as a wonderful opportunity to glorify Your Divine Grace, expand the Kṛṣṇa conscious preaching, and advance in devotional service.

If you had not left Vṛndāvana at such an advanced age for hedonistic New York City, tolerating heart attacks and so many other difficulties, where would we be now?

It is simply mind-boggling to contemplate your steadfast determination to serve the order of your Guru Mahārāja and your deep compassion upon the fallen souls. Never before has the world been so fortunate as to have the association of such a personality.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, at this time, we would like to reaffirm our commitment to show our love for you by cooperating with each other within ISKCON to deliver your mercy to the whole world. We will be making a special effort to strengthen our own spiritual lives as well as distribute your causeless mercy in every direction.

We pray that we may always have the shelter of your divine lotus feet and your wonderful ISKCON movement.

Your fallen servants in Port Moresby, Papua New Guinea.

## Potomac

From a one-man humble beginning  
on Second Avenue  
to a great worldwide society,  
you saw this movement through.

Near the Lower East Side Bowery,  
Amidst winos, bums, and thieves,  
In the heart of New York City,  
You planted divine seeds.

Around the world some fourteen times,  
To lands both far and near,  
Jet-age Parivrājakācārya,  
You never displayed fear.

When most older men sit idle,  
You crossed the raging sea  
To deliver fallen miscreants,  
And somehow you saved me.

You said Vṛndāvan was your residence,  
Your office in Bombay,  
Māyāpur for worship,  
But there you did not stay.

Born under a jackfruit tree,  
Old Calcutta aristocracy.  
One astrologer did foresee  
The work you’d do across the sea.

When you chanted Hare Kṛṣṇa  
With the hippies and the young,  
Who could then have imagined  
The miracle you’ve done?

You led us all in *kīrtan,*  
Your melodies so sweet.  
In the crowds sometimes the people  
Would cry out openly.

Everywhere you went each day  
People stood in lines to say,  
“Obeisances and homages to  
Kṛṣṇa’s representative.”

You put faith where there was none,  
You opened our hearts and minds,  
You changed our darkest destinies  
With Kṛṣṇa’s name, so sublime.

You made so many disciples  
From the ranks of unqualified men.  
The hippies and unhappys  
Raised their arms when told to CHANT!

Great scholars and philosophers  
Gathered when you called  
To discuss the realm of Gokula  
And hear Lord Kṛṣṇa’s words.

You built so many temples  
All around the world.  
You made the holy name of Kṛṣṇa  
A common household word.

Whenever you got money,  
You used it to print books.  
Not a penny or a farthing spent  
Unless you thought it good.

You made so many great ones,  
Devotees big and small,  
From all the world’s professions,  
Who heard your divine call.

You brought love were there was none,  
You gave such light and hope.  
No misery could touch us  
Wearing Kṛṣṇa’s divine cloak.

In this dark Age of Kali  
You showed us the way home.  
Now we’re serving Lord Caitanya  
In all nations of the world.

We’ll chant your name in reverence.  
It was you and you alone  
Who came into this darkened world  
And took us all back home.

Devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
Personal and complete.  
No other path attracts us;  
We worship your lotus feet.

Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!

Dedicated to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda on this Centennial anniversary of his birth (1986–1996).

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants in Potomac, Maryland, USA.  
(written by Tapasya dāsa)

## Rāḍhadeśa

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet, upon which you traversed the whole planet saving the distressed souls like ourselves by offering us the highest benediction of love of Godhead. On this most auspicious day of your 100th anniversary, we are fortunate to be able to make an attempt to glorify you, for the life of one who glorifies a pure devotee like you becomes perfect.

In the year 1971, in London, one day your newly initiated disciple Tīrtha-śrāddhaka dāsa entered your room after spending the whole day collecting for his *dakṣiṇā*. He placed the bag of coins on your desk and offered his prostrated obeisances. When he got up to leave, you gave him a big smile and said, “You follow four regulative principles, chant sixteen good rounds every day, and follow all my instructions, and I promise you that at the time of death I will take you back home, back to Godhead. ” From that day on he carried those words close to his heart, and every time he gave a class he would repeat them, passing on his faith and conviction to the devotees he was preaching to. Little did he know he had to follow those instructions just a few years more, because in 1977, just two weeks before your disappearance, he left this world with the conviction in his heart that you were taking him home.

Now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, many years later we are still here, trying to follow these instructions, which at times seems to be difficult. We lose faith every now and then, we get discouraged or complacent, we become weak or think there must be something higher or more complicated than just following you. We don’t always think going back to Godhead is our only goal, and we don’t always realize that you are the most important person in our lives. We seem satisfied with our peaceful and basically sinless lives. Our family members and occupations are important to us, and we get quite distracted trying to live in and run your ISKCON society. Then again we will remember you and come back to this same conclusion—that by following you we can make it this lifetime. You taught that we have all the necessary instruments—a tongue and ears—so we have no excuse.

Once in 1976 at the New Māyāpur farm in France, you were sitting under a tree preaching to us. I was coming and going, as my small baby kept making noise. Then a devotee asked a question: “In the *guru-pūjā* song we sing, ‘You are my spiritual master life after life.’ Śrīla Prabhupāda, does this mean that if we don’t make it this lifetime you will come back to save us?” You answered firmly, banging your fist on the little bookstand before you, “You make it this lifetime, don’t make me come back.” Again we must remember you gave us all the tools. We have to understand how important it is to follow you and not get distracted by the agents of Kali.

To become your disciple, it is not enough to take initiation from you. We have to deserve it by taking to your discipline.

“Disciple” means “strict follower of the disciplines given by the spiritual master.” Just as a doctor can help a patient only if the patient follows the prescription and diet the doctor gives, so you can save us only if we strictly follow your instructions. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us everything, and we have a lifetime to practice accepting your gifts. You are an ocean of mercy, and you rain it down on us without restriction. May we develop this faith: that if we follow you, you will take us back to you after our time in this body is over.

Your grateful and aspiring disciples of Rāḍhadeśa.  
(written by Nikuñjavāsinī-devī dāsī)

## Rome

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“Knowledge of the Absolute Truth is not very easily understood by ordinary, less intelligent men, but if the spiritual master is kind enough to the disciple, however unintelligent the disciple may be, then by the divine grace of the spiritual master everything is revealed. Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura therefore says,** ***yasya prasādād,*** **by the mercy of the spiritual master, the mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead,** ***bhagavat-prasādaḥ,*** **is revealed.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **3.25.30 purport)**

So please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, accept our humble and respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. You gave your entire life as an example of this kindness. Of the hundred years allotted the human form of life in Kali-yuga, you used all eighty-one of yours triumphantly satisfying the will of the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa. In a lifetime of transcendental activities you revealed to us eternity, and these pastimes have become a door to the Absolute for all humanity, for all time to come.

As the manifested representation of the Supreme Lord, you received and presented the instructions of the Lord as they are—as given by the disciplic succession—guarding their purity and protecting them from adulteration. By your compassionate association as the most outstanding learned *brāhmaṇa* Vaiṣṇava, you were able to clarify all our doubts regarding the Absolute Truth. And you not only clarified all our doubts but simultaneously demonstrated by your personal example the perfection of the brahminical characteristics.

“Such a person, who knows the purpose of the Vedic injunctions, who can employ the principles laid down in the Vedic literatures and who teaches his disciples in that way, is called an *ācārya*.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.29.32 purport)

*Ācāryopāsanam:* one should worship an *ācārya,* a spiritual master who knows things as they are. The spiritual master must be in the disciplic succession from Kṛṣṇa. The predecessors of the spiritual master are his spiritual master, his grand spiritual master, his great-grand spiritual master and so on, who form the disciplic succession of *ācāryas*. (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.29.17 purport)

In this way you offered yourself to us with such humility, declaring that your only merit was that you presented this knowledge as it is, without any modification. “In pure devotional service, the only motive should be to please the Supreme Personality of Godhead. That is not actually a motive; that is the pure condition of the living entity.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.29.8 purport)

We are sure of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as our authentic guide but not of our capacity to follow, so please compensate for our deficiencies so that our integrity may soon be restored to it’s pure condition.

We beseech you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to give us your blessings so we may conquer over the false ego, so we may remain united, collaborating together to carry on your mission. Here in Rome, by your grace, we are realizing one of your desires of a self-sufficient farm community twenty-five miles from the metropolis. And in our efforts, the refuge of your lotus feet has been the solution to all the unexpected obstacles presented by the material energy. Also in accord with your desire, to amplify the preaching activities in important cities such as Rome, we are ambitious to satisfy you by following your design of a cultural revolution. In a city where visitors come from all the world to research religion and culture, we wish to offer worthy facilities where your divine presentations of the true culture and religion of *sanātana-dharma* may be discovered.

Your mercy and the mercy of Śrī Kṛṣṇa are infinite, especially in this year of your Centennial, when all Vaiṣṇavas on this planet are endeavoring to present their special offerings at your lotus feet. If we succeed in offering you the fruits of these projects by the year 2000, which will be celebrated here in Rome with the participation of more than 40 million visitors, it will only be by your causeless mercy, without which we are hopeless.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your unworthy servants in Rome, Italy.

## Rostov

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet! All glories to you!

It’s very hard to find words of gratitude that are really worthy of you. It’s very difficult to describe your divine qualities, whether in Russian or English. It’s impossible to appreciate all that you are doing for us, and hardly anyone has realized it.

Thank you for books, in which we find Kṛṣṇa! Thank you for *prasādam,* which is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa! Thank you for our spiritual masters, your disciples who give us Kṛṣṇa! Their love for you is able to melt the heart of the most materialistic person. The way they are serving you inspires us greatly.

They so carefully give us everything they’ve gotten from you, so that there’s no doubt about how important every detail of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is for us. Their only desire is to satisfy you. They simply ask us to help them in this mission and explain that this is the only way to make progress in our spiritual life.

You’re the favorite disciple of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, because you fulfilled his dream of preaching in the English-speaking countries and performing devotional service. Is it possible to thank you enough for such a gift? Thank you!

Eternally at your feet, we remain  
Your devotees in Rostov, Russia.

## St. Louis

**100 YEARS!**

“One hundred years!” the cry is made, a rally to come forth  
to focus our thoughts, words, and deeds on events like none before.

Yet let us take a moment’s time and ponder earlier ways,  
to study what has brought us to this glorious celebration day.

A view of qualities embodied in the form of one  
whose personage is likened to Gaurāṅga’s rising sun.

Determination—staunch and firm to push the mission on  
despite the field of obstacles put out by *māyā’s* storm.

Commitment—faithful, true, and loyal to the great *ācāryas’* vision  
of giving the message of the *Bhāgavat* to all this planets residents.

Self sacrificing—what to say of one who gave his energy, his time, his life  
to raise all those who suffer from the throes of their Kali-yuga plight?

Visionary—one who sees beyond the frame of present time and space  
to temples, books, and communities spreading Lord Caitanya’s grace.

Nurturing—the blessings that were held within that kindly smile and praise  
encouraged all who followed him to easily abandoned sinful ways.

Humility—the gift that’s given to those whose greatness lies  
beyond the scope of thinking in personal terms of “me,” “my,” and “I.”

Prolific—in both written and the spoken word his never-ending voice  
let forth that transcendental sound for which we all rejoice.

Serving—although his satisfaction was seen to by any one of a hundred hands,  
his desire was to see the Deity established and served in each and every land.

Thrifty—the use of everything for Him, the true and real creator,  
not lightbulb, postage stamp, or water were wasted for His endeavor.

Poetic—that sweet-sounding voice that glorified the Lord and His abode  
described it with such beautiful words that there we want to go.

Strict—Western *yogīs* gave advice: “Adjust your teachings to the modern days,”  
yet you would never change the path: “It’s not the Vaiṣṇava way!”

Respectful—the honor that you gave to others, seeing Paramātmā there,  
was equal to scholar, priest, or mother with child, you treated all with care.

Silent—no babble of the daily world, the talk of wealth and fame,  
just the message of Caitanya giving out the holy name.

Cleanliness—no filth was there in mind or form, or even in the words,  
and this enabled you to move with pureness through this world.

Surrendered to Kṛṣṇa—the list so far is culminated in this item here,  
to act in such a way that faith allows one to be dear  
to Him who guides and shows the way that brings one shelter full,  
by depending on Him in all things done and acting for the ultimate good.

For these qualities are why we cry aloud in jubilation great,  
most thankful for the benefit and merciful hand of fate.

A hundred years! A hundred years! A hundred years! and more  
please allow us to keep serving you and stay free from Māyā’s door,  
to render service to you as a humble servant would,  
to act as you would have us do to spread this mission on  
to every town and village of this planet—to give the name  
and tell of you who spread it wide, let us also tell of your fame.  
The glories of your qualities that make us love you so,  
to help you lay a broad wide path that all the world may go,  
a path that leads all back to home, the place where we belong,  
and joyfully we all engage in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtan* song.

Praying to be your humble servants and followers, we remain  
Your disciples and granddisciples in St. Louis, Missouri.

## Saltillo

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Param Guru Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu predicted, “In every town and village of world, My name will be preached.” (*Caitanya-bhāgavata, Antya* 4.126). Śrīla Locana dāsa, in his *Caitanya-maṅgala,* predicted that a *sena-pati,* or “great general,” would come and accomplish this mission. Also, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura described in vivid detail, “Soon a time is coming when the chanting of Kṛṣṇa’s name will be heard in England, France, Russia, Germany, and America,” in this way predicting your *līlā* of spreading the *harināma-saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya-deva throughout the planet.

But these are not the only prophecies. In the *Bhāgavata-māhātmya* portion of the *Padma Purāṇa,* Śrīla Vyāsadeva himself has explained the historical development of the *bhakti* tradition. Personified Bhakti talks about different places in India she had been—Draviḍa-deśa, Karnataka, Maharashtra, Gujarat, and Vṛndāvana. Then she says she will go to another place, but she does not name it. If she had meant that she were proceeding to another place within India, she would have named the place, as she had done in each previous case. But Bhakti merely said, *idaṁ sthānaṁ parityajya videśāṁ gamyate mayā:* “I will leave this country and go abroad.” (*Videśām* means “not this country” or, in other words, “another country.”)

So Bhakti has been traveling abroad through you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Your Divine Grace was the *ācārya* who completed the mission of Lord Kṛṣṇa Caitanya. When Lord Kṛṣṇa was present on the earth He personally killed some *asuras* and liberated them, but He wanted to give the credit to His beloved devotee Arjuna, and so He empowered him to kill millions in the Kurukṣetra Battle, and they were all liberated. In the same way, Lord Caitanya spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement only in India, but He empowered you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to save the whole planet. This is your great glory. Therefore Your Divine Grace is the Lord’s “matchless gift” to this world.

Now, in the absence of your *vapu* form (so-called absence, since in truth you are always with us), there have arisen some agents of *māyā* who are trying to deviate us from the real, pure *bhakti* process that you presented to us. It is very important to remember that *bhakti* is a transcendental science. Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura has sung: *rūpa-raghunātha-pade-haibe ākuti-kabe hama bujhaba se yugala piriti:* “When I’m eager to understand the literature given by the Gosvāmīs, then I shall be able to understand the transcendental loving affairs of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.” The six Gosvāmīs were great devotees of Kṛṣṇa, yet simultaneously they were tremendous intellects. Their writings systematically analyze the stages of the devotional path, such as *vaidhi-bhakti* and *rāgānuga-bhakti,* along with the various levels and nuances of devotion ecstasy. Therefore there is an intellectual integrity and rational structure to the practice of *bhakti;* it’s not just a “gut feeling,” an emotional thing.

“The highest devotion is attained by slow degrees by the method of constant endeavor for self-realization with the help of scriptural evidence, theistic conduct and perseverance in practice. These preliminary practices of devotion \[*sādhana-bhakti*\] are conducive to the realization of loving devotion. \[Loving devotion\]—than whom there is no superior well-being, who goes hand in hand with the attainment of the exclusive state of supreme bliss and who can lead to Myself.” (Lord Kṛṣṇa in the *Brahma-saṁhitā,* 59–60).

Furthermore, “The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is conducted under the supervision of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī. The Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas, or Bengali Vaiṣṇavas, are mostly followers of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, of whom the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana are direct disciples. … One must very seriously follow in the footsteps of the six Gosvāmīs—Śrī Rūpa, Sanātana, Bhaṭṭa Raghunātha, Śrī Jīva, Gopāla Bhaṭṭa and Dāsa Raghunātha. … In all spiritual affairs, one’s first duty is to control his mind and senses. Unless one controls his mind and senses, one cannot make any advancement in spiritual life. … Advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness depends on the attitude of the follower. A follower of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement should become a perfect *gosvāmī*.” (*The Nectar of Instruction,* pp. *vii–viii*)

There is no doubt that Your Divine Grace made a wise selection of sacred texts to translate. The *Bhāgavatam* is of central importance not only for the Caitanya-sampradāya but for all Vaiṣṇavas and the Vedic tradition as a whole. One Vaiṣṇava scholar said, “There is no exaggeration in saying that the *Bhāgavatam* is the most popular religious book in India.” The importance of the *Gītā* is already known. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* we can find brilliant crystallizations of the philosophical, theological, aesthetic, and poetic works of Gosvāmīs, presenting a full compendium of Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava tradition. Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja states,

“This part of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s pastimes is most confidential. One can derive benefit quickly only by faith; otherwise by arguing one will always remain far away.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya* 8.309)

“By hearing the talks between Rāmānanda Rāya and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, one becomes enlightened with the transcendental knowledge of the mellows of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. Thus one can develop unalloyed love for the lotus feet of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya* 8.307)

Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu,* the basis of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s summary study entitled *The Nectar of Devotion,* is a textbook of the devotional science, an exposition on the philosophy of *bhakti,* and a study of devotional psychology. It is one of the most important of the Gosvāmīs’ works, so we are fortunate that Prabhupāda made this work available in English.

Of all the *Upaniṣads,* the *Īśa* has the most pronounced theistic tendency. It’s a must for Vaiṣṇavas. Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa wrote a commentary on it. The *Kṛṣṇa* book is a deep and scholarly study of the life and times of Lord Kṛṣṇa. And then there are the thousands of tapes, lectures, small books, letters, etc. All these writings and expositions are completely in line with the *paramparā* yet present Kṛṣṇa consciousness in a way comprehensible to contemporary Westerners. As far as fidelity to the important Vaiṣṇava commentators is concerned, as you said, you presented Kṛṣṇa consciousness “as it is.”

*śruti-smṛti-purāṇādi-pañcarātra-vidhiṁ vinā*  
*aikāntikī harer bhaktir utpātāyaiva kalpate*

**“The so-called spontaneous** ***bhakti*** **that rejects the** ***vidhi*** **and** ***sādhana*** **from the** ***Vedas*** **and** ***Upaniṣads,*** **as well as the** ***smṛti*** **(*****Gītā*****), the** ***Purāṇas*** **(*****Bhāgavatam*****), the** ***Pañcarātra—*****this activity simply disturbs the society.” (*****Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu*** **1.2.101,** ***Skanda Purāṇa*****)**

Rūpa Gosvāmī called this so-called devotion *sahajiyā:*

“Some of the disciples strictly accepted the orders of the *ācārya,* and others deviated, independently concocting their own opinions under the spell of *daivī-māyā*. The order of the spiritual master is the active principle in spiritual life. Anyone who disobeys the order of the spiritual master immediately becomes useless. There is no need to name those who are useless. I have mentioned them only to distinguish them from the useful devotees. Paddy is mixed with straw at first, and one must fan it to separate the paddy from the straw.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 12.9–12)

Therefore, O great spiritual master, Your Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, O personification of *bhakti,* please let us become fully surrendered to you and follow your orders. There is no other way for us to be bona fide *kṛṣṇa-bhaktas* and your authentic followers. On this glorious Centennial anniversary of your appearance, we pray to you to please let us be your instruments for carrying the *saṅkīrtana* movement to every soul in this country through the media of your books, *prasādam, harināma-saṅkīrtana,* and your sacred *saṅga,* ISKCON. Please bless us to remain under your sacred protection in ISKCON without falling down. Let us attract more and more souls to your lotus feet.

Thank you, thank you very, very, very much for your sacred mercy. Thank you so much, our dear and venerable Param Guru and savior, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandir in Saltillo, Mexico.

## Scotland

Śrīla Prabhupāda kī Jaya!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet.

We are fallen and full of innumerable faults and foul qualities, yet still out of your sweet and causeless mercy you have accepted us and are engaging us in the highest possible activities. We cannot truly comprehend just what you have done for us and the entire human society. But we are hoping that the Lord will help us understand your great compassion upon us all, so that we can try to glorify you in a way that may be at least a little befitting your truly elevated position.

By your causeless mercy we are now situated in a very fortunate position. You have given us the very beautiful Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi to worship and take shelter off. By your great compassion upon the fallen conditioned souls, now the Lord has come to deliver the fallen souls of this gross, disgusting land known as Scotland. Your devotees here are very excited and grateful for this great mercy. To us it seems very significant that you should make such an arrangement on your Centennial year. Scotland has been starving for such mercy, and now in this great year you have poured the abundant rain of your mercy on this desertlike land. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!!

Never before has such mercy been seen! A complete transcendental inundation of the entire planet is going on at present. Souls being saved everywhere! Books continue to go out by the millions, and in so many different languages. Nāma-haṭṭa is increasing throughout the entire planet! *prasādam* distribution is going on in great abundance world-wide! Great temples are being built in India and other countries! More and more contacts are being made with people who are considered important in the material world! Festivals are being staged on an increasing scale throughout the world! The list is actually endless. This is all happening due to your great mercy and compassion upon the fallen souls of this world. You are actually Jagad-guru!! You are actually the Patita-pāvana!! We are proud to be connected with you and would like to be a source of pleasure to you, but as you know we are weak and very often need much reassuring. Please never kick us insignificant souls away. We ask this mercy, but we are already aware that you are so merciful that you will not do such a thing. We want to keep trying to serve your great mission, and we are very sorry for all our stupidity, our offenses, and our inability to serve you as we should. But we pray that by your mercy we will come to the level of offering unmotivated and uninterrupted devotional service for your pleasure.

All glories to you Śrīla Prabhupāda!! Please forgive this attempt to glorify you; it is certainly not up to a very high standard.

Your insignificant servants in Scotland.

## Sierra Leone

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our insignificant obeisances among the thousands and thousands of devotes offering *daṇḍavats* at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, the *sena-pati* of Lord Caitanya’s army. You have conquered Māyā’s stronghold, the Western world, and are freely distributing t Lord Caitanya’s great gift, Lord Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, to every land.

This year is the most blessed event, the Centennial of your appearance. During your Centennial celebration in India, as we saw all the devotees who had come from different parts of the world to inaugurate your Centennial activities, your grand Puṣpa-samādhi in Māyāpur, and the exposition of preaching activities in every town and village during the festival, we could see that you are indeed re-appearing in this particular year, answering the call of your sincere devotees, who are saying, “Only you can lead us.”

Our only hope is always you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Everyone’s fervent prayer this year is that ISKCON will succeed by your divine grace. So many seminars, workshops, and conferences are being organized to help us refocus our attention and rededicate our lives to your mission. Several of your leading disciples remind us of your attributes through their glorious service toward accomplishing your vision for ISKCON, through their *Bhāgavata* discourses and their personal *sādhana*. We look at them with amazement, overwhelmed by their desire and determination to make your movement a natural continuation of Lord Caitanya’s pastimes by discovering many holy places, establishing memorials at some of the previous *ācāryas’ tīrthas* or *kutirs,* constructing temples, and pushing on various other expansion programs. Again and again we thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being the wonderful inspiration and constant guide of such great, great souls who are carrying on this mission.

However, it is not that our society has become so big that we can stand independently of you. We must always remain helpless before you. You are our Kṛṣṇa conscious past; without you our present Kṛṣṇa consciousness has no meaning whatsoever, and you are also our only hope for the future.

We, your granddisciples here in Sierra Leone, are actually ashamed that we have not got much to offer you, much less than what many other *yātrās* are offering you for your Centennial year. However, we are trying to focus on the two ways you mentioned that we can please you most. One is to develop love for Kṛṣṇa, and the second is to distribute your literatures profusely. While we are trying to distribute your literatures, which are the basis of the well-being of humanity at large, we must confess our inadequacy in developing love for Kṛṣṇa. And this reflects on our quality of service, which is ever wanting.

Therefore, on this auspicious hundredth anniversary of your appearance, we fervently pray that, with the force of your Centennial events, the seeds of *bhakti* you planted in our hearts through your *vāṇī* and through the association of your leading disciples will grow luxuriantly into a mature tree bearing the fragrant blossoms and ripened fruits of our Kṛṣṇa consciousness—and all for the benefit of more conditioned souls who will come and take shelter at your lotus feet.

May your fame spread all over the three worlds.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!!

Your granddisciples Sierra Leone.

## Silchar

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your mood of distributing *bhakti,* which is just like that of Nārada Muni! All glories to your Centennial!

I am an immature devotee and can hardly glorify you and the transcendental service you performed to save the condition souls. One of your Godbrothers, Śrīpād Niketana Mahārāja from Orissa, once told me that your activities are nondifferent from those of Nārada Muni. This is undoubtedly true, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is impossible to thank you sufficiently for having come and saved me from hell and for engaging me in your mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are definitely the topmost devotee of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You are an ocean of mercy, a quality visible only in Kṛṣṇa and His pure representatives like yourself.

No one can know you, because you are unlimited. It is best not to try to know you by unnecessary mental speculation. Therefore kindly bless us here in Silchar so that we can worship you with causeless devotional service. Please help us become mature and useful servants of your society.

The servants of your servants in Silchar, Assam, India.  
(written by Gaura-Nitāi dāsa)

## Singapore

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your transcendental self.

This is your Centennial year, and we are seeing that all over the world everyone is striving very hard to spread your glories. But unfortunate as we are, we are not able to glorify you to our heart’s content. We feel very much like Vasudeva when Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa was born: he could not perform the birth ceremony due to fear of King Kaṁsa. He then mentally performed the necessary ceremony. Although we are also in a somewhat similar plight, we are trying our best to see if we can spread your glories in this part of the world.

We are determined to see that somehow or other your books become established here, since you often remarked that books are the basis. Door-to-door book distribution is difficult here now. Recently, on our trip to Māyāpur, we heard from one of our dear Godbrothers that in a situation like this you remarked that it is best to open bookshops. This information has renewed our enthusiasm to spread your divine message. I feel like I did when you came to me in a dream and remark that it is best to have a restaurant on Orchard Rd., one of the prime areas here in Singapore. By that desire of yours we opened a Govinda’s restaurant on Orchard Road, and it is successful.

Although you may not be physically present here with us, incidents like this prove that you are never far away. So again we are seeing more and more of your desires been fulfilled, enabling us to further help you push this great movement on.

Always praying for your supreme guidance, blessing, and mercy, we remain  
Your eternal servants in Singapore.

## Sofia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Among the huge number of Vyāsa-pūjā homages you are receiving on your hundredth anniversary, which are expressing genuine attachment and devotion to Your Divine Grace, this is one meager attempt by your inexperienced spiritual grandchildren in the Sofia ISKCON temple to glorify you on this all-auspicious day. Although this attempt is just like trying to light up the sun with a candle, please kindly accept it.

The pearls of your instructions that we find in the vast ocean of wisdom you have left us are the only valuable things we possess. We have neither remembrance of personal exchanges with you nor some significant realizations about your greatness. As in the process of Deity worship, in which we decorate the Deity of Lord Kṛṣṇa with jewels and flowers provided by the Lord Himself, so in our attempt to glorify you we have nothing better to offer you than the treasure of your own words.

In a lecture you speak about receiving initiation from your Guru Mahārāja: “In 1933 I was officially initiated, and my only qualification was when I was introduced to my Guru Mahārāja for initiation, so Guru Mahārāja immediately said, ‘Yes, I shall initiate this boy. He is very nice. He hears me very patiently. He does not go away.’ So that was my qualification … the philosophy which was speaking at that time. Personally I could not follow what \[he\] was speaking, but still I liked to hear. That was my habit. … So we got this information from His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and that knowledge is still going on. You are receiving through his servant. And in future the same knowledge will go to your students. This is called *paramparā* system.”

Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, now we, your fortunate granddisciples, are receiving this powerful knowledge from your bona fide disciples. And along with the knowledge we are also receiving the duty to distribute it to all the people who are in ignorance.

In the *Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta* we read: “Śrīla Prabhupāda became emotional, and tears came to his eyes. ‘It is the duty,’ he said. ‘Caitanya Mahāprabhu has explained, *para-upakāra:* Save them! If it is not possible to save everyone—as many as possible. This is human life. This is Kṛṣṇa consciousness—to save these who are in the darkness. Don’t think,’ he said ‘Kṛṣṇa consciousness is my profession. I am getting a living, food, and shelter!’ That is just what the Indians are doing. Not like that. This *para-upakāra*. That is Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Then Kṛṣṇa will be very much pleased.”

In another lecture you describe how “even in my young age or early age—it was Kṛṣṇa’s grace—even amongst my young friends, I was considered the leader. \[Shouts of ‘Jaya!’ and ‘Haribol!’\] In my school days, in my college days, in my private friendship, some way or other I became their leader. And one astrologer, sometimes he read my hand. He said in Hindi, *kukum calena*\[?\]. *Kukum calena* means ‘Your hand speaks that your order will be executed.’ \[Devotees: ‘Jaya!’\]”

As we endeavor to execute your order to save those who are in darkness, we pray for your mercy, because Bulgaria is not an easy place for preaching. The people have been well-trained in atheism during the communist period, and now they are burdened with heavy economic crises that cause them constant anxiety.

I am sure you remember the letter from Ghanaśyāma dāsa (now Bhaktitīrtha Swami) that you received in Vṛndāvana in June 1977:

**Tamāl Kṛṣṇa:** He says the next country is Bulgaria. “So as to give the maximum number of people a chance to participate in Lord Caitanya’s saṅkīrtana program, as we are so fallen and incompetent …”

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** The chanting, did they give?

**Tamāl Kṛṣṇa:** In Bulgaria?

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Hm?

**Tamāl Kṛṣṇa:** Not yet. No devotees have ever entered.

Your main concern is whether the chanting is given. You can be sure, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we will give them chanting, as much as possible. By Kṛṣṇa’s grace, this year we will hold Hare Kṛṣṇa festivals in ten towns and also the first Ratha-yātrā festival in Bulgaria. The goal of the book distributors this year is 108,000 books and magazines distributed in 100 towns and villages. We also hope to successfully develop a Food for Life program. And, of course, the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa is the basis, for this is the way to be always connected with you. As you explain, “There is no question of separation. The sound vibration fixes us up together, even though the material body may not be there. What do we care for this material body? Just go on chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, and we will be packed up together.”

We were not so fortunate to see you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, while you were still on this planet. We never spoke with you. We know you only from your books, from the tapes of your lectures, and from the books about you. But we are like those young persons whom you spoke about in a conversation recorded in Vṛndāvana on July 1, 1977:

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Last time, when I was there, so many young persons, they were giving me, “Thanks, Prabhupāda.” They were feeling some enlightenment. Do you remember?

**Tamāl Kṛṣṇa:** Yes, Prabhupāda.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Out of their own accord, they were giving, “Thanks, Prabhupāda. Thanks, Prabhupāda.” They were not my disciples.

Due to Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement, we also are not your disciples, but we are trying to serve your disciples. And by preaching ourselves we are also feeling enlightenment, and so we can simply say, “Thanks, Prabhupāda!”

Your humble servants in Sofia, Bulgaria.

## Stockholm

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

One hundred years ago you appeared on this planet, a pure devotee of the Lord with a special mission to save the most fallen ones. As they had never been presented before, you presented Śrī Kṛṣṇa and His *Bhagavad-gītā* for us Westerners in such an accessible and sublime way that even the lowest could understand. First you made humans out of animals by introducing the four regulative principles, and then you introduced these newly made humans to spiritual life. Breaking through our false ego like a transcendental warrior, you stunned our senses with *prasādam, kṛṣṇa-kathā, kīrtana,* and beautiful Deity worship. Then you corrected our perverted intelligence and minds with your books and lectures, delivering the essence of the *Vedas,* which we could never have grasped on our own. In this way you broke into our hearts, which had been heavily fortified with various bodily misconceptions.

As we try to follow in your footsteps, we realize how difficult it is to reach the conditioned souls. What you have done! In your presence, to reach Kṛṣṇa consciousness was indeed easy. Māyā was so far away.

And now? You are still here, speaking through your books, my Godbrothers, and their disciples. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for leaving behind the wonderful gift of devotional service, the foremost processes of which are the chanting of the holy name and the distribution of your books. Anyone trying to go out and distribute these books can find out on his or her own what an amazing purification takes place in his heart. Even a dull, spiritually retarded person can become the most inspired and blissful devotee of the Lord by surrendering to the distribution of your books.

You have made everything so simple and easy. But are we so simple? Can we simply take what you have given? If not, life will not he simple. Falsely thinking that “simple” means “primitive,” many people are missing the mercy coming through your instructions. You showed us how to be simply happy instead of complicatedly miserable.

As time goes by, with the speedily progressing degradation of Kali-yuga, your books emerge as the only light in the darkness created by demons and incompetent leaders. Punished by Māyā, they may perish along with their followers.

But there are still a few souls who may reach out for your mercy. Here in Stockholm we have a big Govinda’s restaurant and temple. Even though people here are impersonalists, so introverted that they cannot even look into one’s eyes, you are still gradually changing their hearts. Sometimes I can see you sitting at one of our tables in the restaurant, smiling graciously at the public. Even here at the end of the world, your mercy is shining through

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for making us into humans, and from humans into devotees of the Lord. Once a reporter asked you if you could perform some miracle. In reply, you simply pointed to the devotees present before you and said, “This is my miracle.”

Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is indeed a miracle how you are changing people’s hearts. Thank you for allowing me to be part of this most amazing display of your mercy.

The devotees at the Hare Kṛṣṇa Center Stockholm, Sweden.  
(written by Maṇidhara dāsa)

## Sydney

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

One hundred years today have passed  
Since you appeared in Bhārata-varṣa  
Upon Kṛṣṇa’s request, this nice boy,  
Gour Mohan De’s bundle of joy.

So sweet they were, those early years:  
Family nurture, no fears.  
You pulled the cart and worshiped too;  
All arts you learnt through and through.

College was there, marriage came,  
Job was landed, and Gandhi’s gain.  
But soon your purpose was to be revealed.  
Sarasvatī Mahārāja no longer concealed.

You sat equal on his seat, innocent to know,  
But *siṁha-guru* dealt the death blow.  
He kicked out all assumptions procured  
And requested you, your life’s mission secured.

Some time went by—*dīkṣā* came,  
Guru left, his request the same.  
Great struggle ensued to get it going;  
Years went by, not much growing.

Family life, taste dried up.  
“Tea or me” stated over a cup.  
What to do? No reason to remain.  
Guru’s words rang in your brain.

Mahāprabhu’s proclamation, before was granted:  
“In every town and village My name shall be chanted.”  
But what did it mean? was it quite literal?  
Maybe in India, where people are liberal.

But Bhaktivinoda and Bhaktisiddhānta  
Had other ideas, not to go asunder,  
Their faith in Caitanya’s words so strong.  
But who was to do, and before how long?

“Bhaktivedanta” your peers had proclaimed;  
*Sannyās* by force next was gained.  
Then up the gangplank, all barriers now gone  
On *Jaladuta* you sailed, seas forlorn.

At Boston you docked,  
Needless to say shocked  
At Kali’s great gains,  
Yet, oh, so many pains!

Not wasting precious time,  
Your movements always sublime,  
You appeared to drift here and there,  
Transcendental to all, you seemed not to care.

Bowery bums moved aside;  
Second Avenue, your pride.  
Seekers coming to catch the swami’s beat;  
Those Tompkin’s Square Park *kīrtans,* what a treat!

And then it took off in all directions you traveled:  
San Francisco, Montreal, London—in the holy name they reveled.  
But even more so, they knew they had a friend in you:  
Someone they could trust, and a perfect guide too.

Before you knew it dozens of centers were there,  
Translating progressing, opportunity now there.  
“If you ever get money, print books.” Those eternal words still sounded;  
Your inspired disciples, with book bags they bounded.

Your smile grew wider as the books were sold,  
Smashing *māyā* everywhere. Then demons got bold.  
The court heard the case, “brainwashing” to decide.  
Judge Leahy’s verdict: ISKCON bona fide!

Books, temples, and farms, your goals now complete.  
“Manage them all” and let me retreat  
To write my books and not be disturbed.”  
This you did, Śrīla Prabhupāda, unperturbed.

And so it finally became quite clear,  
The rest, too sad for us to hear,  
Of how you chose to leave us all;  
To Kṛṣṇa’s eternal abode was your call.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all your labor spent.  
You gave us everything, and you never did relent.  
We owe you everything; there is no way to repay.  
We pray to be able to continue serving you day by wonderful day.

Your humble servants in Sydney, Australia.  
(written by Mandapa dāsa)

## Tampa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories, all glories, all glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON.

An *ācārya* does not just mean one who teaches by example, literally speaking. It means one who is especially empowered by Kṛṣṇa and who performs extraordinary devotional activities that lead multitudes of devotees down the path of liberation.

Please allow me to explain further:

A—Always engaged in Kṛṣṇa’s service without interruption.  
C—Caitanya Mahāprabhus very dear servant.  
H—Highest quality of devotional service manifested.  
A—Authorized pure representative of Kṛṣṇa.  
R—Renounced from all tinges of personal motivation.  
Y—Youthful enthusiasm to serve Kṛṣṇa.  
A—Auspicious life in which every moment is utilized for the purpose of delivering the message of Godhead to the fallen conditioned souls.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, there’s no question that you are everybody’s *śikṣā-guru* in ISKCON. To hear your message, so sublime, and accept it in our lives is the real initiation into Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Please allow me to explain:

G—Greatness emanates from your personality—“I am much more than a king.”  
U—Undisturbed by so-called happiness and distress in the material world.  
R—Relevant preacher in all time and circumstances for the benefit of your disciples and disciples of your disciples.  
U—Understanding all Vedic conclusions, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are unbewildered in the confrontation of all challenges.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have so expertly manifested the ideal example of *guru* for many future generations to come and more. Every moment that you breathed on this earthly planet can be studied and contemplated for the benefit of going back to Godhead.

We are thus forever indebted to you for allowing us to sit with you and hear the divine nectar from your golden voice. Our humble prayer is to please give us (ISKCON) special protection so that we don’t lose this golden opportunity to go back to Godhead in this lifetime, and let us take with us as many millions of souls as we can.

Your servants in Tampa, Florida, USA.  
(written by Vivasvān dāsa)

## Tauranga

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The Supreme Personality of Godhead enjoyed so many wonderful transcendental pastimes 5,000 years ago in Bhārata-varṣa. For the benefit of mankind He spoke the *Bhagavad-gītā* to Arjuna, and He gave the process for surrendering to Him.

Freely giving himself to all the conditioned souls in the form of His name, He incarnated as Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu about 4,500 years later.

In 1896 Kṛṣṇa made another arrangement. Śrīla Prabhupāda took birth to pick up Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* mission in a big way.

Jaya Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda kī jaya!!

Obviously Śrīla Prabhupāda has fulfilled the prophecy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu in propagating the chanting of the holy names of the Lord around the world. This *yuga-dharma,* this transcendental chanting, is being heard across all nations on the planet and, indeed, way beyond. “In every town and village” is becoming a reality due to the unconditional mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda. He stepped into the *paramparā* and took the world by storm.

We devotees in Tauranga, New Zealand, are developing a meaningful community to offer to Śrīla Prabhupāda as an example of “simple living, high thinking.” While endeavoring to develop a practical level of self self-sufficiency on the land, we are also committed to developing personal relationships and honest dealings.

We are confident that this project will provide a significant example of the blending of “simple living” and “high thinking” for people in general in the near future. Lost souls will need every bit of assistance they can get as Kali’s agents move into the final stages of their demonic global plans.

Just as Kṛṣṇa and Arjuna kept the demonic intentions of Duryodhana at bay, Lord Caitanya and Śrīla Prabhupāda are effectively foiling the advance of Kali and his agents.

May the Centennial commemoration of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance be a major contribution toward spreading his name, fame, and glories all the way to Kṛṣṇaloka.

Prabhupāda—“The master at whose feet all masters sit.”

In the name of our very dear spiritual father and preceptor Śrīla Prabhupāda, we Tauranga devotees offer our humble obeisances and kindest regard to all fellow Vaiṣṇava siblings.

All glories to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

Your servants, the Tauranga Gaurāṅgas, in Tauranga, New Zealand.

## Tel-Aviv

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your *siddhānta* is *nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe,* and this is best done by book distribution. Because this is the foundation of everything you gave us, we want this preaching and book distribution to be our link to you in this year of your most auspicious Centennial celebrations.

You are the only one who teaches pure personalism (*anyābhilāṣitā-śūnyaṁ jñāna-karmādy-anāvṛtam*). Indeed, everyone else’s teaching is impersonalism. All the different religions of the world are nothing but covered impersonalism, which is the root of materialism and is the cause of all the problems in the world. Thus, the only real solution to all the degradation of Kali-yuga is to establish your teachings all over the world through mass book distribution and vigorous preaching. To bring the Vedic culture, the culture of pure goodness, to the regions of *rajas* and *tamas* required the greatest flexibility and realization, so that the essence could be transmitted in its most powerful form. And this is your incredible contribution. By teaching us “work now, *samādhi* later” you saved future generations of devotees from falling down to a *sahajiyā* mentality. By stressing *vaidhi-bhakti* as the main feature of our movement, you laid the foundation for a unified ISKCON. In this way, your glories are immeasurable.

The only thing we pray for is to be allowed to participate in this wonderful spiritual revolution, just as the spider helped to erect the bridge to Lanka, and Śrī Rāmacandra kindly accepted his insignificant service.

Your servants in Tel-Aviv, Israel.  
(written by Guṇa-avatāra dāsa)

## Ticino

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! By the impetus of your Centennial, devotees all over the world are enthused to preach more than ever before, and thus millions of people are being eternally benefited due to your unlimited mercy.

We are writing this letter from Ticino, Switzerland, where our center is situated within a valley at the southern side of the Alps. Your mercy is reaching the most remote corners and valleys of this planet. Your glories are sung throughout the three worlds (*ebe yaśa ghuśuk-tribhuvana*), and you are famous in all the three times: in the past, in the present, and certainly in the future. Even in the past you were famous because your glories are eternal.

In the spiritual world, when Nārada Muni visited Lord Caitanya, the supremely merciful Lord prophesied your appearance as His *senapati-bhakta,* His leading commander-devotee. One hundred years ago, in the year of your birth, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura said that soon an empowered preacher would appear, and four hundred years ago, Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī predicted your appearance by saying that empowered devotees would visit all countries which were not visited by Lord Caitanya; thus people of all nations will become devotees. This vision was realized five hundred years after Śrī Caitanya’s appearance, by Your Divine Grace:

“To deliver people in regions throughout the universe who could not meet Him, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu personally entered the bodies of pure devotees. Thus He empowered living beings \[His pure devotees\] by manifesting in them so much of His own devotion that people in all other countries became devotees by seeing them.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya-līlā* 2.13–14)

But not only the Supreme Lord and the pure devotees have foreseen your glorious appearance. Even some non-Vaiṣṇavas have indicated it, as we have recently discovered. Although we have full faith in your divine personality, which does not need any confirmation from mundane sources, still it is inspiring to see how others in the past have predicted the influence of Lord Caitanya and His pure devotee. For your delight, as well as for the inspiration of all devotees, we would like to mention the most amazing example of such a prophecy in this historic Vyāsa-pūjā book of your Centennial.

Five centuries ago, no one less than Europe’s most famous prophet of all times, Michel Nostradamus (1503–1566), composed some verses in honor of Lord Caitanya and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, His most intimate representative.

In chapter 3, verse 94, of his famous *Book of Prophecies,* Nostradamus writes (in a translation from the French):

After five hundred years and more people will take into account  
Him who was the ornament of His time.  
Then, all of a sudden, great clarity will be given  
To make them, in this very century, very happy.

Till now, all interpreters of this verse thought that Nostradamus was describing himself as “the ornament of his time,” who will be recognized only after five hundred years. This interpretation cannot be right because Nostradamus received the highest praise during his lifetime, and it is impossible that people would become happy simply by recognizing Nostradamus five hundred years after his death.

However, this verse says that in the century of Nostradamus there was indeed an “ornament of His time.” Who was living on this planet in Nostradamus’ time who would deserve to be called “the ornament of his time” and who would be recognized as such only five hundred years later?

Till now, this question has baffled all interpreters. Their confusion is not amazing, since the questions above can be answered only by those who know the *yuga-avatāra,* Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu! As He started His *saṅkīrtana* movement, Nostradamus was being born on the other side of the globe. Now, five hundred years later, Lord Caitanya’s glories have been revealed to the world by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And now, certainly, “all of a sudden,” by “taking into account” Lord Caitanya, people are getting clarity and happiness “in this very century.”

Another verse (chapter 1, verse 96) is directly related to the above-mentioned verse (3.94), even numerologically. Literally it says,

He who will be in charge of destroying  
Temples and sects that were distorted by speculations  
Would rather harm stones than the living entities  
By his language, which is ornamented by ears fully satisfied.

In the French language these complicated expressions rhyme, and they contain hidden code words. In this verse the most obvious code word is the French expression *qu’aura,* indicating the name of Him (Gaura) whose mission the subject of the prophecy (you, Śrīla Prabhupāda) will accomplish by destroying all speculative philosophies by his language, which is harder than stone but not harmful to any living entity. Rather, everyone who will actually hear him and listen to him will be “fully satisfied.”

Those who doubt that this verse describes you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, should look at the number of the verse: 1.96. It shows the year of your appearance!

In other verses, Nostradamus predicts a new world-changing religion that will come from the East by the end of the twentieth century. Interestingly enough, in verse 9.96, which is supposed to describe the year 1996, Nostradamus writes (in a mathematically decoded version supplied by one of today’s leading interpreters):

For five hundred years are installed within the world practitioners of renunciation.  
In one hundred ideas enter the shaven-headed, travelers of purification.  
In due course there is a time of breakthrough to God.  
From the center enters the age of the shaven-headed.

Speculating about whom this verse might refer to, the leading interpreter guesses, “Maybe there will be some new religious group within Islam whose members will shave their heads.”

But it is obvious: In 1996, after being present in the world already for 500 years, the shaven-headed devotees of him who was born in ’96 are entering “in 100 ideas.” This is even a prediction of your 100th anniversary celebration! And then, in this very century, people will find clarity and happiness due to their reawakened awareness of Lord Caitanya, which again is due only to the tremendous pure devotional service performed by Your Divine Grace.

Now, Nostradamus also predicts many catastrophic events. Whatever may happen in this phase of Kali-yuga, one thing is obvious to all of us: Whatever is auspicious in this world has been brought by Your Divine Grace. The Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, will reveal this truth to the world more and more. Then the suffering living entities will be relieved from all anxieties, and the entire world, full of bliss and gratitude, will chant the names of Kṛṣṇa and His pure devotee.

Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant servants at New Rāmakeli, Ticino, Switzerland.  
(written by Ātma-tattva dāsa)

## Tokyo

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we offer our most humble obeisances unto Your lotus feet. One hundred years ago today you appeared in this world to give the suffering living entities the greatest gift, Kṛṣṇa consciousness. What a sublime gift! At 4 a.m. we rise, take a shower, and go to the temple. We pay our obeisances. We have *maṅgala-ārati* and dance in ecstasy as we chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. Thank you for this wonderful gift. We chant sixteen rounds (at least), hear the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, and take *prasādam*. We try to engage the conditioned souls in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your mercy is inconceivable. We pray we can continue serving you forever.

An astrologer said that when you reach the age of seventy you would cross the ocean, become a great preacher, and open one hundred and eight temples. “I planned that I must go to America. Generally they go to London. I was simply thinking how to go to New York. I was scheming whether I shall go this way, through Tokyo, Japan, or that way? Which way is cheaper? That was my proposal. And I was targeting to New York always. Sometimes I was dreaming that I have come to New York.”

We offer our most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda. One hundred years ago today you appeared in this world to fulfill the prediction of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu—that the holy name would be heard in all the towns and villages of the world. In New York you chanted the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* and very quickly it spread all over the world. We go to Shinjuku, near the busiest train station in the world. There is so much *māyā,* so much noise, broadcasting the ways of sense gratification. There is an ocean of conditioned souls. We begin to chant, we play the *mṛdaṅgas* and *karatālas*. We distribute *prasādam,* we hand out our flyers, “Come and join us.” “Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy.” You said, “I have seen Japan, which is highly advanced in machines and technology. Yet there is no real happiness there.” So we continue to chant in Shinjuku—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

We offer our most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda. One hundred years ago you appeared in this world to give pleasure to the Lord’s dear servant Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, who is the personified energy of Śrī Caitanya’s mercy, a “ray of Viṣṇu.” Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta was very fond of your writing “Absolute is sentient, thou hast proved. Impersonal calamity thou hast removed.” He told the editor of his English magazine, “Whatever he writes, publish it.” So we are trying to carry out this order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s by publishing all your books. Your books are the best preaching.

One young man recently found your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* in a second-hand book shop. After reading it he decided to become a devotee. We met a man at Harajuku recently. We showed him your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. He said, “I already have that book, as well as many other of your books. They’re great. I read them all the time.”

Your books are purifying the consciousness of the people. A great revolution is slowly unfolding.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Centennial!

Your servants in Tokyo, Japan.

## Toronto

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:

What can we say that’s not already been said  
in words more eloquent and fine,  
To thank you for the great gift you have given,  
which has made our poor lives divine?  
And yet we all know—just as with the Lord—  
it’s not merely the choice of each word,  
But what comes from the heart, the soul’s deep devotion,  
that by you both will be heard.

You’ve given yourself in so many ways  
to those who have ears to hear:  
Some know you by simply associating  
with your many disciples, so dear.  
A truly rare privilege—your personal *darśana—*  
belongs to a relative few,  
But also as precious, as often you told us:  
your instructions are the way to know you.

What you have done for these fortunate ones  
an easy description defies.  
By your kindly touch, you’ve injected hope  
into what were once meaningless lives.  
Inconceivable mercy you showered on us,
