who have no qualification.  
You gave us much more than merely salvation.

It’s hard to believe, but believe it we must—  
it’s there for us all in your books:  
The Lord’s deepest and most confidential nature  
is there for us when we look.  
“You’re not this body”—from this basic truth  
you’ve taken your disciples so far:  
To understanding the appearance and nature  
of the Golden Avatāra.

“*Gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*”  
is truly a description of you:  
Your service to the Supreme Lord  
is in the mood of Mahāprabhu.  
Not merely desiring to relish Kṛṣṇa’s love,  
you left Vṛndāvana without fear,  
To unite the lord with His lost parts and parcels.  
How could anyone to Him be more dear?

In the light of the vastness of our debt to you,  
it becomes increasingly clear  
What we all can give, to appreciate you,  
in this, your Centennial year.  
We’ve nothing you need, but something you want:  
the nurture of a tender tree  
You planted in each and all of our hearts  
by your gentle and loving mercy.

As the giver of a gift does not merely do so  
in hopes of something in return,  
You gave Kṛṣṇa to us—we’ll try to accept  
and to live this gift, each in our turn.  
*Śravaṇam kīrtanaṁ viṣṇoḥ* …  
there are various ways we can try  
To show you our thanks for opening the door  
for us to the spiritual sky.

And as your devotees, we pray to engage  
ourselves in your divine mission  
To help Lord Caitanya to spread *kṛṣṇa-prema*  
in every condition.  
We’ve hopes that our lives will be useful to you  
and will help to bring others to see  
What’s available to them—what we all can receive—  
the ocean of causeless mercy.

Our Lords, Gopīnātha and his Rādhikā,  
are here for all comers to see.  
Their service in the temple that you wanted for Them  
gives to many a taste of ecstasy.  
Your influence spreads out so far from there—  
onward and on does it go.  
The *kīrtana* is heard, the holy names ring  
in our homes all across Ontario.

“Thank you, Prabhupāda!” we’ll always be crying,  
from now to time without end—  
Our master, our guide, our *guru,* our lord,  
and most of all, or dearmost friend.

Your humble servants in New Remuṇā Dhāma, Toronto, Canada.

## Trinidad (South Center)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In these times, O Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are witnessing an upsurge of religious activities all over the world. The adherents of all the major religions are pushing their religious ideologies forward, take what it may. The modern day messiahs, missionaries, and evangelists adopt various methods to expound their philosophies. The result? Still utter chaos and confusion in society. The advocates of these various religious tenets become corrupt themselves and usually end up showing the opposite of what they preach.

But you, dear Prabhupāda, stood out as “a giant and true saint amongst them all.” You taught by your living example, practicing what you preached. Your devotion to the Lord was like a towering lighthouse set on the shore of love and devotion, where the waves of despair, godlessness, and chaos broke at your feet. You uplifted the fallen souls thrown about by these waves and gave them shelter. You gave the knowledge of how to escape this calamity by introducing the chanting of God’s holy names—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

Indomitably you upheld the light of the *Vedas* and carried love of Godhead to all corners of the world. You saved many a conditioned soul tossed in the ocean of nescience. You may not be physically present with us now, O Prabhupāda, but your spiritual presence through your books and memories will live on.

Thus:

On this 100thanniversary of your appearance  
The devotees of ISKCON pray  
That the mercy and love you showered  
Will grow with us day by day.  
So we thank you, O Prabhupāda, for your kindness  
And solemnly yet happily pray  
To cherish the memories of your presence  
Eternally on your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

The devotees in Trinidad (South Center).

## Tucson

**Desert Devotion**

It’s not enough to simply chant,  
To let things decline and say “I can’t.”  
The preaching spirit must not go adrift.  
We must be bold distributing this valuable gift.

We see your granddisciples filling in the place.  
Forgoing youthful pursuits, they also have the taste  
Through straight books and congregational preaching  
With their purity many souls they are reaching.

To further expand your glories, we heeded your call  
To open up more centers and give our all and all.  
So now in Phoenix, ISKCON has been planted,  
Being watered by books, *prasād,* and *bhajans* chanted.

We pray our desire does not wain when it’s 110 degrees (plus!!).  
Though we can still head to the Grand Canyon in our *saṅkīrtan* bus.  
Whether it’s by mountains or deserts the preaching always stays sweet;  
The mercy you are giving us here in Arizona never seems to deplete.

We must give purely to others what you have given us;  
*Total* commitment, without becoming frivolous;  
Faithful to your instructions, we pray never to swerve.  
Taking shelter outside of ISKCON? Let us *never* have the nerve!!

Most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. O Supreme devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa and most fearless preacher of Lord Caitanya’s message, you live forever in your instructions, and your followers live with you. As when you were physically present among us, your love for your disciples and compassion for the conditioned souls thrust us into the performance of the *saṅkīrtana-yajña*. The urgency to try and finish our father’s (your) business should weigh more heavily on our heads, should we truly claim to be your children. Please continue to bless these undeserving souls with the ability and empowerment to help cover the planet with *kṛṣṇa-prema,* and protect us from the snares that *māyā* puts in our way, testing our determination to serve and please you. To abandon you or your mission would be spiritual suicide for anyone who has received your merciful glance and instructions.

May your fame, and the fame of your ISKCON, continue to spread most expediently throughout this material creation. And may your prediction that “this movement will go down in history as the movement that saved the whole world in its darkest days of crisis” become fully manifest.

All glories to the most important and significant Centennial celebration this planet earth has come to witness, the Centennial Celebration of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

With full obeisances at your glorious and victorious lotus feet, we remain  
Your fallen servants in Tucson, Arizona, USA.

## Turkey

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept my most humble obeisances prostrated in the dust of your lotus feet.

I offer my respectful obeisances unto Your Divine Grace, who arose on the horizon of West Bengal on this most eventful day to dissipate the darkness of ignorance and thus wonderfully bestow benediction upon all of us, having compassion for the fallen conditioned souls of the world. As the sun drives away darkness by its appearance, similarly you have dissipated the darkness of the living beings and enlightened everyone with knowledge of the Absolute Truth. You have shown us the easiest path of liberation in this Age of Kali by giving the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. You always said, “Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy.”

O saint of saints, your greatness is so rare that no words can describe you. The greatest poets will also fail in trying to chant your glories to their full extent. Like a rising sun you have come to illuminate our dark animalistic life, to bring us to the light of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. With your mercy you have broken through the stonelike covering of our hearts and planted the seed of devotion, and although our hearts were as dry as the desert, you have watered them and made the seeds grow.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, for a long time I was searching for truth. As a student in the Moscow Institute of Economy I read many philosophical books, including the *Vedas, Upaniṣads,* and *Bhagavad-gītā,* and I even practiced *hatha-yoga* and followed the four regulative principles, but I could not understand the meaning of the Vedic philosophy. Finally, by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, I met the ISKCON devotees and they gave me *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. Just by reading the introduction I understood that this was the book I had been searching for for so long. By reading your transcendental purports I realized the meaning of Kṛṣṇa’s words, and I understood that for so many years my time had been wasted reading other books. I also thought that you were my contemporary, living somewhere on the planet. After reading *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* I ran to the devotees and asked, “Where is Bhaktivedanta Swami? I want to meet him.” I could not believe it when they told me you had already left the planet. I was so upset because on reading your book I became completely convinced of your presence. Kṛṣṇa had been on this planet 5,000 years ago and Caitanya Mahāprabhu 510 year ago, but Your Divine Grace had been so recently here. Due to my bad *karma* and misfortune, I missed your personal association. I am greatly lamenting. Still, by your causeless mercy you left for us your books and your pure representatives so that we can follow and progress on the path back to Godhead. Anyway, I was fortunate enough to follow your divine instructions and obey your pure representative, Harikeśa Swami. Since Your Divine Grace is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa, so Harikeśa Swami is nondifferent from you. I realized that without proper guidance I could not follow your instructions and satisfy you.

I always remember you said, “My Guru Mahārāja told me, if you ever get money—print books.” Also Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura told you that he was ready to sell the temple’s marble to print books. By your causeless mercy I have realized that printing and distributing your books is my only mission.

Now I am pushing this mission on in Muslim countries like Turkey, Jordan, Iraq, and Egypt. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for leaving some places where this fallen soul can engaged in your mission and rectify himself. Thank you for establishing this mission meant to save all living entities. In the course of time I realize more and more that this is the only remedy and hope.

By reading your books I always feel your presence and guidance. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me a life’s mission: spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Muslim countries. It is blissfully very difficult, just the proper challenge for my capacities. Actually, I may not be able to do it properly in one lifetime, so to satisfy you I am prepared to take birth as long as necessary just for this purpose. I am only praying to Lord Caitanya and His representatives you have created to kindly guide me in this matter. If they help me, then I think it will be possible to do something even in such places.

No one can understand the activities of a pure devotee of the Lord. The nature of his appearance and his pure motive, which forms the basis for his every expression, are a mystery to the common man. Like the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you appear differently to different people according to their different mentalities. For the research scientists Your Divine Grace is a kick in their faces. For the self-made gods and *gurus* you are the real standard whose character the Vedic scriptures sing of. To the scholars you are in a class of your own: honorary degree, Ph.D. For the pure devotees you are a *mahā-bhāgavata*. To the Kali-yuga statesmen and politicians you are impeachment personified. For the people in general, you are the only hope. For your Guru Mahārāja, you are his pride and joy. For Kṛṣṇa, you are His dearest servant.

Our only hope is to be able to always remember your instructions, to always take refuge under your lotus feet, your beautiful lotus feet that are our Govardhana Hill. Just as the inhabitants of Vṛndāvana took shelter there from the anger of King Indra, so we are taking shelter from the attack of *māyā*.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, as a bona fide spiritual master, you are a pure devotee, an empowered incarnation of the Lord. Who, then, can fully understand or properly glorify you? We can but apply ourselves diligently to the great task you have set before us, dedicating ourselves to the realization of your fond hopes for the spiritual welfare of all beings through the worldwide propagation of the *saṅkīrtana* movement, and endeavoring with great determination to worship you by following your profound and timeless instructions. Then by Kṛṣṇa’s grace we may deserve the intelligence to know you as you are, for certainly no ordinary man can understand a pure devotee by his own endeavor. No, our position is to sincerely try to install you as the great-grand *guru* of all the people of the world by just repeating your message without adulteration. That alone will make a suitable glorification of Your Divine Grace and a suitable Vyāsa-pūjā offering at your lotus feet.

On this holy event of Vyāsa-pūjā day, we were supposed to make an offering unto your lotus feet, but we have come to beg your mercy. Please give us the strength to remain pure and allow us to assist you in your mission of fulfilling Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s injunction—*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*—all over the world.

Your servants in Turkey.  
(written by Airāvata dāsa)

## Udhampur

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Kṛṣṇa! Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotuslike feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who are saving the whole world by spreading pure love of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, all over the planet and saving the bewildered souls like ourselves by giving us shelter in your center here in Udhampur.

This year is a very special year for all of us. It is the hundredth anniversary of your appearance on this planet, and everyone in the movement is glorifying your divine personality. We too have the desire to do so, but sometimes we feel that without your mercy it is not at all possible. For example, if we want to see the sun, only with the help of sun can we see him. This is also the case with us in our attempt to fulfill our desire to glorify you, the purest devotee of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

You are like a desire filling tree, so we must humbly pray at your lotus feet to enable us to glorify your divine personality here in Udhampur. Only if people come to know about your glories will they be benefited, because you came to this planet to arouse pure love for the Supreme Lord, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, in forgetful souls like ourselves. Attaining this love is possible only if we perform Kṛṣṇa conscious activities such as glorifying pure devotees of Lord like Your Divine Grace. So please make us qualified to do so. You are our only hope, dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda kī jaya!

Your insignificant servants in Udhampur and Katra, India.

## Umea

*tapyante loka-tāpena sādhavaḥ prāyaśo janāḥ*  
*paramārādhanaṁ tad dhi puruṣasyākhilātmanaḥ*

**“It is said that great personalities almost always accept voluntary suffering because of the suffering of people in general. This is considered the highest method of worshiping the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is present in everyone’s heart.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **8.7.44)**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace’s Centennial Vyāsa-pūjā. The above verse reminds me particularly of your causeless mercy because at an advanced age you left the shelter of Vṛndāvana and traveled to the modern junglelike cities of the West, where animallike humans are suffering the insurmountable struggle for existence, where only the fittest “survive” again and again in the cycle of birth and death. In August of 1976, Your Divine Grace visited Europe for the next-to-last time. At that time, as a *bhakta* newly on *saṅkīrtana,* I got Your Divine Grace’s *darśana,* together with hundreds of devotees. One night I got a shift at guarding your door with stick in hand and heard through the door that Your Divine Grace was talking. I wondered if your were, as usual, dictating the *Bhāgavatam*. Since you finished the Eighth Canto shortly thereafter on the September 1 of that year, I can only guess that you were working on the Eighth Canto. In any case, I know for sure that you are like Lord Śiva, who acted for the benefit of the endangered people at great personal risk. You gave us suffering souls the shelter of the *Bhāgavatam,* although at that time your were physically not well.

Now, twenty years later, I want to again guard Your Divine Grace by alertly avoiding the pitfalls of mental speculation and fruitive activity in my life.

Please bless me that I can slacken my grip on materiel comforts and take up the mission of spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement, which is the only remedy for the suffering of the masses of fallen souls in this barren wasteland of North Sweden.

Your servants in Umea, Sweden.  
(written by Yogīndra dāsa)

## Uppsala

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Overflowing Mercy**

O aspirant devotee,  
please don’t despair;  
seek that ocean of mercy  
unheard-of, so rare,

High waves of compassion  
billowing everywhere,  
the suffering of man  
unable to bear,

sweet currents of grace,  
a forgiveness so great,  
past sins and faults  
never contemplate,

deep flood of affection,  
immense loving care,  
true heart’s friend indeed,  
his wealth gives us to share.

A drop of that ocean,  
with flavor so sweet,  
brings eager desire  
to serve at his lotus feet.

There his merciful glance,  
full of humility shine,  
greatest fortune of all,  
bestows spiritual love divine.

Behold! Our beloved Prabhupāda,  
ever-shining guiding star,  
foremost carrier of mercy  
from that infinite reservoir.

Your menial servants in Uppsala, Sweden.  
(written by Prapūjaka dāsa)

## Vicenza

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet. This glorious anniversary of your appearance in the material world is another opportunity for us—disciples, granddisciples, and candidates for devotional service—to count our blessings, admit our faults, request your mercy, and express our desire to serve you. Your miracle is still going on: right in the midst of Kali-yuga, Vaiṣṇavas of the highest standard engage daily in the service of the Lord, completely against the mainstream of today’s atheistic society. Thanks to your great faith in the words of your spiritual master, the sound of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa can be heard day and night, chanted by thousands of sincere devotees all over the world. It is because you were able to control the urges of speech, mind, anger, tongue, stomach, and genitals that so many souls are now saved from the clutches of *māyā*.

You always acted according to the essential meaning of the scriptures, having thorough knowledge and realization of them. And that’s how the spotless *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* came into existence in so many languages, in so many houses. You perfectly realized the Supreme Personality of Godhead (*tattva-darśi*), and you were able to present Him in a suitable manner according to time, place, and circumstances, without ever acting out of vanity or selfish interest. Your heart was always full of compassion for all living entities, without ever exploiting your followers, and with your exemplary attitude of servitorship to the Lord you were able to transform the character of your disciples, for whom you were always full of affection.

You did not desire wealth, fame or service from us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but you were peaceful, fully in control of your senses, satisfied in the activities of your devotional service. With your deep faith in the Lord, you established so many “embassies” of the spiritual reality, real shelters for everyone, open gates to Vaikuṇṭha. Expert judge, sweet in speech, pleasant to look at, youthful-looking, expert in defeating those opposed to devotional service, capable of showing favor to and reprimanding your disciples, you felt gratitude for services rendered to you. How can we dare present ourselves to you, lazy, dirty, and sickly as we are? We are angry, proud, lusty, addicted to over-eating, and steeped in the enjoying mood, always coveting material desires. How can we evoke your pleasure when we are constantly critical of others and continually afflicted and lamenting? Do we really have any hope of ceasing to be miserly, malicious, devious, and envious and becoming fully capable of upholding all the vows made to you? When will the time come when we will also be able to engage day and night in the Lord’s service with our body, mind, and words?

Once when a devotee asked you what pleased you the most, you answered, “If you love Kṛṣṇa.” So now that you are not physically present and we cannot serve your physical body anymore, let us serve this dearmost desire of yours by practicing to love and serve the innocent, the devotees, Your Divine Grace, and the Lord, as well as all other living entities. Let us feel appreciation for one another’s service and acquire the trust that can bring us closer together, as you always wanted. Give us the desire to become pure so that our impurities won’t be a hindrance anymore to our unity or to our ability to represent you to others. Enable us to offer our proper respects to elders, initiated Vaiṣṇavas, and the Lord and to become gentle, polite, and spotless in conduct, without attraction for committing sinful activities and always engaged in beneficial, pleasing, transcendental work.

Falling down like an uprooted tree upon approaching you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are expressing our last request: “O spiritual master of the universe, we have been burnt by the fire of material existence and bitten by the fangs of merciless time. We surrender unto you. Please deliver us.”

Your servants in the community of Prabhupāda-deśa, Vicenza, Italy.  
(written by Citraka dāsa)

## Vienna

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Meditating on you, on your mission, and on your instructions is the most fertile way of engaging one’s mind and intelligence in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

This is proved by the outstanding results and effects of this most auspicious Centennial anniversary of the appearance of Your Divine Grace.

Even in Austria, a country that is way behind in its development of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, this week we got the opportunity to sign the purchase contract for the first building that will be in ISKCON’s possession, and two days ago we went to the airport to pick up two big trunks with Gaura-Nitāi Deities sent from Māyāpur, to be installed at the new place.

We can see from this little example that even those who are very slow can become very fortunate by your “Centennial mercy,” which seems a hundred times more intense than usual.

I am very sure that a diagram of ISKCON’s progress over the years would mark a big leap at 1996 and a strong increase in intensity.

“By your cooperation you will show your love for me.” At least to my understanding, this was the most significant change in ISKCON so far, as the devotees all over the planet were forced to work much closer together than they used to do in former years.

Through your Centennial celebration ISKCON has become woven together more tightly, and the devotees have developed a higher taste for cooperation in order to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for engaging us in your mission.

Your granddisciples in Austria.  
(written by Cakṣu dāsa)

## Villaggio Hare Krishna

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept our humble obeisances at the divine dust of your lotus feet. You came from the spiritual world to spread the sacred words of Kṛṣṇa. You traveled fourteen times around the world, forgetting your physical needs, always absorbed in pleasing the Supreme Lord. You met many important people and convinced them to accept Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Personality of Godhead; with great boldness and fine intelligence you preached the mission of *saṅkīrtana*. You spread the holy name in a systematic, wonderful, and devotional way. You are a wonderful personality; you have taught many devotees. Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.68),

*ya idaṁ paramaṁ guhyaṁ mad-bhakteṣv abhidhāsyati*  
*bhaktiṁ mayi parāṁ kṛtvā mām evaiṣyaty asaṁśayaḥ*

**“For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, pure devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come back to Me.”**

You are very dear to Kṛṣṇa, because you have created this wonderful ISKCON, which is a branch of the tree of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. This is a completely spiritual, eternal movement full of bliss and knowledge. All over the world the preaching is spreading by your mercy. Again and again I thank you very much for your mercy. I would like to be more surrendered; please allow me to receive your mercy.

You are fully surrendered to Lord Kṛṣṇa. You have destroyed impersonalism, the false concept of the Māyāvādīs. You have presented to the world the wonderful *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. These books make one feel your spiritual presence. They are wonderful books, full of light—shining books. It is so nice to study and distribute them. So many devotees have become convinced by your powerful purports. You said that if we read your books we will never fall down. Your books are liked by both small and big devotees because your books are completely transcendental, speaking right to the soul. How much love you have for Kṛṣṇa! You are able to speak about Him incessantly. You love His parts and parcels so much. By your personal behavior and example you show this love, this pure love for everyone. You have offered the most elevated conclusion: complete surrender to the spiritual master.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

All glory to the Centennial of the greatest spiritual master and pure devotee, who preached Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. !Prabhupāda.

Please let us remain your servants. Thank you.

Your devotees in Villagio Hare Krishna, Bergamo, Italy.  
(written by Durgama dāsa)

## Vṛndāvana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our respectful obeisances. We often wonder who you are. At home in Vṛndāvana you appeared as a loving father, sometimes hard as a thunderbolt, sometimes soft as a rose. One astrologer said that anyone who thinks he can read your horoscope is a fool. The stars especially arranged themselves for your appearance. You appeared here by your own free will and departed by your own free will.

As a protective father, when we first came to Vṛndāvana you encouraged us to appreciate its power to spiritually uplift us, yet simultaneously you warned us to renounce our doggish mentality, lest we become one of the Vṛndāvana dogs or hogs in our next life. May we always remain your dog, rejoicing to have such a wonderful master.

At Rādhā-Dāmodara you once bewildered both Yamunā dāsī and a monkey by conversing with the monkey in its own language, convincing it to go away and not disturb you. Another time you compared us to monkeys, saying, “As Lord Rāma crossed the ocean and brought back Lakṣmī, so did I. And as Lord Rāma was assisted by an army of monkeys, so was I.” May we always remain one of your monkey soldiers, fighting for you.

Sometimes hard as a thunderbolt, you would chastise the Vṛndāvana devotees, especially when money was wasted. When one devotee bought an excess of bananas, which quickly went rotten, you chastised him, saying, “You are a monkey because you like bananas so much.” May you always chastise us so we will learn to use our intelligence in your service.

Sometimes you were soft as a rose. While you lived at Rādhā-Dāmodara, Hayagrīva returned to take shelter of you after having broken the regulative principles. Crying, he said, “Oh, Prabhupāda, Māyā is so strong. You are so merciful and I am such a rascal.” You gently accepted him and consoled him saying, “Just don’t forget Kṛṣṇa. You have already rendered so much service, and Kṛṣṇa does not forget.” May we never forget to serve your lotus feet at every moment.

Once you were glorifying the residents of Vṛndāvana and Guru dāsa said, “I think it is better to have you as a spiritual master than to be born in Vṛndāvana.” And you answered, “That is a Vaiṣṇava attitude. I remember that wherever my Guru Mahārāja went, Vṛndāvana was there also. Since the bona fide spiritual master carries the Vṛndāvana atmosphere with him, then it’s better to be with him.” May we always remain with you in Vṛndāvana by keeping your words in our hearts.

On Rādhāṣṭamī you said, “In Vṛndāvana, everyone says ‘Jaya Rādhe!’ approaching Kṛṣṇa through Rādhārāṇī’s mercy, because however fallen I may be, if I can please Rādhārāṇī, then I’ll easily understand Kṛṣṇa.” Imbued with Rādhārāṇī’s mercy, Lord Caitanya chased after the fallen souls to capture them from Māyā’s embrace. And Rūpa Gosvāmī’s special mission is to establish the inner feelings of Lord Caitanya—His desire to give everyone His special mercy. At Rādhā-Dāmodara you said, “I feel that Rūpa Gosvāmī’s immortal grace is flowing. He is here and hearing everything.” Inspired by his mercy, you translated and commented on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. And one astrologer said, “Ink will not flow from your pen; fire will flow from it.” May the fire of your words penetrate our ironclad hearts, burning up our *anarthas* so we can see Vṛndāvana with purified eyes.

You said that Vṛndāvana is an inspiration only, but our real fieldwork is all over the world. Although Vṛndāvana is your home, you rarely stayed here, being cursed like Nārada Muni to constantly travel. But you carried Vṛndāvana wherever you went, transforming hell into Vaikuṇṭha. And you said, “I’m simply Vṛndāvana’s messenger. Vṛndāvana is revealed in the heart of the sincere devotee. In reality, it is not on any map, nor is it part of any country.”

As your humble servants in Vṛndāvana, we cannot imitate your wonderful activities, but we can aspire to follow in your footsteps. Begging for your mercy, for only you can give us the mercy of Rūpa Gosvāmī, Lord Caitanya, and Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, and keeping the Vṛndāvana spirit in our hearts, let us become messengers of Vṛndāvana, giving Vṛndāvana to everyone we meet, to dissipate the darkness covering their eyes and bring them back to their real home at your lotus feet in Vṛndāvana.

Your servants in Vṛndāvana, India.  
(written by Nārāyaṇī-devī dāsī)

## Zagreb

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of the Centennial anniversary of your appearance in this world.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, here in Zagreb, Croatia, we did not have an opportunity to see you personally present in our country, but you are so much present in your books and in your instructions. You are stressing in so many places how association with the instruction of the spiritual master is much more important than his physical presence. Therefore, reading your books and listening to your lectures is the best way for all of us to contact your divine personality.

In our temple so much stress is given to book distribution. We find that this is the best way for the wonderful message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to reach so many conditioned souls.

More and more people are becoming interested in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, reading your books and practicing the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which you have so mercifully brought from the spiritual sky.

Every day we witness how your books are changing the lives of so many people. They are acting as a torchlight in the darkness of Kali-yuga. Especially during the recent war in Croatia, many people found their only shelter in your books. The books were found untouched in the burnt houses; they were carried by the refugees as their only property. On the front lines the soldiers chanted the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and read your books. This is proof that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the only solution to the problems of this world. You have built a house—ISKCON—in which the whole world can live very peacefully.

To bring as many conditioned souls as possible under the shelter of your lotus feet and to strengthen the relationships in our great family of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, we have started the cell-group preaching program. The congregational members from the cell groups took part in previous Christmas book distribution marathons. For many of them it was the first opportunity to distribute your books. In this way we can in the future increase our book distribution for your pleasure.

We are also trying very hard to find a suitable place for our new temple, with a large temple room to receive more guests and friends and give them the opportunity to have your *darśana* and the *darśana* of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi.

Please, our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us your blessings so that we can cooperate nicely together and in this way be able to continue in spreading this wonderful *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu under the victorious flag of ISKCON.

The servants of the servants of your servants in Zagreb, Croatia.

## Zurich

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our most humble obeisances thrown in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to you, who came to this material world just to bless everyone with the highest gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness!

While reading your books, we marvel at the depth and spiritual potency of your teachings. They are the topmost literatures for mankind, attracting everybody to Kṛṣṇa. After absorbing the nectar of your sublime instructions for two hours every day, we feel fully enlivened and armed with transcendental knowledge, and we are inspired to go out and preach and distribute your books profusely. The books speak for themselves; we just have to learn the art of presenting them in an attractive way.

How is it possible that a new *bhakta* can go out and distribute hundreds of books every week? It is simply by your special mercy that a devotee gets the *śakti* to do this.

In Switzerland, approximately half the population already has received a hard-bound book. So on the street we don’t ask the people anymore, “Did you already get a book?” But rather we often ask, “How many do you have at home? Take these new ones!” The public is very eager to participate with the devotees in fulfilling your desire that every man, woman, and child have a book about Kṛṣṇa at home, and that every gentleman or lady have a full set. We are aware that most people will not surrender immediately, but still, their spiritual life has started in a very powerful way. And that is for their eternal benefit.

We may ask ourselves, “Why is Switzerland getting so many of your books?” The answer is very clear: you take special pity upon the most attached and foolish persons. As the true representative of Lord Nityānanda Prabhu, you are delivering the fallen and wretched characters first. This is surely our only qualification for getting your mercy.

Although you came to Switzerland only for short visits, your potent preaching is obviously bearing fruits, for the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is growing larger and its influence on the people stronger. You are also fully present in your dedicated disciples, who are keeping your instructions in the forefront of all the devotees’ consciousness and are providing strong spiritual guidance. We are happy and satisfied trying to serve you by assisting your empowered representatives. They are doing the actual service.

We would like to make an experiment, for which we request your blessings. We want to distribute so many books that Switzerland becomes overflooded with an ocean of books. Let us apply this medicine like a balm, massaging it into the society. Let it sink in, and then we will apply some more, until it becomes completely saturated. Then what will happen? Kṛṣṇa will appear more and more, new devotees will join, the rascals and cheaters will be exposed, and real *dharma* will be established.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, grant us the purity and spiritual strength necessary to become constantly absorbed in the wonderful practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and to thus become inspired to share this nectar with everyone we meet. Let us be part of your glorious mission and serve you obediently. You are our eternal guide and master.

Your aspiring servants in Zurich, Switzerland.  
(written by Navīna-nirada dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the Publishers

## The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust International

Our most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who are directly empowered by Kṛṣṇa to deliver the fallen souls, and who have gone through so much hardship to record His glories and show the way to attain Him. This is compassion beyond comparison. Exerting yourself in your advanced age, you have shown us, by instruction and example, how to attain pure happiness. Your are therefore sustaining our spiritual existence. We cannot imagine how the world would look if you had never come. You came at the right time, and to the right place, where you were most needed. Thank you again and again.

Although you are not physically present anymore, and although, when you were, we did not qualify to have your *darśana,* the message that you delivered on behalf of Lord Kṛṣṇa is very much alive. Today, more than ever, its remarkable potency touches the hearts of millions, and gradually it is bringing all of us closer to the real goal of life. You have made this message of Godhead especially palatable, since you have beautified it with your personal spirit and ecstasy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have made a delicious cake and invited us all for a feast. Your books are like the sponge cake, which builds it all up. Your lectures and morning walks, then, are like the layers of filling, and your personal letters are like the icing and the different decorations on top. Some may get a piece with green berries, another a piece with red fruits. Let us all munch, relishing enjoyment for the soul, and wash it down with the nectar of the holy name.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we beg you to ever increase our spiritual appetite for your words, and bless us that we never look at a different menu. Please forgive us for our shortcomings and inability to glorify and serve you. Just allow us to try harder in our service of preserving and protecting the integrity of your legacy.

Your servants at The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust International.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at the saffron mercy-particles covering your lotus feet.

On this glorious hundredth anniversary of your appearance in this world, I would like to offer you a few prayers, scriptural quotations, remembrances, and meager realizations concerning your books. The intelligence to do so has of course come from Kṛṣṇa via you, as have the substance of these offerings, so I am doing nothing but “offering Ganges water unto the Ganges.” Even so, you have explained, such an offering evokes the mercy of the worshipable personality when made sincerely. I pray for a drop of sincerity.

I first began working on your books twenty-three years ago at the old ISKCON Press in Brooklyn, a few months after joining. I had no extraordinary qualification—no degree in English, no experience with Sanskrit or Bengali. But I had a great desire to help your *saṅkīrtana* movement, especially book distribution, and feeling traumatized by my efforts to distribute books in New York City, I was overjoyed to find that my natural fault-finding propensity could be dovetailed for proofreading your books. Looking back, I can only offer millions of “Thank yous” as I think of the amazing good fortune I enjoyed by your mercy—as a new *bhakta* to be able to scrutinzingly study the *Kṛṣṇa* book and the Third and Fourth Cantos of your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* hour after hour, day after day, and at the same time assist you in your mission of book production and distribution*.* What a boon! In the course of this blissful service, I came upon the beautiful “saffron mercy-particle” purport, which perfectly describes the effect of reading or hearing your words:

“A pure devotee always engages in the service of the Lord, taking shelter of His lotus feet, and therefore he has a direct connection with the saffron mercy-particles that are strewn over the lotus feet of the Lord. Although when a pure devotee speaks the articulation of his voice may resemble the sound of this material sky, the voice is spiritually very powerful because it touches the particles of saffron dust on the lotus feet of the Lord. As soon as a sleeping living entity hears the powerful voice emanating from the mouth of a pure devotee, he immediately remembers his eternal relationship with the Lord, although up until that moment he had forgotten everything.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.20.25 purport)

I had certainly forgotten everything, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but by your ineffable compassion I was now swimming in your nectarean words and being coated with your saffron mercy-particles, and so even such a dullard as I began to remember my true position as a servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Soon ISKCON Press became the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, and in early 1975 the whole kit and caboodle of your press operation moved west from Brooklyn to Los Angeles. I was now an editor for your *Back to Godhead*  magazine, and still my main job was to help bring forth your words so that the masses could best understand them and be attracted to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Suddenly the now-famous seventeen-books-in-two-months marathon was upon us (again by your mercy), and although at first I sat serenely in the eye of the hurricane assembling BTG issues as your book producers frenetically worked twenty-hour shifts to meet the deadline, a couple of weeks before D-day I also got drafted to edit a few chapters of the Fifth Canto and the *Antya-līlā*. I saw it as Deity worship: every word was already perfect, but if I could dress your words up with a little grammatical correction here, a little spelling correction there, your words might have even more impact on the benighted brains of the Kali-yuga. There was no higher pleasure after the marathon than seeing that photo of you happily perusing your newly published Fifth Canto.

Again I can only thank you unlimited times for allowing me to dip my toe into the nectar-ocean of producing your books. I was and am completely unqualified for this service, but you tolerated my innumerable imperfections and allowed me to touch the supremely pure *Bhāgavatam* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that had emanated from your lotus mouth. I pray for a chance to work on your books birth after birth.

Time went on, and the shock of your disappearance in 1977 was almost too much to bear. That all-merciful friend and master who had saved us from countless births of agonizing suffering and bestowed upon us the priceless gift of *kṛṣṇa-prema* was now gone! That transcendental personality who poured forth *śabda-brahma* hour by hour, day after day, was now silenced! How could we tolerate this void in our hearts! But soon it became clear that you were not gone, for you lived on in your words—in your recorded lectures and conversations, in your letters, and especially in your books. It was in the crafting of these books that you put so much care and attention, so much of your unlimited store of pure *bhakti,* for you knew that it would be through these books, in all the languages of the world, that people for millennia to come would associate with you and absorb your pure message of loving devotional service unto the transcendent Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Your books are like the polestar for ISKCON, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We may have so many plans for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness and becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious, but if they do not revolve around the substance of your glorious *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* they will fly off into outer space and take us with them.

Your books are like a great arsenal of weapons to defeat the formidable army of the Age of Kali. Your many sincere followers who distribute your books—the frontline soldiers and real heroes of the war against illusion—take out the Cruise missiles, mortars, and land mines of your books onto the field of battle. Enthused with devotion to *guru* and Kṛṣṇa and inspired by your words in the very books they distribute, they face all kinds of enemies to deliver their weapons. Often your books act exactly like land mines: they may sit for months or even years on someone’s bookshelf, until the person with the required receptivity opens it and—boom! your words penetrate his armor of illusion and destroy his material life forever.

Your books are like an exquisitely cooked Sunday feast with an endless variety of preparations, a feast that one may partake of till the end of time without becoming satiated. There’s hot chutney—*nāyaṁ deho deha-bhājāṁ nṛ-loke* … *puṁsaḥ striyā mithunī-bhāvam etam;* there are sweet rice and nectar drinks—*madhuraṁ madhuraṁ vapur asya vibhor* … *anarpita-carīṁ cirāt karuṇayāvatīrṇaḥ kalau;* there’s the “bread and butter” of spiritual life—*dehino ’smin yathā dehe … sarva-dharmān parityajya*. I am eating these preparations every day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and I cannot thank you enough for undergoing the severe austerities you did just so I and countless others can enjoy them and get spiritual nourishment from them.

But most of all your books are a nectar-ocean in which so many of the saffron mercy-particles covering your lotus feet have been dissolved. This ocean abounds with the precious jewels of your purports, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The only price one need pay for these jewels is the willingness to read and think. Recently I have been preparing a final edition of your *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.* In the course of the two-month marathon back in 1975, in which an “impossible” seventeen books were sent to the printer, some of your precious words were not properly edited, and some, sadly, were entirely omitted. As I went over the original transcript, correcting the text, I came across the following one-paragraph jewel of a purport that had been overlooked. I would like to close by presenting you with this purport, Śrīla Prabhupāda—an offering of a jewel unto the jeweler—for it sums up perfectly who you are and what you have given us. Here it is, known until now only to you, Lord Kṛṣṇa, and your transcriber:

Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā 13.137  
TRANSLATION

Speaking in the mood of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, “For most people, the mind and heart are one, but because My mind is never separated from Vṛndāvana, I consider My mind and Vṛndāvana to be one. My mind is already Vṛndāvana, and since You like Vṛndāvana, will You please place Your lotus feet there? I would deem that Your full mercy.

PURPORT

The mind’s activities are thinking, feeling and willing, by which the mind accepts materially favorable things and rejects the unfavorable. This is the consciousness of people in general. But when one’s mind does not accept and reject but simply becomes fixed on the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, then one’s mind becomes as good as Vṛndāvana. Wherever Kṛṣṇa is, there also are Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, the *gopīs,* the cowherd boys and all the other inhabitants of Vṛndāvana. Thus as soon as one fixes Kṛṣṇa in his mind, his mind becomes identical with Vṛndāvana. In other words, when one’s mind is completely free from all material desires and is engaged only in the service of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, then one always lives in Vṛndāvana, and nowhere else.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, North America.  
(written by Draviḍa dāsa)

## The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you on the hundredth anniversary of your appearance day!

A century ago, Your Divine Grace appeared in Calcutta. This most auspicious appearance heralded “a revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization.” Things would never be the same. For the first time the population of the entire world would have an alternative to the materialistic way of life. The blazing sun of Kṛṣṇa consciousness would gradually rise, dispelling the dense darkness of Kali-yuga. The history of the world would change.

You would spend a lifetime in preparation. You would come to the West, suffer severe illness while onboard the *Jaladuta,* and then place your lotus feet on the American continent—all alone. You would start ISKCON, write all your books, establish more than one hundred centers, accept, nurture, and train thousands of disciples, manage all aspects of your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, continuously travel around the world preaching the message of Godhead—all single-handedly, with unparalleled determination and devotion. We feel that we will never be able to comprehend even a fraction of the hardships you had to go through to fulfill the orders of your spiritual master.

As ISKCON devotees, we are all feeling happy in your movement in the association of your disciples and granddisciples. Before you came to the West and before your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement had spread to all the continents of this world, where and how could persons interested in spiritual matters obtain any knowledge of these things? Our parents and grandparents did not have any wisdom to pass on to us. Our friends and teachers could not help us, nor could the books that were available, nor the priests and other so-called spiritual authorities. Before you came, Śrīla Prabhupāda, what did we know about the spirit soul, about Kṛṣṇa or devotional service? “You are not this body”—even this was unknown to us. All we knew was material sense gratification.

Before your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement began spreading in your own country, some Westerners went to India seeking spiritual knowledge. But even if a few of these persons came in touch with genuine spiritual teachings, still they didn’t seem to really grasp them, nor could they apply them practically in their lives with any success. People could roam all over India in search of ways and means to take up spiritual life, but somehow they couldn’t find it or understand that which was available at the time. India’s ancient wisdom, the treasure house of Vedic knowledge, remained as inaccessible as the highest peaks of the Himalayan Mountains. That was before you came. Your appearance in the West changed everything. Since you started ISKCON, this very knowledge has gradually become available in every corner of this planet.

Now an interested person does not even have to go to India: he will most likely be approached on the street in his hometown by your disciples and granddisciples who will be distributing your books—*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, *Bhagavad-gītā, Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *The Nectar of Devotion*—right on the street or even on his doorstep, no matter where he lives in the world. What was once a hidden and practically inaccessible spiritual knowledge is now, by your mercy, being handed out in every town and village, not only to a few interested spiritual seekers but to everyone—even to the uninterested!

Lord Caitanya’s name is gradually becoming known to every person on this planet, and His mercy is being distributed even in the remotest places. The world revolution through book production and book distribution is your mercy upon the fallen souls of this otherwise hopeless civilization, and the heart of this whole operation is your BBT. As long as this heart is pumping out more and more books in more and more languages, the body of ISKCON will remain healthy and strong. We feel very fortunate to be engaged in this service, but being fallen and without much spiritual realization or desire, we need your blessings. We pray that you give us the ability and intelligence to increase the production of your books more and more, and that we will come to understand how important this mission really is. Please bestow upon us a drop of the determination and sense of urgency that you have. Thank you for being so kind to us.

Your humble servants at the Northern European BBT production center in Korsnas Gard, Sweden.

## The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Spain)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Many times in your purports you repeat and explain these words by Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura: *sādhu-śāstra-guru-vākya, cittete kariyā aikya.* One should accept a thing as genuine by studying the words of saintly people, the spiritual master, and the *śāstra,* the revealed scripture. In *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you explain that the actual center is *śāstra*. If a spiritual master does not speak according to revealed scripture, he is not to be accepted. Similarly, if a saintly person does not speak according to the *śāstra,* he is not a saintly person. *Śāstra* is the center for all.

Your books are the *śāstra*. You came to this world, to our material world, by the desire of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, with the specific mission and potency to distill all the teachings of all *śāstras* and all *ācāryas* and condense them in your books, to guide the lives of the conditioned souls to the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Your books are the Absolute Truth. Your books are the actual reference to illuminate our darkened intelligences.

Therefore, you are not just a great spiritual master and a great saintly person, but you, like Vyāsadeva, have been empowered by the Lord to deliver *śāstra*. All your words are the *śāstra* that the Lord wants to give to the people of this age as reference to lead them toward Him.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us continue serving you by delivering your teachings to the Spanish-speaking people, inspire us to go deep into your books, and bless us so our intelligence never deviates even an inch from your words.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust in Spain.

## The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Thailand)

All glory to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga on this hundredth anniversary of the appearance day of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda!

My eternal father Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious occasion I humbly prostrate myself in the dust under your divine lotus feet with great awe and reverence. Again and again, from all directions, I fall at your feet, offering you my body, mind, and words as your own.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came when I was in the greatest darkness and you opened my eyes to the Supreme Absolute Truth: God is a person, and He has His name, Kṛṣṇa, and His place, Goloka, and His consort, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, and all Her servants.

I, lost in the web of karmic reactions, seeking peace but finding none, was mercifully guided to you by the grace of Lord Kṛṣṇa. I had only heard of Kṛṣṇa as the “mythological eighth incarnation of Vishnu in Hinduism.” That’s what they taught me in school.

By your mercy, now I am cent percent certain of who God is and what my relationship with Him is. You mercifully gave me the name Bhakta dāsa so that I can eternally remember that I am an infinitesimal servant of the servant of the servants of the Lord of Vṛndāvana, Śrī Giridhārī.

Although I have struggled with my mind and senses, often falling from the platform of pure devotional service, by the strength of your blessings again and again and again, I have been brought back on your ship. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you so very much!

My prayer, my ardent longing, is that you will one day, in one lifetime or another, bestow your most gracious smile upon this poor soul once again. I pray I may be a source of pride and happiness to you eternally.

Your eternal servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust in Thailand.  
(written by Bhakta dāsa)

## The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Tokyo)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept out most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

It is due only to your special mercy that such fallen souls as we can offer a Vyāsa-pūjā offering on this most auspicious Mahā-Vyāsa-pūjā.

You are not here in this world physically, but people are getting strong enlightenment from you. Whenever we think of you, we always remember your favorite words, “Purity is the force.” For us, you are the way to Kṛṣṇa. You have shown us the perfect way how we can take shelter of the lotus feet of the Lord. You are completely absorbed in service to the Lord. Conditioned souls in this age of Kali are being purified and saved by your strong determination and enthusiasm to serve the Lord. People are influenced by your purity. Those who have been influenced by your purity take as their life and soul the *saṅkīrtana* mission, the propagation of which is directly ordered by you, and are serving you wholeheartedly. You have shown us how by pushing on the *saṅkīrtana* movement we can go back home, back to Godhead. By reading and distributing your books we can associate with you. Just by touching one of your books, just by reading one line in one of your books, anyone can get unlimited benediction.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how merciful you are! You have given us whatever is necessary for us to go back home, back to Godhead. All over the world there are temples, your books are being distributed, *harināma-saṅkīrtana* is being performed, *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* is being distributed, and there are devotees dedicated to your mission. Your association is available all over the world.

We remember how pleased you were whenever you received a newly printed book. We remember how you smiled whenever you received the *saṅkīrtana* scores. Your followers are trying very hard to distribute your books just to please you. The *saṅkīrtana* movement is getting bigger and bigger. A temple is not a place for eating and sleeping. It is a place where devotees are trained to expertly distribute this wonderful mercy Your Divine Grace has bestowed upon us. The ISKCON temples are bases where tens of thousands of *saṅkīrtana* soldiers stay. “Godhead is light. Nescience is darkness. Wherever there is Godhead, there is no nescience.” The Kali-yuga darkness produced by *māyā* is destroyed by the spiritual bombs dropped by your *saṅkīrtana* soldiers.

On this very auspicious occasion of the hundredth anniversary of your appearance day, we offer our humble obeisances and pray again and again to your lotus feet that more and more devotees distribute your books, the most wonderful gift you have given us, and that we absorb ourselves only in distributing your books.

“The spiritual master lives eternally, and his followers live with him.” Please give us your blessings so that we may be your faithful soldiers life after life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s transcendental book distribution kī jaya!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Centennial kī jaya!

Your most fallen servants at Bhaktivedanta Book Trust in Tokyo.

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Back to Godhead Magazine

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet.

FOR THE READERS

In 1944 you wrote:

“Sugar-candy is never sweet to those who are suffering from the disease of the bile. But still, sugar-candy is the medicine for bilious patients. The taste of sugar-candy will gradually be revived if the bilious patient goes on taking sugar-candy regularly for the cure of the disease. We recommend the same process to the readers of *Back to Godhead.*”

FOR THE PUBLISHERS

You spoke the following words twenty-seven years ago:

“If our movement is going to be recognized as a scientific God conscious movement, then this *Back to Godhead* will be referred to as authorized scripture. Therefore, we have to prepare it in an authorized way. Nothing nonconclusive can be introduced in *Back to Godhead.* That should be our policy.”

This year marks the hundredth anniversary of your influence in this world, and we at BTG—who are both publishers *and* readers—are especially praying for your guidance and inspiration in helping us realize the importance of the above two statements while producing this transcendental literature.

The sugar-candy referred to is the revealed scripture, and the magazine format is a particularly powerful way to present the *siddhānta* to the conditioned souls.

We can extend the analogy in the following way: Just as when a child refuses medicine because of its bitter taste and the mother tricks the child by hiding the medicine in a sweet, similarly we can mask the “bitter” sugar-candy of transcendental knowledge with an extra coating of sweetness in the format of BTG—exciting photographs, beautiful artwork illustrating the philosophy, and a variety of articles by devotees from around the world. These are the tools we are using under your guidance to do battle with ignorance.

Recently, some BTG readers felt we were wrong to publish articles about demigods. In these articles we glorify the demigods as devotees, but we also clearly present their position in relation to Kṛṣṇa. As Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (7.23):

*antavat tu phalaṁ teṣāṁ tad bhavaty alpa-medhasām*  
*devān deva-yajo yānti mad-bhaktā yānti mām api*

**“Men of small intelligence worship the demigods, and their fruits are limited and temporary. Those who worship the demigods go to the planets of the demigods, but My devotees ultimately reach My supreme planet.”**

This is *siddhānta,* and so it belongs in *Back to Godhead.* As long as we present the conclusions you and your predecessors have taught us, we feel justified. But without the *siddhānta* (medicine), the sugar-coating (BTG format) by itself will have no effect.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us develop the skills needed to present this knowledge in the most palatable way, given the medium in which we are working.

When we feel challenged by the burden of our responsibility, we simply remember you, penniless and alone but determined to push *Back to Godhead* in the streets of Delhi. Your transcendental tenacity is our inspiration forever.

Your servants at Back to Godhead magazine.  
(written by Yamarāja dāsa)

## Back to Godhead Magazine (South Africa)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

We found it strange that when you came to the West you instructed your new disciples to continue the publication of *Back to Godhead* magazine. While in India you had decided to direct your efforts toward writing books. Books would last longer than magazines. We therefore wondered what was the need to continue the publication of *Back to Godhead* magazine. We even wondered if it was out of some sentiment that you wanted *Back to Godhead* revived. From your recorded pastimes and your statements we learn how hard you worked to publish *Back to Godhead* in India:

“I consider the sale of *Back to Godhead* so valuable because in the beginning I worked for *Back to Godhead* day and night, alone in India. I still remember the hardship for pushing on this *Back to Godhead*. In the beginning, when I was householder, I did not care if somebody paid or not paid; I used to distribute liberally. But when I left my household life and I was living alone, sometimes in Vṛndāvana and sometimes in Delhi, or sometimes traveling for pushing on *Back to Godhead,* they were very hard days. Therefore, when *Back to Godhead* will be published not in hundreds of thousands but in millions, that will give me great solace.” (letter to Madhudviṣa dāsa, 14 April, 1970

Fortunately, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have seen the effect of a magazine in the preaching mission. We are now convinced that the reason you requested that *Back to Godhead* be continued is that you knew the potential of a magazine as a powerful visual and literary medium for propagating Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

There are many more thousands of different magazines now than when you were present. *Time* and *Life* magazines, which you mentioned in your letters, are not even as popular anymore as some specialist magazines. In every home, every office, one can find a stack of magazines. *Back to Godhead* should be everywhere also. Here in South Africa, *Back to Godhead* will soon be a household name. Since we started the subscription program in 1992, approximately 5,400 people have subscribed to your magazine, and *Back to Godhead* now has an extremely loyal following.

It is so ecstatic to hear how even people who have no interest in spiritual life are drawn toward this colorful magazine. We know for certain that there was no sentimental reason for you continuing *Back to Godhead*. You knew very well what a compelling medium the magazine would be for presenting Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Now we have made *Back to Godhead* the central focus of a large follow-up *saṅkīrtana* program that will also distribute all your big books to subscribers.

All glories to your *Back to Godhead* magazine!

Your insignificant servants at Back to Godhead South Africa.  
(written by Bhakta Viven)

## Bhaktivedanta Academy

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful worship and offerings of obeisances on this one-hundredth anniversary of your appearance. As your books are being translated gradually into all languages of the world, devotees of the world are learning to glorify the Supreme Lord in all those languages. As Lord Kṛṣṇa’s trusted emissary, Your Divine Grace is also being glorified in all languages, including Sanskrit, the mother of all languages and sublime vehicle of Vedic knowledge. As you have given us Vedic literature intact in its original language, with your perfect English translations, we now humbly beg you to hear our awkward attempt to praise you with Sanskrit prayers of our own composition, which can but roughly outline a few of your transcendental qualities.

*1) namo bhaktivedāntāya nitya-kṣema-varāya te*  
*kṛṣṇa-vidhi-suprajñāya prabhupādāya te namaḥ*

**Obeisances unto Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is (our) ever well-wisher, and who possesses full wisdom in the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.**

*2) vidyāvidyā-vivekāya māyāvāda-pranāśine*  
*satyāya bhakta-netrāya prabhupāda namo ’stu te*

**O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Obeisances unto you, who discriminate (clearly) between knowledge and ignorance and dismantle Māyāvāda conceptions. You are (utterly) truthful, and you are the devotees’ power of sight \[that which leads\].**

*3) mahā-mantra-praśikṣāya nityānanda-priyāya ca*  
*māna-śūnyāyābhayāya prabhupādāya te namaḥ*

**Obeisances unto Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who instructs how to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra*** **and who is (thus) very dear to Lord Nityānanda. He is devoid of false prestige and is (completely) fearless.**

*4) vaiṣṇava-dharma-jīvāya śrī-kṛṣṇārjuna-sevine*  
*parivrājakācāryāya prabhupāda namo ’stu te*

**O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Obeisances unto you, whose very life is** ***vaiṣṇava-dharma,*** **who are (utterly) dedicated to the service of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and His eternal friend Arjuna, and who wander everywhere, teaching (*****bhāgavata-dharma*****) perfectly by your perfect example.**

*5) prabhupāda namas tubhyaṁ jaya-dhvani-prakarṣiṇe*  
*sarva-jīva-sukhārthāya jagat-priyāya te namaḥ*

**O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Obeisances unto you, who draw shouts of “*****Jaya, jaya*****!” whose goal is the happiness of all living entities, and who is therefore very dear to the whole universe.**

*6) caitanya-guṇānandāya gaura-tattva-pracāriṇe*  
*kṛṣṇa-kīrtanākārāya prabhupādāya te namaḥ*

**Obeisances unto Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who takes pleasure in the qualities of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who preaches about the identity of Lord Gaurāṅga, and who is a veritable mine of glorification of the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa.**

*7) prabhupāda namas tubhyaṁ tāpa-trayonmūla-kṛte*  
*bhakta-saṅga-sandīpāya bhakta-hṛd-vedine namaḥ*

**O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Obeisances unto you, who are the uprooter of the threefold miseries, who are the very light of the association of devotees, and who know the hearts of the devotees.**

*8) mando dambhaḥ pramattaś ca vimohito ’ham eva ca*  
*tasmāt sarveṣu kāleṣu vande prabhupādaṁ hi tam*

**I am slow, proud, mad, and utterly bewildered. Therefore in all times (circumstances) I (have no recourse but to) offer homage unto (my eternal master) Śrīla Prabhupāda (that very one who is ever free from all inebriation).**

Your servants at the budding Bhaktivedanta Academy in Śrīdhāma Māyāpur, West Bengal, India.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa-kṣetra dāsa)

## The Bhaktivedanta Archives

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You are the hope for this fallen age. You are the captain of the ship that will deliver the fallen souls from the ocean of *māyā*. Your direction is the beacon and guide for all. Your dedication and determination is the example for all to emulate. You are the representative of Lord Caitanya, predicted by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, who would spread the holy name throughout the world. You are the manifestation of Lord Nityānanda, the eternal spiritual master and great practitioner of *harināma-saṅkīrtana*. You are the purport of the following verses in *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.68–69):

*ya idaṁ paramaṁ guhyaṁ mad-bhakteṣv abhidhāsyati*  
*bhaktiṁ mayi parāṁ kṛtvā mām evaiṣyaty asaṁśayaḥ*  
*na ca tasmān manuṣyeṣu kaścin me priya-kṛttamaḥ*  
*bhavitā na ca me tasmād anyaḥ priyataro bhuvi*

**“For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, pure devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come back to Me. There is no servant in this world more dear to Me than he, nor will there ever be one more dear.”**

Your identity and relationship to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa can only be dimly guessed at by those of us caught up in the murky depths of a vicious Kali-yuga. We are praying that we can have the desire to serve you and your great mission. This desire alone is the key to unlocking the life of eternity, knowledge and bliss which is your reality and which we can only vaguely imagine.

This year at your Archives, we hope to please you by accomplishing the following services: We have uncovered and re-mastered the tapes for eighteen new compact disks of your *kīrtanas, bhajanas,* and purports. We are publishing your 1966 diary, one of the most remarkable documents in the history of Vaiṣṇavism. In collaboration with the North European BBT division, we have begun the project of digital scanning of your windows to the spiritual world, the BBT paintings that you began to commission just a couple of months after you established ISKCON in July 1966. We are working to release a new and improved version of your VedaBase which will include many as yet unreleased tape transcripts. With your faithful servant Yadubara Prabhu we have started the preservation of the film footage of you that he shot so lovingly twenty years and more ago. We have expanded our operations in Europe so that distribution of your archival publications, compact disks, tapes, and photos will be able to increase manyfold.

We feel especially blessed that we have received the mercy of service to your *vapu,* in the form of your moving and still pictures, and your *vāṇī,* the great audio and literary legacy that will endure for ten thousand years. We pray that we can continue this service by the mercy of your kind devotees and followers. We hope that the Centennial events in your honor this year will be remembered as the time of a great surge in preaching activities by your ISKCON society.

Your humble servants at the Bhaktivedanta Archives in Prabhupāda Village, North Carolina, and Rādhādesh, Belgium.

## The Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances All glories to Your Divine Grace!

It is quite clear that the best way to get your mercy is through your books. While millions of your books have been distributed, you always stressed that the devotees should study your books and become conversant with every aspect so that we can become expert preachers.

You wanted the devotees to also develop a structured study of your books:

“I have also suggested for the GBC’s consideration that we introduce a system of examinations for the devotees to take. Sometimes there is criticism that our men are not sufficiently learned, especially the *brāhmaṇas*. Of course, second initiation does not depend upon passing an examination. How one has molded his life—chanting, attending *ārati,* etc., these are essential. Still, *brāhmaṇa* means *paṇḍita*. Therefore I am suggesting examinations. Bhakti-śāstri—(for all *brāhmaṇas*) based on *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrī Īśopaniṣad, The Nectar of Devotion, Nectar of Instruction,* and all the small paperbacks. Bhakti-vaibhava—the above plus first six cantos of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Bhaktivedānta—the above plus cantos 7–12 *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Bhakti-sārvabhauma—the above plus *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. These titles can correspond to entrance, B.A., M.A., Ph.D. So just consider how to organize this Institute. At Māyāpur we shall finalize everything.” (Śrīla Prabhupāda letter, 10 January 1976)

Thus in accordance with your desires and instructions, the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture was formed in 1991 with an enrollment of forty-eight students. Since then it has been offering the Junior Certificate for children, Senior Certificate and Diploma, and Bhakti-śāstri degree. Only by your mercy, the College has grown tremendously, and a total of 1,121 students have graduated to date!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us everything, so what can we offer you in return? As our humble attempt we can simply report what your College is doing:

1\. There has been a steady increase on the part of our congregation here to deeply study your books. As the devotees qualify through the College, they are encouraged to become teachers in their own areas. To date we have 33 classes here in South Africa, with approximately 400 students this year.

2\. By studying the books, the devotees have increased the spiritual standards. We have held courses in Spiritual Didactics, Vaiṣṇava Etiquette, Counseling, and Management for the general congregation as well as for Nāma Haṭṭa leaders. Furthermore, intensive courses are held over long weekends and conducted by our *sannyāsīs* and spiritual masters. In this way Bhaktivedanta College has also committed itself to improving management and leadership of your ISKCON temples and projects.

3\. The students also have to distribute your books for their practical assessment. In this way the students are encouraged to preach and increase your book distribution.

4\. Many of your senior disciples all over the world have also shown keen interest in the College program for congregational development. Requests have come from many countries for Bhaktivedanta College to be established there. We have already expanded to Mauritius this year and have plans to go to other countries. If you wish, Śrīla Prabhupāda, then this project will spread further by your mercy.

5\. Your Bhaktivedanta College, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is also being appreciated within government circles. The Department of Education and Training here in South Africa has recognized your contribution in translating the voluminous Vedic scriptures into English and the College’s study of this knowledge in a systematic way. Letters expressing appreciation for the College’s activities have been received from Professor S.M.E. Bengu, the National Minister of Education and Training, Dr. Vincerit Zulu, the Provincial Minister of Education and Culture, Mr. Dd. Pillay, former Executive Director of the Department of Education and Culture, and Mr. Sipho Ngvenya, the Mayor of Durban.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, as we read the appreciations and realizations of the students in the College, we see how your books are transforming the lives of hundreds of people. We ourselves are seeing that by teaching your books we are forced to learn more and more. And as we try to study your books in the association of your devotees, we realize that there is so much more to learn and to practice. Thank you for giving us your association through your purports and sending to us your swanlike devotees, whose churning of the nectar of your books make it more relishable.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, everything is in your books. Please bless us that we can become instruments to understand them, realize them, and distribute them.

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture, South Africa.  
(written by Smita Kṛṣṇa dāsa)

## The Bhaktivedanta Center

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda:

Please accept our humble obeisances on this momentously auspicious occasion of your Centennial appearance day. Your glories are limitless.

This year, working on a book for your Bhaktivedanta Archives that contains your 1966 journal, we have had the opportunity, day after day, to read your handwritten entries. Reading your actual handwriting infuses the words with even more meaning. The entries chronicle the struggle and personal sacrifice you endured to begin this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in a country inhabited by *mlecchas*.

America loves a success story. It takes great pride in stories of poor immigrants who become wealthy after coming to America. But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, most Americans have no understanding of this: You are the only *real* success story this country has ever known. Before you it was all just “zeros.” You supplied the “one,” Kṛṣṇa. You didn’t come to take from us, for we had nothing you wanted or needed. You came to give Kṛṣṇa’s mercy to anyone who was willing to hear from you.

When you walked up the gangplank to board the Jaladuta, Mr. Bhattacarya remembered you as a “lone warrior.” And you are a warrior, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the lone general in Lord Caitanya’s army. And without so much as one troop, you came ashore and conquered America.
