Generally we see pictures or films of you either being interviewed by reporters or preaching to dignitaries, surrounded by enthusiastic disciples. But these last two years, as we have labored to establish a center for you in this college town, we have been especially moved by those early days when you worked alone, tirelessly, laying the cornerstone for what would become your worldwide movement. America didn’t just “part like the Red Sea” for you. Day by day you chronicle in your journal the contacts you made, the bus trips you took to place your books in bookstores, the letters you wrote to gain attention and funding for your mission. You were careful, methodical, and always gentlemanly. ISKCON didn’t just appear. With Kṛṣṇa’s grace you made it happen, one spirit soul at a time. And you always gave Kṛṣṇa credit for any success you achieved. As we read your humble entries, we find strength and inspiration. We wish we could go back in time and help you from the very start.

We can’t.

But we can help you now.

And on your Centennial appearance anniversary we re-pledge ourselves to the vows we took at our initiation. We re-commit these aging bodies (but willing hearts) to your vision. And we beg that you will eternally engage us as foot soldiers in your spiritual army.

Your servants at The Bhaktivedanta Center, Charlottesville, Virginia.  
(written by Māyāpriyā-devī dāsī)

## Bhakta Program Institute (New Vṛndāvana)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Life with Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Lost and found in the darkest of night.  
Our beloved master so dear to God.  
Blind eyes see by Hare Kṛṣṇa light.  
Most cherished Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Jaladuta* treasure, you arrived in time,  
You didn’t even know which way to go,  
Carrying lessons for a mad mankind,  
Lord sent you to save our souls.

What did we do to deserve such mercy?  
Where shall we go, who shall we see?  
Where can we find you now?  
Where can we find you now?

Clean and prompt and decorations galore,  
Gaurāṅga’s path—don’t be too clever,  
For divine forms—offer more and more,  
Simply chant, dance, and feast forever.

Hear and preach now, ’cause if *guru’s* delighted,  
Confidential message you do carry,  
Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa party—sure to be invited.  
O beloved master, you’re not ordinary.

What did we do to deserve such mercy?  
Where shall we go, who shall we see?  
Where can we find you now?  
Where can we find you now?

Your servants at the Bhakta Program Institute in New Vṛndāvana, West Virginia, USA.  
(written by Dānavīr dāsa Vānacārī)

## The Bhaktivedanta Institute (Bombay and Oakland)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Unless one gets the dust of your lotus feet, one cannot get free of unwanted habits (*anarthas*). Unless we glorify you and remember Your Divine Grace at every step, even the most simple thing becomes very difficult to perform.

On the most auspicious occasion of the Centennial of your divine appearance, we meditate on some of your instructions on how to preach:

“Preaching means that you have to turn the rascals and unfortunate to become intelligent and fortunate. That is preaching.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* lecture, 18 November, 1975, Johannesburg) Śrīla Prabhupāda, you yourself have provided the perfect exemplification of this perfect definition of preaching by delivering innumerable conditioned souls.

“All preachers will have to meet opponents, but they should not make them more inimical. They are already enemies, and if we talk with them harshly or impolitely their enmity will merely increase.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Ādi-līlā* 7.99)

“One must be confident before challenging others. Not that theoretical. Challenge is no good. In all stages he must be able to defend himself from the opposing elements. Then such challenge is all right.” (Morning Walk, Los Angeles, 3 May, 1973)

“Regarding your questions, your first question was, Should the devotee speak the truth to everyone in all circumstances? The answer is yes, one should always speak the truth, but qualified devotees will be able to speak the truth always very palatably to everyone so that it will sound pleasing even to his enemy. That is the art of speaking the truth,” (Letter, 2 January, 1972)”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are actually the greatest scientist. You understood what a scientist does. A scientist wants to empirically test things. A scientist does not want to depend only on mental speculation to understand material nature. Your Divine Grace therefore challenged the material scientists on their own terms, not to simply speculate that matter is the origin of life but to test it by performing an experiment: “Synthesize life from its nonliving components,” you challenged.

The real point is that material scientists should elevate their conception of reality at least from gross matter to subtle matter before they can at all make any empirically testable statements about the nature of life. In your transcendental and effulgent Bhaktivedanta purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.26.34 (beginning *bhūtānāṁ chidra-dātṛtvaṁ*),Your Divine Grace has, out of your causeless mercy, given a clue to every scientist:

“This verse is the potential basis of great scientific research work, for it explains how subtle forms are generated from the ethereal element, what their characteristics and actions are, and how the tangible elements, namely air, fire, water, and earth, are manifested from the subtle form. Mental activities, or the psychological actions of thinking, feeling, and willing, are also activities on the platform of ethereal existence.”

Lord Caitanya is the *mahā-vadānyāya* incarnation; that is, Lord Caitanya is unlimitedly merciful. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja helps us to understand that mercy:

“Let me repeatedly offer my respectful obeisances unto the beelike devotees who always taste the honey of the lotus feet of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. If even a doggish nondevotee somehow takes shelter of such devotees, he enjoys the aroma of the lotus flower.”

“\[Purport:\] Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement is so strong that even a doglike nondevotee can gradually become a devotee by the association of a devotee of Lord Caitanya.”

Your Divine Grace is Lord Caitanya’s instrument for the delivery of this mercy. Like Lord Caitanya, you distribute this mercy without considering who is fit and who is not (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 7.23). Why should not the scientists be able to receive that mercy? Your instruction to kick the scientists was meant to awaken your disciples, but for the scientists themselves you wanted to convince them of the nature of life and of God’s direction of matter through the time energy. (You had many other goals as well.)

“So, we should preach like this, any rascal, because anyone who has no information of Kṛṣṇa, who is not Kṛṣṇa conscious, he is a rascal. That’s all. That we must know. That is definite. There is no mistaking it. But if you say that ‘You are rascal; there is no mistaking it,’ then you cannot preach. If you directly call a rascal a rascal, he will be angry. *Murkhāyopadeśo hi prakopāya na śāntaye:* ‘To give good instruction to a fool, rascal, will increase his anger, that’s all. . . .’

“In this way preaching practical. Otherwise it is not possible. Everyone is thinking he is the most exalted personality, scientist, philosopher, great man. That is material disease. … You are dealing with all rascals. So if we call them directly ‘rascal,’ they will be angry. Your preaching will not be successful. So follow the principles enunciated by Prabodhānanda Sarasvatī and Caitanya Mahāprabhu.” (Morning Walk, Nairobi, 28 October, 1975)

You wanted to encourage the scientists also to become devotees by taking part in the glorification of Lord Hari, as Nārada Muni instructed:

*idaṁ hi puṁsas tapasaḥ śrutasya vā*  
*sviṣṭasya sūktasya ca buddhi-dattayoḥ*  
*avicyuto ’rthaḥ kavibhir nirūpito*  
*yad-uttamaśloka-guṇānuvarṇanam*

**“Learned circles have positively concluded that the infallible purpose of the advancement of knowledge, namely austerities, study of the** ***Vedas,*** **sacrifice, chanting of hymns, and charity culminates in the transcendental descriptions of the Lord, who is defined in choice poetry” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.5.22)**

Purport: “When advancement of knowledge is applied in the service of the Lord, the whole process becomes absolute. The Personality of Godhead and His transcendental name, fame, glory, etc., are all nondifferent from Him. Therefore, all the sages and devotees of the Lord have recommended that the subject matter of art, science, philosophy, physics, chemistry, psychology, and all other branches of knowledge should be wholly and solely applied in the service of the Lord. . . .

“Science and philosophy also should be applied in the service of the Lord. There is no use presenting dry speculative theories for sense gratification. Philosophy and science should be engaged to establish the glory of the Lord. Advanced people are eager to understand the Absolute Truth through the medium of science, and therefore a great scientist should endeavor to prove the existence of the Lord on a scientific basis.”

Your Divine Grace challenged at Harvard University, “Where is that department here that teaches the difference between a living body and dead body \[i.e., consciousness\]?” They had no reply? By your own desire, such a department has manifested itself in your own Bhaktivedanta Institute, offering a M.S./Ph.D. program for Consciousness Studies in Bombay in collaboration with Birla Institute of Technology, Pilani, Rajasthan. The very first semester, with four courses on consciousness from the viewpoint of philosophy, physics, neuroscience, and biology, is being started in August 1996.

Whatever we can do for you is insignificant compared to what Your Divine Grace has given to us, and on this auspicious day we pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa that we may become qualified to be engaged forever in spreading your increasing transcendental glories.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute, Bombay and Oakland.

## The Bhaktivedanta Swami Library

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“So you and Gargamuni take charge of distributing my books. … Your service in this connection is approved by Kṛṣṇa and naturally by me also.”

Recently on a tape, we heard you describing the nature of the soul with an analogy to sex. You explained that a covering, or a condom, is used during sex. The condom is not much enjoyed, however, because it reduces pleasure. So, scientists invented the pill. Now without the need of the covering, enjoyment is greater, and people are more happy. Again, you elucidated the analogy of sex, by pointing out that during sex, the partners’ clothes are always removed. No one has sex while clothed; enjoyment increases by removing the garments, or the covering.

Then you revealed the purport, that the covering on the body is what the body is to the soul. As the body relishes without any covering, so does the soul enjoy without the body. Pure soul, the spiritual form, is the greatest delight. By associating with Lord Kṛṣṇa via devotional service, we can acquire our spiritual form.

When we hear that sex, the greatest elation of the body, is merely an analogy for the ecstasy of the soul, it makes us reflect upon what we have done with our lives. Your description of spiritual pleasure fascinates us, our perpetual absorption in the body wanes, and we want to know what it is you are teaching and what you must be experiencing. You have made us want to be something of what you are. You have woken us up from the slumber of *māyā*. You have inspired us to crawl out of this horrible world to come and serve you on the sublime spiritual platform, where you cavort with Kṛṣṇa and His pleasure potencies.

Your analogy is readily understandable by us Westerners, and also by newly Westernized Easterners, who are deeply encumbered by sex. Although previous *ācāryas* have used the analogy of sex, none have used it in this unique way to teach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Here is another proof of your genius. We could cite scores of other examples of your brilliant preaching, but this is one we happened to appreciate recently.

We are proud to have you as our spiritual master and to be your followers. We can only hope that everyone else in the world will discover what we have. You are the greatest spiritual master to have ever appeared and are worthy of being followed by all with life, soul, and love.

You once wrote to me that it is not enough to only glorify you; it is more important to try to do what you are doing and try to be what you are. Although it is not pleasant, we must admit our hypocrisy publicly. We fantasize about one day going back to Godhead, yet we are not liberated from the chief attraction of the material world. Help us to be free. We want to be fully absorbed in the loving service of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. Only you can help us.

May we be humble, as you always are. You told us that arrogance is the characteristic of those who have not realized themselves to be the one-ten-thousandth-tip-of-the-hair soul but rather their body. This is a cardinal instruction, because only the humble can efficaciously chant the name of the Lord constantly. There is no other way. This is what you have graciously and selflessly taught.

Your servants at The Bhaktivedanta Swami Library.  
(written by Brahmānanda dāsa and Gargamuni dāsa)

## Bharata Hills /New Vṛndāvana Saṅkīrtana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Our *saṅkīrtana* party started about three years ago by your divine grace. It was then known under the name Sunshine Saṅkīrtana Party. This party was created under the guidance and inspiration of your dear disciple and bona fide representative His Holiness Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami with the purpose to increase the *saṅkīrtana* movement here in America, solely for your pleasure. At that time the party consisted of only a few of your granddisciples. Even though small, it was the first full-time traveling book distribution party to preach in America for many years. At that time it was a common misconception around the world that book distribution in America was practically impossible. Since that time our small party has changed its name and quadrupled its book distribution results, devotees, and preaching. This is undoubtedly due to your mercy, wholly and fully. Not only has our *saṅkīrtana* party increased dramatically, but along with it the entire North American yātrā. By your mercy America is once again back in its position as a leading distributor of your transcendental books and will soon be number one in the world.

On this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we would like to remind ourselves and the world that you are actually the most munificent, compassionate, giving, and surrendered personality to descend to this material world. This is undoubtedly true, and anyone who has some sense of objectivity will have to concede this point. Who else is spreading the holy names of Kṛṣṇa as far and wide as you? Who else is saving as many conditioned souls as you? You are the *patita-pāvana,* savior of the world. Under your direction the *saṅkīrtana* movement will flood the entire world with the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. This is best achieved, as you often said, by flooding the world with your books. With this instruction in mind, let us all in some way connect ourselves to the *saṅkīrtana* mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In this way we can be sure without question that we are actually pleasing you and can receive your mercy.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*

The secret to success in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is to receive the mercy of the spiritual master. This is best accomplished when one pleases the spiritual master. So let us whole-heartedly take up your instructions as our life and soul and allow you to purify the whole world and ourselves simultaneously. Please allow us to somehow or other act as your instruments and enter into your pastime of saving the world from the corrupt influence of Kali-yuga. You are our only shelter, inspiration, and motivation. But for you and your causeless mercy, there is no other way for success.

Praying to stay engaged in your devotional service eternally, we remain  
The Bharata Hills /New Vṛndāvana saṅkīrtana devotees.  
(written by Tripurāri dāsa)

## Festival of India

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you and your family of Kṛṣṇa conscious children, grandchildren, and great-grandchildren. May all the people of the world follow your perfect example. May all people come to realize their constitutional position as servants of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Please be kind to your lowest off all workers. We are befooled and bewildered, yet you have given us the opportunity to perform laborious tasks for you and your sincerest followers. Thank you from the bottom of our hearts for this greatest of all privileges.

We celebrate your appearance day, yet truly this observance is the celebration of the appearance of every devotee in modern times, for it is Your Divine Grace that has revealed the genuine purpose of living. We are nothing without you.

All the world pursues victory, adventure, splendor, beauty, and strength. The opulences of knowledge, fame, and renunciation are attractive to every person. You have given us access to these most sought-after qualities by allowing us to be part of your Festival of India program. Certainly the timeless romance of heroes and their activities can be felt by the crew of your Festival of India.

As we seemingly sail across the continent of North America, stopping at ports to offer goods and services, the life of seafaring men is enacted within the mind. As we unload our precious cargo, the excitement of establishing a settlement in an uncivilized land is experienced. We unfold the tops of the tents, secure the rigging, and raise the signs. Then we anchor in by driving stakes and tie down the ropes. We do all this like sailors, cowboys, or circus workers. Then we change roles once again.

The environment of a transcendental town has emerged from the doors of Kṛṣṇa’s eighteen-wheeled form (the Festival of India tractor trailer), but now your books in the form of giant pages make their way into the canvas-topped city. The story of your life, the paintings you brought to life, the song of the Supreme Lord you sing so clearly, and the instructions you deliver freely are all placed on display. And we, your humble servants, become your representatives. We act on your behalf. You have taken rowdy fellows with so many rough edges and misconceptions and transformed us into saintly men attempting to present the culture of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Wherever we find ourselves, whatever engagement we have, you are standing before us. The function of our existence has been so expertly dovetailed by Your Divine Grace into a blissful orchestration that plays within our collective heart. We drive, load, sing, dance, speak, distribute books and *prasādam,* guard, clean up, repack, and drive again simply to please you. Kindly continue to shower your blessings upon us. Thank you very much.

Sincerely,  
The Festival of India crew.  
(written by Pāṇi-bhūṣaṇa dāsa)

## Food for Life Global

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On behalf of all your *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* distributors around the world, please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you on this most auspicious occasion of your Centennial Mahā-Vyāsa-pūjā.

“My ‘disease’ was that I always thought *big,*” you once said, and in your commentary on the story of how Mādhavendra Purī observed the Annakūṭa ceremony, you wrote, “This activity \[*prasādam* distribution\] should be expanded universally.” Your big thinking, however, was not something born out of a desire for name and fame. On the contrary, it flowed naturally from your genuine desire to help others, to bring as many people as possible back home, back to Godhead. You were self-realized, compassionate, but above all fully convinced of the words of your spiritual master and the potency of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s sublime and practical method of *saṅkīrtana*.

“The attempt must go on to convert unwilling men to become His devotees. Lord Caitanya has taught a very simple method in this connection. He has taught the lesson for preaching the transcendental message through singing, dancing, and refreshment. As such, fifty percent of our income may be spent for this purpose.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.36)

“Our Hare Kṛṣṇa movement is standing on three principle things: chanting, dancing, and eating *prasādam*.” (lecture at the 1973 London Ratha-yātrā).

By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* is going out more than ever in the history of Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism. We calculate that your Food for Life devotees are now distributing *prasādam* at a rate of one meal every two seconds! And the total of all *prasādam* meals distributed since 1972 has gone way beyond 70 million! Our plan is to reach 100 million pieces of *prasādam* distributed by your Food for Life devotees by the end of this year. Here is a brief report on our present situation:

Right now, in Heidelberg, Germany, there is a *prasādam* revolution going on: a team of *brahmacārīs* headed by Bhāgavata-dharma dāsa is producing 45,000 *prasādam* “energy balls” every day! On Feed the World Day, November 23, 100,000 of these “energy balls” will go out for free all over Germany. In Wroclaw, Poland, Madhyadvīpa dāsa leads the way in the fastest developing Food for Life programs in the world, with ten programs distributing 12,000 free meals a week. While Mahāsiṁha dāsa, the Polish FFL *ācārya,* claims a personal record of 1.5 tons of *prasādam* and 300 books distributed in one day! In Belgrade, Serbia, Śrīnivāsa dāsa has done excellent work establishing relationships with the United Nations High Commission for Refugees (UNHCR), which recently cooperated with FFL on a special fund-raising campaign called A Smile of Hope, with Lord Jagannātha as the smiling face. Posters were put up all over the city. Śrīnivāsa has also secured donations from OXFAM and support from other international aid agencies like Save the Children. Down under in Australia and New Zealand, the devotees continue their popular FFL programs of more than eighteen years’ standing, and the public continues to praise your movement, with the media regularly highlighting its activities.

In Russia, Food for Life has expanded to more than 50 cities, and the devotees are distributing at a rate of 13,000 *prasādam* meals every day, while your “Sukhumi soldiers” continue to serve 1,200 hot meals daily to the remaining poor citizens of that battered city in Abkhazia. In Armenia, where the devotees were assaulted with iron bars and chains, Food for Life continues under the direction of Advaita dāsa and is ISKCON’s only presence in this oppressive country. In Tokyo, Japan, hundreds of hot plates of *kicharī* go out daily to the destitute men and women on the subways, and in Bulgaria, where the facility is very limited, your devotees are distributing fruit *prasādam* to hundreds of orphans and spastic children.

In India, Jayapatāka Swami, one of the pioneers of big *prasādam* distribution, flew back to Calcutta to help organize ISKCON’s food-relief efforts during the massive Māyāpur floods. Boatloads of hot *kicharī,* cooked in a foot of floodwater, went out to the thousands of distressed villagers. Further north, in Vṛndāvana, the home of *prasādam* distribution, Bhakti-bhṛṅga Govinda Mahārāja and his FFL team, headed by Rūpa Raghunātha and Madhusūdana Prabhus, continue to serve thousands of *prasādam* meals to widows, holy men, and children. Every year in India, at the famous Shantipur festival, devotees from all over the world participate in distributing hot *kicharī* to well over 30,000 local people.

In London, Jagannātha Baladeva Subhadrā dāsa and Rādhā-Mādhava dāsa march on, bringing hundreds of delicious meals to the homeless. For your pleasure, they will be celebrating the two millionth free meal this year. In Ecuador, Peru, Bolivia, Brazil, and other parts of South America, the public have overwhelming love and respect for your devotees, principally as a result of their colorful festivals and *prasādam* distribution, while in Singapore, the Food for Life restaurant is gradually building a bridge of understanding between ISKCON and the conservative government.

In Riga, Latvia, Siṁha-bhaṭṭa dāsa’s team have claimed the honor of the largest Food for Life service in Europe, with an average distribution of 1,500 hot meals daily! But they are still way behind Durban, South Africa, where Kapiladeva das is setting a screaming pace of 25,000 hot *prasādam* meals every week! Inspired by their efforts, the Mayor of Durban is officially declaring November 23 (Feed the World Day) Feed Durban Day. Devotees will attempt to serve over 500,000 full *prasādam* meals in one day!

But there are even more glorious efforts going on in the most dangerous places on earth, where your granddisciples are risking their lives to bring *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* to distressed women and children in war zones. For example, in Sarajevo, Bosnia, Jānukanyakā dāsī courageously headed up a team of mostly women devotees in bringing fresh bread and cookies daily to thousands during the brutal four-year conflict. “I would see death all around me,” she said later. “But we always knew that Prabhupāda and Kṛṣṇa were with us, and that we just had to help these people. … When their distraught and weathered faces lit up after we gave them *prasādam,* we felt as if Kṛṣṇa was smiling back at us,” she explained.

In Grozny, Russia, Sukhānanda dāsa from the St. Petersburg temple heroically commanded a team of men and women devotees to set up a makeshift Food for Life kitchen in an abandoned hospital “right-smack-bang in the middle of the besieged capital” while shells and bullets rained down from Russian soldiers and leopard tanks. “It was snowing when we arrived,” he recalled. “The Russian Emergency Ministry gave us food supplies, and armed cars to drive into the city. We had a whole convoy of trucks, and some tanks to protect us. It was amazing! \[On Gaura-pūrṇimā day 1995, while the two armies were fighting fiercely and millions of Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas were celebrating the appearance of Śrī Śacīnandana, another transcendental army, carrying a new kind of weapon, entered the battle arena.\] The Russians tried to subdue the Chechens with aggressive tactics, but we were able to conquer their hearts with *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. The Chechen people are Muslims, but now some of them are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, and many of them told their relatives, ‘Kṛṣṇa saved us.’”

For your pleasure, Śrīla Prabhupāda, over 800,000 servings of hot porridge *prasādam* have been given out since Sukhānanda’s convoy arrived in Grozny. Food for Life was the only serious food-relief program in the city. Although the war has officially ended, fighting and bombings continue in pockets around the city, and so the devotees are earnestly praying for your protection.

You’ll be pleased to know that their valiant efforts are having a profound effect on the media as well. On December 12, 1995, *The New York Times* printed a wonderful article about Sukhānanda’s Food for Life program in Grozny. “Many people in the devastated city of Grozny swear the Hare Krishnas are saints,” wrote the reporter. The article was featured on page four and included a picture. It was true testimony to the potency of *prasādam* distribution and clear verification of your statement to Satyahita dāsa in a letter dated 16 March, 1974:

“I am hopeful that if we can widely distribute free foodstuffs to the people of India by giving it out at our centers as well as by travelling parties to villagers, we will win over the whole country and the whole world by this activity on Krishna’s behalf.” *The New York Times* is the world’s leading newspaper. Certainly this is what you meant when you said, “We would win over the world by this activity \[*prasādam* distribution\].”

Recently, the devotees in Sarajevo held their first Sunday Feast program in three years. It was a big event in the city. Many prominent citizens attended, including a United Nations chief, who was later quoted in a French newspaper as saying, “If there were more people like the Hare Krishnas, the world would be a much more peaceful place.” There is no doubt in our minds, and we can see that there was no doubt in your mind, about the effectiveness of Lord Caitanya’s sublime and practical method of *saṅkīrtana:* “singing, dancing and refreshment \[*prasādam*\].”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us this sublime and practical method of *saṅkīrtana*. A formula that can bring real peace and harmony to the world. A formula that is universally applicable, fun, and the ultimate panacea for all material illusions.

“If you eat and if you distribute that sort of *prasādam* to the public, you are doing great service and the public is getting spiritual consciousness. Just establish, just propagate this everywhere. In the hospital, in the charitable societies, in industrial places, everywhere distribute this *prasādam* and chant this Hare Kṛṣṇa. Just see what is the result. You want peace? These are the processes of peace.” \[lecture in New York City, 16 December, 1966)

Unfortunately, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the meat-eaters of this world are not aware of this sublime gift, nor do they have faith in Kṛṣṇa. They cannot understand God, what to speak of Lord Kṛṣṇa and devotional service. It appears, however, that the Lord has a divine plan to change this hellish mentality by forcing the conditioned souls to change their ways. Just now there is a huge scandal in England, where it has been reported that half of cattle in Britain may have the so-called mad cow disease. “Prices and sales are falling, slaughterhouses are temporarily closing; this whole industry is shaken up by this crisis,” reported *The Herald Tribune*. It’s a catastrophe with multiple ramifications (karmic reactions) for the whole European economy and psyche.

From the spiritual perspective, we can understand that Kṛṣṇa’s hand is at work, pushing millions of meat-eaters to consider vegetarianism. It’s very timely, too, because a vegetarian revolution is currently taking place on the planet. According to one survey, twenty-five percent of all teenagers in the United States are now vegetarian, and in the United Kingdom (before the mad-cow-disease panic), more than ten thousand people were becoming vegetarians every week! Without doubt, ISKCON’s restaurants, festivals, and cook books deserve much of the credit for this vegetarian revolution, which is indirectly helping the *saṅkīrtana* movement. After all, vegetarians are just one small step away from becoming prasādamarians and hence qualified to understand our sublime and practical peace formula. Of course, whatever the result of this mad-cow disease scare, there is one thing for certain: millions of cows will be slaughtered, and this is disturbing to the devotees. For as long as the cows are unhappy, human society will never be happy—another good reason to increase *prasādam* distribution universally.

For your pleasure, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are making *big, big* plans to honor you during this glorious Centennial year by observing Hare Kṛṣṇa Feed the World Day on November 23. Our vision is as follows:

Because spiritual life begins with the tongue, and because *prasādam* distribution is our Vaiṣṇava tradition and essential for the spiritual progress of the conditioned souls, we, the followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda, are dedicated to seeing that every man, women, and child receive an opportunity to taste *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* on Feed the World Day, November 23, 1996.

In our attempt, we hope to achieve the following four objectives:

1\) To make the people of the world aware of the enormous social contributions that ISKCON is making through its Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life programs and vegetarian restaurants;  
2\) To bring to the world’s attention the fact that all the credit for the success of *prasādam* distribution and Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life belongs to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda;  
3\) To practically demonstrate the Vaiṣṇava principle of respecting all living beings by liberally distributing *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* without discrimination;  
4\) To unite the people of the world through food (*kṛṣṇa-prasādam*) and music and dance (*harināma-saṅkīrtana*), and in this way to demonstrate Lord Caitanya’s sublime and practical peace formula.

Falling at your feet, begging your blessings and empowerment to carry out this mission, we remain  
Your prasādam distributors at Food for Life Global.  
(written by Priyavrata dāsa \[Prasādam dāsa\])

## Indian Leaders

I am not an expert on Swami A.C. Bhaktivedanta’s life, on the Krishna consciousness movement, or on the devotional literature of India. My field is linguistic philosophy and other branches of Sanskrit literature. However, as one who has occasionally been to the temples established by Swami Bhaktivedanta and has read some of the literature written and inspired by him, I can say that I am awed by what he achieved. Unlike many other teachers from India, he did not modify the message of his teachers to suit the West. His requirements were strict by contemporary standards. Yet he managed to turn a large number of young Western adults away from a life of drugs, sex, and mindless consumption. He filled them with a sense of purpose, love, devotion, and amazing enthusiasm. This is an outstanding example of what dedication can achieve.

Sincerely,  
Ashok Aklujkar  
Professor of linguistics,  
University of British Columbia,  
Vancouver, B.C., Canada

\* \* \*

I am happy to know that the centenary celebrations of Poojyasri A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who spread the cult of Krishna consciousness throughout the world, is being celebrated. His particular emphasis on *nāma-saṅkīrtana* is unique. In fact, the scriptures say that in Kali-yuga *nāma-saṅkīrtana* is the only means by which one can attain salvation. It is he who brought peace of mind to the entire world by preaching the philosophy of devotion to Lord Krishna. I submit my humble *praṇāmas* to this great *ācārya*.

Dr. M. A. Venkatakrishnan, M.A., M.Phil., Ph.D.  
Reader  
Dept. of Vaishnavism  
University of Madras, India

\* \* \*

It is only because of Śrī A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda that the *bhakti* movement started in the Western world and spread like wildfire for good all over the world It appears as if it is a miracle, but it is so true. The greatness of *bhakti* is such, as is the greatness of Lord Krishna and the spirit of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Tridandi Chinna Sriman Narayana Ramanuja Jeear Swami  
Jeear Educational Trust  
Sithanagaram, India

\* \* \*

We have great pleasure in extending our greetings, and we pray for the blessings of Digvijaya Rama upon the Hare Krishna movement. The founder-*ācārya* of the movement (ISKCON), Srimad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami, has done a great service for the spreading of *sanātana-dharma* all over the world. We wish te movement will continue to spread the message of *bhāgavata-dharma*. We share your joy in the Centennial celebrations in honor of Swami Prabhupāda in this year 1996.

Diwan, Prahlada Achar Upadyaya  
Sri Uttaradi Mutt  
Bangalore, India

## Institute for Vaiṣṇava Studies

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept my most respectful and humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

With great joy I write this Vyāsa-pūjā letter to you. This sense of joy comes from the gratitude that I feel toward you for all that you have done for me, for all those dear to me, and for humanity in general.

This is the year in which those who have known you and love you celebrate with solemnity the Centennial of your birth. For this occasion, even with all of my own limits, I wish to unite with those who, grateful toward Your Divine Grace, endeavor to present to the world the glories of your holy personality in order to attract the attention of those yet unaware of your spiritual message. In the past, I too was as anonymous and unaware as many others. Perplexed, I was living an unsatisfactory existence in this world of palatable illusions. Impatient I searched and searched for the sense of life, constantly questioning the fleeting figures I found along my path, and, hopeless, I heard no answers. I sharpened my attention, listened harder, and heard nothing but the hollow echo of my questions. Then the almighty God, imperceptibly, through my heart led me to you, and I was quenched at the source of your holy and powerful personality. You smiled at me, grave and paternal, you encouraged me, saying, “Become a devotee, chant the holy name, and don’t worry. Kṛṣṇa will protect you!”

You asked me nothing and you revealed to me the true sense of life, indicating the right path to take. Thanks to you I was revived, and I began to live a new life following your path.

Your generous heart, always full of compassion for all conditioned souls, your courage and your clarity in the presentation of *sanātana-dharma* as *kṛṣṇa-bhakti,* together with the wisdom of your practical teachings and the saintliness of your behavior, conquered me instantly, as if I had been waiting for you .

I am grateful that you awakened me to a new life in which the fear of death is no longer a threat.

Your clear spiritual vision, your mature and solid *śraddhā* in the *guru* and the *śāstras,* your deep and intense love for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, and your spirit of self-sacrifice for Śrī Gaurāṅgadeva’s mission attracted me instantly, then gradually melted my stonelike heart.

Thanks to your thoughtful example and to your teachings, I feel myself transformed, and I have come to take shelter at the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa and of the Vaiṣṇava saints.

By God’s divine grace I had the good fortune to meet you. I consider myself in debt to you and would like to give to others that which I have received from you. I hope to become capable of offering pure loving service at your lotus feet, speaking about you and about Lord Kṛṣṇa to all those sincere souls I will meet.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, continue to inspire me in my preaching, give me protection, vigor, and courage to sustain all of the inevitable trials of the illusory material energy, and always keep me close to your lotus feet and to your saintly devotees with unwavering memory, so that my thoughts, speech, and actions may become pure and to your liking.

With the desire to serve you better and better, we remain  
Your devotees at the Institute for Vaiṣṇava Studies (Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta), Italy.  
(written by Matsya Avatāra dāsa)

## ISCOWP (The International Society for Cow Protection)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In Sweden the devotees are building with clay and straw.

In Bangladesh, devotees are making mustard oil by ox-power and paper from water hyacinth and rice hay.

In Latvia the devotees use ceramic tile stoves for efficient wood heating.

In America, devotees are increasing the number of teamsters and trained oxen who are hauling wood from the forest for firewood and plowing the fields for planting.

In the former Yugoslavia, devotees have received government permission to establish a cow protection organization.

In France the devotees harvested 2 1/2 tons of buckwheat by ox-power.

In Siberia, devotees are plowing with oxen and giving vegetables to the temple.

In Belgium, devotees have said “no” to economic gain rather than be implicated in cow slaughter.

Throughout the world devotees are striving to follow your instructions of “simple living and high thinking.” They are fighting the mainstream attraction to absolute use of high technology, economic gain without integrity, lethargy in the face of obstacles, and a total disrespect for spiritual life involving sense control. Almost thirty years ago you gave instructions to implement self-sufficiency (a life of simple living and high thinking) based on the protection of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s cows and bulls. Yet now we are only at the beginning stages of following those instructions.

“We shall have to live there self-independently, simply by raising cows, grains, fruits, and flowers.” (letter to Kīrtanānanda, 12 January, 1968)

“If we can keep cows sufficiently and grow our necessary foodstuffs, then we shall show a new life to your countrymen—completely spiritual life in healthy atmosphere in divine consciousness.” (letter to Satyabhāmā, 30 March, 1969)

Since you have given your earlier instruction, the world situation has become far more desperate and demoniac. The list of offenses to Mother Earth, Mother Cow and Father Bull is lengthy and disturbing. The karmic results for the world are frightening.

Following your instructions will provide an alternative lifestyle which will attract the world’s suffering inhabitants. The world’s karmic results will fructify, and no shelter will be as potent as your “simple living and high thinking” based on Lord Kṛṣṇa and his loving relationship with the cows and bulls. No shelter will be as strong, bountiful, peaceful, and joyful as your manifested visionary lifestyle.

Your instructions are there, but the strength and wisdom to follow them are only gained by your mercy. Please bless us with your mercy. Please be merciful unto us and the devotees world-wide so that we may fulfill your vision of “simple living and high thinking.”

Your humble servants at ISCOWP.

## The ISKCON Foundation

**A Hundred Times**

*(At Śrīla Prabhupada’s Puṣpa-samādhi, February, 1996)*

Again I cross the pot-holed road  
Until it comes in view;  
Surely all of Nadia is  
Staring at it, too,  
Thinking of the person whom  
This lofty dome stands for,  
A hundred times a hundred times,  
Times a hundred more.

The massive marble slabs are fine,  
But finer than their grade:  
The love and sweat with which each piece  
Was planned and bought and laid.  
May Gaṅgā’s reach to touch his feet  
Be turned back to her shore,  
A hundred times a hundred times,  
Times a hundred more.

Here sits Jagannātha, and  
Over there sits Gaura Kiśore;  
Siddhānta looks on gravely, while  
Vinode smiles more and more.  
And in the center sits the great  
*Ācārya* they prayed for,  
A hundred times a hundred times,  
Times a hundred more.

Presiding from an inner shrine  
Of marble, green, and white,  
Built high above the flowers he wore  
Before he left our sight,  
His gentle *bhajans* fill the sky  
And draw me to his doors,  
A hundred times a hundred times,  
Times a hundred more.

Above, his holy pastimes gleam  
In bright mosaic tile,  
Each tile a brilliant moment when  
He made his *guru* smile.  
Below I count them carefully  
And proudly tell the score:  
A hundred times a hundred times,  
Times a hundred more.

*Pūjārīs* light his incense, and his  
Cooks prepare his dahl;  
His preachers sing his glories to  
The pilgrims, one and all.  
His golden footprints bless the head  
Of one whose heart is poor,  
A hundred times a hundred times,  
Times a hundred more.

Before the dawn a conchshell roars,  
And echoes flood the hall;  
A tidal wave of holy sound  
Surrounds the hearts of all.  
O Prabhupāda! Please glance upon  
A servant, head to floor,  
A hundred times a hundred times,  
Times a hundred more.

Like circles from a single stone  
Tossed in a tranquil pond,  
His forceful words surge forth from here,  
Through this world and beyond.  
His arms and legs are everywhere,  
His voice shall grace all shores,  
A hundred times a hundred times,  
Times a hundred more.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the ISKCON Foundation.  
(written by Kalakaṇṭha dāsa)

## ISKCON Prison Ministry

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I am forty-four years old and presently serving a seven-year sentence at the Institute for Men in Chino, California.

After reading your book *Science of Self-Realization,* I started chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and decided to begin following the four regulative principles. Overnight I stopped smoking cigarettes, drinking coffee, and eating meat, fish, and eggs! That was the first week of December, 1994. Immediately experiencing many benefits, I knew that something extraordinarily precious had arrived. This Kṛṣṇa consciousness was not to be taken lightly! It was time to take the first step toward spiritual advancement. So, on December 28, 1994, I made the commitment to complete at least sixteen rounds of *japa* every day.

Over the next six months the physical rewards became readily apparent in that I went from an obese 235 lbs. to a comfortable 185 lbs. Before I began chanting, my moment-to-moment concern was to escape my miserable condition with one drug after another (cocaine, heroin, or speed). Chanting the *mahā-mantra* has freed me from that destructive madness forever. Thank you so very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Currently I am doing my best to live as a *brahmacārī*. I rise at 2:45 a.m., wash, put on *tilaka,* offer obeisances and prayers, and then begin my rounds at 3:45 a.m. After finishing my rounds, I clean the altar in my locker and offer fruit, flowers, and water to our dear Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda (in Their photograph manifestation). Most mornings I have about an hour to study before going to my computer-aided drafting class.

After school (4:00 p.m.) I shower, re-apply *tilaka,* and then do a few more rounds of *japa*. Early evenings are filled with study and writing until about 7:00 p.m. At that time the yard opens and I go out on the grass and do about a half hour of *haṭha-yoga*.

Amala-bhakta Prabhu kindly sent me a pair of *karatālas*. So now I have the auspicious good fortune of doing *harināma-saṅkīrtana* around the 4/5 mile inside perimeter of the prison compound.

I have been doing *saṅkīrtana* faithfully every other night since he beginning of September 1995. At first I was very self-conscious, walking along by myself quietly ringing the *karatālas* and softly singing the *mahā-mantra*. But without my realizing their limitless potencies, the holy names steadily cleared away *mahā-māyā’s* illusion.

Now when I go on *saṅkīrtana,* the crisp and clear vibrations take the form of a strong transcendental wind that easily sweeps away the dark and heavy cloak of anxiety that, like an oppressive storm-cloud, had been covering my spirit soul and the whole prison as well.

Immersed in Lord Kṛṣṇa’s love and protection, I feel all inhibitions and feelings of vulnerability vanish! The original eternal state of the *jīva* soul nearly overwhelms me as it reveals itself to be light-filled, radiantly joyous—a reservoir full of boundless pleasure. This all serves to sweetly remind me of our beautiful Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Beside myself with enthusiasm and filled with a new-found certainty of purpose, I am struck with wonder as I listen to my own voice echoing back with ascending authority. Is this really happening? I’m singing so loudly and clearly right here in the middle of the prison yard!

The sky above seems to have turned a deep spiritual blue, and the moon’s translucent radiance calls to mind a pleasant Vṛndāvana night. Śrī Govinda is playing His flute somewhere in the distance . . .

For the time being I’ve forgotten where I am! I am unaware that dance has begun to rhythmically lift my feet, and it gradually dawns on me that I’m being carried aloft on an invisible wave, gently floating down some sacred river, fully absorbed in the vibrations of the holy names.

Exposing itself for the temporary facade that it really is, the prison environment with all its imposing drama has fallen away with the material world and drifted off into impotent obscurity.

Then, calling me back from my revelry, the *karatālas* take on a thrilling life of their own! It is all I can do just to hang on to them as their piercing and powerful transcendental melody purifies the area far and wide. ISKCON Chino kī jaya!

Hardened convicts with an image to maintain, or should I just say conditioned souls caught with their guard down, find themselves smiling, laughing, clapping, and calling out “Haribol! Hare Kṛṣṇa!” Then, like expert whirling dervishes (who never give a thought to the scrutinizing eyes of on-lookers), a few choice individuals engage themselves with jubilant, reckless abandon in dancing wildly and drinking in the nectar of the holy names.

Again Kali-yuga is cheated of its reign of misery as an air of spiritual celebration prevails. Jaya Śrī Kṛṣṇa! Jaya Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurāṅga! Everyone within earshot is unwittingly saturated with the nectar of the holy names. All glories to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu! *Saṅkīrtana kī jaya!*

How can I show my gratitude? You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have made all this possible. Thank you! I fall at your feet in unending appreciation. You have made living cherishable and have given this life a worthwhile purpose. I am eternally in your debt. Please always allow me to help you serve Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Candrśekhara dāsa and all the devotees assisting the ISKCON Prison Ministry.  
(written by Bhakta Dan)

## The ISKCON School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

Thank you for enthusing us to inspire our students with what you called “fresh enthusiasm,” the proof of Kṛṣṇa conscious instruction. Please give us your mercy so that we can always direct our students to voluntarily serve Kṛṣṇa with a spontaneous surge of individual enthusiasm. When our students sacrifice their time to distribute your books, when they dedicate their concentration to the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s name, when they surrender their lives to the feet of your disciples, and when they study their courses to enable them to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we feel that you are pleased with us.

In this world of ignorance that comes disguised as education, we pray to present a revolution in consciousness through *gurukula*. Please bless us so that we may be successful in this mission.

Your servants at The ISKCON School in Hillsborough, North Carolina  
(written by Urmilā-devī dāsī)

## ISKCON Television (ITV)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

A new “World of Hare Kṛṣṇa” is in the making, and Your Divine Grace is producing it. Yes, you are practically forcing it to manifest! We started the project with no official support, nor any money, nor hardly a speck of the determination with which you sustained in your desire to satisfy your spiritual master. Yet somehow, day by day, you inspired your disciples to help us, and you manifested miracles to clear the path for production. You sent us to all the corners of the earth so we could record—in amazement—the results of preaching the glories of the holy names. Our travels took us on a transcendental worldwide tour to such remote places as Govinda’s Cantina in the jungles of Machu Picchu, Peru, to big-city surprises like the skyscraper temple in St. Petersburg, Russia. We learned to expect the unexpected, like the llama /radio preaching center in Utah, USA, and the dangerous Food for Life program in Grozny, Chechnya. Everywhere we traveled, we found dedicated devotees engaged in devotional practices that had been inspired by you.

There are too many towns and villages to visit in a lifetime. Yet, you have instructed us to spread this *saṅkīrtana* movement to every one of them. “Impossible,” you reminded us, “is a word in a fool’s dictionary.” Unfortunately, I still can’t get it out of mine.

You know that I am weak, and therefore you have engaged me in this service, which bestows association of devotees like an incessant monsoon rain upon a parched desert. I have gathered strength from their actions, their counsel, and their adherence to the knowledge in your books. When I joined in 1971, I felt a great fear of isolation and alienation from “normal” society. I thought that my world had shrunk and that I had only a few eccentric monks as my social circle. My future seemed lonely. Little did I realize that a real society was being born—like a lotus from a navel—sprouting a stalk that would soon flower into an international confederation of *varṇas* and *āśramas* expanding like sound waves from your lotus lips. Now I can’t keep up with the “free forum,” letters, phone calls, friends, congregational members, acquaintances, and family—what to speak of my own *sādhana* and duties at ITV to the work and the staff. My social calendar is overflowing, and my mind is brimming with appointments and “to-dos.” How am I ever going to accomplish it all? IMPOSSIBLE! (Whoops. I just don’t know how to eliminate that word from my computer.)

I should have known that ISKCON would expand, but I didn’t comprehend the potency of your desire. In fact, ISKCON has grown so fast in the fertilized soil of your mercy that we had to have a Social Development Conference in Māyāpur this year. (I didn’t have time to stay for the entire three days of discussions; ironically, there were too many devotees with whom to talk and “socialize” while I was there. I guess my “social circle” worries are over.)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have delivered on your promises a million times over. I am still trying to fulfill mine to you. Please continue to influence and guide me. Sometimes I feel so very alone in the midst of such exalted company—you, the Deities of their Lordships, the devotees, and all the paraphernalia of spiritual life. I want that feeling to just disappear, but it seems so impossible. Only your mercy can eliminate this *māyā*.

I cling to the hope that some day my mind will find its satisfaction in serving the Vaiṣṇavas—in any capacity, in any condition, in any country, in any universe. You showed us how to be truly self-sufficient—*ātmārāma*. Please don’t give up on me. Enable me to attain that truly Kṛṣṇa conscious platform.

We have lists of programs that we want to produce. The endeavor could take a lifetime. We would like it to take a lot less. “The History of Personalism” is my favorite of those potential programs because you gave us the gift of the Personality of Godhead. We hope to broadcast the legacy of that gift to the entire world and to distribute the glories of your divine personality along with it. You are an exalted expansion of the personality of the Lord. Thank you for the privilege of another year of service. I beg to remain your servant.

The devotees doing service at ITV.  
(written by Nṛsiṁhānanda dāsa)

## ISKCON World Review

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In this, your Centennial year, I’ve been looking for ways in my own life in which I can glorify you. Unfortunately for me, I have found none. But still, as a result of your great mercy, I have an opportunity to serve you as a member of your *ISKCON World Review* staff. This allows me the privilege of helping call attention to the great service being performed by those who are actually qualified to serve you.

Devotees are holding special events this year like Feed the World Day, World Holy Name Day, Padayātrā Week, and World Enlightenment Day that will get more and more devotees out there distributing your books and preaching. There are Ratha-yātrās in so many countries now, with important people attending as honored guests—all springing from your desire, even as a small child, to glorify Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Moreover, your followers are taking seriously your desire to establish the Vedic social system. Devotees around the world are earnestly trying to understand your instructions on this and how members of different *āśramas* can work together for your satisfaction. Just this year at the annual Māyāpur meetings, the GBC wisely decided to serve your international society by establishing much-needed ministries for women, gṛhasthas, and youth. We are proud of them. They are taking the steps necessary to unite our movement and make it the spiritual family you described.

Back here at IWR we are working more and more with your grandchildren, the devotees of the second generation. We are discovering that they are your natural servants—bright, brave, and loving. By your mercy we hope to help train them as future leaders of this movement and of the world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, only the miracle of your divine association can make us behave properly toward one another. We feel that it is so important for devotees to appreciate and respect one another. We pray continuously for the success of all your devotees. Thank you for not giving up on me although I am a foolish rascal. Thank you for inspiring Lokanāth Swami to organize this Centennial Celebration of your appearance in this world. Being conscious of you and what you want us to do is the best way to heal our movement and propel it into the next millennium.

Thank you for showing us how to work with the GBC, with the temple presidents, and with so many people you are saving. Please help us to serve your devotees well, to inspire the youth and help them understand that this is their Society and that their places in it are vitally important.

Your servants at ISKCON World Review.  
(written by Sarva-satya dāsa)

## ISKCON Youth Ministry

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances on behalf of your many gurukula grandchildren, the second generation. Please accept this humble offering on your most glorious Centennial appearance day.

Many of us have now grown into young adults. You had a lot of faith in us to push forward your preaching mission and carry on the dynamic spirit of the *harināma-saṅkīrtana* revolution. I am inspired when I read how you began, seemingly alone, in Tompkins Square Park. You were chanting tinder a tree, and for many days no one paid any attention. Then finally, some people joined in the chanting, and then more and more came. Before long, your firm desire to carry out the order of your Guru Maharaja was rewarded: so many seeds of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* were spread all over the world. You always took the desire of your Guru Maharaja as your life and soul.

You wanted us *gurukula* children to become the future preachers who would “turn the world by their preaching.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, in so many ways I feel incapable of such a task. I gasp in awe at what your disciples have accomplished, at what even the new *bhaktas* are accomplishing on book distribution, so selflessly surrendering their lives. I pray that all of us will take your desire seriously, that we will make it our life and soul to repeat your sublime message without any addition or change, and that I will find the strength and courage to finally submit my pride at the lotus feet of your dear disciples.

I have witnessed many “wrongs” growing up in the helter skelter of a young counter-material-culture ISKCON. Many of your wishes regarding *gurukula* remain to be implemented. When you opened the first *gurukula* school in Dallas, in 1971, you wanted it to become a model school to educate and train tomorrow's leaders. We still have a long way to go. Devotees need to truly recognize *gurukula* as a long-term, very important preaching project worthy of major support. In your Folio database, you've stressed three things as “most important”: book distribution, Deity worship, and *gurukula! “Gurukula* is our most important task ahead.”

Still, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I would never trade my *gurukula āśrama* experiences for anything. We did have a lot of good times. The bottom line was that we learned to love and depend on Kṛṣṇa in all circumstances. Like many of my peers, I got side-tracked for a while and experienced the socalled normal world of economic development and sense gratification, but when I got tired of that and was ready to fall back on something, it was my Kṛṣṇa conscious upbringing.

This year, for the first time, a member of your *gurukula* second generation participated in the entire GBC meetings in Māyāpura. The Governing Body Commissioners passed resolutions that all ISKCON leaders, department heads, and businessmen should see it as their moral and spiritual duty to train your older *gurukula* students and to consider them first, before anyone else, when it comes to filling service or employment positions.

There was a Vaiṣṇava youth bus tour last year that inspired many young and older devotees. Many of the temples we visited wanted us to stay and take over. Śrīla Prabhupāda, only by your causeless mercy will we become serious and sincere enough to take on such responsibility and commitment. Some *gurukulīs* are already dedicating their lives to your mission. Śruta Kīrti’s son, Māyāpura Candra dāsa, went out for the first time on book distribution at L.A. airport today. Karṇa dāsa from New Vṛndāvana is on traveling book distribution. Kālindī dāsī, Babhruvahana's daughter, has started a Bhakti Yoga Club at her college. She walks around campus between classes, playing her mṛdaṅga and chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. For Vegetarian Week, Rāsalīlā dāsī from New Tālavana appeared on television in Mississippi, telling her story of growing up as a vegetarian in the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. Another one of your *gurukulī* soldiers, Sūrya dāsī, is a flight attendant at Delta Airlines and has no qualms about telling people about Krsna. In Laguna Beach, Jagāi, Kuñjī, Jaya Jagannātha, Nāma, Abhay, and Rūpa are running the temple and restaurant. They have had eleven new *bhaktas and* five new *bhaktins* in the last six months! Śālagrāma Śilā dāsa, whom I remember as one of the “small boys” from our *āśrama* at the New Māyāpur *gurukula,* now has a scholarship to study law at Cambridge University in England. He wants to be a high court magistrate!

Dear Srila Prabhupada, by your mercy even a lame man can walk. Please give us the shade of your lotus feet. By your mercy we can become fearless and fully dependent on Kṛṣṇa, as You are. Please give us guidance and encouragement through your representatives, the older devotees, as wonderful and sweet as the *mahā* cookies you used to give out during *guru-pūjā,* enthusing us to chant and dance and fearlessly play our part in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* party.

Your humble servants at the ISKCON Youth Ministry.  
(written by Manu dāsa)

## Lotus Voices

**“Although we are unable to offer prayers to the Lord in an adequate fashion, our duty is to make the attempt to purify ourselves. … If we are serious and sincere devotees, the Lord will give us the intelligence to offer prayers properly.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **4.16.2, purport)**

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humblest obeisances at your divine lotus feet, which are the source of unlimited bliss for all the devotees.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most blessed event of your Vyāsa-pūjā we would like to express our deepest gratitude to you. However, no words are sufficient to begin to praise your transcendental qualities. Surely Śrī Ananta Śeṣa sings your glories with His unlimited mouths and still this is not sufficient. You’ve saved the world.

Just as in the *Rāmāyaṇa* a small spider assist the monkey devotees to serve Śrī Rāmacandra, we beg you to allow us to serve your great Kṛṣṇa consciousness society though our service is immature and imperfect, and we beg you to forgive our offenses.

You taught that “the grandfather always takes care of the children better than the father.” Your are our spiritual grandfather, and you have mercifully allowed us to serve you through your *vāṇī* and through the disciplic succession. Though we are stumbling along like small infants, and we are also swimming in the continuous flow of nectar that you’ve benedicted the world with.

Please help us to repeat—though our voices may be imperfect—the words emanating from your lotus mouth

Begging to be the servants of your servants, we remain  
The staff of “Lotus Voices,” the newsletter of the ISKCON New York Youth Forum.

## Murāri-sevaka /Murfreesboro Sunday School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

Thank you for leading Kṛṣṇa here so we can have a good life.

Sincerely,  
Nikhil Reddy, age 7

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

Thank you for your *Kṛṣṇa* books so we can read them. I am thankful for my van so we can drive to the Murāri-sevaka farm.

Sincerely,  
Manjari Singh, age 6

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Haribol! Please accept my humble obeisances.

I am very thankful for everything you have given me. But the three most important things I am grateful for are: the beautiful deities of Rādhā-Natabara to worship every day, *prasādam,* and my Sunday school class so I can learn about Kṛṣṇa and your books. But something that tops all is that you brought Kṛṣṇa consciousness to America.

Hare Kṛṣṇa!  
Shruti Singh, age 11

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

I’m thankful for the books on Kṛṣṇa so I can learn about Kṛṣṇa’s life.

Sincerely,  
Naveen Reddy, age 7

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept my humble obeisances.

I am thankful for many things. Three things are: the books on Kṛṣṇa to help us become Kṛṣṇa conscious, knowing about Kṛṣṇa so I can become a devotee, and *prasādam,* so I know what holy food tastes like.

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.*

Swapna Reddy, age 9

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept my humble obeisances.

Thank you for all the things you have given me: the Deities so I can worship Them; the books, translated into English so I can understand what they say; and teaching us how to cook *prasādam* so I can eat it. I also thank you for coming to America, because I never would have known about Kṛṣṇa otherwise.

Sincerely,  
Malati Johnson, age 12

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

I am thankful for your bringing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to America because it is good to be a devotee. Thank you for bringing the devotees to my house so I can chant with them. Thank you for the *Kṛṣṇa* books so I can read them.

Sincerely,  
Abhishek Singh, age 9

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

I appreciate you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us *kīrtana* and arati. Without you I wouldn’t have *prasādam,* Kṛṣṇa Camp, Janmāṣṭamī, Gaura-pūrṇimā, and Rādhāṣṭamī. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please bless me that I become a good devotee.

Deva Campbell

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

It is by your grace that I am fortunate enough to have the association of devotees. Please allow me to always remain in their association and to continue on the path of self- realization. I am forever indebted.

Your unworthy servant,  
Basudhā-devī dāsī

\* \* \*

In closing, we at the Murāri-sevaka /Murfreesboro Sunday School are feeling like the sages who spoke to Sūta Gosvāmī like this: “Śrīla Prabhupāda, we think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.1.22)

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!!

## Nāma Haṭṭa (Israel)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to Lord Caitanya’s divine *saṅkīrtana* movement!

In 1971 you wrote several letters to two disciples Your Divine Grace had sent to preach in Israel. From these letters it is clear that you strongly desire that the *saṅkīrtana* movement spread in this “far-off land.” We have to admit that we haven’t been successful yet in achieving great things here. But by your mercy we are following at least some of your instructions, as indicated in these letters. For example, you write:

“Yes, you can set up a Guru Gauranga altar, and if you can offer full artik ceremony, that is nice. Pancha Tatwa are most merciful.” We have a Guru Gaurāṅga altar, and in our meetings we offer full *ārati* to our Gaura-Nitāi Deities.

You write, “You can stay in your apartment, have discourses, and hold kirtana, and whoever voluntarily may join, no one can object. Whoever comes, give him nice prasadam.” We regularly hold discourses on your books in our apartments, hold *kīrtana,* and distribute *prasādam* to our members and visitors. Furthermore, we are trying to serve the *saṅkīrtana* movement by our professions. Thus we operate a catering business, as well as a cake distribution program. We also hope to be able to open a *prasādam* restaurant soon. By your mercy we hope to distribute *prasādam* to many conditioned souls here.

You write, “Just try to give this Krishna philosophy to the intelligent class of men, and distribute our books as many as possible,” and “you have to introduce the matter of God scientifically, and that will be appreciated by any reasonable man.” We have a lecturing program aimed at the intelligent class of men and women. The basic book taught is your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*.” So far we have spoken before over two thousand people in this program and have distributed *Bhagavad-gītās* to the audience, as well as an introductory book we wrote.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are low, fallen, and devoid of good qualities. However, we have strong faith in your instructions. In your books you have described the *gṛhastha-āśrama* as an ideal situation for preaching, as well as for establishing Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You describe the *gṛhastha* as pure, a preacher who maintains his home as a temple and is a positive and auspicious member of society at large. This is our aim—to try to become auspicious persons through following your simple instructions, and thereby to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

On this auspicious day of the hundredth anniversary of your appearance, we would like to ask Your Divine Grace for a benediction: Please allow us to help fulfill the prediction that you will build a house in which the whole world will reside by further manifesting this prediction in Israel.

Your servants in the Israel Nāma Haṭṭa and the Amirim project.  
(written by Īśvara Kṛṣṇa dāsa)

## Padayātrā England

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus like feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Belfast—June 1, 1990.

Padayātrā Europe starts amid a flurry of publicity. Cameras flash and cameras roll. We make the 6 o’clock news. A peace march from war-torn Belfast to Moscow. The plan is to arrive in Moscow, where our Russian Godbrothers and -sisters are imprisoned in labor camps and psychiatric hospitals, by your Centennial year. “Will we ever get to Moscow?”, I wonder Of course we will. Let’s buy a map. More immediate problems pre-occupy my mind. We got off to a fantastic start, but where do we spend the night? It’s going to rain—no, it *is* raining. At least I remembered the matches, but did the stoves get packed? 1996 seems ten thousand years away.

Three months latter the first stage is over. Ireland circumambulated. Twenty newspaper reports, all favorable. Five hundred *Bhāgavatams* distributed, by Subala Prabhu’s grace. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came with us the whole way. Now you have seen this small island, the place of my birth. You shared the adventure, the joys, the austerities. You’ve spent nights in the monasteries of the different Catholic orders, tasted the spring water fresh from the streams, worn the wildflowers around your neck. I hope you have enjoyed it as much as we have. One of your last desires was to travel by ox-cart around Govardhana Hill. So we took you around Ireland as well. Next year we can go the length of England on a new adventure.

May 1, 1991—a field outside Newcastle, England.

I don’t think I’ve ever been so cold in all my life. Thankfully, after a winter spent comfortably in the Dublin temple, you are nice and warm at the Newcastle temple. We’re installing Nitāi-Gaurasundara today. They will also travel with us to Moscow. Five years to go.

England passes in a blaze of publicity. “On the road for change” is our motto. I’m your *pūjārī*. I’m so fortunate. Making sure you don’t get wet or cold. Quite a hard job considering the weather—months of rain. On to Holland, Belgium, and France. After six months of carrying you on and off the cart, into tents, into cars, into temples, I finally carry you into your bedroom in New Māyāpur. It’s a bit of a relief. The last few weeks I had to break the ice in the *ācamana* cup before waking you. You’ll spend a comfy winter in France before starting our adventure again in the spring.

1992—Spain.

There is a new motto now. Lokanātha Mahārāja is saying “100 countries by 1996.” We look at an atlas and start counting countries. I thought there were more countries than that in the world. Is that all there are? The world really is small and insignificant. How will we ever cover 100 countries by 1996? 1996 seems to be looming increasingly closer.

Prabhupāda, you are sitting sweetly on your cart in Spain. I think our adventure is over.

Padayātrā Europe has many devotees to care for you. I’m going to resurrect the Irish Padayātrā cart and start a fresh party to spread your wonders further afield.

Prabhupāda, we are together again, only now you are brass and fifteen inches high. Those little Nitāi-Gaurasundara Deities you are sitting next to—you held them when you were physically present. And those four-foot wooden ones are a gift directly from Kṛṣṇa—well that’s how it seems. Thankfully, Kṛṣṇa has also arranged that the U.S.S.R. has broken down into its former countries. Maybe we’ll get 100 done yet. Lets get going.

1993—Sweden, Estonia, Lithuania, Russia, Luxembourg.

1994—Germany, Poland, Latvia, Finland.

**1995—**Only one year to go, and still so many countries to cover if we are to reach 100. Prabhupāda, it’s time to fly. I’ll carry you carefully as hand baggage. Lets get going. Denmark, Norway, Iceland, Cyprus, Israel, Jordan, Palestine, Egypt, Portugal.

At last it’s here, your Centennial year! 37 countries to go. Can we help complete the goal? We’ll give it a good go. We’ll have to go off adventuring in Africa, maybe more Muslim countries.

Prabhupāda, I’ve seen some amazing things on our adventures. On that first day when we left Belfast, the farmer who saved us from the rain was so surprised when he saw you that he spontaneously touched your foot and then his head. In one monastery the priests came to *maṅgala-ārati* in their chapel and worshiped you as you sat on their own altar. At one festival in Tipperary, people were talking to you and asking if they could take a photo. You appeared to be answering them. In England you had a graze on your head one day, and while I wondered how to fix it, it disappeared. In France, a car pulled up beside you, and the people asked you directions.

Many, many, pastimes you have had, and you continue to have, changing our stonelike hearts. We pray that you continue to engage us in your service, for it is you who are the driving force behind our endeavors. Without your mercy we would be unable to do anything. Without you there would be no point to anything. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. After all, it is not we who have taken you on Padayātrā but you who have taken us on Padayātrā.

Your servants on Padayātrā England and the innumerable devotees who have helped us over the last six years.  
(written by Paraśurāma dāsa)

## Padayātrā India

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda! How can we express in a few words our feelings for being able to accompany you and Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara during the past twelve years, circumambulating Bhārata-varṣa more than three times, covering some 50,000 kms? What have we done to deserve such a glorified position, being able to walk with you, chant alongside you, distribute your books, instruct others with your instructions, preach to everyone about your wonderful pastimes, show your ISKCON films, or simply observe the thousands upon thousands of fortunate souls observing you in amazement, as you sit majestically on Their Lordships’ chariot?

Due to the heavy influence of Kali-yuga, India is experiencing a rapid decline in real pure spiritual devotion to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. But your presence on Padayātrā turns the horrible situation into another Satya-yuga. You are the Golden Ācārya during these ten thousand years that are upon us now, which makes you a very rare, precious gift to all of the conditioned souls, not only in India but throughout this world and certainly beyond, to other planetary systems. Simply by your preaching in the Indian villages, all the plans of Kali are slowly coming to an end, which is something unbelievable yet most wonderful.

Sometimes, while we are on the road, the heavy load of austerities can be very demanding. We tend to forget our positions as *padayātrīs* and occasionally commit offenses toward each other or even toward others. But when you bring us back to our proper consciousness by so many subtle and direct methods, it only brings us down on our knees with shame and at the same time increases our genuine love for you.

Recently during the Gaura Purnima festival in Māyāpur, the *padayātrīs* were glorified for their many years of *tapasya* and service. But what is the meaning of such glorification, because without you being in the center of our lives, every step, every difficulty, every encounter, both joyous and sad, would simply be a waste of time? You are also very fortunate to have two very dear dedicated disciples, His Holiness Lokanāth Swami and His Holiness Jayādvaita Swami, who had the clear vision to begin your Centennial glorification in 1986, making the Padayātrā the first ISKCON *yātrā* to preach about your Centennial, which proves that your Padayātrā in India is the root of all Centennial activities.

During the time Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was in Navadvīpa *dhāma* performing his pastimes in Kāśī Miśra’s house, Kṛṣṇadāsa inquired about the glories of the holy name. Caitanya Mahāprabhu replied at great length and quoted from various Vedic sources more than seventy *ślokas* describing the unlimited potencies and glories of the holy name.
