Afterwards, Kṛṣṇadāsa had one last doubt. He wondered why chanting does not give everyone the same result. The Lord replied *śraddhā* (faith) was the root of everyone’s spiritual life and that the Supersoul reciprocates according to that faith. But when our faith is lacking or when we are disturbed, or when our minds are far from our services, we tend to drift away and become entangled in material consciousness again. Then, when we simply glance upon you or remember your pastimes, our faith instantly returns, not at the same level but with deeper love and appreciation for you, O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

From deep within our impure hearts, we cannot begin to thank you enough for everything you have given us.

Hare Kṛṣṇa !

Your insignificant walking servants on Padayātrā India.  
(written by Jaya Vijaya dāsa)

## The Prabhupāda Trust

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Your divine presence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your overwhelming mercy is profoundly felt everywhere in your ISKCON wherever sincere devotees gather to respect each other and co-operate together to glorify you, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava.

When I returned to your glorious Śrī Mayapur project on this hundredth anniversary of your divine appearance on this planet, it was literally impossible not to see your lotus hand at work.

I remember guarding your quarters on the second floor of what is now called the Lotus Building, but what was then our one and only temple building. You walked out of your room, so gracefully, and leaned against the bannister to look at the construction of the original Long Building (now the Cakra Building) in progress. You looked for a long time. You saw it all then—a vast Vedic City dedicated to the universal culture of love of God; thousands of devotees from every nation sincerely offering their lives to your divine mission, with all of the worldwide preaching inspired from the central whorl of the lotus at this Śrī Mayapur City. Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s prediction perfectly fulfilled.

And now, two short decades later, your Temple of the Vedic Planetarium is only a small step away—the natural and inevitable next step.

The unfathomable depth of your love for the Lord and of your mercy on the conditioned souls becomes clearer to us as the days and years go by.

Please continue to shower your mercy so that we may continue to help with your transcendental book production. Please somehow engage us forever in devotional service and in your causeless mercy mission to reawaken every conditioned soul to their eternally joyful position as the servant of the servant of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava. Śrīla Prabhupāda Ki jaya!!!

Your eternal servant at The Prabhupāda Trust  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa dāsa)

## Prabhupāda Village

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. The residents at Prabhupāda Village would like to submit this humble offering for your pleasure.

When the earth was overburdened  
Bhūmi cried and the Lord appeared,  
Kṛṣṇa lifted the burden and killed the demons  
but now once again the earth cries.  
Being raped and pillaged by huge corporations  
that scar her with chemicals  
and dissect her body with huge machines,  
stealing her vital parts to make useless contraptions  
that rush us quicker to our death.  
The unfortunate live in cities, the abodes of sin,  
working in prisonlike offices and factories,  
spending the sweat of their brows on bottled water  
or cancer surgery because the river-water is poison.

But in the capital of Kali appears Your Divine Grace,  
Vaikuṇṭha ambassador, with the courage of Prahlāda,  
determination of Dhruva, and potency of Nārada.  
With your weapons of knowledge, you have made  
Kali stop dead in his tracks.  
Gradually, the medicine you injected will revive man’s  
sanity and save the earth once again.  
“The country is made by God—village life is real life—simple living, high thinking.”  
One day, by your mercy, millions of people  
will live peacefully in the lap of nature.

It seems inconceivable that society can change  
back to the simple life.  
But who are we to doubt you, who changed bell metal to gold?  
How could we ever reject you, after you have given us the nectar that eluded us so long?  
The seed you planted in our hearts is slowly  
giving the fruit of love of God.  
The seeds we plant here will also one day  
make a beautiful offering for our dear Lord Kṛṣṇa  
and all those souls, in all those seeds, will also owe you so much, for you are freeing  
all living entities on this planet.

We have a long way to fulfill your dream,  
so please continue to inspire us with your vision  
so all living entities will gather together  
on this day and sing “Jaya Prabhupāda!”  
and humbly beg you to engage them in  
devotional service.

Your servants at Prabhupāda Village, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina.  
(written by Ādi-kartā)

## Radio Krishna Centrale

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, all glories to the most magnanimous Lords Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and all glories to the supreme divine couple Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. This poor defeated soul is crawling on his knees, striving to reach protection under the soles of your eternal lotus feet.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, if it pleases you I’ll offer unto your lotus feet some of the understandings that I have gained from Your Divine Grace.

The whole meaning of anyone’s existence is to satisfy the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. For me, a conditioned soul, any effort done in any direction, be it those aiming to make this world a better place to live or those aiming to gain one freedom from the clutches of material existence—or any other effort, even if it aims to secure one “spiritual advancement”—if it does not really satisfy Kṛṣṇa, it’s useless and makes my existence meaningless.

I can’t please Kṛṣṇa if I do not satisfy you. Of course, Kṛṣṇa and His pure devotee are always fully satisfied and do not require anyone’s support, but I need to be part of their satisfaction if I want the meaning of my existence to be manifested. With the proper attitude of submissive humbleness, engaging in serving your instructions and orders, always remaining a fool in front of you, without personal, concocted ideas, I can please you and satisfy Kṛṣṇa. I should not trust my intelligence or anyone’s intelligence if it’s not coming from you. I should not think, “This is Prabhupāda’s way of doing things, but I’m a different person and I’ve got my way,” covering up my sense of independence under the excuse that I should not imitate you.

From you I have learned that a devotee should not care much for his personal comforts or whether he is liberated or not, but he should be anxious only for devotional service at the lotus feet of the pure devotee spiritual master. That should be his desire, life after life. Devotional service means that one does not do anything different from the directions of the spiritual master, the pure devotee. I should therefore not do anything whimsically and then offer the result to you. “That sort of duty is not in the devotional service of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 18.57, purport)

(Am I wrong when, if called to do something that does not reflect your mentality and your conclusions, even if it sounds very intelligent, I do not feel enthusiastic about it, knowing that it will only be a useless waste of time and energy, since it will be a failure? Am I wrong when, if I hear somebody saying “Śrīla Prabhupāda said like that at that time, but now the times have changed. ISKCON has changed, and many of those things do no apply anymore, Prabhu. We have to adjust things for the new situation,” I feel disturbed and a little angry?)

“By serving the servants of the Lord, one gradually gets the quality of such servants.”

I do not want to be a rascal and be separated from you. My desire is to get your quality and, by pleasing Your Divine Grace, obtain your eternal association.

I haven’t got good qualities. Still, I have strong and firm faith in your words. I do not exist without them. They are my life and my only solace and the only words that I want to hear. Please give me the strength to help in executing your mission so I may gain your eternal association. I’ll do whatever is needed.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, this is my only prayer: Please let me become the eternal servant of your lotus feet and let me be satisfied by your satisfaction. Then even though your are far from this place, I’ll always be with Your Divine Grace.

Your insignificant servants at Radio Krishna Centrale, Terni, Italy.  
(written by Tridaṇḍi dāsa)

## Śaraṇāgati

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The residents of one of your most outlying outposts of *saṅkīrtana* and *seva,* Śaraṇāgati Dhāma, would like to humbly bring forward this offering to you on the hundredth anniversary of your appearance in this world. We collectively prostrate ourselves at your feet in relief, thanks, and awe for being allowed, by Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement, to gather together here with one common ideal—that is, to try to lovingly please you by fulfilling your desire to develop our Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to promote it to others.

The devotees here are seeing this bit of land as a place of spiritual refuge, where purification is in full force, much like any holy *dhāma*. Because of Śaraṇāgati’s remote setting, we have been coaxed to adopt the simple lifestyle that you have often recommended. In this way, we are required to relinquish many of the so-called conveniences of the modern world. Māyā’s glare is slightly diminished, and we can see that many of these attachments are simply *anarthas* when used to further the material conception of life. As we attempt to refine our simplicity and self-sufficiency, the harsh reality of survival on the material plane becomes less and less concealed. This only increases our desire for full absorption in devotional service. This place is called Śaraṇāgati Dhāma, the place of full surrender. We are feeling the urgency to do just that—to surrender our selfish plans, our fruitive mentality, and our material desires unto the service of Lord Jagannātha and Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

It is by your grace that we are now positioned to accept a greater understanding of true spiritual reality. We can conclude that any place can be transformed into a holy *dhāma* when the residents follow your instructions. On this day, as every day, we are again asking more from you. We implore you to guide us in establishing your vision of a Kṛṣṇa conscious rural community. Please help us see how *saṅkīrtana, varṇa-āśrama,* and devotional service can flourish here as you would like to see them.

This day marks the conclusion of one hundred years since your birth. But we would prefer to see it as the first day of the next hundred years—years that will be gloriously marked as a time when your instructions will ever-increasingly be taken to heart by multitudes of conditioned souls. We will be trying our best to assist you in fulfilling your mission, which is the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

The residents of Śaraṇāgati Dhāma, British Columbia, Canada.

## Śrīla Prabhupāda Centennial Office (North America)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept my humble obeisances. Today is the Centennial anniversary of your appearance day, a day eagerly anticipated by your followers and well-wishers around the world.

What were your glorious achievements while you were here in this earthly realm? Only the Supreme Personality of Godhead fully knows your glories. I will mention a few of them.

You established 108 temples in major cities around the world, thus bringing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the doorstep of millions.

You published many volumes of Vedic literature in English and other languages. In this way you made the topmost philosophy available to every common man.

You opened restaurants serving only *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* to its guests (which is coveted even by the demigods). With this secret weapon you attracted millions of atheists to Kṛṣṇa.

You instituted *gurukulas* to provide proper training for our youth. Thus they will not be misguided by the materialistic views taught in nondevotee schools.

You initiated thousands of devotees, saving them from a hellish condition in their present and future lives. You created our good fortune and made us into human beings.

You introduced the idea of rural Vedic life, which taught self-sufficiency and environmentalism before they became popular. In this way you demonstrated simple living and high thinking quite effectively.

You established cow protection. What society is advanced if it condones the killing of its mothers? You taught us to love all Kṛṣṇa’s parts and parcels, not just those in human society.

You uplifted America’s hippie youth and made them into Vaiṣṇavas. Now they are no longer hippies but happies.

You established that God is a person and saved the Western world from the all-pervasive ideas of impersonalism. You accomplished this by your preaching to defeat the materialistic scientists and pseudo religionists.

You founded a spiritual city in Māyāpur, which, when completed, will be home to 50,000 Vaiṣṇavas. This city will fulfill Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s prediction of a wonderful temple in Māyāpur to glorify Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

You instituted Deity worship of the highest standard in cities and towns around the world, making Their temples places of pilgrimage and spiritual advancement.

You circled the globe twelve times, purifying temples, parks, airports, roads, and auditoriums, making them places of pilgrimage for your followers.

You inspired hundreds and thousands, guiding them toward the ultimate goal of life, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, a goal that had been previously completely unknown to them.

Your influence will live on in this world for the next ten thousand years. I have been deeply blessed by your presence in my life. I can never repay my debt to you, nor can the world.

Your insignificant servants at the North American Centennial Office in Alachua, Florida.  
(written by Ananta-devī dāsī)

## Thailand and Siam Vedic Society

*iti tad-bhajanād gatavān pramadaṁ*  
*praṇamāmi sadā prabhupāda-pādam*

**I always bow down to the lotus feet of my spiritual master, His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, due to whose devotion my laziness has dissipated.**

Offering my humble obeisances to my Godbrothers, headed by Śrī Mukunda Goswami Mahārāja, and by his mercy, I now attempt to gather together the pieces of my scattered and erstwhile wasted human span of life and mold them into some form of steady purpose.

We all know from Śrīla Prabhupāda and Prahlāda Mahārāja that taking the dust from the feet of the Vaiṣṇavas is the only way to achieve the most sacred gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness; so I beg to take dust from the feet of all the devotees, both my Godbrothers and Godsisters and all other devotees in the line of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. May they all help me to one day chant Śrī Hari-Nāma with relish and without any offense or other distraction.

Taking a key from the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, I will try to organize my mission statement into three divisions:

RELATIONSHIP: While wandering through unlimited *brahmāṇḍas,* birth after birth, somehow, by great good fortune, I came into contact with Śrī Gurudeva, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in 1967. I was taken before him by Śrī Mukunda Goswami, and on the day of my initiation I was so unqualified that I shook hands with Śrīla Prabhupāda on our first meeting. So I believe that I have a deal with His Divine Grace, i.e., I agree to follow the four regulative principles, chant sixteen rounds daily of the *mahā-mantra* (with concentration), eat *kṛṣṇa-prasadam,* and associate with Kṛṣṇa’s devotees, and then His Divine Grace will surely benedict me with *śraddhā* (firm faith), *ruci* (a little taste for Kṛṣṇa), and *vijñāna* (a little knowledge of our eternal relationship with Guru and Kṛṣṇa). “According to all *śāstras,* Śrī Guru is a form of Kṛṣṇa; in the form of Guru, Kṛṣṇa is giving mercy to the devotees” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 1.45).

Early in my relationship with Śrīla Prabhupāda, I went to join His Divine Grace at Vṛndāvana and was misled into a false relationship with one of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Godbrothers. I tried my best to understand Kṛṣṇa consciousness and Vraja-dhāma during those early years, living as a fugitive in the Gauḍīya Maṭha. Vraja-rasa was everywhere in the *dhāma,* and I tried my best to imbibe at least one drop. But later, by the mercy of His Divine Grace, I could understand that my real, eternal *relationship* to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa was through Śrīla Prabhupāda; and Śrīla Prabhupāda so kindly confirmed this when I rejoined him in Śrīdhām Māyāpur in 1973.

But since my reunion with Śrīla Prabhupāda and my Godbrothers and Godsisters I became very offensive, often thinking myself learned in the so-called details of *bhakti-yoga*. This false arrogance caused me to fall down from the eternal spiritual path and pursue material goals and worldly pleasures without restriction. I became devoid of *ātma-tattva,* as best described by Śrīla Śukadeva Goswami in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.1.4. (“Persons devoid of *ātma-tattva* do not inquire into the problems of life, being too attached to the fallible soldiers like the body, children and wife. Although sufficiently experienced, they still do not see their inevitable destruction.”) It was only by the strong bond of my love and respect for Śrī Mukunda Mahārāja that I have not completely forgotten my real self as the servant of the servants of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

“Glory to the all-merciful Rādhā and Madana-mohana! I am lame (offensive) and ill-advised (by nondevotees), yet They are my directors, and Their lotus feet are everything to me.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 1.15). I resolve to take this devotional attitude to heart, and as my humble offering to Śrīla Prabhupāda, on the occasion of his great Centennial, I vow to strictly follow his “living” instructions and thereby regain his association by *vāṇī*.

METHOD: In Śrīla Prabhupāda’s last letter to me, His Divine Grace ordered, “Worship Salagram Sila nicely, husband and wife. Do not forget to follow strictly the four regulative principles and chant sixteen rounds minimum daily.” (3/11/76); and again through his secretary (Aug. 2nd, 1977), “Śrīla Prabhupāda is pleased to know that you are chanting sixteen rounds daily of the Hare Kṛṣṇa Maha-mantra. It seems to be the arrangement of the Supreme Personality of Godhead that you are engaged in the jewelry business. … Please continue this chanting regularly, and when your business is a little successful, try to use the money for spreading the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.” This is my humble mission: to help spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement (ISKCON) by running and expanding my Vedic jewelry business, together with my devotee wife, Śrīmatī Campakalatā-devī dāsī, a disciple of Śrī Mukunda Goswami. In this regard I have taken special shelter of Śrī Mukunda Goswami, to whom I owe my life as a servant of the servants of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

“In a temple of jewels in Vṛndāvana, underneath a desire tree, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, served by their most confidential associates, sit upon an effulgent throne. I offer my humble obeisances unto Them.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 1.16). According to Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī in his *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu*, we must find a particular associate of Kṛṣṇa and we must worship Kṛṣṇa following in his or her footsteps; this is applicable both in the state of bondage as well as when one is a *siddha-puruṣa,* or completely self-realized soul. From Mukunda Mahārāja I have seen how to apply this teaching, i.e., by his humility, tolerance, and one-pointed dedication; I know that he is the senior-most devotee in ISKCON, and I wish to always show him my respect and appreciation for protecting ISKCON all these long years through his ISKCON Communications Global service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s method of worship was *pracāra,* PREACHING, spreading the glories of Kṛṣṇa world-wide; and serving a devotee fixed-up in that cause is the same as serving Kṛṣṇa in the foot-steps of the Vraja-vāsīs. I have accepted the humble service of Śrī Mukunda Goswami as my Rūpānuga method. On this occasion of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s great Centennial, I humbly offer myself and all I temporarily possess at the feet of Śrī Mukunda Goswami, and through him, to Śrīla Prabhupāda.

GOAL: *Premā pum-artho mahān!* We all know that the goal is *kṛṣṇa-prema-bhakti*. In one of the last verses of the *Brahma-saṁhitā,* it is taught that by constantly performing devotional activities, directed by Guru and *śāstra,* one will gradually realize the Supreme Self and one’s own self, and then one will have achieved Kṛṣṇa’s highest devotion. Śrīla Prabhupāda also told me that at the same time we would also realize his *svarūpa* in the spiritual world and regain his personal association in the eternal service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha.

“Śrī Śrīla Gopīnātha, who originated the transcendental mellow of the *rāsa* dance, stands on the shore in Vaṁśīvaṭa and attracts the attention of the cowherd damsels with the sound of His celebrated flute. May they all confer upon us Their benediction.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Ādi* 1.17). Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has warned us not to see beautiful Lord Kṛṣṇa on the banks of the Yamunā if we ever again want to enjoy material life. Having seen Kṛṣṇa, I tried to forget Him; but now, frustrated with material life, I pray to all my Godbrothers and Godsisters to kindly bless me with Kṛṣṇa’s and Śrīla Prabhupāda’s constant remembrance. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!!!

The servants of the servants of Śrīla Prabhupāda at the Thailand and Siam Vedic Society.  
(written by Hṛṣīkeśānanda dāsa)

## Vaiṣṇava Academy Elementary School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you, who are so merciful even to the very fallen souls of Kali-yuga.

Where would we be, Śrīla Prabhupāda, if you had not so bravely come to America decades before we were born? You spoke the truth about God, and some listeners were able to transform their lives, trusting in your words. These transformed persons were to someday become our mothers and fathers, our neighbors and teachers, our guides and well-wishers. We now live, cradled by the spiritual community you created, a life unique in the materialistic land called Florida.

Our parents don’t feed us the toasted flesh of your beloved cows; rather, we eat only that food that has been offered to you and Kṛṣṇa We gather together each morning, not to pledge allegiance to a flag but to sing the glories of the beautiful Lords Caitanya and Nityānanda. We don’t ponder so much the words of Thomas Jefferson or Winston Churchill; we hear the sublime words of Kṛṣṇa spoken to Arjuna, delivered to us by you. Our faith is not in a vague concept of a Higher Being but in the wonderful pastimes of the youthful son of Mahārāja Nanda. All of this sets us apart from our American counterparts, and all of this is your inconceivable blessing upon us.

Fifty years ago, devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa took their birth in pious Hindu families inside of India. Where else could they manifest their devotional sentiments? Who could have guessed that in such a short time there would be devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa taking birth all over the world! Only you, with your transcendent vision and faith, saw us coming. Only you considered that we would need special schools to strengthen our faith and intellect, so that we might take our part in your preaching mission. Only you worried how we would be properly trained, in accordance with our natures, to render the fullest service possible.

Although our bodies are young, we are blessed with the wisdom of the ancient sages, the knowledge of temporal human life and its ultimate goal of service to God. This knowledge has been handed to us by your disciples and followers, just as a ripe mango is passed carefully down from the heights of a tree. Please help us to receive this knowledge with proper respect and to pass it on to others. Please help us to embrace the gift of devotional service that will bring real happiness to the world, and to take our place in a transcendental society that will continue to flourish for countless generations.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Everything that we know comes from you. Everything that we aspire for is possessed by you. We love you very much.

Your servants at the Vaiṣṇava Academy Elementary School in Alachua, Florida.

## Vaiṣṇava Academy, Boys’ Division

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you  
came here  
to America  
to preach to  
fallen souls by  
your *guru’s* orders.

You didn’t know how  
you’d survive in this land so fallen.  
You endured so much, preaching to  
us, your lowly servants.

We strove to serve  
the only way we could,  
by giving you  
whatever we could.

You accepted everything  
we did and  
encouraged us to  
do more to make  
the Movement grow.

You watched as ISKCON  
grew, and you were  
pleased for it was  
your *guru’s*  
desire.

We watched  
anxiously as you  
became sick,  
hoping you wouldn’t leave us.  
You said, “Don’t worry! I’ll  
leave when Kṛṣṇa wants.”

You left us without  
a leader;  
your creation  
fell into chaos,  
but you somehow helped  
us save your work.

We are all fallible soldiers—  
some more so than others.  
As I stumble along the  
road to Kṛṣṇa, I  
fall and trip many times  
and I call out as a  
conditioned soul—  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help me serve you and your Movement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the Vaiṣṇava Academy (Boys’ Division) in Alachua, Florida.  
(written by Nāmānanda dāsa).

## Vaiṣṇava Academy, Girls’ Division

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances unto your exalted lotus feet on this most auspicious occasion. All glories to Śrī Guru-Gaurāṅga! All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa *saṅkīrtana*!

This is a very special year: it’s your Centennial. We thank you for being so kind to us fallen souls, who are drowning in the ocean of sorrow. You’ve given us the supreme chance of a lifetime, and because of this we are forever indebted to you.

Please accept this small offering from the Vaiṣṇava Academy for Girls, offered with love and devotion to your lotus feet in glorification of your great deeds for humankind. We hope this pleases you:

When Śrīla Prabhupāda was 70 years old  
He came to teach a message that was bold.  
He taught us to chant Kṛṣṇa’s holy name  
And further spread Kṛṣṇa’s endless fame.

The winds were blowing, the waves so high;  
Śrīla Prabhupāda almost died.  
By divine intervention, circumstances changed  
So Śrīla Prabhupāda could give us the holy names.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our guiding light—  
Without Kṛṣṇa consciousness our lives are like night.  
He gave us this movement as our shield  
To protect us from whatever Māyā would yield.

He came to the West to wake up all the sleeping souls.  
He came to say, “*Jīv jāgo, gaura-candra bol*!”  
He said that Kṛṣṇa’s open-armed and He’s saying  
“Wake up sleeping souls; come back home, I’m waiting.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda has melted my heart.  
I wish I could have been there from the very start,  
To see him laugh, smile, and instruct with grace  
Would make my heart beat and enthusiasm race.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, with strong conviction,  
Preached to people without restriction.  
He showed us the way to go back to God.  
We take shelter of him, our dear Prabhupāda!

He went through hardships to save the fallen souls.  
He did it for his *guru;* that was his goal.  
His mercy and compassion were ever aflow.  
He gave us our strength and watched our devotion grow.

Who knows where we’d be if it weren’t for him?  
Drowning in Māyā’s ocean, not knowing how to swim.  
He built a house in which the whole world could live.  
We thank Prabhupāda for the ecstasy he did give.

As merciful as Gaura-Nitāi,  
He came here without pride.  
He didn’t come to gain some fame;  
Just to spread Kṛṣṇa’s holy name.

He is the lord and master of devotees.  
To him I offer my most humble obeisances.  
He was a friend of both rich and poor,  
Giving us his endless mercy. Who could love us more?

Like the moon that soothes  
The earth from the scorching sun,  
His compassion toward us  
Like a father’s love for his son.

His smile shines with purity;  
It attracts souls far and wide  
To come surrender to Kṛṣṇa  
And put mundane thoughts aside.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, most beautiful was he.  
He gave us everything you need to live happily.  
His grace spreading everywhere, over land and sea,  
Eternally we take our seat at his lotus feet.

Your aspiring servants at the Vaiṣṇava Academy (Girls’ Division) in Alachua, Florida.

## Vṛndāvana Institute for Higher Education

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet.

July, 18, 1976

My friend Bernie DeAngeles, who had for months been supplying me with the latest BTGs, told me of a Hare Kṛṣṇa parade down Fifth Avenue. I decided to attend.

The whole scene was new—simultaneously attractive and strange. I decided, however, to participate as fully as my mind would let me. I already agreed with the thrust of the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but I had never met shaven-headed, wildly dancing devotees before (if I recall, Jayo dāsa was especially dancing like a madman). The enthusiasm of the devotees blended with the bright colors of their dress, the Ratha cart, the flags and balloons, and of course, Their Lordships Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā to make a feast for the eyes.

Then somewhere near 34th Street I felt a shove from behind. I turned around, and there was a group of devotees surrounding you, making a path so you could go to Subhadrā’s cart. You walked right past me. I thought to myself, “Hey, that’s Prabhupāda!” I decided to pull Subhadrā’s cart the rest of the way. I remember feeling elated when chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, but I couldn’t understand why the devotees often chanted “Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!”

Though my mind fought, I was hooked. The entire day was wonderful. I even enjoyed watching a devotee slug that foolish Christian who was disturbing your speech and carrying the sign “Turn or burn.”

From that day, I decided I wanted to become your follower.

Of course, the 1976 New York Ratha-yātrā was one of your great preaching conquests, but daily you, and your movement, are attracting souls to Kṛṣṇa—souls, like me, who had no prior interest in being attracted. Kṛṣṇa is all-attractive, but who knew it? I certainly didn’t, living my cloistered existence, filed away in the crazy world called Long Island, New York. That day, it was so obvious. Your unparalleled Kṛṣṇa consciousness, preaching spirit, and hard work brought Kṛṣṇa to life. Your presence made everyone joyful and carefree.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to be part of your joyful mission. Sure we have so many problems trying to execute your orders and dealing with a movement that is growing, shrinking, aging, resurging, uniting, dividing, etc. But the real thing is that we are trying to present the All-attractive based on your realizations, your preaching strategies, your desire. In that there is life, and a lifetime’s work. Thank you for that opportunity of service.

Your servants at the Vṛndāvana Institute for Higher Education.  
(written by Braja Bihārī dāsa)
